1. 九鍼十二原 Nine Needles and Twelve Yuan Points

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：余子萬民，養百姓而收其租稅；余哀其不給而屬有疾病。余欲勿使被毒藥，無用砭石，欲以微鍼通其經脈，調其血氣，榮其逆順出入之會。令可傳於後世，必明為之法，令終而不滅，久而不絕，易用難忘，為之經紀，異其章，別其表裏，為之終始。令各有形，先立鍼經。願聞其情。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo: "I am the father of the ten thousand people, I nurture the common folk and collect their taxes; I feel sorrow for those who cannot provide for themselves and are afflicted with illness. I wish not to have them subjected to toxic medicines or the use of moxibustion stones; I want instead to employ fine needles to unblock their meridians, regulate their blood and qi, and harmonize the convergence points where their flow is obstructed in its forward and backward movement. Let it be passed down to future generations, and I must clearly establish its methods so that they endure without fading away or being extinguished over time. Let them be easy to use and hard to forget; let this form a system of organization, distinguishing their chapters and differentiating their exterior and interior aspects, thus establishing clear beginnings and endings. Let each have its own form, beginning with the establishment of the Classic of Acupuncture. I wish to hear about their nature.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
歧伯答曰：臣請推而次之，令有綱紀，始於一，終於九焉。請言其道！小鍼之要，易陳而難入。麤守形，上守神。神乎神，客在門。未覩其疾，惡知其原？刺之微在速遲。麤守關，上守機，機之動，不離其空。空中之機，清靜而微。其來不可逢，其往不可追。知機之道者，不可掛以發。不知機道，扣之不發。知其往來，要與之期。麤之闇乎，妙哉，工獨有之。往者為逆，來者為順，明知逆順，正行無問。迎而奪之，惡得無虛？追而濟之，惡得無實？迎之隨之，以意和之，鍼道畢矣。
		Qi Bo replied: "May I respectfully present and arrange them in order, so that they have a systematic structure, beginning with one and ending with nine. Please speak of the principles! The essentials of acupuncture needles are easy to state but difficult to master. Common practitioners guard the physical form, while superior ones safeguard the spirit. Spiritual indeed is the spirit; the guest resides at the gate. Before seeing the disease, how can one know its origin? The subtlety of acupuncture lies in the speed and timing. 𝑙𝑜𝑎𝑑𝑖𝑛𝑔... 粗 practitioners guard the gates, while superior ones guard the mechanisms; when the mechanism moves, it does not depart from its emptiness. The mechanism within emptiness is clear, tranquil, and subtle. Its arrival cannot be met head-on; its departure cannot be pursued. He who understands the principle of mechanisms cannot let a single hair's weight hang in his mind. He who does not understand the mechanism, even when striking it, will not elicit a response. Knowing its coming and going, one must await its appointed time. Common practitioners remain in ignorance; how wondrous! Only the skilled master possesses this understanding. What has passed is termed "reversal," and what is coming is called "smoothness." Knowing clearly the reversal and smoothness, one proceeds correctly without hesitation. Meeting it head-on and forcefully opposing it—how can emptiness not result? Following after it and aiding its flow—how can fullness not arise? Meeting it or following it, harmonizing with the intention—this is the complete way of acupuncture.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
凡用鍼者，虛則實之，滿則泄之，宛陳則除之，邪勝則虛之。大要曰：徐而疾則實，疾而徐則虛。言實與虛，若有若無。察後與先。若存若亡。為虛與實，若得若失。
		In general, when using needles: if there is deficiency, tonify it; if fullness exists, drain it; if stagnation accumulates, remove it; and if evil influences prevail, reduce them. The essential principle says: slow insertion followed by rapid withdrawal results in tonification; rapid insertion followed by slow withdrawal results in reduction. Speaking of tonification and reduction, they are as if present or absent. Examine what comes after and what comes before. As though existing, as though gone. In creating deficiency and fullness, it is as if one gains or loses something.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
虛實之要，九鍼最妙，補寫之時，以鍼為之。寫曰，必持內之，放而出之，排陽得鍼，邪氣得泄。按而引鍼，是謂內溫，血不得散，氣不得出也。補曰，隨之隨之，意若妄之。若行若按，如蚊虻止，如留如還，去如弦絕，令左屬右，其氣故止，外門已閉，中氣乃實，必無留血，急取誅之。
		The essential aspect of tonification and reduction lies in the nine needles; at the time of supplementation or drainage, use the needle to accomplish it. For drainage: one must first hold inwardly and then release outwardly; by expelling the needle, pathogenic qi is discharged. Pressing while withdrawing the needle is called internal warming; this prevents blood from dispersing and qi from escaping. For tonification: follow it, follow it again, with an intention as if lost in thought. As if moving or pressing, like a mosquito or horsefly stopping; as if lingering and returning, departing like the sudden breaking of a string. Let the left connect to the right so that its qi naturally ceases. When the outer gate is closed, internal qi becomes solidified. There must be no stagnation of blood—quickly remove it decisively.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
持鍼之道，堅者為寶。正指直刺，無鍼左右。神在秋毫，屬意病者。審視血脈者，刺之無殆。方刺之時，必在懸陽，及與兩衛。神屬勿去，知病存亡。血脈者在俞橫居，視之獨澄，切之獨堅。
		The principle of holding the needle: firmness is most precious. Hold the finger straight and insert perpendicularly, without deviating to left or right. The spirit resides in the finest detail; one's attention must be directed toward the patient's condition. Carefully examining blood vessels, one can needle without danger. At the moment of insertion, it must be at Xuyang and its two accompanying points. Do not leave the spirit behind; understand the patient's life or death condition. Blood vessels reside horizontally at the shu points; observe them as uniquely clear, and palpate them as distinctly firm.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
九鍼之名，各不同形。一曰鑱鍼，長一寸六分；二曰員鍼，長一寸六分；三曰鍉鍼，長三寸半；四曰鋒鍼，長一寸六分；五曰鈹鍼，長四寸，廣二分半；六曰員利鍼，長一寸六分；七曰毫鍼，長三寸六分；八曰長鍼，長七寸；九曰大鍼，長四寸。鑱鍼者，頭大末銳，去寫陽氣；員鍼者，鍼如卵形，揩摩分間，不得傷肌肉，以寫分氣；鍉鍼者，鋒如黍粟之銳，主按脈勿陷，以致其氣；鋒鍼者，刃三隅以發痼疾，鈹鍼者，末如劍鋒，以取大膿；員利鍼者，大如釐，且員且銳，中身微大，以取暴氣；毫鍼者，尖如蚊虻喙，靜以徐往，微以久留之而養，以取痛痺；長鍼者，鋒利身薄，可以取遠痺；大鍼者，尖如梃，其鋒微員，以寫機關之水也。九鍼畢矣。
		The nine needles have different names and each has a distinct form. First is the Chan needle, one cun six fen in length; Second is the Yuan needle, also one cun six fen long; Third is the Di needle, three cun five fen in length; Fourth is the Feng needle, one cun six fen long. Fifth is the Bi needle, four cun in length and two cun five fen wide; Sixth is the Yuanli needle, one cun six fenlong; Seventh is the Hao needle, three cun six fen long; Eighth is the Chang needle, seven cun long; Ninth is the Da needle, four cun long. The Chan needle has a large head and sharp tip; it is used to remove excess yang qi; The Yuan needle resembles an oval in shape; it is used for rubbing and separating the tissues, without injuring muscles, to drain interstitial qi; The Di needle has a tip as sharp as millet grain; it is used to press the pulse without puncturing deeply, in order to guide its qi; The Feng needle has a three-edged tip to treat chronic and stubborn diseases; the Bi needle has an end like a sword's edge, used for draining large abscesses; The Yuanli needle is as thick as a li, round and sharp, with the middle part slightly thicker; it is used to treat sudden surges of qi; The Hao needle has a tip as fine as the beak of a mosquito or horsefly; it is inserted gently and slowly, left in place for a long time to nourish, used to treat pain and numbness; The Chang needle has a sharp, thin body; it is suitable for treating distant numbness; The Da needle has a tip like a stick, its point slightly rounded; it is used to drain water from the joints. These are all of the nine needles.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
夫氣之在脈也，邪氣在上，濁氣在中，清氣在下。故鍼陷脈則邪氣出，鍼中脈則濁氣出，鍼太深則邪氣反沉、病益。故曰：皮肉筋脈，各有所處。病各有所宜。各不同形，各以任其所宜，無實無虛。損不足而益有餘，是謂甚病。病益甚，取五脈者死，取三脈者恇；奪陰者死，奪陽者狂，鍼害畢矣。
		When qi resides in the vessels, pathogenic influences reside above, turbid qi resides in the middle, and clear qi resides below. Therefore, if a needle pierces deeply into the vessel, pathogenic influences will be expelled; if it strikes the middle of the vessel, turbid qi is removed. However, needling too deeply causes pathogenic influences to sink further and worsen the condition. Hence it is said: skin, flesh, tendons, and vessels each have their own locations. Diseases also have their respective appropriate treatments. Each has a different form, and each must be treated according to its suitability; there should be neither excess nor deficiency. Damaging what is insufficient while adding to what is already excessive—this is called a serious illness. When the disease worsens greatly, needling five vessels leads to death; needling three vessels causes fear and weakness; Depleting yin leads to death, depleting yang causes mania—this is the ultimate harm caused by acupuncture.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
刺之而氣不至，無問其數。刺之而氣至，乃去之，勿復鍼。鍼各有所宜，各不同形，各任其所，為刺之要。氣至而有效，效之信，若風之吹云，明乎若見蒼天，刺之道畢矣。
		If after needling qi does not arrive, do not question the number of insertions. When needling and the qi arrives, then remove the needle; do not reinsert it. Each needle has its appropriate use; each has a different form and is suited to specific functions—this is the essential principle of acupuncture. When qi arrives, there will be an effect; when effective, the result is clear—like wind blowing away clouds, revealing a clear blue sky. This marks the completion of the art of acupuncture.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
黃帝曰：願聞五藏六府所出之處。歧伯曰：五藏五俞，五五二十五俞，六府六俞，六六三十六俞，經脈十二，絡脈十五，凡二十七氣，以上下。所出為井，所溜為滎，所注為俞，所行為經，所入為合，二十七氣所行，皆在五俞也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear about the locations from which the five zang and six fu organs emerge." Qi Bo said: "The five zang organs have five shu points each, making twenty-five in total. The six fu organs have six shu points each, totaling thirty-six. There are twelve meridians and fifteen collateral vessels, altogether twenty-seven qi pathways that run both upward and downward." The points where the qi emerges are called Jing (Well); those where it flows are Ying (Spring); those where it is infused are Shu (Stream); those through which it travels are Jing (River); and those where it enters deeply are He (Confluence). The pathways of these twenty-seven qi all pass through the five shu points.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
節之交，三百六十五會，知其要者，一言而終，不知其要，流散無窮。所言節者，神氣之所遊行出入也。非皮肉筋骨也。
		The junctions of the joints total three hundred and sixty-five converging points. He who understands their essentials can summarize them in one sentence; he who does not grasp these essentials will find himself lost in endless dispersion. What is referred to as "joints" are the places where divine qi circulates, enters, and exits. They are not merely skin, flesh, sinews, or bones.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
觀其色，察其目，知其散復。一其形，聽其動靜，知其邪正，右主推之，左持而御之，氣至而去之。
		Observing their complexion and examining their eyes allows one to know whether the qi is dispersed or restored. Unify your form, listen to their movements and stillness, discern the nature of pathogenic or healthy qi. Use the right hand to push forward and the left hand to hold and guide; when the qi arrives, remove the needle.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
凡將用鍼，必先診脈，視氣之劇易，乃可以治也。五藏之氣，已絕於內，而用鍼者反實其外，是謂重竭。重竭必死，其死也靜。治之者輒反其氣，取腋與膺。五藏之氣，已絕於外，而用鍼者反實其內，是謂逆厥。逆厥則必死，其死也躁。治之者反取四末。
		Whenever one is about to use a needle, it is essential first to diagnose the pulse and observe whether the qi condition is severe or mild; only then can treatment be administered. When the five zang organs' qi has already ceased internally, yet one uses needles to further strengthen externally—this is termed double exhaustion. Double exhaustion inevitably leads to death, and such a death occurs quietly. Those who treat it merely reverse the qi flow, taking points at the armpit and chest. When the five zang organs' qi has already ceased externally, yet one uses needles to strengthen internally—this is termed counter-jue. Counter-jue inevitably leads to death, but such a death is marked by restlessness. Those who treat it take points at the four extremities instead.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
刺之害中而不去，則精泄；害中而去，則致氣。精泄則病益甚而恇，致氣則生為癰瘍。
		If needling harms the middle and is not removed, essence will be lost; If harm to the middle occurs but the needle is promptly removed, then qi may still arrive. When essence is lost, the disease worsens and weakness results; when qi arrives despite injury, it may lead to abscesses or sores.

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
五藏有六府，六府有十二原，十二原出於四關，四關主治五藏。五藏有疾，當取之十二原。十二原者，五藏之所以稟三百六十五節氣味也。五藏有疾也，應出十二原。
十1二原各有所出。明知其原，睹其應，而知五藏之害矣。陽中之少陰，肺也，其原出於太淵，太淵二。陽中之太陽，心也，其原出於大陵，大陵二。陰中之少陽，肝也，其原出於太沖，太沖二。陰中之至陰，脾也，其原出於太白，太白二。陰中之太陰，腎也，其原出於太溪，太溪二。膏之原，出於鳩尾，鳩尾一。肓之原，出於脖胦，脖胦一。凡此十二原者，主治五藏六府之有疾者也。
		The five zang organs are associated with the six fu organs, and the six fu organs have twelve yuan (source) points. These twelve yuan points emerge from four major passageways, which primarily govern the five zang organs. When diseases affect the five zang organs, treatment should be directed at these twelve yuan points. The twelve yuan points are where the five zang organs receive the qi and flavors of the three hundred and sixty-five joints and meridians. When diseases occur in the five zang, they manifest at these twelve yuan points. Each of the two yuan points has its own specific emergence point. Clearly understanding their yuan points and observing their responses allows one to discern the damage affecting the five zang organs. The lesser yin within yang, the lung, has its yuan point at Taiyuan; there are two Taiyuans. The greater yang within yang, the heart, has its yuan at Daling; there are two Dalings. The lesser yang within yin, the liver, has its yuan at Taichong; there are two Taichongs. The utmost yin within yin, the spleen, has its yuan at Tai Bai; there are two Tais. The greater yin within yin, kidney, has its yuan at Taixi; there are two Taixis. The yuan of gao emerges at Jiwei; there is one Jiwei. The yuan of huang emerges at Bosuo; there is one Bosuo. All these twelve yuan points mainly treat diseases of the five zang and six fu organs.

1. 十 : 舊脫。
15
打開字典
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
脹取三陽，飧泄取三陰。
		For distension, select the three yang meridians; for dysentery with loose stools, select the three yin meridians.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
今夫五藏之有疾也，譬猶刺也，猶污也，猶結也，猶閉也。刺雖久猶可拔也，污雖久猶可雪也，結雖久猶可解也，閉雖久猶可決也。或言久疾之不可取者，非其說也。夫善用鍼者，取其疾也，猶拔刺也，猶雪污也，猶解結也，猶決閉也。疾雖久，猶可畢也。言不可治者，未得其術也。
		Now, when the five zang organs suffer from disease, it is like a thorn prick, like dirt, like a knot, or like a blockage. Even if a thorn has been embedded for a long time, it can still be removed; even if dirt has remained for a long time, it may yet be cleansed; even if a knot has persisted for a long time, it remains possible to untie it; and even if a blockage has lasted for a long time, there is still hope of clearing it. Those who say that chronic diseases cannot be treated are not correct in their assertion. A skilled practitioner of acupuncture treats diseases just as one removes a thorn, cleanses dirt, unties a knot, or clears a blockage. Even if the disease has persisted for a long time, complete recovery is still possible. Those who claim that it cannot be cured have not mastered the proper method.

17
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼十二... :	
刺諸熱者，如以手探湯；刺寒清者，如人不欲行。陰有陽疾者，取之下陵三里，正往無殆，氣下乃止，不下復始也。疾高而內者，取之陰之陵泉；疾高而外者，取之陽之陵泉也。
		Treating heat conditions is like plunging one's hand into hot soup; treating cold and cool conditions is like a person who does not wish to move. When yin is affected by yang disease, treat at Xiaoling Sanli (Three Miles Below the Knee). Proceed directly without hesitation; when the qi descends, stop treatment. If it does not descend, begin again. When the disease is high but inward, treat at Yinqiaquan of yin; łoż When the disease is high and outward, treat at Yangqiaquan of yang; 

2. 本輸 Transport Points

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本輸:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：凡刺之道，必通十二經絡之所終始，絡脈之所別處，五俞之所留，六府之所與合，四時之所出入，五藏之所溜處，闊數之度，淺深之狀，高下所至。願聞其解。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Chi Bo, "In the art of acupuncture, one must understand the beginning and end of the twelve meridians, where the collateral vessels branch off, where the five Shu points reside, where the six fu organs converge, how the four seasons enter and exit, where the five zang organs circulate, the spacing and measurements, the depth and condition, and to what heights and depths they reach." I would like to hear your explanation.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本輸:	
歧伯曰：請言其次也。肺出於少商，少商者，手大指端內側也，為井木；溜于魚際，魚際者，手魚也，為滎；注于太淵，太淵魚後一寸陷者中也，為俞；行於經渠，經渠寸口中也，動而不居為經；入于尺澤，尺澤肘中之動脈也，為合。手太陰經也。
		Chi Bo said, "Please allow me to explain in order." The lung emerges at Shaoshang. Shaoshang is located on the inner side of the tip of the thumb; it is classified as Jing (Well) point of Wood; it flows to Yuji. Yuji refers to the fish-shaped area on the hand, and it is classified as Ying (Spring) point; it flows into Taiyuan. Taiyuan is located one cun behind the fish-shaped area, in a depressed spot; it is classified as Shu (Stream) point; it travels through Jingqu. Jingqu is located at the center of the radial artery in the wrist, where it moves but does not reside; it is classified as Jing (River) point; it enters Chize. Chize is located at the pulsating artery in the center of the elbow, and it is classified as He (Confluence) point. This is the Taiyin meridian of the hand.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本輸:	
心出於中沖，中沖，手中指之端也，為井木；流於勞宮，勞宮掌中中指本節之內間也，為滎；注于大陵，大陵掌後兩骨之間方下者也，為俞；行於間使，間使之道，兩筋之間，三寸之中也，有過則至，無過則止，為經；入于曲澤，曲澤，肘內廉下陷者之中也，屈而得之，為合。手少陰也。
		The heart emerges at Zhongchong. Zhongchong is located on the tip of the middle finger; it is classified as Jing (well) point of Wood; it flows to Laogong. Laogong is located in the palm, between the base joint of the middle finger and the center; it is classified as Ying (spring) point; it flows into Daling. Daling is located behind the palm, in the square depression between two bones; it is classified as Shu(stream) point; it travels through Jian Shi. The path of Jian Shi is between two tendons, in the middle three cun; it reaches if there is excess, and stops if there is none; it is classified as Jing(river) point; it enters Quze. Quze is located in the depression below the inner side of the elbow, which can be obtained by bending the arm; it is classified as He(confluence) point. This is the Shaoyin meridian of the hand.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本輸:

		The liver emerges at Dadun. Dadun is located on the tip of the big toe and in the area where three hairs meet; it is classified as a Jing (Well) point of Wood. It flows into Taichong, which lies two cun above Xingjian in the depressed area; it is classified as a Shu (Stream) point. It travels through Zhongfeng, which is located one and a half cun in front of the medial malleolus in the depressed area. If the qi flows against it, it becomes obstructed; if harmonized, it becomes unobstructed. It can be obtained by moving the foot; it is classified as an Jing (River) point. It enters Ququan, which lies beneath the supporting bone and above a large tendon; it can be obtained by bending the knee; it is classified as a He (Confluence) point. This is the Jueyin meridian of the foot.

	本輸:	
春取絡脈諸滎大經分肉之間，甚者深取之，間者淺取之。夏取諸俞孫絡肌肉皮膚之上。秋取諸合，餘如春法。冬取諸井諸俞之分，欲深而留之。此四時之序，氣之所處，病之所舍，藏之所宜。轉筋者，立而取之，可令遂已。痿厥者，張而刺之，可令立快也。
		In spring, select collateral vessels at the Ying points between major meridians and muscle layers; for severe conditions, needle deeply; for mild ones, needle shallowly. In summer, select Shu points at the level of collateral vessels on muscles and skin surfaces. In autumn, select He points; otherwise follow spring methods. In winter, select Jing and Shu points in their respective regions, needling deeply and retaining the needle. This is the sequence of four seasons, where qi resides, where disease lodges, and what zang organs are appropriate. For cramping muscles, needle immediately while standing; this can quickly relieve the condition. For atrophy and cold extremities, stretch the body before needling; this can bring immediate relief. 

3. 小鍼解 Explanation of the Small Needle

1
打開字典
顯示影印本
	小鍼解:	
所謂易陳者，易言也。難入者，難著于人也。麤守形者，守刺法也。上守神者，守人之血氣有餘不足可補寫也。神客者，正邪共會也。神者，正氣也，客者邪氣也。在門者，邪循正氣之所出入也。未睹其疾者，先知邪正何經之疾也。惡知其原者，先知何經之病所取之處也。
		What is called Yi Chen is simply easy to speak. Those who are difficult to enter are those who find it hard to be understood by others. The one who coarsely guards the form is guarding the acupuncture method. The superior guardian of spirit guards whether a person's vital energy and blood are in excess or deficient, thus determining if they can be supplemented or drained. Those who host the spirit are where the righteous and pathogenic influences converge. The "Shen" refers to the righteous qi, while the "Ke" refers to the pathogenic qi. Those at the gate are where the pathogenic influences follow the pathways of the righteous qi in and out. Before seeing the disease, one should first know which meridian is affected by the pathogenic or righteous qi. To hate knowing its source means to first understand where on which meridian the disease should be treated.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	小鍼解:	
刺之微在數遲者，徐疾之意也。麤守關者，守四肢而不知血氣正邪之往來也。上守機者，知守氣也。機之動不離其空中者，知氣之虛實，用鍼之徐疾也。空中之機，清淨以微者，鍼以得氣，密意守氣勿失也。其來不可逢者，氣盛不可補也。其往不可追者，氣虛不可寫也。不可掛以髮者，言氣易失也。扣之不發者，言不知補寫之意也。血氣已盡而氣不下也。
		The subtlety of acupuncture in terms of speed and slowness refers to the meaning of slow or fast manipulation. The one who coarsely guards at the pass merely guards the limbs without knowing the ebb and flow of vital energy, blood, righteous, and pathogenic influences. The superior guardian of opportunity knows how to guard the qi. When the movement of the mechanism does not leave its space, it means understanding whether the qi is vacuous or solid and employing acupuncture with slow or rapid manipulation. The mechanism in space, clear and subtle, allows the needle to obtain qi; one must carefully guard the qi without losing it. What comes cannot be met head-on, for when the qi is excessive, it should not be supplemented. What has gone cannot be pursued, for when the qi is deficient, it should not be drained. It cannot be held by a hair, meaning that the qi is easily lost. If striking it does not elicit a response, it means one does not understand the principles of supplementation and drainage. The blood and qi have already been exhausted, so the qi cannot descend further.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	小鍼解:	
知其往來者，知氣之逆順盛虛也。要與之期者，知氣之可取之時也。麤之闇者，冥冥不知氣之微密也。妙哉！工獨有之者，盡知鍼意也。往者為逆者，言氣之虛而小，小者逆也。來者為順者，言形氣之平，平者順也。明知逆順正行無間者，言知所取之處也。迎而奪之者，寫也；追而濟之者，補也。
		To know its coming and going is to understand whether the qi flows in a normal or abnormal direction, and whether it is excessive or deficient. To await an appointment with it means knowing the appropriate time to obtain the qi. The coarse and ignorant are in darkness, unaware of the subtle and intricate nature of qi. How wondrous! Only the skilled practitioner possesses this, for they fully understand the intent of acupuncture. What goes away is called "Ni," meaning the qi is deficient and weak; weakness signifies reversal. What comes is called "Shun," meaning the form and qi are balanced; balance signifies harmony. To clearly know the reversal, harmony, and correct flow without interruption means knowing where to take action. Meeting it head-on and taking from it is drainage; Following after it and assisting it is supplementation.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	小鍼解:	
所謂虛則實之者，氣口虛而當補之也。滿則泄之者，氣口盛而當寫之也。宛陳則除之者，去血脈也。邪勝則虛之者，言諸經有盛者，皆寫其邪也。徐而疾則實者，言徐內而疾出也。疾而徐則虛者，言疾內而徐出也。言實與虛若有若無者，言實者有氣，虛者無氣也。察後與先若亡若存者，言氣之虛實，補寫之先後也，察其氣之已下與常存也。為虛為實，若得若失者，言補者佖然若有得也，寫則怳然若有失也。
		What is meant by "when deficiency occurs, one should tonify" refers to a vacuous Qi Kou that should be supplemented. "When fullness occurs, one should drain it" means when the Qi Kou is excessive, one should perform drainage. "To remove what lingers and accumulates" refers to eliminating stagnant blood vessels. "When pathogenic influences prevail, one should weaken them," meaning that when any meridian is excessive, the pathogenic factors in it should be drained. Slow insertion followed by rapid withdrawal results in tonification. Rapid insertion followed by slow withdrawal results in depletion. To say that tonification and depletion are as if present or absent means that when there is qi, it is a case of tonification; when there is no qi, it is one of depletion. To observe what comes after and before, as if lost or present, refers to understanding the order of supplementation and drainage according to whether the qi has already descended or remains constant. To be vacuous or substantial, as if gained or lost, means that during supplementation one feels a clear sense of gain, while during drainage one feels an ambiguous sense of loss.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	小鍼解:	
夫氣之在脈也，邪氣在上者，言邪氣之中人也高，故邪氣在上也。濁氣在中者，言水穀皆入于胃，其精氣上注于肺，濁溜于腸胃，言寒溫不適，飲食不節，而病生于腸胃，故命曰濁氣在中也。清氣在下者，言清濕地氣之中人也，必從足始，故曰清氣在下也。鍼陷脈，則邪氣出者取之上，鍼中脈則濁氣出者，取之陽明合也。鍼太深則邪氣反沉者，言淺浮之病，不欲深刺也。深則邪氣從之入，故曰反沉也。皮肉筋脈各有所處者，言經絡各有所主也。取五脈者死，言病在中氣不足，但用鍼盡大寫其諸陰之脈也。取三陽之脈者，唯言盡寫三陽之氣，令病人恇然不復也。奪陰者死，言取尺之五裏五往者也。奪陽者狂，正言也。
		When qi resides in the vessels, if pathogenic influences are located above, it means that these harmful forces have invaded a high part of the body; thus, they reside at an upper level. Impure qi residing in the middle refers to the situation where water and grains all enter the stomach, with its refined essence ascending to nourish the lungs while impurities flow into the intestines and stomach. This condition arises from improper regulation of temperature exposure or dietary habits, leading to illness within the gastrointestinal tract; thus it is termed "impure qi in the middle." Clear qi residing below refers to how damp, earthy qi affects people—it must begin from the feet. Hence it is said that clear qi resides at the lower part. When a needle pierces deeply into the vessels and pathogenic qi is expelled, it should be taken from above; when a needle strikes the middle of the vessel and impure qi is expelled, it corresponds to the Yangming Confluence points. If a needle is inserted too deeply and pathogenic qi sinks further, it means that for superficial or floating illnesses, deep needling should be avoided. Needling too deeply allows the pathogenic influences to follow and enter further; hence it is called "reversely sinking." The skin, flesh, tendons, and vessels each have their respective locations, meaning the meridians and collaterals each govern specific areas. Needling five vessels leads to death, meaning when the disease resides in a state of middle qi deficiency and needles are used excessively to drain all yin meridians. Needling the three yang vessels refers only to completely draining the three yang qi, causing the patient to become frightened and unable to recover. Depriving yin leads to death; this refers to needling the Wuli point on the Cun (inch) level of the pulse five times. Depriving yang causes mania—this is a correct statement.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	小鍼解:	
睹其色，察其目，知其散復，一其形，聽其動靜者，言上工知相五色於目。有知調尺寸小大緩急滑澀以言所病也。知其邪正者，知論虛邪與正邪之風也。右主推之，左持而御之者，言持鍼而出入也。氣至而去之者，言補寫氣調而去之也。調氣在于終始一者，持心也。節之交三百六十五會者，絡脈之滲灌諸節者也。
		Observing one's complexion, examining the eyes, understanding whether the condition is dispersed or restored, unifying the form, and listening to movements and stillness—this means a superior physician knows how to diagnose the five colors through the eyes. There is knowledge of adjusting the size, speed, smoothness, or roughness of the Cun and Chi pulses to determine the illness. To know its righteous and pathogenic aspects is to understand the discussion on vacuous evils and righteous evils caused by wind. The right hand pushes forward, while the left hand holds and controls it—this refers to holding the needle for insertion and withdrawal. When the qi arrives and is then released means that after adjusting the supplementation or drainage of the qi, one removes the needle. Regulating the qi lies in maintaining consistency from beginning to end—this refers to a focused and steady mind. The 365 junctions of the joints refer to the collateral vessels that seep and irrigate all the joint areas.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	小鍼解:	
所謂五藏之氣，已絕于內者，脈口氣內絕不至，反取其外之病處，與陽經之合，有留鍼以致陽氣，陽氣至則內重竭，重竭則死矣。其死也，無氣以動，故靜。所謂五藏之氣，已絕于外者，脈口氣外絕不至，反取其四末之輸，有留鍼以致其陰氣，陰氣至則陽氣反入，入則逆，逆則死矣。其死也，陰氣有餘，故躁。
		What is referred to as the five zang qi having already ceased internally means that the pulse at the Cun Kou and internal qi have completely cut off. If one instead takes points on external disease locations and the yang meridians' He (Confluence) points, leaving needles there in an attempt to summon yang qi, when yang qi arrives it will cause further exhaustion of the interior; such extreme depletion leads to death. When they die, there is no qi to move them, hence stillness. What is referred to as the qi of the five zang having already ceased externally means that at the pulse Cun Kou, external qi has completely cut off. If instead one takes points on the four extremities' Shu (Transport) points and leaves needles there in an attempt to bring yin qi inward, when yin qi arrives yang qi will be driven back inside; this reversal causes disharmony, which leads to death. When they die, the yin qi is excessive, hence agitation.

	小鍼解:	
所以察其目者，五藏使五色循明。循明則聲章。聲章者，則言聲與平生異也。
		The reason for examining the eyes is that the five zang organs manifest their five colors through clarity and brightness. When the clarity follows, the voice becomes distinct. Distinctive voice means that it differs from what is normal in life. 

4. 邪氣藏府病形 Pathogenic Influences on Zang-Fu Organs and Their Disease Manifestations

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：邪氣之中人也奈何？歧伯答曰：邪氣之中人高也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "How does pathogenic qi invade a person?" Qi Bo replied, "Pathogenic qi invades the upper part of the body."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：高下有度乎？歧伯曰：身半以上者，邪中之也。身半已下者，濕中之也。故曰：邪之中人也。無有常，中于陰則溜于府，中于陽則溜于經。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "Is there a standard for what is high or low?" Qi Bo said, "Pathogenic qi invades above the middle of the body." Below the middle of the body, dampness invades. Therefore it is said: "Pathogenic qi invading a person." There is no fixed pattern; if it invades the yin, it flows into the fu-organs, and if it invades the yang, it flows into the meridians.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：陰之與陽也，異名同類，上下相會，經絡之相貫，如環無端。邪之中人，或中于陰，或中于陽，上下左右，無有恆常，其故何也？
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Yin and yang have different names but belong to the same category. They meet from above and below; the meridians and collaterals interconnect like a ring without end." The invasion of pathogenic qi into the body may strike yin or yang, upper or lower parts, left or right sides—there is no constant pattern. What is the reason for this?

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
歧伯曰：諸陽之會，皆在于面。中人也，方乘虛時及新用力，若飲食汗出，腠理開而中于邪。中于面，則下陽明。中于項，則下太陽。中于頰，則下少陽。其中于膺背兩脅，亦中其經。
		Qi Bo said, "The convergence of all yang meridians occurs on the face." When invading a person, it takes advantage of moments when the body is weakened or after exertion, such as during perspiration from eating and drinking, when the pores are open and pathogenic qi can enter. łoż If invaded at the face, it descends to the Yangming meridian. If invaded at the nape of the neck, it descends to the Taiyang meridian. If invaded at the cheek, it descends to the Shaoyang meridian. When invading the chest, back, and both hypochondriac regions, it also strikes the corresponding meridians.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：其中于陰，奈何？歧伯答曰：中于陰者，常從臂胻始。夫臂與胻，其陰皮薄，其肉淖澤，故俱受于風，獨傷其陰。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What if pathogenic qi invades yin?" Qi Bo replied, "When invading yin, it often begins from the arms and shins." The arms and shins have thin yin skin, and their flesh is moist and slippery; therefore, when both are exposed to wind, it uniquely harms the yin.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：此故傷其藏乎？歧伯答曰：身之中於風也，不必動藏。故邪入于陰經，則其藏氣實，邪氣入而不能客，故還之於府。故中陽則溜于經，中陰則溜于府。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "Does this then injure the zang-organs?" Qi Bo replied, "When the body is invaded by wind, it does not necessarily affect the zang-organs." Therefore, when pathogenic qi enters a yin meridian, if the qi of the zang-organ is strong, the pathogenic qi cannot remain and thus returns to the fu-organs. Therefore, when invading yang it flows into the meridians, and when invading yin it flows into the fu-organs.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：邪之中人藏奈何？歧伯曰：愁憂恐懼則傷心。形寒寒飲則傷肺，以其兩寒相感，中外皆傷，故氣逆而上行。有所墮墜，惡血留內；若有所大怒，氣上而不下，積於脅下，則傷肝。有所擊仆，若醉入房，汗出當風，則傷脾。有所用力舉重，若入房過度，汗出浴水，則傷腎。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "How does pathogenic qi invade the zang-organs?" Qi Bo said, "Worry, sorrow, and fear injure the heart." Cold exposure combined with consumption of cold food or drink injures the lungs, as internal and external cold interact to harm both internally and externally; thus, qi rebels and moves upward. If someone falls from a height, stagnant blood remains inside; Or if one experiences intense anger, the qi rises upward and does not descend, accumulating beneath the hypochondrium, thus injuring the liver. If someone is struck or falls down, or enters a room while drunk and perspires in the presence of wind, it injures the spleen. If someone exerts excessive force to lift heavy objects or engages in sexual activity excessively, and then bathes in cold water while perspiring, it injures the kidneys.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：五藏之中風，奈何？歧伯曰：陰陽俱感，邪乃得往。黃帝曰：善哉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "How does wind invade the five zang-organs?" Qi Bo said, "When both yin and yang are affected, pathogenic qi is able to enter." The Yellow Emperor said, "Well said."

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：首面與身形也，屬骨連筋，同血合於氣耳。天寒則裂地凌冰，其卒寒，或手足懈惰，然而其面不衣，何也？歧伯答曰：十二經脈，三百六十五絡，其血氣皆上于面而走空竅。其精陽氣上走於目而為睛。其別氣走於耳而為聽。其宗氣上出於鼻而為臭。其濁氣出於胃，走唇舌而為味。其氣之津液，皆上燻于面，而皮又厚，其肉堅，故天氣甚寒，不能勝之也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo: "The head and face together with the body are connected by bones and tendons, sharing blood and qi." When the weather is cold, the earth cracks and ice forms; sudden cold may cause the hands and feet to become sluggish or inactive. Yet the face does not wear clothing—why is this? Qi Bo replied: "The twelve meridians and 365 collateral vessels all direct their blood and qi upward to the face, which then flows into the orifices." Its refined yang qi ascends to the eyes and becomes the clarity of vision. The collateral qi reaches the ears and enables hearing. Its zongqi rises from the nose to produce the sense of smell. The turbid qi arises from the stomach, reaches the lips and tongue, and produces taste. The body's jinye (body fluids) all ascend to nourish the face, and since the skin is thick and the flesh firm, even very cold weather cannot overcome it.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：邪之中人，其病形何如？歧伯曰：虛邪之中身也，洒淅動形。正邪之中人也，微，先見于色，不知于身，若有若無，若亡若存，有形無形，莫知其情。黃帝曰：善哉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When pathogenic qi invades a person, what are its manifestations?" łó Qi Bo said: "When xie (pathogenic) wind invades the body, it causes chills and shivering, with visible physical agitation." When zheng (direct) pathogenic factors invade a person, the symptoms are subtle; they first appear in the complexion and are not yet felt by the body. They seem to be present or absent, existing or non-existent—having form without definite shape, their true nature is difficult to discern. The Yellow Emperor said: "Well said."

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：余聞之，見其色，知其病，命曰明。按其脈，知其病，命曰神。問其病，知其處，命曰工。余願聞見而知之，按而得之，問而極之，為之奈何？
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo: "I have heard that one who sees the complexion and knows the disease is called 'ming' (wise)." One who palpates the pulse and understands the disease is called "shen" (divine). One who asks about the illness and knows its location is called "gong" (skillful). I wish to learn how one can know the disease by observation, discern it through palpation, and fully understand it by inquiry. How may this be done?

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
歧伯答曰：夫色脈與尺之相應也，如桴鼓影響之相應也，不得相失也，此亦本末根葉之出候也，故根死則葉枯矣。色脈形肉，不得相失也。故知一則為工，知二則為神，知三則神且明矣。
		Qi Bo replied: "The relationship between complexion, pulse, and the cun (inch) measurement is like that of a drumstick striking a drum or an echo responding to sound—they must correspond without deviation. This also reflects the principle of root and branch, stem and leaf in diagnosis; therefore, if the root perishes, the leaves wither." The complexion, pulse, body form, and flesh must not be out of harmony. Therefore, one who understands one aspect is skillful; one who understands two aspects is divine; and one who understands three aspects is both divine and wise.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯答曰：色青者，其脈弦也，赤者，其脈鉤也，黃者，其脈代也，白者，其脈毛，黑者，其脈石。見其色而不得其脈，反得其相勝之脈，則死矣；得其相生之脈，則病已矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear it in full." Qi Bo replied: "A blue complexion corresponds to a xuan (tense) pulse; a red complexion corresponds to a gou (hollow, hook-like) pulse; a yellow complexion corresponds to a dai (irregularly intermittent) pulse; a white complexion corresponds to a mao (floating and fine) pulse; and a black complexion corresponds to a shi (deep and heavy) pulse." If one sees the complexion but does not find the corresponding pulse, instead finding a pulse that is incompatible with it, then death will occur; If one finds a pulse compatible and harmonious with the complexion, then the illness will be cured.

14
打開字典
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：五藏之所生，變化之病形何如？
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo: "What are the forms of diseases resulting from changes in the five zang-organs?"

15
打開字典
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
歧伯答曰：先定其五色五脈之應，其病乃可別也。
		Qi Bo replied: "First determine the correspondence between the five complexions and the five pulses; only then can the disease be differentiated."

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：色脈已定，別之奈何？
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Once complexion and pulse are determined, how should one differentiate them?"

17
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
歧伯說：調其脈之緩、急、小、大、滑、澀，而病變定矣。
		Qi Bo said: "By adjusting the pulse's qualities of slow, rapid, small, large, slippery, or dry, the nature of the disease can be determined."

18
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：調之奈何？歧伯答曰：脈急者，尺之皮膚亦急；脈緩者，尺之皮膚亦緩；脈小者，尺之皮膚亦減而少氣；脈大者，尺之皮膚亦賁而起；脈滑者，尺之皮膚亦滑；脈澀者，尺之皮膚亦澀。凡此變者，有微有甚。故善調尺者，不待於寸，善調脈者，不待於色。能參合而行之者，可以為上工，上工十全九。行二者，為中工，中工十全七。行一者，為下工，下工十全六。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How should one adjust it?" Qi Bo replied: "When the pulse is taut, the skin at the cun position is also tense;" when the pulse is slow, the skin at the cun is also relaxed; when the pulse is small, the skin at the cun becomes thinner and deficient in qi; when the pulse is large, the skin at the cun swells and rises; when the pulse is slippery, the skin at the cun also feels slippery; when the pulse is dry and rough, the skin at the cun likewise feels dry and rough. All these changes vary in degree, some being subtle and others more pronounced. Therefore, those skilled in examining the cun measurement do not need to wait for signs from the inch position; those skilled in pulse diagnosis do not rely on complexion. Those who can integrate and apply these methods are considered superior physicians, and such superior physicians achieve nine out of ten successful outcomes. Those who practice only two of the methods are considered intermediate physicians, achieving seven out of ten successes. Those who apply only one method are considered inferior physicians, achieving six out of ten successful outcomes.

19
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：請問脈之緩、急，小、大，滑、澀之病形何如？
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Please explain the disease manifestations associated with a pulse that is slow, taut, small, large, slippery or dry."

20
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
歧伯曰：臣請言五藏之病變也。心脈急甚者為瘈瘲；徵急，為心痛引背，食不下。緩甚，為狂笑；微緩，為伏梁，在心下，上下行，時唾血。大甚，為喉吤；微大，為心痺引背，善淚出。小甚為善噦；微小為消病。滑甚為善渴；微滑為心疝，引臍，小腹鳴。澀為為瘖；微澀為血溢，維厥耳鳴，顛疾。
		Qi Bo said: "I will now describe the pathological changes of the five zang-organs." When the heart pulse is extremely taut, it results in convulsions. When the zheng (fire) aspect is excessively taut, it causes chest pain radiating to the back and difficulty swallowing food. If excessive in slowness, it results in maniacal laughter; if slightly slow, it manifests as fuliang (a type of abdominal mass), located beneath the heart, moving up and down, with occasional hemoptysis. If excessive in largeness, it causes sore throat; if slightly large, it results in heart bi (a type of obstruction syndrome) radiating to the back, with frequent tearing. If excessive in smallness, it causes frequent sighing; if slightly small, it indicates a wasting disease. If excessive in slipperiness, it causes intense thirst; if slightly slippery, it indicates a heart hernia, drawing to the navel, with rumbling in the lower abdomen. If dry and rough (sè), it causes aphonia; if slightly dry and rough, it results in blood overflow, vertigo with tinnitus, and dizziness.

21
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
肺脈急甚，為癲疾；微急，為肺寒熱，怠惰，咳唾血，引腰背胸，若鼻息肉不通。緩甚，為多汗；微緩，為痿，痿，偏風，頭以下汗出不可止。大甚，為脛腫；微大，為肺痺，引胸背，起惡見日光。小甚，為泄；微小，為消痺。滑甚，為息賁上氣；微滑，為上下出血。澀甚，為嘔血；微澀，為鼠，在頸支腋之間，下不勝其上，其應善痠矣。
		When the lung pulse is extremely taut, epilepsy occurs; if slightly taut, it indicates cold and heat in the lungs, fatigue, coughing with blood, pain radiating to the waist, back, and chest, or nasal polyps causing obstruction. If excessive in slowness causes profuse sweating; if slightly slow, it results in withering syndrome (wěi bìng), which may manifest as hemiplegia or stroke-like symptoms, with profuse sweating from the head downward that cannot be stopped. If excessive in largeness causes swelling of the shins; if slightly large, it indicates lung bi (obstruction syndrome), causing pain radiating to the chest and back, with aversion to light. If excessive in smallness causes diarrhea; if slightly small, it results in xiao bi (a wasting and obstruction syndrome). If excessive in slipperiness causes dyspnea with upward rushing qi; if slightly slippery, it results in bleeding from upper and lower orifices. If excessive dryness causes vomiting of blood; if slightly dry, it indicates "shǔ" (a type of tumor or swelling), located between the neck and armpit. The lower part cannot support the upper, and its manifestation is frequent sour taste in the mouth.

22
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
肝脈急甚者為惡言；微急為肥氣在脅下，若復杯。緩甚為善嘔，微緩為水瘕痺也。大甚為內癰，善嘔衄；微大為肝痺，陰縮，咳引小腹。小甚為多飲；微小為消癉。滑甚為㿉疝；微滑為遺溺。澀甚為溢飲；微澀為瘈攣筋痺。
		When the liver pulse is extremely taut, one may speak harshly or with hostility; if slightly taut indicates "fei qi" (a type of distention), located beneath the hypochondrium, as if a cup were placed there. If excessive in slowness results in frequent vomiting; if slightly slow indicates water accumulation and obstruction (shui jia bi). If excessively large, it signifies an internal abscess, with a tendency to vomit blood or nosebleeds; if slightly large indicates liver obstruction (gan bi), with retraction of the genitalia and coughing that draws pain to the lower abdomen. If excessive smallness results in excessive thirst; if slightly small indicates xiao luan (a wasting syndrome). If excessively slippery, it signifies a carbuncle or abscess; if slightly slippery indicates enuresis. If excessively dry, it signifies excessive fluid retention; if slightly dry indicates convulsions and spasticity of the tendons (chi luan jin bi).

打開字典

	邪氣藏府... :	
脾脈急甚為瘈瘲；微急為膈中，食飲入而還出，後沃沫。緩甚為痿厥；微緩為風痿，四肢不用，心慧然若無病。大甚為擊仆；微大為疝氣，腹裏大膿血在腸胃之外。小甚為寒熱；微小為消癉。滑甚為㿉癃；微滑為蟲毒蛕蝎腹熱。澀甚為腸㿉；微澀為內㿉，多下膿血。
		When the spleen pulse is extremely taut, it results in convulsions; if slightly taut indicates a condition between the diaphragm, where food and drink are ingested but regurgitated back out, followed by frothy saliva. If excessively slow results in atrophy and cold extremities (wei jue); if slightly slow indicates wind-induced atrophy (feng wei), with the four limbs being paralyzed, yet the mind is clear as if no illness exists. If excessively large results in sudden collapse or fainting; if slightly large indicates hernia with a mass of pus and blood inside the abdomen, located outside the intestines. If excessive smallness causes alternating chills and fever; if slightly small indicates xiao lun (a wasting syndrome). If excessively slippery results in carbuncles and urinary retention; if slightly slippery indicates parasitic toxins, such as roundworms or scorpions, causing abdominal heat. If excessively dry results in intestinal abscesses; if slightly dry indicates internal abscess, with frequent discharge of pus and blood.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
腎脈急甚為骨癲疾；微急為沉厥奔豚，足不收，不得前後。緩甚為折脊；微緩為洞，洞者，食不化，下嗌逐出。大甚為陰痿；微大為石水，起臍已下至小腹睡睡然，上至胃脘，死不治。小甚為洞泄；微小為消癉。滑甚為癃㿉；微滑為骨痿，坐不能起，起則目無所見。澀甚為大癰；微澀為不月，沉痔。
		When the kidney pulse is extremely taut results in bone-related epilepsy; if slightly taut indicates deep cold obstruction and running pig syndrome (chun jue bentun), with the feet not being controlled, unable to move forward or backward. If excessively slow results in a broken spine; if slightly slow indicates "dong" (a digestive disorder), where food is not properly digested and expelled immediately after being swallowed. If excessively large results in genital atrophy; if slightly large indicates stone water (shi shui), which begins below the navel and extends to the lower abdomen, feeling as if sleeping there; it may ascend to the epigastrium, and is fatal if left untreated. If excessive smallness results in dysentery with complete evacuation (dou xie); if slightly small indicates wasting syndrome (xiao lun). If excessively slippery results in urinary retention and abscesses; if slightly slippery indicates bone atrophy (gu wei), where one cannot sit up, and upon standing, sees nothing. If excessively dry results in a large abscess; lø if slightly dry indicates absence of menstruation and hemorrhoids.

25
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：病之六變者，刺之奈何？歧伯答曰：諸急者多寒；緩者多熱；大者多氣少血；小者血氣皆少；滑者陽氣盛，微有熱；澀者多血、少氣，微有寒。是故刺急者，深內而久留之；刺緩者，淺內而疾發鍼，以去其熱；刺大者，微寫其氣，無出其血；刺滑者，疾發鍼而淺內之，以寫其陽氣而去其熱；刺澀者，必中其脈，隨其逆順而久留之，必先按而循之，已發鍼，已按其疝，無令其血出，以和其脈；諸小者，陰陽形氣俱不足，勿取以鍼而調以甘藥也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "For the six transformations of disease, how should acupuncture be applied?" Qi Bo replied: "Those with excessive taut pulses are mostly affected by cold; those with slow pulses are mostly affected by heat; those with large pulses have excessive qi and deficient blood; those with small pulses have both deficient blood and qi; those with slippery pulses indicate excessive yang qi, slightly hot; those with dry pulses have abundant blood but deficient qi, and are slightly cold." Therefore, for those with taut pulses, acupuncture should be inserted deeply and retained for a long time; for those with slow pulses, insert the needle shallowly and remove it quickly to dispel heat; for those with large pulses, slightly drain their qi without drawing blood; for those with slippery pulses, remove the needle quickly after shallow insertion to drain excessive yang and dispel heat; for those with dry pulses, the needle must strike the pulse directly. Follow its direction or reverse it and retain for a long time. First press and trace along the meridian before inserting the needle; after removing the needle, press on the area to prevent bleeding, in order to harmonize the pulse; Those with small pulses indicate insufficiency of both yin and yang, as well as deficiency in form and qi. Do not treat them with acupuncture; instead, regulate their condition using sweet-tasting medicines.

顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：余聞五藏六府之氣，滎、俞所入為合，令何道從入，入安連過，願聞其故。歧伯答曰：此陽脈之別入于內，屬於府者也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I have heard that the qi of the five zang and six fu organs flows into the He points through their Ying and Shu points. I would like to know by what pathways they enter, how they pass through, and wish to understand the reason behind this." Qi Bo replied: "These are the divergent pathways of yang meridians that branch inward and connect to the fu organs."

		The Yellow Emperor said: "Do the Ying, Shu, and He points each have their own names?" Qi Bo replied: "Ying and Shu points treat conditions of the exterior meridians, while He points treat interior fu organs."

	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：合各有名乎？歧伯答曰：胃合於三里，大腸合入于巨虛上廉，小腸合入于巨虛下廉，三焦合入于委陽，膀胱合入于委中央，膽合入于陽陵泉。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Do the He points each have specific names?" Qi Bo replied: "The stomach connects with the point Zusanli (ST36); the large intestine enters at Shanglian of Ji xu (ST37); the small intestine enters at Xilian of Ji xu (ST41); the triple energizer enters at Weiyang (BL58); the bladder enters at Weizhongyang (BL40); and the gallbladder enters at Yanglingquan (GB34)."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：取之奈何？歧伯答曰：取之三里者，低跗取之；巨虛者，舉足取之；委陽者，屈伸而索之；委中者，屈而取之；陽陵泉者，正豎膝予之齊下，至委陽之陽取之；取諸外經者，揄申而從之。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "How should they be needled?" Qi Bo replied: "To needle Zusanli (ST36), lower the foot and locate it; for Ji xu, position is obtained by raising the leg; for Weiyang (BL58), locate it by flexing and extending the knee; for Weizhongyang (BL41), needle it while the knee is bent; and for Yanglingquan (GB35), locate it by straightening the knee, below the hip to Weiyang on its yang aspect." For points along the exterior meridians, stretch and extend the limb accordingly.

		The Yellow Emperor said: "I would like to hear about diseases of the six fu organs." Qi Bo replied: "Facial heat indicates disease of the foot Yangming; blood vessels on the fish part of the hand indicate disease of the hand Yangming. Vertical and sunken pulses above both feet indicate disease of the foot Yangming, which is the stomach meridian."

		Those with large intestine disease experience sharp pain in the intestines and a gurgling sound like water. In winter, if re-infected by cold, it causes diarrhea with pain around the navel and inability to stand for long. Its symptoms are similar to those of stomach disease; treat at Shanglian of Ji Xu (ST37).

胃病者，腹䐜脹，胃脘當心而痛，上肢兩脅，膈咽不通，食飲不下，取之三里也。
		Those with stomach disease experience abdominal distension, pain in the epigastrium directly below the heart, extending to both hypochondria. There is obstruction between the diaphragm and throat, making it difficult to swallow food or drink; treat at Zusanli (ST36).

打開字典

小腸病者，小腹痛，腰脊控睪而痛，時窘之後，當耳前熱，若寒甚，若獨肩上熱甚，及手小指次指之間熱，若脈陷者，此其候也。手太陽病也，取之巨虛下廉。
		Those with small intestine disease experience lower abdominal pain, with the waist and spine pulling at the testicles in pain. There may be urgency after urination, heat before the ear, or severe cold; there might also be intense heat on the shoulder alone, as well as between the little finger and ring finger of the hand, or a sunken pulse—these are its symptoms. This is disease of the Hand Taiyang meridian; treat at Xilian of Ji Xu (ST41).

三焦病者，腹氣滿，小腹尤堅，不得小便，窘急，溢則水留，即為脹。候在足太陽之外大絡，大絡在太陽少陽之間，亦見于脈，取委陽。
		Those with triple energizer disease experience abdominal fullness, especially firmness in the lower abdomen, inability to urinate, urgency and tightness. If fluids overflow, they accumulate as water retention, leading to swelling. Its signs appear in the major collateral of the Foot Taiyang outside; this collateral lies between the Foot Taiyang and Foot Shaoyang meridians. It can also be observed in the pulse; treat at Weiyang (BL39).

	邪氣藏府... :	
膀胱病者，小腹偏腫而痛，以手按之，即欲小便而不得，肩上熱，若脈陷，及足小指外廉及脛踝後皆熱，若脈陷，取委中央。
		Those with bladder disease experience unilateral swelling and pain in the lower abdomen; pressing on it creates an urge to urinate but is ineffective. There may be heat on the shoulders, or a sunken pulse, as well as heat along the lateral side of the little toe of the foot, and behind the tibia and ankle—also possibly with a sunken pulse. Treat at Weizhongyang (BL39).
