	邪氣藏府... :	
膽病者，善太息，口苦，嘔宿汁，心下淡淡，恐人將捕之，嗌中吤吤然數唾。在足少陽之本末，亦視其脈之陷下者灸之；其寒熱者取陽陵泉。
		Those with gallbladder disease often sigh deeply, have a bitter taste in the mouth, vomit undigested food, feel uneasy beneath the heart, and fear being captured. They may also experience a dry sensation in the throat and frequent spitting. The condition is located at the root and branch of the Foot Shaoyang meridian; also observe for sunken pulses, which should be treated with moxibustion; for those experiencing chills and fever, treat at Yanglingquan (GB41).

38
打開字典
顯示影印本
	邪氣藏府... :	
黃帝曰：刺之有道乎？歧伯答曰：刺此者，必中氣穴，無中肉節。中氣穴，則鍼游於巷；中肉節，即皮膚痛；補寫反，則病益篤。中筋則筋緩，邪氣不出，與其真相搏亂而不去，反還內著。用鍼不審，以順為逆也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Is there a method to acupuncture?" Qi Bo replied: "When needling these points, one must strike the qi points and avoid hitting the flesh or joints." If you strike the correct qi point, the needle will move smoothly like a traveler in an alley; if you hit flesh or joints, it causes skin and muscle pain; If the tonification and dispersion techniques are reversed, the illness will become more severe. If a tendon is struck, it causes tendinous relaxation; pathogenic qi cannot be expelled and combines with the body's true qi in confusion, remaining unresolved and returning inward to settle. Improper use of needles turns what should be harmonious into disharmony. 

5. 根結 Root and Termination Points

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
歧伯曰：天地相感，寒暖相移，陰陽之道，孰少孰多，陰道偶，陽道奇。發于春夏，陰氣少，陽氣多，陰陽不調，何補何寫。發于秋冬，陽氣少，陰氣多；陰氣盛而陽氣衰，故莖葉枯槁，濕雨下歸，陰陽相移，何寫何補。奇邪離經，不可勝數，不知根結，五藏六府，折關敗樞，開閤而走，陰陽大失，不可復取。九鍼之玄，要在終始；故能知終始，一言而畢，不知終始，鍼道咸絕。
		Qi Bo said: Heaven and Earth influence each other, cold and warmth shift into one another; the Dao of Yin and Yang, which is less and which is more? The Dao of Yin is even, while the Dao of Yang is odd. If it originates in spring and summer, Yin qi is scarce, while Yang qi is abundant; when Yin and Yang are disharmonious, what should be supplemented and what should be purged? If it originates in autumn and winter, Yang qi is scarce, while Yin qi is abundant; Yin qi becomes excessive and Yang qi declines, so the stems and leaves wither; heavy rains descend below, Yin and Yang shift into one another—what should be purged and what should be supplemented? Abnormal pathogenic influences deviate from the meridians, too numerous to count; without knowledge of the root and junctions, the five zang and six fu organs suffer broken joints and failed pivots, their openings and closings disrupted, leading to a great loss of Yin and Yang that cannot be restored. The subtlety of the Nine Needles lies in understanding the beginning and end; Therefore, those who understand the beginning and end can fully grasp it with a single statement; those who do not know the beginning and end will find the Dao of acupuncture completely cut off.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
太陽根于至陰，結于命門。命門者，目也。陽明根于厲兌，結于顙大。顙大者，鉗耳也。少陽根于竅陰，結于窗籠。窗籠者，耳中也。太陽為開，陽明為閤，少陽為樞，故開折，則肉節瀆而暴病起矣。故暴病者，取之太陽，視有餘不足。瀆者，皮肉宛膲而弱也。閤折，則氣無所止息而痿疾起矣。故痿疾者，取之陽明，視有餘不足。無所止息者，真氣稽留，邪氣居之也。樞折，即骨繇而不安于地。故骨繇者，取之少陽，視有餘不足。骨繇者，節緩而不收也。所謂骨繇者，搖故也。當竊其本也。
		Taiyang originates at Zhiyin and terminates at Mingmen. Mingmen refers to the eyes. Yangming originates at Liedui and terminates at Sangda. Sangda refers to Qianer. Shaoyang originates at Qiyan and terminates at Chuanglong. Chuanglong refers to the ear canal. Taiyang functions as opening, Yangming as closing, and Shaoyang as the pivot. Therefore, if the function of opening is impaired, the muscles and joints become disordered and sudden illness arises. Therefore, for sudden illnesses, treatment should be directed at Taiyang, assessing whether there is excess or deficiency. Disorder of the muscles and joints refers to soft and weak skin and flesh. If the function of closing is impaired, Qi has no place to rest and atrophy-related diseases arise. Therefore, for atrophy-related diseases, treatment should be directed toward Yangming, assessing whether there is excess or insufficiency. When Qi has no place to rest, the true qi lingers and pathogenic influences take residence. If the pivot function is impaired, then the bones become unsteady and cannot remain stable on the ground. Therefore, for bone instability, treatment should be directed to Shaoyang, assessing whether there is excess or insufficient condition. Bone instability refers to loose joints that do not contract properly. What is referred to as bone instability is the condition of shaking or unsteadiness. It should be addressed by targeting its root.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
太陰根于隱白，結于太倉。少陰根于湧泉，結于廉泉。厥陰根于大敦，結于玉英，絡于膻中。太陰為開，厥陰為闔，少陽為樞。故開折，則倉廩無所輸，膈洞。膈洞者，取之太陰，視有餘不足，故開折者，氣不足而生病也。閤折，即氣絕而喜悲。悲者取之厥陰，視有餘不足。樞折，則脈有所結而不通。不通者，取之少陰，視有餘不足，有結者，皆取之不足。
		Taiyin originates at Yinbai and terminates at Taicang. Shaoyin originates at Yongquan and terminates at Lianquan. Jueyin originates at Dadun, terminates at Yuying, and connects to Zhongfu (Shanzhong). Taiyin functions as opening, Jueyin as closing, and Shaoyin as the pivot. Therefore, if the function of "opening" is impaired, then there will be no transportation from the granaries (stomach and intestines), resulting in a sensation of emptiness or openness behind the sternum. The condition of "di dong" (a sense of openness in the chest) should be treated by targeting Taiyin, assessing whether there is an excess or deficiency. Therefore, when the function of opening is impaired, it results from insufficient Qi and leads to illness. If the closing function is impaired, then Qi becomes cut off and one tends to feel sorrowful or melancholic. For those who experience sadness, treatment should target Jueyin, assessing whether there is excess or deficient condition. If the pivot function is impaired, meridians become obstructed and Qi circulation does not flow smoothly. For obstruction, treatment should target Shaoyin, assessing whether there is excessive or deficient condition; for those with blockages, always treat the deficiency.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
足太陽根于至陰，溜于京骨，注于昆侖，入于天柱、飛揚也。足少陽根于竅陰，溜于丘墟，注于陽輔，入于天容、光明也。足陽明根于厲兌，溜于沖陽，注于下陵，入于人迎，豐隆也。手太陽根于少澤，溜于陽谷，注于小海，入于天窗，支正也。手少陽根于關沖，溜于陽池，注于支溝，入于天牖、外關也。手陽明根于商陽，溜于合谷，注于陽谿，入于扶突、偏歷也。此所謂十二經者，盛絡皆當取之。
		The foot Taiyang originates at Zhiyin, flows through Jinggu, converges at Kunlun, and enters Tianzhu and Feiyang. The foot Shaoyang originates at Qiayin, flows through Qiuxu, converges at Yangfu, and enters Tianrong and Guangming. The foot Yangming originates at Liedui, flows through Chongyang, converges at Xiaoling, and enters Renying and Fenglong. The hand Taiyang originates at Shaoze, flows through Yanggu, converges at Xiaohai, and enters Tianchuang and Zhizheng. The hand Shaoyang originates at Guanchong, flows through Yangchi, converges at Zhigou, and enters Tianyou and Waiguan. The hand Yangming originates at Shangyang, flows through Hegu, converges at Yangxi, and enters Futu and Bianli. These are the twelve meridians; when their collateral networks become excessive, they should all be treated accordingly.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
一日一夜五十營，以營五藏之精，不應數者，名曰狂生。所謂五十營者，五藏皆受氣，持其脈口，數其至也。五十動而不一代者，五藏皆受氣。四十動一代者，一藏無氣。三十動一代者，二藏無氣。二十動一代者，三藏無氣。十動一代者，四藏無氣。不滿十動一代者，五藏無氣。予之短期，要在終始。所謂五十動而不一代者，以為常也。以知五藏之期，予之短期者，乍數乍疏也。
		In one day and night, the Qi circulates fifty times to nourish the essence of the five zang organs; those whose circulation does not conform to this number are called "kuangsheng," meaning mentally disturbed or unbalanced. What is referred to as the fifty circulations means that all five zang organs receive Qi; this can be observed by holding the pulse at the radial artery and counting its arrivals. If there are fifty pulses without a single missed beat, it indicates that all five zang organs have received Qi properly. If there is one missed beat in forty pulses, it means one of the zang organs lacks Qi. If there is one missed beat every thirty pulses, two of the zang organs lack Qi. If there is a missed beat in twenty pulses, three of the zang organs are without Qi. If there is one missed pulse every ten beats, four of the zang organs have no Qi. If a beat is missed before reaching ten pulses, all five zang organs lack Qi. The key to predicting the short-term prognosis lies in understanding the beginning and the end (of the pulse pattern). What is referred to as fifty pulses without a missed beat is considered normal. To know the timing of the five zang organs and predict their short-term prognosis, one must observe irregularities in pulse frequency—sudden rapidity or looseness.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
黃帝曰：逆順五體者，言人骨節之大小，肉之堅脆，皮之厚薄，血之清濁，氣之滑澀，脈之長短，血之多少，經絡之數，余已知之矣，此皆布衣匹夫之士也。夫王公大人，血食之君，身體柔脆，肌肉軟弱，血氣慓悍滑利，其刺之徐疾淺深多少，可得同之乎。歧伯答曰：膏梁菽藿之味，何可同也？氣滑即出疾，其氣澀則出遲，氣悍則鍼小而入淺，氣澀則鍼大而入深，深則欲留，淺則欲疾。以此觀之，刺布衣者，深以留之，刺大人者，微以徐之，此皆因氣慓悍滑利也。
		Huangdi said: When it is said that the Dao of harmony and disharmony (nishing) in relation to five types of body constitution refers to the size of a person's bones and joints, the firmness or softness of their flesh, the thickness or thinness of their skin, the clarity or turbidity of their blood, the smoothness or stickiness of their Qi, the length of their pulses, the amount of their blood, and the number of meridians and collaterals—I already understand these. These are all characteristics of ordinary common people. As for kings, dukes, and high-ranking officials—those who enjoy sacrificial offerings of blood—their bodies are tender and fragile, their muscles soft and weak, their blood and Qi swift, agile, and smooth. Can the speed, depth, and amount of acupuncture applied to them be the same as that used on ordinary people? Qi Bo replied: How can they possibly be the same when their diets consist of rich delicacies and legumes, unlike those who subsist on coarse grains? If the Qi is smooth, it will exit quickly; if the Qi is sticky, it exits slowly. If the Qi is fierce and rapid, a fine needle should be inserted shallowly; if the Qi is sluggish, a thicker needle should be used with deeper insertion. When inserting deeply, one wishes to retain the needle longer; when inserting shallowly, one desires quick action. Judging from this, when treating common people, one should insert the needle deeply and retain it for a longer time; but when treating high-ranking individuals, one should use gentle and slow techniques. This is all due to their Qi being swift, fierce, smooth, and agile.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
黃帝曰：形氣之逆順奈何？歧伯曰：形氣不足，病氣有餘，是邪勝也，急寫之；形氣有餘，病氣不足，急補之；形氣不足，病氣不足，此陰陽氣俱不足也，不可刺之，刺之則重不足。重不足則陰陽俱竭，血氣皆盡，五藏空虛，筋骨髓枯，老者絕滅，壯者不復矣。形氣有餘，病氣有餘，此謂陰陽俱有餘也。急寫其邪，調其虛實。故曰：有餘者寫之，不足者補之，此之謂也。
		Huangdi asked: How can we determine the harmony or disharmony of body constitution and Qi? Qi Bo said: When the form and Qi are insufficient but pathogenic influences are excessive, it means evil forces have prevailed; in such cases, urgent purging is required; When the form and Qi are abundant but the pathological condition is insufficient, urgent tonification is needed; When both the form and Qi are insufficent and the pathological condition also shows deficiency, this indicates that both Yin and Yang Qi are insufficient; in such a case, acupuncture should not be used, for needling would further deplete what is already lacking. If there is excessive depletion, both Yin and Yang will be exhausted; blood and Qi will all be depleted, the five zang organs become hollow and empty, tendons, bones, and marrow wither away. In the elderly, this leads to death; in the strong, recovery becomes impossible. When both form and Qi are excessive and the pathological condition is also excessive, this is called an excess of both Yin and Yang. Urgent purging should be applied to expel pathogenic influences, while adjusting between deficiency and excess conditions. Therefore it is said: "In cases of excess, purge; in cases of deficiency, tonify." This is what that means.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
故曰：刺不知逆順，真邪相搏。滿而補之，則陰陽四溢，腸胃充郭，肝肺內䐜，陰陽相錯。虛而寫之，則經脈空虛，血氣竭枯，腸胃㒤辟，皮膚薄著，毛腠夭膲，予之死期。
		Hence it is said: "If acupuncture does not follow the principles of harmony and disharmony, true Qi and pathogenic influences will clash." If one tonifies when the body is already full, Yin and Yang will overflow; the intestines and stomach become distended, the liver and lungs internally congested, and Yin and Yang become disordered. If one purges when the body is already deficient, meridians and vessels become empty, blood and Qi are exhausted; the intestines and stomach collapse inward, skin becomes thin and loosely attached, hair follicles wither, and death approaches.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	根結:	
故曰：用鍼之要，在於知調陰與陽。調陰與陽，精氣乃光，合形與氣，使神內藏。故曰：上工平氣，中工亂脈，下工絕氣危生。故曰：下工不可不慎也，必審五藏變化之病，五脈之應，經絡之實虛，皮之柔麤，而後取之也。
		Therefore it is said: The key to using acupuncture lies in knowing how to regulate Yin and Yang. Regulating Yin and Yang brings clarity and brilliance to the essence and Qi; harmonizing body constitution with Qi allows Shen (spirit) to be internally stored. Therefore it is said: A superior physician regulates the Qi, a mediocre one disrupts the meridians, and an inferior one cuts off the Qi and endangers life. Therefore it is said: An inferior physician must be especially cautious, carefully examining the changes in diseases of the five zang organs, the responses of the five pulses, the fullness or emptiness of meridians and collateral vessels, and whether the skin is soft or coarse—only then should treatment be administered. 

6. 壽夭剛柔 Longevity and Short Lifespan: Firmness and Flexibility

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝問於少師曰：余聞人之生也，有剛有柔，有弱有強，有短有長，有陰有陽，願聞其方。
		The Yellow Emperor asked the Shaoshi, "I have heard that when people are born, some are strong and others weak, some frail and others robust, some short-lived and others long-lived, some yin and others yang. I would like to hear about their principles."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
少師答曰：陰中有陰，陽中有陽，審知陰陽，刺之有方。得病所始，刺之有理。謹度病端，與時相應。內合於五藏六府，外合於筋骨皮膚。是故內有陰陽，外亦有陰陽。在內者，五藏為陰，六府為陽，在外者，筋骨為陰，皮膚為陽。故曰，病在陰之陰者，刺陰之滎俞，病在陽之陽者，刺陽之合，病在陽之陰者，刺陰之經，病在陰之陽者，刺絡脈。故曰，病在陽者名曰風，病在陰者名曰痺，陰陽俱病名曰風痺。病有形而不痛者，陽之類也；無形而痛者，陰之類也。無形而痛者，其陽完而陰傷之也。急治其陰，無攻其陽。有形而不痛者，其陰完而陽傷之也。急治其陽，無攻其陰。陰陽俱動，乍有形，乍無形，加以煩心，命曰陰勝其陽。此謂不表不裏，其形不久。
		Shaoshi replied, "There is yin within yin and yang within yang. If one thoroughly understands yin and yang, acupuncture can be applied with proper methods." Understanding the origin of a disease allows for treatment based on sound principles. Carefully measure the beginning of an illness, and it will correspond with the appropriate time. Internally, they are associated with the five zang and six fu organs; externally, they relate to muscles, bones, and skin. Therefore, there is yin and yang internally, as well as yin and yang externally. Internally, the five zang organs are yin, while the six fu organs are yang. Externally, muscles and bones are yin, whereas skin is yang. Therefore, it is said that when a disease occurs in the yin of yin, one should needle the 荥俞 (Xingyu) points of yin; when a disease occurs in yang of yang, one should needle the 合 (He) points of yang. When a disease occurs in yin within yang, one should needle the 经 (Jing) points of yin. When a disease occurs in yang within yin, one should need the collateral vessels (络脉). Therefore, it is said: a disease located in yang is called wind (feng); a disease located in yin is called bi; when both yin and yang are affected, it is called fengbi. A disease that has physical manifestations but no pain belongs to the category of yang. An invisible yet painful condition belongs to the category of yin. A condition that is invisible but causes pain indicates intact yang and injured yin. Urgently treat the yin, do not attack the yang. A condition with physical manifestations but no pain indicates intact yin and injured yang. Urgently treat the yang, do not attack the yin. When both yin and yang become active, symptoms appear alternately with physical manifestations and invisibility, accompanied by mental distress; this is called yin overpowering yang. This is referred to as neither exterior nor interior, and its symptoms do not last long.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝問於伯高曰：余聞形氣之病先後，外內之應奈何？伯高答曰：風寒傷形，憂恐忿怒傷氣；氣傷藏，乃病藏，寒傷形，乃應形；風傷筋脈，筋脈乃應。此形氣外內之相應也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao, "I have heard about the sequence of diseases related to form and qi. How do external and internal responses correspond?" Bo Gao replied, "Wind and cold injure the body's form; worry, fear, anger, and resentment injure qi." When qi is injured, it affects the zang organs, thus causing disease in the zang. When cold injures form, it manifests as a response in the body's physical structure. Wind injures sinews and vessels; accordingly, the sinews and vessels respond. This is how form and qi correspond internally and externally.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：刺之奈何？伯高答曰：病九日者，三刺而已；病一月者，十刺而已；多少遠近，以此衰之。久痺不去身者，視其血絡，盡出其血。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "How should one perform acupuncture?" Bo Gao replied, "For a disease lasting nine days, three needle treatments will suffice; " for a disease lasting one month, ten acupuncture sessions are enough; The number of treatments and the duration should be adjusted according to this principle. For a long-lasting bi syndrome that does not leave the body, examine its blood vessels and completely remove the stagnant blood.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：外內之病，難易之治奈何？伯高答曰：形先病而未入藏者，刺之半其日。藏先病而形乃應者，刺之倍其日。此月內難易之應也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "How do external and internal diseases differ in terms of difficulty for treatment?" Bo Gao replied, "When the disease first affects the body's form but has not yet entered the zang organs, acupuncture should be performed for half the number of days." If the zang organ is affected first and then the body responds, acupuncture treatment should last twice as many days. This represents the relative difficulty of treating diseases within a month.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝問於伯高曰：余聞形有緩急，氣有盛衰，骨有大小，肉有堅脆，皮有厚薄，其以立壽夭奈何？伯高答曰：形與氣相任則壽，不相任則夭。皮與肉相果則壽，不相果則夭，血氣經絡勝形則壽，不勝形則夭。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao: "I have heard that the body has varying degrees of flexibility and rigidity, qi varies in strength and decline, bones vary in size, flesh varies in firmness or fragility, skin varies in thickness. How do these factors determine longevity or a short life?" Bo Gao replied: "When form and qi support each other, one will live long; when they do not support each other, a short life results." When skin and flesh are in harmony, one will live long; if they are not harmonious, a short life results. When blood, qi, and meridians prevail over the body's structure, longevity follows; when they do not prevail, a short life occurs.

7
打開字典
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：何謂形之緩急？伯高答曰：形充而皮膚緩者則壽，形充而皮膚急者則夭，形充而脈堅大者順也，形充而脈小以弱者氣衰，衰則危矣。若形充而顴不起者骨小，骨小則夭矣。形充而大肉胭堅而有分者肉堅，肉堅則壽矣；形充而大肉無分理不堅者肉脆，肉脆則夭矣。此天之生命，所以立形定氣而視壽夭者，必明乎此立形定氣，而後以臨病人，決生死。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "What is meant by the flexibility and rigidity of form?" Bo Gao replied: "When the body is well-developed and the skin is relaxed, longevity results; when the body is well-developed but the skin is taut, a short life follows. When the body is full and the pulse is strong and large, it indicates harmony. If the body is full but the pulse is small and weak, this signifies qi deficiency, which brings danger." If the face appears well-developed yet the cheekbones are not prominent, it indicates small bones; those with small bones will have a short life. If the body is well-developed, and the thick flesh on the hips is firm and clearly defined, this indicates strong flesh; those with strong flesh will live long. If the body appears well-developed but the thick flesh lacks clear texture and is not firm, this indicates fragile flesh; those with fragile flesh will have a short life. This is the natural basis of life and death, determining longevity or a short lifespan by observing form and establishing qi. One must clearly understand how to assess form and determine qi before treating patients and deciding between life and death.

8
打開字典
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：余聞壽夭，無以度之。伯高答曰：牆基卑，高不及其地者，不滿三十而死。其有因加疾者，不及二十而死也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I have heard of longevity and short lifespan, but I do not know how to measure them." Bo Gao replied: "If the foundation of a wall is low and its height does not reach that of the surrounding ground, it will collapse before reaching thirty." Those who suffer from additional severe illnesses will die before reaching twenty.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：形氣之相勝，以立壽夭奈何？伯高答曰：平人而氣勝形者壽；病而形肉脫，氣勝形者死，形勝氣者危矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How does the mutual dominance of form and qi determine longevity or a short lifespan?" Bo Gao replied: "In an ordinary person, if qi prevails over form, they will live long; " in a diseased individual, if the body and flesh are wasting away and qi still prevails over form, death will occur. If form prevails over qi, it is already in grave danger.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：余聞刺有三變，何謂三變？伯高答曰：有刺營者，有刺衛者，有刺寒痺之留經者。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "I have heard that acupuncture has three transformations. What are these three transformations?" Bo Gao replied: "There is needling of the ying (nutritive) aspect, there is needling of the wei (defensive) aspect, and there is needling for cold bi syndrome that lingers in the meridians."

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：刺三變者奈何？伯高答曰：刺營者出血，刺衛者出氣，刺寒痺者內熱。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How should one perform these three types of acupuncture?" Bo Gao replied: "Needling the ying aspect causes blood to be expelled; needling the wei aspect expels qi; and needling for cold bi syndrome generates internal warmth."

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：營衛寒痺之為病奈何？伯高答曰：營之生病也，寒熱少氣，血上下行。衛之生病也，氣痛時來時去，怫愾賁響，風寒客於腸胃之中。寒痺之為病也，留而不去，時痛而皮不仁。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "What are the symptoms of diseases caused by ying, wei, and cold bi?" Bo Gao replied: "Diseases arising from the ying aspect manifest as chills, fever, shortness of breath, and abnormal blood circulation upward or downward." Diseases arising from the wei aspect manifest as intermittent pain, a feeling of oppression and distension in the chest, and wind-cold lodging within the intestines and stomach. The disease caused by cold bi syndrome lingers without leaving, causing intermittent pain and numbness of the skin.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：刺寒痺內熱奈何？伯高答曰：刺布衣者，以火焠之；刺大人者，以藥熨之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How should one needle for internal warmth in cases of cold bi syndrome?" Bo Gao replied: "For common people, use fire moxibustion; " for high-ranking individuals, apply medicinal fomentation.

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	壽夭剛柔:	
黃帝曰：藥熨奈何？伯高答曰：用淳酒二十斤，蜀椒一斤，干姜一斤，桂心一斤，凡四種，皆嚼咀，漬酒中，用綿絮一斤，細白布四丈，並內酒中，置酒馬矢熅中，封涂封，勿使泄。五日五夜，出布綿絮曝乾之，干復潰，以盡其汁。每漬必晬其日，乃出乾。乾，並用滓與綿絮，復布為復巾，長六七尺，為六七巾，則用之生桑炭炙巾，以熨寒痺所刺之處，令熱入至於病所，寒復炙巾以熨之，三十遍而止。汗出以巾拭身，亦三十遍而止。起步內中，無見風。每刺必熨，如此病已矣。此所謂內熱也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "What is medicinal fomentation?" Bo Gao replied: "Take twenty sheng of pure wine, one jin of Sichuan pepper, one jin of dried ginger, and one jin of cassia bark. These four ingredients should all be chewed and crushed, then soaked in the wine. Add one jin of cotton wadding and four zhang of fine white cloth to the mixture as well. Place everything into a container made from horse manure and sealed with clay, ensuring no vapor escapes." After five days and nights, remove the cloth and cotton wadding to dry in the sun. Once dried, crush them again until all the liquid is fully extracted. Each time it is soaked, it must remain for one full day before being removed and dried. Once dried, combine the residue and cotton wadding with cloth to make a compress. The compress should be six or seven chi long, making six or seven such cloths. When using it, heat the compress over green mulberry charcoal until warm, then apply it by fomenting the area where cold bi syndrome was needled, allowing the warmth to penetrate to the site of illness. Repeat this process thirty times and stop. Wipe the body with a cloth after perspiration appears; do this thirty times as well, then stop. During the process, remain indoors and avoid exposure to wind. Each time acupuncture is performed, fomentation must follow; in this way, the illness will be cured. This is what is referred to as generating internal warmth. 

7. 官鍼 Official Needles

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官鍼:	
凡刺之要，官鍼最妙。九鍼之宜，各有所為，長、短、大、小，各有所施也。不得其用，病弗能移。疾淺鍼深，內傷良肉，皮膚為癰；病深鍼淺，病氣不寫，支為大膿。病小鍼大，氣寫太甚，疾必為害；病大鍼小，氣不泄寫，亦復為敗。失鍼之宜。大者寫，小者不移。已言其過，請言其所施。
		The essential principle of acupuncture is that official needles are most effective. The nine types of needles each have their appropriate uses; long, short, large, and small needles are applied according to specific needs. If the proper use is not followed, the illness cannot be alleviated. If a shallow condition is needled too deeply, it may injure healthy tissue and cause abscesses on the skin; If a deep condition is needled superficially, the pathogenic qi will not be expelled, leading to the formation of large abscesses. For minor ailments, using a large needle causes excessive release of qi and may harm the condition; For major ailments, using a small needle prevents adequate release of pathogenic qi, which also results in failure. This is the consequence of failing to apply needles appropriately. Large conditions require drainage, while small ones will not resolve if left untreated. Having discussed the mistakes, I shall now explain their proper applications.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	官鍼:	
病在皮膚無常處者，取以鑱鍼於病所，膚白勿取。病在分肉間，取以圓鍼於病所。病在經絡痼痺者，取以鋒鍼。病在脈，氣少，當補之者，取以鍉鍼於井滎分俞。病為大膿者，取以鈹鍼。病痺氣暴發者，取以圓利鍼。病痺氣痛而不去者，取以毫鍼。病在中者，取以長鍼。病水腫不能通關節者，取以大鍼。病在五藏固居者，取以鋒鍼，寫於井滎分俞，取以四時。
		For diseases located in the skin without fixed locations, use a lancet needle at the affected site; avoid needling areas where the skin appears pale. For ailments located between muscle layers, apply round needles at the affected area. For diseases in meridians and collaterals with chronic numbness or stiffness, use sharp-pointed needles. For diseases in the vessels where qi is deficient and tonification is needed, apply a broad needle at the Well, Spring, Stream, and Transport points. For conditions involving large abscesses, use a needle with a broad head (bi zhen). For diseases characterized by sudden onset of bi syndrome and qi disturbance, apply smooth round needles. For bi syndrome with persistent pain that does not subside, use fine filiform needles. For diseases located internally, use long needles. For edema that prevents the joints from moving freely, use large needles. For diseases deeply rooted in the five zang organs, use sharp-pointed needles to drain at Well, Spring, Stream, and Confluent points according to the four seasons.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官鍼:	
凡刺有九，以應九變。一曰俞刺，俞刺者，刺諸經滎俞藏俞也；二曰遠道刺，遠道刺者，病在上，取之下，刺府俞也；三曰經刺，經刺者，刺大經之結絡經分也；四曰絡刺，絡刺者，刺小絡之血脈也；五曰分刺，分刺者，刺分肉之間也；六曰大寫刺，大寫刺者，刺大膿以鈹鍼也；七曰毛刺，毛刺者，刺浮痺皮膚也；八曰巨刺，巨刺者，左取右，右取左；九曰焠刺，焠刺者，刺燔鍼則取痺也。
		There are nine methods of acupuncture, corresponding to nine types of changes in conditions. The first is Shu needling: this refers to needling the Spring and Stream points, as well as the Cang (Storage) points of the meridians; The second is Yuan Dao needling: this refers to a condition located above, but treated by needling points below, specifically the Fu Shu (Mansion) points; The third is Jing needling: this refers to needling knots and junctions along major meridians. The fourth is Lao needling: this involves puncturing the small collateral blood vessels; The fifth is Fen needling: this refers to needling between muscle layers; The sixth is Da Xie needling: this involves draining large abscesses using broad-headed needles (bi zhen); The seventh is Mao needling: this refers to treating superficial bi syndrome on the skin's surface; The eighth is Ju needling: this involves treating a condition by needling opposite sides of the body, such as taking points on the right for left-sided ailments and vice versa; The ninth is Cui needling: this refers to using moxibustion with heated needles to treat bi syndrome.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官鍼:	
凡刺有十二節，以應十二經。一曰偶刺，偶刺者，以手直心若背，直痛所，一刺前，一刺後，以治心痺。刺此者，傍鍼之也。二曰報刺，報刺者，刺痛無常處也。上下行者，直內無拔鍼，以左手隨病所按之，乃出鍼，復刺之也。三曰恢刺，恢刺
者1，直刺傍之，舉之前後，恢筋急，以治筋痺也。四曰齊刺，齊刺者，直入一，傍入二，以治寒氣小深者；或曰三刺，三刺者，治痺氣小深者也。五曰揚刺，揚刺者，正內一，傍內四，而浮之，以治寒氣之搏大者也。六曰直鍼刺，直鍼刺者，引皮乃刺之，以治寒氣之淺者也。七曰輸鍼，輸刺者，直入直出，稀發鍼而深之，以治氣盛而熱者也。八曰短刺，短刺者，刺骨痺，稍搖而深之，致鍼骨所，以上下摩骨也。九曰浮刺，浮刺者，傍入而浮之，以治肌急而寒者也。十曰陰刺，陰刺者，左右率刺之，以治寒厥；中寒厥，足踝後少陰也。十一曰傍鍼刺，傍鍼刺者，直刺傍刺各一，以治留痺久居者也。十二曰贊刺，贊刺者，直入直出，數發鍼而淺之，出血是謂治癰腫也。
		There are twelve acupuncture sections, corresponding to the twelve meridians. The first is Ou needling: this involves positioning the hands directly in front of or behind the heart, at the site of pain; one needle is inserted anteriorly and another posteriorly to treat cardiac bi syndrome. When performing this technique, needles are placed on either side. The second is Bao needling: this refers to treating pain that occurs at irregular locations. For conditions where the pain moves upward or downward, insert the needle directly without withdrawing it. With the left hand, follow the location of the disease and press on it; then remove the needle and reinsert it at that site. The third is Hui needling: this involves inserting the needle straight and then manipulating it forward and backward to relax tense sinews, used for treating bi syndrome of the tendons. The fourth is Qi needling: this involves inserting one needle straight and two needles on either side, used to treat shallow cold conditions; Some call it San Cui (three needling): this refers to treating slightly deep bi syndrome. The fifth is Yang needling: this involves inserting one central needle and four surrounding needles superficially, used to treat large accumulations of cold qi. The sixth is Zhi zhen needling: this involves stretching the skin before inserting the needle, used to treat superficial cold conditions. The seventh is Shu zhen needling: this involves inserting and withdrawing the needle directly, using fewer needles but at greater depth, used to treat conditions with excessive qi and heat. The eighth is Duan zhen needling: this involves treating bone-related bi syndrome by slightly manipulating the needle to a deeper level, reaching the site of the bones and rubbing up and down along them. The ninth is Fu zhen needling: this involves inserting needles on the sides superficially, used to treat tight muscles and cold conditions. The tenth is Yin zhen needling: this involves inserting needles symmetrically on both sides, used to treat cold extremities (han jue); For internal cold extremity conditions, the Shao Yin points behind the ankle are needled. The eleventh is Bang zhen needling: this involves inserting one direct needle and one lateral needle, used to treat lingering bi syndrome that has persisted for a long time. The twelfth is Zan zhen needling: this involves inserting and removing the needle directly multiple times at a shallow depth to draw out blood, used for treating abscesses and swellings.

1. 者 : 舊脫。
5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官鍼:	
脈之所居，深不見者，刺之微內鍼而久留之，以致其空脈氣也。脈淺者，勿刺，按絕其脈乃刺之，無令精出，獨出其邪氣耳。
		For vessels whose locations are deep and not visible, insert the needle slightly and leave it in place for a prolonged time to reach the empty vessel and regulate its qi. For shallow vessels, do not needling directly; first press to stop the pulse and then needle it, ensuring that vital essence does not escape, but only expelling pathogenic qi.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官鍼:	
所謂三刺，則穀氣出者。先淺刺絕皮，以出陽邪，再刺則陰邪出者，少益深絕皮，致肌肉，未入分肉間也；已入分肉之間，則穀氣出。故刺法曰：始刺淺之，以逐邪氣，而來血氣，後刺深之，以致陰氣之邪，最後刺極深之，以下穀氣。此之謂也。
		What is referred to as San Ci (three punctures) is the method by which grain qi emerges. First, a superficial puncture is made through the skin to expel yang pathogenic factors. A second puncture goes slightly deeper into the muscles but does not yet reach between muscle layers; this allows yin pathogens to be expelled. Once it reaches between the muscle layers, grain qi emerges. Therefore, the acupuncture method states: initially insert shallow to expel pathogenic factors and promote blood and qi circulation; then insert deeper to reach yin pathogenic factors; finally insert very deeply to draw out grain qi. This is what it means.

	官鍼:	
故用鍼者，不知年之所加，氣之盛衰，虛實之所起，不可以為工也。
		Therefore, those who use needles without understanding the annual cycles, the waxing and waning of qi, or the origins of deficiency and excess cannot be considered skilled practitioners.

		There are five acupuncture methods corresponding to the five zang organs. The first is Ban Ci (half needling): this involves shallow insertion and quick needle withdrawal, without damaging the flesh, like plucking a hair, to draw out superficial qi; it corresponds to the lung.

		The second is Bao Wen Ci (leopard pattern needling): this involves inserting needles in multiple directions—left, right, front, and back—with the central vessel as a guide to draw blood from meridians and collaterals; it corresponds to the heart.

		The third is Guan Ci (gate needling): this involves inserting needles straight into the sides along the tendons to treat tendon bi syndrome, being careful not to draw blood; it corresponds to the liver; Some refer to it as Yuan Ci (deep needling); One may also call it Qi Ci.

		The fourth is He Gu Ci (He Gu needling): this involves inserting needles like the toes of a chicken into the spaces between muscle layers to treat muscular bi syndrome; it corresponds to the spleen.

8. 本神 Spirit

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：凡刺之法，先必本于神。血、脈、營、氣、精神，此五藏之所藏也。至其淫泆離藏則精失、魂魄飛揚、志意恍亂、智慮去身者，何因而然乎？天之罪與？人之過乎？何謂德、氣、生、精、神、魂、魄、心、意、志、思、智、慮？請問其故。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "In all methods of acupuncture, one must first take as a basis the spirit." Blood, vessels, nutritive essence, qi, and spirit—these are what the five zang organs store. When these become excessive and depart from their respective zang, essence is lost, the soul and spirit scatter, willpower becomes confused, and wisdom departs from the body—what causes such conditions? Is it a punishment from Heaven? Or is it due to human faults? What are meant by virtue, qi, life, essence, spirit, soul, corporeal spirit, mind, intention, will, thought, wisdom, and deliberation? Please explain the reasons.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本神:	
歧伯答曰：天之在我者德也，地之在我者氣也。德流氣薄而生者也。故生之來謂之精；兩精相搏謂之神；隨神往來者謂之魂；並精而出入者謂之魄；所以任物者謂之心；心有所憶謂之意；意之所存謂之志；因志而存變謂之思；因思而遠慕謂之慮；因慮而處物謂之智。
		Qi Bo replied, "Heaven's aspect within me is virtue; Earth's aspect within me is qi." Life arises from the flow of virtue and the subtlety of qi. Therefore, that which brings forth life is called essence; When two essences interact, it is called spirit; That which accompanies the spirit in its comings and goings is called the soul; That which emerges and enters together with essence is called the corporeal spirit; What enables one to deal with external things is called the mind; When the mind recalls something, it is called intention; Where intention resides is called will; Thought that arises from will and undergoes change is called thought (cognition); Deliberation that extends from thought to distant aspirations is called deliberation; Wisdom arises when one deliberates and deals with things appropriately.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
故智者之養生也，必順四時而適寒暑，和喜怒而安居處，節陰陽而調剛柔。如是，則僻邪不至，長生久視。
		Therefore, the wise person in cultivating life must follow the four seasons and adapt to cold and heat, harmonize joy and anger while living in peace and stability, regulate yin and yang, and balance rigidity and gentleness. If one does so, then pernicious influences will not arise, and longevity with enduring vision can be achieved.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
是故怵惕思慮者則傷神，神傷則恐懼流淫而不止。因悲哀動中者，竭絕而失生。喜樂者，神憚散而不藏。愁憂者，氣閉塞而不行。盛怒者，迷惑而不治。恐懼者，神蕩憚而不收。
		Therefore, those who are constantly anxious and preoccupied injure the spirit; when the spirit is injured, fear and anxiety flow unchecked. Those whose inner being is deeply moved by sorrow and grief will exhaust their essence and lose life. Excessive joy and pleasure cause the spirit to scatter and not be stored. Those who are troubled by sorrow and worry experience qi that becomes blocked and stagnant. Intense anger causes confusion and disorder. Those who are fearful experience a spirit that is scattered and cannot be gathered.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
心，怵惕思慮則傷神，神傷則恐懼自失。破䐃脫肉，毛悴色夭死于冬。
		The mind, when constantly anxious and preoccupied, injures the spirit; when the spirit is damaged, one experiences fear and loses self-control. This leads to broken flesh and muscle loss, withered hair, faded complexion, and death in winter.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
脾愁憂而不解則傷意，意傷則悗亂，四肢不舉，毛悴色夭死於春。
		The spleen, when burdened by sorrow and worry without relief, injures intention; when intention is injured, confusion and disarray arise, the limbs cannot move, hair withers, complexion fades, and death occurs in spring.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
肝悲哀動中則傷魂，魂傷則狂忘不精，不精則不正，當人陰縮而攣筋，兩脅骨不舉，毛悴色夭死於秋。
		The liver, when deeply moved by sorrow and grief, injures the soul; when the soul is injured, one becomes frenzied and forgetful, lacking clarity. Without clarity, there is disorder: in such a person, the yin contracts and tendons become spastic, the two flanks and bones cannot be raised, hair withers, complexion fades—and death occurs in autumn.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
肺喜樂無極則傷魄，魄傷則狂，狂者意不存人，皮革焦，毛悴色夭死於夏。
		The lungs, when subjected to excessive joy and pleasure without limit, injure the corporeal spirit; when the corporeal spirit is injured, one becomes manic. A maniacal person lacks intention toward others, their skin and leather become parched, hair withers, complexion fades—leading to death in summer.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
腎盛怒而不止則傷志，志傷則喜忘其前言，腰脊不可以俛仰屈伸，毛悴色夭死於季夏。
		The kidneys, when subjected to intense anger without cessation, injure will; when will is injured, one tends to forget previous words. The waist and spine cannot bend forward or backward, nor can they flex or extend. Hair withers, complexion fades—resulting in death during the late summer (season of the earth).

10
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本神:	
恐懼而不解則傷精，精傷則骨痠痿厥，精時自下。是故五藏主藏精者也，不可傷，傷則失守而陰虛；陰虛則無氣，無氣則死矣。
		Fear and anxiety without resolution injure essence; when essence is injured, bones ache, atrophy occurs with cold extremities, and seminal fluid escapes involuntarily. Therefore, the five zang organs are primarily responsible for storing essence; they must not be injured. If injured, the body loses its foundation and yin deficiency results; When yin is deficient, there is no qi; without qi, death occurs.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
是故用鍼者，察觀病人之態，以知精、神、魂、魄之存亡，得失之意，五者以傷，鍼不可以治之也。
		Therefore, those who use acupuncture must observe the patient's condition to discern whether essence, spirit, soul, and corporeal spirit are present or lost, and understand gains and losses. If all five aspects are injured, acupuncture cannot cure it.

12
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本神:	
肝藏血，血舍魂，肝氣虛則恐，實則怒。
		The liver stores blood; blood houses the soul. When liver qi is deficient, fear arises; when excessive, anger results.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
脾藏營，營舍意，脾氣虛則四肢不用，五藏不安，實則腹脹經溲不利。
		The spleen stores nutritive essence; nutritive essence houses intention. When spleen qi is deficient, the four limbs become immobile and the five zang organs feel unrestful; when excessive, abdominal distension occurs with impaired urination and defecation.

14
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本神:	
心藏脈，脈舍神，心氣虛則悲，實則笑不休。
		The heart stores the vessels; the vessels house the spirit. When heart qi is deficient, sorrow arises; when excessive, one laughs incessantly.

15
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本神:	
肺藏氣，氣舍魄，肺氣虛，則鼻塞不利少氣，實則喘喝胸盈仰息。
		The lungs store qi; qi houses the corporeal spirit. When lung qi is deficient, nasal congestion and shortness of breath occur; when excessive, there is wheezing, loud breathing, and labored inhalation with the chest raised.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本神:	
腎藏精，精舍志，腎氣虛則厥，實則脹。五藏不安。必審五藏之病形，以知其氣之虛實，謹而調之也。
		The kidneys store essence; essence houses will. When kidney qi is deficient, coldness and numbness in the extremities (厥) occur; when excessive, distension results. This leads to disharmony among the five zang organs. One must carefully examine the pathological conditions of the five zang organs to understand whether their qi is deficient or excessive, and then regulate them with caution. 

9. 終始 Beginning and End

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
凡刺之道，畢於終始，明知終始，五藏為紀，陰陽定矣。陰者主藏，陽者主府，陽受氣於四末，陰受氣於五藏，故寫者迎之，補者隨之，知迎知隨，氣可令和，和氣之方，必通陰陽。五藏為陰，六府為陽，傳之後世，以血為盟。敬之者昌，慢之者亡。無道行私，必得夭殃。
		The method of acupuncture is fully realized from beginning to end. Fully understanding the beginning and end, taking the five zang organs as a standard, yin and yang are then established. Yin is in charge of storage, while Yang is in charge of the fu organs. Yang receives qi from the four extremities, whereas Yin receives qi from the five zang organs. Therefore, for draining (reducing), one should go against the flow; for tonifying, one should follow it. Knowing when to oppose and when to follow, the qi can be harmonized. The method of harmonizing qi must necessarily involve understanding yin and yang. The five zang organs are Yin, while the six fu organs are Yang. Pass this knowledge to future generations, sealing it with blood as an oath. Respect it and you will flourish; neglect it and you will perish. To act selfishly without the Way is to surely invite an untimely calamity.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
謹奉天道，請言終始。終始者，經脈為紀。持其脈口人迎，以知陰陽有餘不足，平與不平，天道畢矣。所謂平人者不病，不病者，脈口人迎應四時也，上下相應而俱往來也，六經之脈不結動也，本末之寒溫之相守司也。形肉血氣必相稱也，是謂平人。少氣者，脈口人迎俱少，而不稱尺寸也。如是者，則陰陽俱不足，補陽則陰竭，寫陰則陽脫。如是者，可將以甘藥，不可飲以至劑，如此者弗灸。不巳者因而寫之，則五藏氣壞矣。
		Carefully following the principles of Heaven, I shall now speak on the beginning and end. The beginning and end are marked by the meridians as a standard. By examining the pulse at the mouth of the wrist and the Renying, one can discern whether yin and yang are in excess or deficiency, balanced or unbalanced. Thus is the Way of Heaven fully understood. What is meant by a "normal person" is one who does not fall ill. A healthy individual has the pulse at the mouth of the radial artery and Renying that correspond to the four seasons, with upward and downward movements harmonizing and reciprocating each other. The pulses of the six meridians do not become stagnant or agitated; the coldness and warmth at their root and branch are in mutual regulation and balance. The form, flesh, blood, and qi must necessarily be proportionate—this is what is called a normal person. One with deficient qi will have both the pulse at the radial artery's mouth and Renying showing deficiency, not corresponding to the standard measurements of cun and chi. In such cases, both yin and yang are insufficient; if Yang is supplemented, Yin will be exhausted; if Yin is drained, Yang will detach. In such cases, one may be treated with sweet herbs, but should not be given strong medicinal doses; acupuncture should also be avoided. If the condition is not yet resolved and draining therapy is applied, then the qi of the five zang organs will be damaged.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
人迎一盛，病在足少陽，一盛而躁，病在手少陽。人迎二盛，病在足太陽，二盛而躁，病在手太陽，人迎三盛，病在足陽明，三盛而躁，病在手陽明。人迎四盛，且大且數，名曰溢陽，溢陽為外格。
		If the Renying pulse is one degree stronger than normal, the illness lies in the foot Shaoyang meridian; if it is one degree stronger and agitated, the illness is in the hand Shaoyang meridian. If the Renying pulse is two degrees stronger than normal, the illness resides in the foot Taiyang meridian; if it is two degrees stronger and agitated, it lies in the hand Taiyang. If the Renying pulse is three degrees stronger, the illness is located in the foot Yangming meridian; if three degrees stronger and agitated, then it is in the hand Yangming. If the Renying pulse shows four degrees of excess, being both large and rapid, this is called "Yiyang," which signifies an external obstruction.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
脈口一盛，病在足厥陰；厥陰一盛而躁，在手心主。脈口二盛，病在足少陰；二盛而躁，在手少陰。脈口三盛，病在足太陰；三盛而躁，在手太陰。脈口四盛，且大且數者，名曰溢陰。溢陰為內關，內關不通，死不治。人迎與太陰脈口俱盛四倍以上，名曰關格。關格者，與之短期。
		If the radial artery's pulse at the mouth is one degree stronger than normal, illness lies in the foot Jueyin meridian; If it is one degree stronger and accompanied by agitation, the illness resides in the hand Jueyin (Heart-Pericardium) meridian. If the radial artery's pulse is two degrees stronger, the disease lies in the foot Shaoyin meridian; If it is two degrees stronger and accompanied by agitation, then the illness resides in the hand Shaoyin (Heart) meridian. If the radial artery's pulse shows three degrees of excess, the disease is located in the foot Taiyin meridian; If it is three degrees stronger and accompanied by agitation, it resides in the hand Taiyin (Lung) meridian. If the radial artery's pulse exhibits four degrees of excess and is both large and rapid, it is called "Yiyin." "Yiyin" signifies an internal obstruction; if the interior becomes blocked, death will occur and treatment will be ineffective. If both the Renying pulse and the radial artery's Taiyin pulse are four times stronger than normal, this condition is called "Guan Ge." Those with "Guan Ge" have a poor prognosis.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
人迎一盛，寫足少陽而補足厥陰，二寫一補，日一取之，必切而驗之，疏取之，上氣和乃止。人迎二盛，寫足太陽補足少陰，二寫一補，二日一取之，必切而驗之，疏取之，上氣和乃止。人迎三盛，寫足陽明而補足太陰，二寫一補，日二取之，必切而驗之，疏取之，上氣和乃止。
		If the Renying pulse is one-degree excessive, drain the foot Shaoyang and tonify the foot Jueyin; perform two draining treatments for every one tonifying treatment, once daily. It must be carefully tested and verified. Gently apply acupuncture until the upward movement of qi becomes harmonious, then stop. If the Renying pulse is twice excessive, drain the foot Taiyang and tonify the foot Shaoyin; perform two draining sessions for every one tonifying session, once every two days. It must be carefully examined and verified. Apply acupuncture gently until the upward movement of Qi becomes harmonious, then cease. If the Renying pulse is thrice excessive, drain the foot Yangming and tonify the foot Taiyin; perform two draining procedures for every one tonifying procedure, twice daily. It must be closely examined and verified. Administer acupuncture gently until the ascending Qi becomes harmonious, then discontinue.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
脈口一盛，寫足厥陰而補足少陽，二補一寫，日一取之，必切而驗之，疏而取，上氣和乃止。脈口二盛，寫足少陰而補足太陽，二補一寫，二日一取之，必切而驗之，疏取之，上氣和乃止。脈口三盛，寫足太陰而補足陽明，二補一寫，日二取之，必切而驗之，疏而取之，上氣和乃止。所以日二取之者，太、陽主胃，大富於穀氣，故可日二取之也。
		If the radial artery pulse is one degree excessive, drain the foot Jueyin and tonify the foot Shaoyang; perform two tonifying sessions for every one draining session, once daily. It must always be carefully tested and verified; apply acupuncture gently until the ascending qi becomes harmonious, then cease treatment. If the radial artery pulse shows two degrees of excess, drain the foot Shaoyin and tonify the foot Taiyang; perform two tonifying treatments for every one draining treatment, once every two days. This must be carefully tested and confirmed; apply acupuncture gently until upward movement of Qi is harmonious, then stop treatment. If the radial artery pulse exhibits three degrees of excess, drain the foot Taiyin and tonify the foot Yangming; perform two tonifying procedures for every one draining procedure, twice daily. This must always be closely examined and verified; administer acupuncture gently until the ascending Qi is harmonized, then discontinue treatment. The reason for treating twice daily is that the Taiyang and Yangming meridians govern the stomach, which is rich in grain qi; therefore, treatment can be administered twice a day.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
人迎與脈口俱盛三倍以上，命曰陰陽俱溢，如是者不開，則血脈閉塞，氣無所行，流淫於中，五藏內傷。如此者，因而灸之，則變易而為他病矣。
		If both the Renying and radial artery pulses are three times stronger than normal, it is termed "both Yin and Yang overflowing." In such cases, if the condition does not resolve, blood vessels will become blocked, Qi will have no passage to flow, stagnating internally, thereby causing internal injury to the five zang organs. In such cases, if one proceeds with moxibustion treatment, it will transform into another illness.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
凡刺之道，氣調而止，補陰寫陽，音氣益彰，耳目聰明。反此者，血氣不行。
		In general, the principle of acupuncture is to cease when the Qi is regulated; by tonifying Yin and draining Yang, one's voice and breath will become stronger, and hearing and vision will improve. If this principle is violated, blood and qi will not circulate properly.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
所謂氣至而有效者，寫則益虛，虛者，脈大如其故而不堅也；堅如其故者，適雖言故，病未去也。補則益實，實者，脈大如其故而益堅也；夫如其故而不堅者，適雖言快，病未去也。故補則實、寫則虛，痛雖不隨鍼，病必衰去。必先通十二經脈之所生病，而後可得傳於終始矣。故陰陽不相移，虛實不相傾，取之其經。
		What is meant by "the arrival of Qi producing an effect" is that when draining, the deficiency becomes more pronounced; in a deficient condition, the pulse appears large as before but lacks firmness. If the pulse remains firm as it was originally, even though symptoms may appear to have resolved, the illness has not truly departed. When tonifying, excess will increase; in an excessive condition, the pulse is large as before but becomes even firmer. If the pulse remains as it was originally without firmness, although one may feel momentarily relieved, the illness has not been eliminated. Therefore, tonification increases fullness and draining reduces deficiency; even if pain does not immediately subside with the needle, the illness will certainly diminish and depart. One must first understand the diseases that arise in the twelve meridians before one can fully grasp the principles of beginning and end. Therefore, yin and yang do not transform into each other, deficiency and excess do not tip over into one another; treatment should be taken from the affected meridian.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
凡刺之屬，三刺至穀氣，邪僻妄合，陰陽易居，逆順相反，沉浮異處，四時不得，稽留淫泆須鍼而去。故一刺則陽邪出，再刺則陰邪出，三刺則穀氣至，穀氣至而止。所謂穀氣至者，已補而實，已寫而虛，故以知穀氣至也。邪氣獨去者，陰與陽未能調而病知愈也。故曰：補則實，寫則虛，痛雖不隨鍼，病必衰去矣。
		All acupuncture treatments involve three punctures to reach the grain Qi. Pathogenic influences and abnormal combinations disrupt yin and yang, reversing their normal positions; the directions of flow become inverted, with sinking and floating energies misplaced, out of harmony with the four seasons. Such stagnation and excess must be dispelled through acupuncture. Therefore, the first puncture expels Yang pathogenic influences; the second puncture removes Yin pathogenic influences; and the third puncture brings the grain Qi to arrive. When the grain Qi arrives, treatment should cease. What is meant by "the arrival of grain Qi" is that after tonification, the body becomes full; after draining, it becomes empty. Thus one knows when the grain Qi has arrived. When pathogenic influences have departed alone, yin and yang have not yet been harmonized; thus, although symptoms may appear to be resolved, the illness is not truly cured. Therefore it is said: "Tonification increases fullness; draining reduces deficiency." Even if pain does not immediately disappear with the needle, illness will certainly weaken and depart.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
陰盛而陽虛，先補其陽，後寫其陰而和之。陰虛而陽盛，先補其陰，後寫其陽而和之。
		When Yin is excessive and Yang is deficient, first tonify Yang, then drain Yin to restore harmony. When Yin is deficient and Yang is excessive, first tonify Yin, then drain Yang to achieve balance.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
三脈動於足大趾之間，必審其實虛，虛而寫之，是謂重虛。重虛病益甚。凡刺此者，以指按之，脈動而實且疾者疾寫之，虛而徐者則補之。反此者，病益甚。其動也，陽明在上，厥陰在中，少陰在下。
		If three pulses are active between the big toes of the feet, one must carefully examine whether it is real or deficient. To drain a deficiency is called "double deficiency." Double deficiency will only worsen the illness. In treating such conditions, press with your fingers; if the pulse is active, full, and rapid, drain it quickly. If it is deficient and slow, tonify it. If this principle is reversed, the illness will become more severe. When there is movement in the pulse, Yangming is located above, Jueyin in the middle, and Shaoyin below.

膺俞中膺，背俞中背，肩膊虛者，取之上。
		For diseases affecting the chest region, select points on the front; for those affecting the back, choose points on the back. For emptiness in the shoulders and upper arms, take points from above.

重舌，刺舌柱以鈹鍼也。
		For a condition of double tongue (tongue swelling), pierce the base of the tongue with a broad needle (bi zhen).

打開字典
顯示影印本
	終始:	
手屈而不伸者，其病在筋，伸而不屈者，其病在骨，在骨守骨，在筋守筋。
		If the hand is bent but cannot be extended, the illness lies in the tendons; if it can extend but not bend, the problem resides in the bones. When the disease is in the bone, treat the bone; when it is in the tendon, treat the tendon.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
補須一方實，深取之，稀按其痏，以極出其邪氣。一方虛，淺刺之，以養其脈，疾按其痏，無使邪氣得入。邪氣來也緊而疾，穀氣來也徐而和。脈實者深刺之，以泄其氣；脈虛者，淺刺之，使精氣無寫出，以養其脈，獨出其邪氣。
		For tonification treatment, when one side is full and solid, insert the needle deeply; press lightly on the site after needling to fully expel pathogenic influences. When one side is deficient, insert the needle shallowly; this nourishes the pulse. Press firmly on the site immediately after needling to prevent pathogenic influences from entering. Pathogenic Qi arrives quickly and forcefully, whereas grain Qi comes slowly and harmoniously. When the pulse is full, insert deeply to release its excess Qi; If the pulse is deficient, insert shallowly so that vital essence and Qi are not lost, thus nourishing the pulse while expelling only pathogenic influences.

	終始:	
刺諸痛者，其脈皆實。
		When treating various types of pain, all pulses will be found to be full or excessive.

		Therefore it is said: for conditions above the waist, both the hand Taiyin and Yangming meridians are primarily responsible; for conditions below the waist, the foot Taiyin and Yangming channels are mainly in charge.

		When illness is located above, treat it by taking points below; when the disease lies below, take points higher up for treatment; if illness is in the head, treat it at the feet; ծ If the disease is located in the waist, take points at the popliteal region (back of the knee).

		Illness originating from the head causes heaviness in the head; illness arising from the hands causes heaviness in the arms; illness originating from the feet causes heaviness in the feet. In treating illness, one should first needle the location where the disease originates.

	終始:	
春氣在毛，夏氣在皮膚，秋氣在分肉，冬氣在筋骨。刺此病者，各以其時為齊。故刺肥人者，
以1秋冬之齊，刺瘦人者，以春夏之齊。
		Spring Qi resides in the hair; summer Qi is located in the skin and flesh; autumn Qi dwells between the muscles; winter Qi resides in the tendons and bones. When treating these conditions, one should adjust according to their respective seasons. Therefore, when needling a fat person, use the treatment principles of autumn and winter; for thin individuals, apply those of spring and summer.

1. 以 : 舊脫。
22
打開字典
顯示影印本
	終始:	
病痛者，陰也，痛而以手按之不得者，陰也，深刺之。病在上者，陽也。病在下者，陰也。癢者，陽也，淺刺之。
		Pain is a Yin condition. If the pain worsens or cannot be soothed by pressing with the hand, it is also Yin; in such cases, deep needling should be used. When illness resides above, it belongs to Yang. When the disease is located below, it belongs to Yin. Itching is a Yang condition; treat with shallow needling.

打開字典

	終始:	
病先起陰者，先治其陰，而後治其陽；病先起陽者，先治其陽，而後治其陰。
		If the illness first arises in Yin, first treat the Yin aspect and then address the Yang; if the disease initially manifests in Yang, first treat the Yang aspect and subsequently attend to the Yin.

24
打開字典
顯示影印本
	終始:	
刺熱厥者，留鍼反為寒；刺寒厥者，留鍼反為熱。刺熱厥者，二陰一陽；刺寒厥者，二陽一陰。所謂二陰者，二刺陰也；一陽者，一刺陽也。
		When needling for heat syncope (heat extremity), prolonged needle retention can transform it into cold; when needling for cold syncope, prolonged needle retention may reverse it to produce heat. When treating heat syncope, use two Yin points and one Yang point; when treating cold syncope, employ two Yang points and one Yin point. What is meant by "two Yins" refers to needling two Yin meridians; one Yang means needling one Yang meridian.

25
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
久病者，邪氣入深。刺此病者，深內而久留之，間日而復刺之，必先調其左右，去其血脈，刺道畢矣。
		Long-standing illnesses mean that pathogenic influences have penetrated deeply. To treat such chronic conditions, insert the needle deeply and retain it for a long time; repeat needling every other day. First adjust both sides of the body to remove blood stasis from the vessels—this completes the acupuncture method.

凡刺之法，必察其形氣。形肉未脫，少氣而脈又躁，躁厥者，必為繆刺之，散氣可收，聚氣可布。
		The fundamental principle of acupuncture is always to examine one's physical form and Qi condition. If the flesh has not yet atrophied, but there is Qi deficiency and a rapid pulse, in cases of syncope with agitation, one must employ "miu ci" (crossed needling). This method can gather dispersed Qi and distribute accumulated Qi.

		Reside in a quiet and secluded place, observe the ebb and flow of spirit. Close doors and windows to block out disturbances; keep one's soul and spirit from dispersing. Focus the mind on a single intent, distinguishing between essence and Qi. Avoid hearing human voices so as to gather one's essence. Concentrate the spirit entirely, directing one's will toward acupuncture.

		For males, insert inwardly; for females, outwardly. Firmly resist and do not withdraw the needle prematurely; carefully guard without further insertion—this is called "obtaining Qi."

30
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	終始:	
凡刺之禁：新內勿刺，新刺勿內；已醉勿刺，已刺勿醉；新怒勿刺，已刺勿怒；新勞勿刺，已刺勿勞；已飽勿刺，已刺勿飽；已飢勿刺，已刺勿飢；已渴勿刺，已刺勿渴；大驚大恐，必定其氣乃刺之。乘車來者，臥而休之，如食頃乃刺之。出行來者，坐而休之，如行千里頃乃刺之。凡此十二禁者，其脈亂氣散，逆其營衛，經氣不次，因而刺之，則陽病入于陰，陰病出為陽，則邪氣復生。麤工勿察，是謂伐身，形體淫泆，乃消腦髓，津液不化，脫其五味，是謂失氣也。
		General acupuncture taboos: Do not needling immediately after sexual activity, and do not engage in sexual activity immediately after acupuncture; do not needle when drunk, nor drink alcohol after receiving acupuncture; do not needle immediately after anger, and avoid becoming angry after receiving acupuncture; do not needle after excessive exertion, nor engage in strenuous activity after acupuncture. Do not needle immediately after a full meal, and do not eat heavily after receiving acupuncture; do not needle when extremely hungry, nor fast after receiving acupuncture. Do not needle when very thirsty, and do not become dehydrated after receiving acupuncture; After great shock or fear, stabilize the Qi before needling. Those who have traveled by carriage should rest lying down for a while—like the time it takes to eat a meal—before needling. Those who have come after traveling on foot should sit and rest for a while, as long as it takes to walk a thousand li, before needling. All these twelve taboos, if violated, will cause disordered pulse and dispersed Qi, disrupt the ying and wei circulation, disorder the meridian energies. If needling is performed under such conditions, yang pathologies may enter into yin, while yin disorders emerge as yang, thus allowing new pathogenic influences to arise. An unskilled practitioner who fails to observe these taboos harms the body. The physical form becomes weakened, brain marrow is depleted, bodily fluids fail to transform, and the five flavors are lost—this is known as losing one's Qi.

		The Taiyang meridian pulse marks its end. Eyes rolled upward, body convulsing and stiffened, spasms occurring; the complexion turns pale. When skin becomes dry with no perspiration, and sweating ceases entirely—this marks the end of life.

		In cases where the Shaoyang meridian reaches its end, hearing is lost and all joints become flaccid. The optic nerve severs; once this occurs, death follows within a day and a half. When death occurs, the complexion turns blue-white—then death is certain.

打開字典

	終始:	
太陰終者，腹脹閉，不得息，氣噫，善嘔，嘔則逆，逆則面赤，不逆則上下不通，上下不通則面黑，皮毛憔而終矣。
		In cases where the Taiyin meridian reaches its end: abdominal distension and obstruction occur, breathing becomes difficult; belching arises frequently, vomiting is common. If vomiting occurs, Qi reverses upward, causing a red face. If no vomiting occurs, there will be blockage both upward and downward. When passage is blocked in all directions, the complexion turns black, skin and hair wither—this marks the end. 

10. 經脈 Meridians

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
雷公問於黃帝曰：「禁脈」之言，凡刺之理，經脈為始，營其所行，制其度量，內次五藏，外別六府，願盡聞其道。
		Lei Gong asked the Yellow Emperor, "The term 'jinmai'—in general, the principles of acupuncture begin with the meridians. One must understand their courses and regulate their measurements; internally they correspond to the five zang organs, externally they distinguish the six fu organs. I wish to hear fully about this principle."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
黃帝曰：人始生，先成精，精成而腦髓生，骨為幹，脈為營，筋為剛，肉為牆，皮膚堅而毛髮長，穀入于胃，脈道以通，血氣乃行。
		The Yellow Emperor said: When a human being is first born, essence (jing) forms first. Once the jing has formed, brain marrow comes into existence. Bones form the framework; meridians serve as the nutritive system; tendons provide rigidity; flesh serves as walls; skin becomes firm and hair grows long. Grains enter the stomach, the channels become unblocked, and then blood and qi begin to circulate.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	經脈:	
雷公曰：願卒聞經脈之始生。黃帝曰：經脈者，所以能決死生、處百病、調虛實，不可不通。
		Lei Gong said: I wish to hear at once about how the meridians first come into being. The Yellow Emperor said: The meridians are what enable one to determine life and death, diagnose all kinds of diseases, and regulate deficiency and excess. Therefore, they cannot be left ununderstood.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
肺手太陰之脈，起於中焦，下絡大腸，還循胃口，上膈屬肺，從肺系橫出腋下，下循臑內，行少陰心主之前，下肘中，循臂內上骨下廉，入寸口，上魚，循魚際，出大指之端；其支者，從腕後直出次指內廉出其端。
		The Lung meridian of the hand-taiyin originates in the middle jiao, descends to connect with the large intestine, then turns back along the stomach orifice, ascends through the diaphragm to belong to the lung. From the lung root, it extends horizontally out from beneath the armpit, downward along the inner side of the upper arm, running anterior to the Shaoyin and Heart-Pericardium meridians, then down into the elbow center, follows the inner side of the forearm along the lower border of the radial bone, enters the cun Kou (radial artery), ascends past the fish-shaped prominence on the palm, runs along the fish 际 (a point at the base of the thumb), and emerges at the tip of the thumb. Its collateral branch originates behind the wrist, proceeds straight out along the medial side of the second finger, and terminates at its tip.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
是動則病肺脹滿，膨脹而喘咳，缺盆中痛，甚則交兩手而瞀，此為臂厥。是主肺所生病者，咳上氣，喘渴，煩心，胸滿，臑臂內前廉痛厥，掌中熱。氣盛有餘，則肩背痛，風寒汗出中風，小便數而欠。氣虛則肩背痛，寒，少氣不足以息，溺色變。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，寸口大三倍於人迎，虛者，則寸口反小於人迎也。
		When this meridian is disturbed, it causes disease characterized by lung distension and fullness, swelling with shortness of breath and coughing, pain in the hollow at the base of the neck (the "quepen"), and in severe cases, one may cross both hands while experiencing dizziness; this condition is known as bi jue. This meridian governs and treats diseases of the lung, including cough with upward rushing qi, shortness of breath, thirst, irritability of the heart, fullness in the chest, pain or numbness along the anterior medial side of the upper arm and forearm, and heat in the palm. When the qi is excessive or abundant, it causes pain in the shoulders and back; wind-cold with perspiration may result in external wind invasion (wind syndrome), frequent urination, and sighing. When the qi is deficient, it causes pain and coldness in the shoulders and back, shortness of breath with insufficient air to sustain respiration, and changes in urine color. For these various conditions, if the condition is excessive, drain it; if deficient, tonify it. If there is heat, treat quickly; if cold, retain the needle longer. For depressed deficiency, use moxibustion. When neither excess nor deficiency is present, select points according to the meridian itself. Excess condition is indicated when the cun kou pulse is three times larger than the renying pulse; deficiency is indicated when the cunkou pulse is actually smaller than the renying.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
大腸手陽明之脈，起於大指次指之端，循指上廉，出合谷兩骨之間，上入兩筋之中，循臂上廉，入肘外廉，上臑外前廉，上肩，出髃骨之前廉，上出於柱骨之會上，下入缺盆，絡肺，下膈，屬大腸。其支者，從缺盆上頸，貫頰，入下齒中，還出挾口，交人中，左之右，右之左，上挾鼻孔。
		The Large Intestine meridian of the hand-yangming originates at the tip of the second finger. It runs along the lateral side of the finger, emerges between the two bones at Hegu (LI4), ascends into the space between two tendons, follows the anterior lateral aspect of the upper arm downward, enters the outer lateral part of the elbow, continues upward along the anterior lateral side of the upper arm to the shoulder, exits in front of the yu bone, proceeds upward through the junction with the columnar bone (clavicle), then descends into the quepen hollow, connects with the lung, passes downward through the diaphragm, and belongs to the large intestine. Its collateral branch originates from the quepen, ascends along the neck, passes through the cheeks, enters into the lower teeth, then emerges to run alongside the mouth, crosses at the hua tiao (philtrum), with the left side crossing over to the right and vice versa, ascending to flank the nostrils.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
是動則病齒痛，頸腫。是主津液所生病者，目黃，口乾，鼽衄，喉痺，肩前臑痛，大指次指痛不用，氣有餘則當脈所過者熱腫；虛則寒慄不復。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，人迎大三倍於寸口；虛者，人迎反小於寸口也。
		When this meridian is disturbed by excess or dysfunction, it causes toothache and swelling of the neck. This meridian governs conditions related to body fluids. Diseases associated with it include yellowing of the eyes, dry mouth, nasal discharge or nosebleeds, pharyngeal obstruction (hoarseness), pain in front of the shoulder and upper arm, inability to use the thumb and second finger due to pain; if there is excess qi, heat and swelling occur along the course of the meridian. Deficiency results in cold shivering that does not subside. For these various conditions, when excessive, drain it; when deficient, tonify it. When there is heat, treat swiftly; when cold, retain the needle for a longer time. For depressed deficiency, apply moxibustion; if neither excess nor deficiency, select points according to this meridian itself. Excess is indicated when the renying pulse is three times greater than the cunkou. Deficiency is indicated when the renying is actually smaller than the cunkou.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
胃足陽明之脈，起於鼻之交頞中，旁納太陽之脈，下循鼻外，入上齒中，還出挾口環唇，下交承漿，卻循頤後下廉，出大迎，循頰車，上耳前，過客主人，循髮際，至額顱；其支者，從大迎前下人迎，循喉嚨，入缺盆，下膈，屬胃，絡脾；其直者，從缺盆下乳內廉，下挾臍，入氣沖中；其支者，起於胃口，下循腹裏，下至氣沖中而合，以下髀關，抵伏兔，下膝臏中，下循脛外廉，下足跗，入中指內間；其支者，下廉三寸而別下入中趾外間；其支者，別跗上，入大趾間出其端。
		The Stomach meridian of the foot-yangming originates in the nasal bridge at the junction of the nose, laterally connects with the Taiyang meridians, descends along the outer side of the nose into the upper teeth, emerges to flank the mouth and encircle the lips, then downward to meet Chengqiang (ST1), turns back down behind the jawline, exits at Daying (ST5), follows Jiache (ST6) upward in front of the ear, passes over the guest host (a point near the temple), runs along the hairline, and reaches the forehead. Its collateral branch originates in front of Daying (ST5) and descends to Renying, follows the throat, enters the quepen hollow, passes downward through the diaphragm into the stomach, and connects with the spleen. The direct branch proceeds from the quepen downward along the medial side of the breast, descends to flank the navel, and enters the qichong (CV4). Its collateral branch originates at the stomach orifice, descends along the abdominal interior to reach qichong where it converges with other branches; then proceeds downward through Biguan (ST31), reaches Futu (ST32), continues down past the patella, follows the lateral side of the tibia, descends onto the dorsum of the foot, and enters between the inner sides of the second and third toes. Another collateral branch diverges three cun below the lateral knee (Xiaolian) and proceeds downward to enter between the outer sides of the second and third toes; Another collateral branch diverges above the ankle, enters between the first and second toes, and emerges at their tips.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
是動則病洒洒振寒，善呻，數欠，顏黑，病至則惡人與火，聞木聲則惕然而惊，心欲動，獨閉戶塞牖而處。甚則欲上高而歌，棄衣而走，賁嚮腹脹，是為骭厥。是主血所生病者，狂瘧溫淫，汗出，鼽衄，口喎，唇胗，頸腫，喉痺，大腹水腫，膝臏腫痛，循膺乳、氣沖、股、伏兔、骭外廉、足跗上皆痛，中趾不用，氣盛則身以前皆熱，其有餘於胃，則消穀善飢，溺色黃；氣不足則身以前皆寒慄，胃中寒則脹滿。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，人迎大三倍於寸口，虛者，人迎反小於寸口也。
		When this meridian is affected, it causes chills and shivering, frequent groaning, frequent sighing, a dark facial complexion. When the condition manifests, one becomes afraid of people and fire; hearing the sound of wood causes sudden alarm and startle response, with an urge to move in the heart, and one prefers to stay indoors with doors and windows closed. In severe cases, it leads to a desire to climb high places and sing, or one may discard clothing and run about; there is also distension in the abdomen. This condition is known as gan jue. This meridian governs blood-related diseases, including mania, malarial fever with warmth and dampness, perspiration, nasal discharge or nosebleed, facial deviation (oral skew), lip sores, neck swelling, pharyngeal obstruction, abdominal distension with edema, pain and soreness in the knee cap, along the chest, breasts, qichong, thigh, futu, lateral side of the lower leg, dorsum of the foot; the middle toe becomes nonfunctional. When qi is excessive, there is heat over the anterior part of the body; if excess remains in the stomach, it causes rapid digestion and frequent hunger with yellow-colored urine. When qi is insufficient, there is coldness and shivering over the anterior part of the body. Cold in the stomach causes distension and fullness. For these various conditions, treat with draining for excess and tonifying for deficiency. For heat, use rapid needling; for cold, retain the needle. Apply moxibustion for depressed deficiency. When neither excessive nor deficient, select points according to this channel itself. Excess is indicated when the Renying pulse is three times larger than Cunkou; deficiency, when the Renying is actually smaller than Cunkou.

	經脈:	
脾足太陰之脈，起於大趾之端，循趾內側白肉際，過核骨後，上內踝前廉，上踹內，循脛骨後，交出厥陰之前，上膝股內前廉，入腹，屬脾，絡胃，上膈，挾咽，連吞本，散舌下；其支者，復從胃，別上膈、注心中。
		The Spleen meridian of the foot-taiyin originates at the tip of the big toe, runs along the medial side between the skin and white flesh of the toe, passes behind the navicular bone (tuberosity), ascends to the anterior aspect of the inner ankle, continues upward into the thigh's interior, follows the posterior side of the tibia; it crosses in front of the jueyin meridian, ascends along the medial anterior side of the knee and thigh, enters the abdomen, belongs to the spleen, connects with the stomach, passes upward through the diaphragm alongside the throat, links to the root of the tongue, and spreads beneath the tongue; Its collateral branch again diverges from the stomach, ascends separately through the diaphragm and terminates in the heart.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
是動則病舌本強，食則嘔，胃脘痛，腹脹，善噫，得後與氣，則快然如衰，身體皆重。是主脾所生病者，舌本痛，體不能動搖，食不下，煩心，心下急痛，溏瘕泄，水閉，黃疸，不能臥，強立，股膝內腫厥，足大趾不用。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，寸口大三倍於人迎，虛者，寸口反小於人迎。
		When this meridian is disturbed or dysfunctional, it causes stiffness at the root of the tongue; vomiting after eating, epigastric pain, abdominal distension, frequent belching. After passing stool or gas, one feels momentarily relieved as if symptoms have diminished, yet the body remains heavy and sluggish. This meridian governs diseases of the spleen, including pain at the root of the tongue, inability to move or shake the body, difficulty in swallowing food, irritability of the mind, urgent and painful epigastric discomfort, loose stools with mucus (tang jia xie), water retention with urinary obstruction, jaundice, inability to lie down comfortably, compulsive upright posture, swelling and numbness in the inner thighs and knees, and nonfunctionality of the big toe. For these various conditions, drain for excess and tonify for deficiency. Treat quickly for heat; retain the needle for cold. Apply moxibustion in cases of depressed deficiency. If neither excessive nor deficient, treat according to this meridian's points. Excess is indicated when the Cunkou pulse is three times greater than Renying; deficiency, when Cunkou is actually smaller than Renying.

	經脈:	
心手少陰之脈，起於心中，出屬心系，下膈，絡小腸；其支者，從心系，上挾咽，系目系；其直者，復從心系卻上肺，下出腋下，下循臑內後廉，行太陰心主之後，下肘內，循臂內後廉，抵掌後銳骨之端，入掌內後廉，循小指之內，出其端。
		The Heart meridian of the hand-shaoyin originates in the heart, emerges and connects to the pericardial root (xinxi), descends through the diaphragm, connecting with the small intestine; Its collateral branch originates from xinxi and ascends alongside the throat to connect with the optic nerve (mu xi); The direct branch again originates from xinxi, turns upward to the lung, then descends out beneath the armpit. It follows the posterior medial side of the upper arm behind the taiyin and heart-pericardium meridians; proceeds downward into the elbow interior, runs along the posterior inner aspect of the forearm, reaches the end of the sharp bone at the wrist (wrist styloid process), enters the palm from its posterior medial side, follows the inside of the little finger, and emerges at its tip.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
是動則病嗌干，心痛，渴而欲飲，是為臂厥。是主心所生病者，目黃，脅痛，臑臂內後廉痛厥，掌中熱痛。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，寸口大再倍於人迎，虛者，寸口反小於人迎也。
		When this meridian is dysfunctional or disturbed, it causes dryness in the throat (yidi), heart pain, thirst with a desire to drink; this condition is known as arm-jue. This meridian governs heart-related diseases, including yellowing of the eyes (mu huang), flank pain, pain or numbness on the posterior medial side of the arm and forearm, as well as heat and pain in the palm. For these various conditions, use draining for excess and tonification for deficiency. Treat swiftly for heat; retain the needle longer for cold. Apply moxiburnion in cases of depression or deficiency. When neither excessive nor insufficient, select points according to this meridians' course. Excess is indicated when the cunkou (radial) pulse is twice as large as renying; deficiency, when cunkou is actually smaller than renying.

	經脈:	
小腸手太陽之脈，起於小指之端，循手外側，上腕，出踝中，直上循臂骨下廉，出肘內側兩筋之間，上循臑外後廉，出肩解，繞肩胛，交肩上，入缺盆，絡心，循咽，下膈，抵胃，屬小腸；其支者，從缺盆循頸上頰，至目銳眥，卻入耳中；其支者，別頰上䪼，抵鼻，至目內眥，斜絡於顴。
		The Small Intestine meridian of hand-taiyang originates at the tip of the little finger. It runs along the ulnar side of the hand, ascends to the wrist, emerges from between the bones at Wangu (SI4), proceeds straight up along the lower lateral aspect of the arm bone, exits between two tendons on the inner side of the elbow, ascends along the posterior lateral side of the upper arm; it then emerges at Jianjie (shoulder joint), circles around the scapula, crosses over the shoulder, enters the quepen hollow to connect with the heart, follows the throat downward through the diaphragm toward the stomach, and belongs to the small intestine. Its collateral branch originates from quepen, follows the neck upward along the cheeks to reach the lateral canthus of the eye (outer corner), then turns inward into the ear. Another collateral branch diverges from the cheeks upward to the forehead, reaches the nose, arrives at the medial canthus of the eye, and obliquely connects with the cheekbone.

	經脈:	
是動則病嗌痛，頷腫，不可以顧，肩似拔，臑似折。是主液所生病者，耳聾、目黃，頰腫，頸、頷、肩、臑、肘、臂外後廉痛。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，人迎大再倍於寸口，虛者，人迎反小於寸口也。
