	經脈:	
膀胱足太陽之脈，起於目內眥，上額，交巔；其支者，從巔至耳上角；其直者，從巔入絡腦，還出別下項，循肩髆內，挾脊，抵腰中，入循膂，絡腎，屬膀胱；其支者，從腰中下挾脊，貫臀，入膕中；其支者，從髆內左右，別下，貫胛，挾脊內，過髀樞，循髀外，從後廉，下合膕中，以下貫踹內，出外踝之後，循京骨，至小趾外側。
		The Bladder meridian of foot-taiyang originates at the medial canthus (inner corner) of the eye, ascends to the forehead and crosses over the vertex of the head; Its collateral branch extends from the vertex up to the upper angle of the ear; The direct branch proceeds from the vertex into the brain, then exits separately downward along the back of the neck; it follows the inner side of the shoulder blade, flanks the spine, reaches the waist region, enters and runs along the erector spinae muscles, connects with the kidney, and belongs to the bladder. Its collateral branch descends from the waist along either side of the spine, passes through the buttocks, and enters into the popliteal fossa. Another collateral branch originates from the inner side of each shoulder blade, diverges downward to pass through the scapulae and flank the spine internally; it passes over the hip joint (髀樞), follows the outer aspect of the thigh along its posterior border, descends into the popliteal region, continues down through the medial part of the leg, emerges behind the lateral malleolus, runs along Jinggu (BL58), and terminates at the outer side of the little toe.

17
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
是動則病沖頭痛，目似脫，項如拔，脊痛，腰似折，髀不可以曲，膕如結，踹如裂，是為踝厥。是主筋所生病者，痔、瘧、狂、癲疾、頭𩕄項痛，目黃、淚出，鼽衄，項、背、腰、尻、膕踹、腳皆痛，小趾不用。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，人迎大再倍於寸口，虛者，人迎反小於寸口也。
		When this meridian is in motion or disturbed, it causes headache at the vertex as if being pierced (chong tou tòng), a sensation that the eyes are about to fall out (mu si tuo), stiffness in the neck like something pulling upward (xiang ru ba), back pain, a feeling of the waist being broken (yao si zhe), inability to bend the thigh (bi bu ke yi qu), tightness and immobility at the popliteal area as if knotted (gui ru jie), and splitting-like pain in the lower leg (chuan ru lie). This condition is known as hui jue. This meridian governs disorders related to tendons and muscles, including hemorrhoids, malaria, mania, epilepsy, headache at the vertex with neck pain (tou hua xiang tòng), yellowing of the eyes (yu huang), excessive tearing, nasal discharge or nosebleeding, as well as pain in the neck, back, waist, buttocks, popliteal region, lower leg, and feet. It also causes dysfunction of the little toe. For these various conditions, apply draining for excess and tonification for deficient states. Treat swiftly in cases of heat; retain the needle longer when cold is present. Apply moxibustion to depressed or collapsed deficiency. When neither excessively full nor deficient, select acupuncture points according to this meridian's course. Excess is indicated when the renying pulse is twice as large as the cunkou; deficiency is when the renying is actually smaller than the cunkoi.

18
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
腎足少陰之脈，起於小趾之下，邪走足心，出於然谷之下，循內踝之後，別入跟中，以上踹內，出膕內廉，上股內後廉，貫脊，屬腎，絡膀胱；其直者，從腎上貫肝膈，入肺中，循喉嚨，挾舌本；其支者，從肺出絡心，注胸中。
		The Kidney meridian of foot-shaoyin originates beneath the little toe, obliquely runs toward the sole of the foot, emerges below Ran Gu (KI2), follows behind the inner ankle, diverges into the heel, ascends along the medial side of the lower leg to emerge from the popliteal interior, continues upward along the posterior medial aspect of the thigh, passes through the spine, belongs to the kidney, and connects with the bladder; The direct branch originates from the kidney, passes upward through the liver and diaphragm into the lungs, follows the throat to flank the root of the tongue. Its collateral branch diverges from the lung, connects with the heart, and terminates in the chest cavity.

19
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
是動則病飢不欲食，面如漆柴，咳唾則有血，喝喝而喘，坐而欲起，目𥆨𥆨如無所見，心如懸若飢狀。氣不足則善恐，心惕惕如人將捕之，是為骨厥。是主腎所生病者，口熱，舌乾，咽腫，上氣，嗌乾及痛，煩心，心痛，黃疸，腸澼，脊股內後廉痛，痿厥，嗜臥，足下熱而痛。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。灸則強食生肉，緩帶披發，大杖重履而步。盛者，寸口大再倍於人迎，虛者，寸口反小於人迎也。
		When this meridian is disturbed and in motion, it causes a sensation of hunger without desire to eat; the face appears ashen like charred lacquerwood (mian ru qi chai); coughing or spitting brings up blood; one breathes with a sound like "he he" while experiencing shortness of breath. One may sit but wish to stand up, and experience visual disturbances where the eyes appear dim and unable to see clearly, as if nothing is in sight. The heart feels suspended, as though suffering from hunger. When the qi of this meridian is insufficient, it causes frequent fear and anxiety; one feels a constant sense of apprehension as if someone were about to capture them. This condition is known as gu jue (bone-jue). This meridian governs kidney-related diseases, including oral heat (kou re), dryness of the tongue (she gan), throat swelling (yan zhong), upward rushing qi (shang qi), dry and painful throat (ai gan ji tong), irritability of the heart (fan xin), cardiac pain (xin tòng), jaundice (huang dan), intestinal disorders with mucus or diarrhea (chang pi), pain along the posterior medial sides of the back, thighs, and lower legs; atrophy and coldness in extremities (wei jue), excessive desire to lie down (shi wo), as well as heat and pain beneath the feet. For these various conditions, employ draining for excess and tonification in cases of deficiency. Treat swiftly when there is heat; retain the needle longer if cold is present. Use moxibustion where there is depressed or collapsed deficiency. In cases that are neither excessive nor deficient, choose points according to this meridian's course. When using moxibustion, one should eat more nourishing raw meat, loosen the belt and let down the hair, walk slowly with a heavy staff in hand and wearing sturdy shoes. Excess is indicated when Cunkou (radial pulse) is twice as large as Renying; deficiency, when it is actually smaller.

	經脈:	
心主手厥陰心包絡之脈，起於胸中，出屬心包絡，下膈，歷絡三焦；其支者，循胸出脅，下腋三寸，上抵腋下，循臑內，行太陰、少陰之間，入肘中，下臂，行兩筋之間，入掌中，循中指，出其端；其支者，別掌中，循小指次指，出其端。
		The Pericardium meridian of hand-jueyin, which belongs to the heart and connects with its pericardial network, originates in the chest cavity, emerges to connect with the pericardium, descends through the diaphragm (ge), and interconnects with the triple heater (sanjiao); Its collateral branch runs along the chest and emerges from beneath the ribs, descends three cun below the armpit to ascend again toward the armpit area; it follows the medial side of the upper arm between the taiyin (lung) and shaoyin (heart) meridians, enters into the elbow center, proceeds down the forearm along two tendons, enters the palm, runs alongside the middle finger, and emerges at its tip; Another collateral branch diverges from within the palm, follows along the second toe (the index finger), and terminates at its end.

	經脈:	
是動則病手心熱，臂肘攣急，腋腫，甚則胸脅支滿，心中憺憺大動，面赤，目黃，喜笑不休。是主脈所生病者，煩心，心痛，掌中熱。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，寸口大一倍於人迎，虛者，寸口反小於人迎也。
		When this meridian is disturbed in motion, it causes heat in the palm of the hand (shou xin re), cramping and tension in the arm and elbow (bi zhou luan ji), swelling at the armpit (ya zhong). In severe cases, there may be distension and fullness extending from the chest to the flanks (xiong xie zhi man), a strong palpitation sensation in the heart (xin zhong dan dan da dong), red face (mian chi), yellow eyes (mu huang), and uncontrollable laughter (xi xiao bu xiu). This meridian governs pulse-related diseases, including irritability of the heart (fen xin), cardiac pain (xiong tòng), and heat in the palm (zhang zhong re). For these various conditions, draining is used for excess and tonification is applied in cases of deficiency. For heat symptoms, treat quickly; retain the needle longer in cold conditions. Excess is indicated when the radial pulse (cunkou) is one and a half times larger than renying; deficiency is when cunkou is smaller than renying.

三焦手少陽之脈，起於小指次指之端，上出兩指之間，循手表腕，出臂外兩骨之間，上貫肘，循臑外，上肩，而交出足少陽之後，入缺盆，布膻中，散落心包，下膈，循屬三焦；其支者，從膻中上出缺盆，上項系耳後，直上出耳上角，以屈下頰至䪼，其支者，從耳後入耳中，出走耳前，過客主人前，交頰，至目銳眥。
		The Triple Heater meridian of hand-shaoyang originates at the tip of the index finger, ascends between two fingers, follows along the back side of the wrist and forearm, emerges between two bones on the outer aspect of the arm, passes upward through the elbow, runs along the exterior of the upper arm to reach the shoulder; it then crosses behind the foot-shaoyang meridian before entering the quepen hollow. Another collateral branch diverges behind the ear into the ear canal, exits in front of it, passes before the guest host (temporal region), crosses the cheeks, and terminates at the lateral canthus of the eyes.

	經脈:	
膽足少陽之脈，起於目銳眥，上抵頭角下耳後，循頸行手少陽之前，至肩上卻交出手少陽之後，入缺盆；其支者，從耳後入耳中，出走耳前，至目銳眥後；其支者，別銳眥，下大迎，合於手少陽，抵於䪼下，加頰車，下頸，合缺盆，以下胸中，貫膈，絡肝，屬膽，循脅裏，出氣沖，繞毛際，橫入髀厭中；其直者，從缺盆下腋，循胸，過季脅下合髀厭中，以下循髀陽，出膝外廉，下外輔骨之前，直下抵絕骨之端，下出外踝之前，循足跗上，入小趾次趾之間；其支者，別跗上，入大指之間，循大指歧骨內，出其端，還貫爪甲，出三毛。
		The Gallbladder meridian of foot-shaoyang originates at the lateral canthus (outer corner) of the eye. It ascends to reach the vertex, then descends behind the ears; it runs along the neck in front of the hand-shaoyang meridians and reaches the shoulder region before crossing over behind the hand-shaoyang, entering into the quepen hollow; Its collateral branch originates from behind the ear, enters the ear canal, exits to run in front of the ear and terminates at the posterior aspect of the lateral canthus. Another collateral branch diverges posterior to the lateral canthus, descends along Daying (ST5), converges with the hand-shaoyang at the forehead region, reaches beneath the temporal bone, passes Jiache (ST6), proceeds downward through the neck into quepen hollow. From there it continues down into the thoracic cavity, pierces the diaphragm, connects to the liver, belongs to the gallbladder, follows along the interior of the flanks, emerges at qichong (CV4), circles around the pubic region, and horizontally enters the upper part of the hip. The direct branch proceeds downward from quepen into the armpit region, follows along the chest wall, passes beneath the seasonal flanks (ji xie) to converge with the upper part of the thigh. It continues down along the anterior aspect of the outer thigh, descends in front of the fibula bone, straightens downward to reach the end of the calcaneus (the lateral malleolar region), emerges before the lateral ankle, follows up the dorsum of the foot and enters between the little toe and second toe; Its collateral branch diverges above the foot's dorsum, enters between the big toe and second toe, follows along the inner side of its bony prominence (the metatarsal head), emerges at its tip, returns to pass through the nail bed, and exits at the three tufts of hair near the base.

是動則病口苦，善太息，心脅痛，不能轉側，甚則面微有塵，體無膏澤，足外反熱，是為陽厥。是主骨所生病者，頭痛，頷痛，目銳眥痛，缺盆中腫痛，腋下腫，馬刀俠癭，汗出振寒，瘧，胸、脅、肋、髀、膝外至脛、絕骨、外踝前及諸節皆痛，小趾次趾不用。為此諸病，盛則寫之，虛則補之，熱則疾之，寒則留之，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。盛者，人迎大一倍於寸口，虛者，人迎反小於寸口也。

	經脈:	
肝足厥陰之脈，起於大趾叢毛之際，上循足跗上廉，去內踝一寸，上踝八寸，交出太陰之後，上膕內廉，循股陰，入毛中，過陰器，抵小腹，挾胃，屬肝，絡膽，上貫膈，布脅肋，循喉嚨之後，上入頏顙，連目系，上出額，與督脈會於巔；其支者，從目系下頰裏，環唇內；其支者，復從肝，別貫膈，上注肺。

打開字典

顯示影印本
相關討論
	經脈:	
手太陰氣絕，則皮毛焦。太陰者，行氣溫於皮毛者也。故氣不榮，則皮毛焦；皮毛焦，則津液去皮節；津液去皮節者，則爪枯毛折；毛折者，則毛先死。丙篤丁死，火勝金也。
		When the qi of the hand-taiyin meridian ceases entirely, the skin and hair become dry and scorched (jiao). The taiyin channels circulate qi to warm the skin and fur. Therefore, when this qi fails to nourish properly, the skin and hair dry out and become scorched; When the skin and fur are scorched, body fluids (jin ye) depart from the skin and joints. When body fluids leave the skin and joints, then nails become dry and brittle, and hair breaks easily. When hair breaks (mao zhe), it indicates that the hair has already died first. Death occurs on days of Bing Du or Ding Si, when fire overcomes metal.

29
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
手少陰氣絕，則脈不通；脈不通，則血不流；血不流，則髮色不澤，故其面黑如漆柴者，血先死。壬篤癸死，水勝火也。
		When the qi of hand-shaoyin ceases entirely, then the vessels become obstructed; If the vessels are not unobstructed, blood cannot circulate. When blood does not flow properly, hair loses its luster; thus when the face appears black as if covered with pitch or charred wood (qi chai), it indicates that blood has already died. Death occurs on days of Ren Du or Gui Si, when water overcomes fire.

	經脈:	
足太陰氣絕者，則脈不榮肌肉。唇舌者，肌肉之本也。脈不榮，則肌肉軟；肌肉軟，則舌萎人中滿；人中滿，則唇反；唇反者，肉先死。甲篤乙死，木勝土也。
		When the qi of foot-taiyin ceases entirely, vessels fail to nourish the muscles. The lips and tongue are the root of muscle function. When vessels fail to nourish, muscles become soft; Soft and weak muscles cause the tongue to atrophy (wei) and fill up the philtrum. When the philtrum is full, the lips turn outward; Lips turning outward indicate that muscle tissue has already died first. Death occurs on days of Jia Du or Yi Si, when wood overcomes earth.

打開字典

	經脈:	
足少陰氣絕，則骨枯。少陰者，冬脈也，伏行而濡骨髓者也，故骨不濡，則肉不能著也；骨肉不相親，則肉軟卻；肉軟卻，故齒長而垢，髮無澤；髮無澤者，骨先死。戊篤己死，土勝水也。
		When the qi of foot-shaoyin ceases completely, bones become dry and withered (gu ku). The shaoyin is the winter pulse, which flows internally to moisten bone marrow. Therefore, when bones are not moisturized, muscles cannot adhere properly; When bone and muscle no longer support each other, the muscles become soft and recede; Soft and receding muscles cause teeth to lengthen with plaque buildup and hair to lose its luster. When the hair loses luster, it indicates that bones have already died first. Death occurs on days of Wu Du or Ji Si, when earth overcomes water.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
足厥陰氣絕，則筋絕。厥陰者，肝脈也，肝者，筋之合也，筋者，聚於陰氣，而脈絡於舌本也。故脈弗榮，則筋急；筋急則引舌與卵，故唇青舌卷卵縮，則筋先死。庚篤辛死，金勝木也。
		When the qi of foot-jueyin ceases completely, tendons become severed (jin jue). The jueyin is the liver meridian. The liver governs tendons, and tendons gather in yin energy while their vessels connect to the base of the tongue. Therefore, when vessels fail to nourish properly, tendons become tense (jin ji); Tense tendons pull the tongue and testicles upward; thus when lips appear blue, the tongue curls back and testicles retract, it indicates that tendons have already died first. Death occurs on days of Geng Du or Xin Si, when metal overcomes wood.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
五陰氣俱絕，則目系轉，轉則目運；目運者，為志先死；志先死，則遠一日半死矣。六陽氣絕，則陰與陽相離，離則腠理發泄，絕汗乃出，故旦占夕死，夕占旦死。
		When the qi of all five yin meridians ceases entirely, the optic nerve (mu xi) rotates; this rotation causes the eyes to move uncontrollably. Uncontrolled eye movement indicates that mental willpower has already died first. When the mind (zhi) dies first, death occurs within one and a half days. When the qi of all six yang meridians ceases, yin and yang separate. When they separate, pores open and release fluids, resulting in a final sweat (jue han). Therefore, if this occurs at dawn, death follows by dusk; if it occurs at dusk, death follows by dawn.

34
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
經脈十二者，伏行分肉之間，深而不見；其常見者，足太陰過於外踝之上，無所隱故也。諸脈之浮而常見者，皆絡脈也。六經絡，手陽明少陽之大絡，起於五指間，上合肘中。飲酒者，衛氣先行皮膚，先充絡脈，絡脈先盛。故衛氣已平，營氣乃滿，而經脈大盛。脈之卒然動者，皆邪氣居之，留於本末，不動則熱，不堅則陷且空，不與眾同，是以知其何脈之動也。
		The twelve meridians run concealed within the layers of muscle tissue, lying deep and unseen; The only one that is commonly visible is the foot-taiyin which passes above the outer ankle because it lies unhidden. All vessels that are superficial and frequently seen belong to the collateral (luo) network. The six major collaterals include the large collaterals of hand-yangming and shaoyang, which originate between the fingers and ascend to converge in the elbow region. When one consumes alcohol, defensive qi first moves outward to the skin, filling the collateral vessels first; thus, collaterals become full before other channels. Therefore, after defensive qi has stabilized, nutritive qi becomes abundant and the main meridians greatly expand in energy flow. Sudden pulsations in the vessels indicate that pathogenic qi has taken residence, lingering at either ends or middle of a channel. If it does not move, heat arises; if it is not firm, it becomes depressed and hollow. Since such pulses differ from normal patterns, one can thereby determine which vessel's pulse is affected.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
雷公曰：何以知經脈之與絡脈異也？黃帝曰：經脈者，常不可見也，其虛實也，以氣口知之。脈之見者，皆絡脈也。
		Lei Gong asked: How do we know the difference between meridians and collateral vessels? The Yellow Emperor said: Meridians are normally invisible; their deficiency or excess can be determined by examining the cunkou pulse. All vessels that appear visible belong to collateral (luo) channels.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經脈:	
雷公曰：細子無以明其然也。黃帝曰：諸絡脈皆不能經大節之間，必行絕道而出入，復合於皮中，其會皆見於外。故諸刺絡脈者，必刺其結上甚血者。雖無結，急取之，以寫其邪而出其血。留之發為痺也。凡診絡脈，脈色青，則寒，且痛；赤則有熱。胃中寒，手魚之絡多青矣；胃中有熱，魚際絡赤。其暴黑者，留久痺也。其有赤、有黑、有青者，寒熱氣也。其青短者，少氣也。凡刺寒熱者，皆多血絡，必間日而一取之，血盡而止，乃調其虛實。其小而短者，少氣，甚者，寫之則悶，悶甚則仆，不得言，悶則急坐之也。
		Lei Gong said: I, a young student, cannot yet fully understand this clearly. The Yellow Emperor said: All collateral vessels cannot pass through the major joints; they must travel along secluded pathways, entering and exiting, then reuniting within the skin. Their junctions are all visible externally. Therefore, when needling collateral vessels, one must needle at their knots where blood is most concentrated and prominent. Even if no knot exists, quickly needle the area to drain pathogenic factors and release blood. If left untreated, it may develop into bi syndrome (bi zheng). In general, when diagnosing collateral vessels: if the vessel color is blue, cold and pain are present; If red, there is heat. When the stomach is cold, collateral vessels on the fish's belly area of the hand often appear blue; When there is heat in the stomach, the collateral vessel at Yuji appears red. Those that appear suddenly black indicate long-standing bi syndrome. Those showing red, black or blue colors reflect the presence of cold and heat pathogenic influences. Short and blue vessels indicate qi deficiency. When needling conditions involving cold and heat, one often encounters blood collaterals. Needling should be performed every other day until the blood is exhausted; then adjust according to deficiency or excess patterns. Those with small and short vessels indicate qi deficiency; in severe cases, drainage needling may cause dizziness (men), extreme dizziness can lead to fainting or loss of speech. When dizziness occurs, the patient should be made to sit upright immediately.

11. 經別 Separate Meridians

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經別:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：余聞人之合於天道也，內有五藏，以應五音、五色、五時、五味、五位也；外有六府，以應六律。六律建陰陽諸經而合之十二月、十二辰、十二節、十二經水、十二時、十二經脈者，此五藏六府之所以應天道。夫十二經脈者，人之所以生，病之所以成，人之所以治，病之所以起，學之所始，工之所止也。麤之所易，上之所難也。請問其離合，出入奈何？歧伯稽首再拜曰：明乎哉問也！此麤之所過，上之所息也，請卒言之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "I have heard that human beings correspond to the principles of Heaven and Earth. Internally there are five viscera corresponding to the Five Tones, the Five Colors, the Five Seasons, the Five Flavors, and the Five Positions." Externally there are six bowels corresponding to the Six Laws. The Six Laws establish the Yin and Yang meridians, corresponding to the twelve months, the twelve earthly branches, the twelve solar terms, the twelve major rivers, the twelve hours, and the twelve primary meridians. This is how the five viscera and six bowels correspond to the principles of Heaven. The twelve primary meridians are what sustain human life, the basis for disease formation, the foundation of health treatment, and the origin of illness. They mark the beginning of medical study and the culmination of a physician's expertise. What is easy for the unskilled is difficult for those at the highest level. Please explain their convergence and divergence, as well as their entry and exit points. Qi Bo bowed respectfully twice and said, "What a profound question!" This is what the unskilled overlook but the most skilled rest upon. Please allow me to explain it fully.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	經別:	
足太陽之正，別入于膕中，其一道下尻五寸，別入于肛，屬於膀胱，散之腎，循膂，當心入散；直者，從膂上出於項，復屬於太陽，此為一經也。足少陰之正，至膕中，別走太陽而合，上至腎，當十四椎出屬帶脈；直者，系舌本，復出於項，合於太陽，此為一合。成以諸陰之別，皆為正也。
		The main branch of the Foot Taiyang meridian branches off and enters into the popliteal region. One pathway descends five cun below the coccyx, another enters the anus, connects to the bladder, spreads to the kidney, follows along the spine, and disperses at the heart. The direct branch ascends from the spine through the neck and reassociates with the Taiyang meridian; this constitutes one meridian. The main branch of the Foot Shaoyin meridian reaches the popliteal region, branches off to associate with the Taiyang and converge, ascends to the kidney, at the fourteenth vertebra it connects to the Belt Vessel; The direct branch is connected to the root of the tongue, exits through the neck again, and converges with the Taiyang; this constitutes one convergence. All these branches from the Yin meridians are considered main branches.

12. 經水 Great Rivers

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經水:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：經脈十二者，外合於十二經水，而內屬於五藏六府。夫十二經水者，其有大小、深淺、廣狹、遠近各不同；五藏六府之高下、大小、受谷之多少亦不等，相應奈何？夫經水者，受水而行之；五藏者，合神氣魂魄而藏之；六府者，受穀而行之，受氣而揚之；經脈者，受血而營之。合而以治，奈何？刺之深淺，灸之壯數，可得聞乎？
		The Yellow Emperor asked Chi Bo: "The twelve meridians externally correspond to the twelve great rivers, and internally belong to the five zang and six fu organs." The twelve great rivers differ in size, depth, width, and distance. The five zang and six fu organs also vary in height, size, and the amount of food they receive; how do these correspond? The great rivers receive water and carry it along; the five zang organs combine spirit, qi, soul, and corporeal soul and store them; the six fu organs receive food and transport it, receive qi and circulate it; The meridians receive blood and nourish the body with it. How do these combine to produce harmony? May I ask about the depth of needling and the number of moxibustion applications?

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經水:	
歧伯答曰：善哉問也！天至高不可度，地至廣不可量，此之謂也。且夫人生於天地之間，六合之內，此天之高，地之廣也，非人力之所能度量而至也。若夫八尺之士，皮肉在此，外可度量切循而得之，其死可解剖而視之。其藏之堅脆，府之大小，穀之多少，脈之長短，血之清濁，氣之多少，十二經之多血少氣，與其少血多氣，與其皆多血氣，與其皆少血氣，皆有大數。其治以鍼艾，各調其經氣，固其常有合乎。
		Chi Bo replied: "What a good question!" "Heaven is so high it cannot be measured, and earth is so vast it cannot be quantified; this is what I mean." Moreover, human beings live between heaven and earth, within the six directions; this is the height of heaven and the breadth of earth, which cannot be measured or reached by human effort. As for a man of eight chi in height, his skin and flesh are here; externally they can be measured, palpated, and understood. When he is dead, one can dissect him to see them. The hardness or fragility of the zang organs, the size of the fu organs, the amount of food they receive, the length of the meridians, the clarity or turbidity of blood, and the quantity of qi—all these factors, whether the twelve meridians have more blood and less qi, or less blood and more qi, or both in abundance, or both deficient—each has a general standard. Their treatment with acupuncture and moxibustion should each regulate the qi of their respective meridians; thus, there is a consistent principle to follow.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經水:	
黃帝曰：余聞之，快於耳不解於心，願卒聞之。歧伯答曰：此人之所以參天地而應陰陽也，不可不察。足太陽外合清水，內屬膀胱，而通水道焉。足少陽外合于渭水，內屬于膽。足陽明外合于海水，內屬于胃。足太陰外合于湖水，內屬于脾。足少陰外合于汝水，內屬于腎。足厥陰外合于澠水，內屬于肝。手太陽外合淮水，內屬小腸，而水道出焉。手少陽外合于漯水，內屬于三焦。手陽明外合于江水，內屬于大腸。手太陰外合于河水，內屬于肺。手少陰外合于濟水，內屬于心。手心主外合于漳水，內屬于心包。凡此五藏六府十二經水者，外有源泉，而內有所稟，此皆內外相貫，如環無端，人經亦然。故天為陽，地為陰，腰以上為天，腰以下為地。故海以北者為陰，湖以北者為陰中之陰；漳以南者為陽，河以北至漳者為陽中之陰；漯以南至江者，為陽中之太陽，此一隅之陰陽也，所以人與天地相參也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I have heard this, and it sounds pleasing to my ears but I do not yet understand in my heart. I wish to hear the full explanation." Chi Bo replied: "This is how human beings correspond with heaven and earth and respond to yin and yang; it cannot be ignored." The Foot Taiyang externally corresponds to the Qingshui River, internally belongs to the bladder, and communicates with the water passages. The Foot Shaoyang externally corresponds to the Weishui River, and internally belongs to the gallbladder. The Foot Yangming externally corresponds to the Hai Shui River, and internally belongs to stomach. The Foot Taiyin externally corresponds to the Hushui River, and internally belongs to spleen. The Foot Shaoyin externally corresponds to Ruhui River, and internally belongs kidney. The Foot Jueyin externally corresponds to Mianshui River, and internally belong liver. The Hand Taiyang externally corresponds to Huaishui River, internally belongs small intestine, and water passages emerge from it. The Hand Shaoyang externally corresponds to Luoshui River, and internally belong Sanjiao. The Hand Yangming externally corresponds to Jiangshui River, and internally belongs large intestine. The Hand Taiyin externally corresponds Heishui River, and internally belong lung. The Hand Shaoyin externally corresponds Jishui River, and internally heart. The Hand Jueyin externally correspond Zhangshui River, and internally pericardium. All these five zang, six fu, and twelve great rivers have external sources of spring water and internal receptacles; they are internally and externally interconnected like a ring with no beginning or end. The meridians in the human body are likewise. Therefore, heaven is yang and earth is yin; above the waist corresponds to heaven, below the waist corresponds to earth. Thus, areas north of the Hai Shui are yin, and those north of the Hushui belong to yin within yin; areas south of Zhangshui are yang, and areas north of Heishui up to Zhangshui belong to yin within yang; areas south of Luoshui up to Jiangshui belong to taiyang within yang. These are the yin and yang characteristics of one corner, which is how human beings correspond with heaven an earth.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經水:	
黃帝曰：夫經水之應經脈也，其遠近淺深，水血之多少，各不同，合而以刺之奈何？歧伯答曰：足陽明，五藏六府之海也，其脈大，血多氣盛，熱壯，刺此者不深勿散，不留不寫也。足陽明刺深六分，留十呼。足太陽深五分，留七呼。足少陽深四分，留五呼。足太陰深三分，留四呼。足少陰深二分，留三呼。足厥陰深一分，留二呼。手之陰陽，其受氣之道近，其氣之來疾，其刺深者，皆無過二分，其留，皆無過一呼。其少長、大小、肥瘦，以心擦之，命曰法天之常，灸之亦然。灸而過此者，得惡火則骨枯脈澀，刺而過此者，則脫氣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Regarding the correspondence between the great rivers and the meridians, their distances, depths, and amounts of water and blood differ. How should acupuncture be applied in accordance with these differences?" Chi Bo replied: "The Foot Yangming is the sea of the five zang and six fu. Its meridian is large, with abundant blood and strong qi, and it generates intense heat. When needling this meridian, do not needle too shallowly to disperse; do not leave the needle in place without draining." Needle the Foot Yangming six fen deep and leave the needle for ten breaths. Needle the Foot Taiyang five fen deep, and leave the needle in place for seven breaths. Needle the Foot Shaoyang four fen deep, and leave it in place for five breaths. Needle the Foot Taiyin three fen deep, and leave for four breaths. Needle the Foot Shaoyin two fen deep, and leave it for three breaths. Needle the Foot Jueyin one fen deep, and leave in place for two breaths. For the yin and yang meridians of the hands, their pathways for receiving qi are close to the body surface, and their qi arrives quickly. Therefore, when needling deeply in these meridians, do not exceed two fen in depth; the needle should remain no longer than one breath. Their height, age, size, and body type must be carefully considered; this is called following the constant principles of heaven. Moxibustion should also follow these same principles. If moxibustion exceeds this standard, the patient may suffer from harmful fire, leading to bone dryness and sluggish meridians. If needling exceeds this standard, it will cause qi deficiency.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經水:	
黃帝曰：夫經脈之大小，血之多少，膚之厚薄，肉之堅脆及膕之大小，可為量度乎？歧伯答曰：其可為度量者，取其中度也。不甚脫肉，而血氣不衰也。若夫度之人，消瘦而形肉脫者，惡可以度量刺乎。審、切、循、捫、按，視其寒溫盛衰而調之，是謂因適而為之真也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Can the size of meridians, amount of blood, thickness or thinness of skin, firmness or fragility of flesh, and size of joints be measured?" Chi Bo replied: "Those that can be measured should take the moderate standard." As long as there is not excessive wasting of flesh and blood and qi are not weakened. But for those who are emaciated with loss of body form and flesh, how can one measure and needle them? Carefully examine by palpation, pressing, feeling along the meridians and touching them; observe their coldness or warmth, excess or deficiency, and regulate accordingly. This is called truly adapting to the individual's condition. 

13. 經筋 Meridians and Tendons

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經筋:	
足太陽之筋，起於足小趾，上結于踝，邪上結于膝，其下循足外側，結于踵，上循跟，結於膕；其別者，結于腨外，上膕中內廉，與膕中并上結于臀，上挾脊上項；其支者，別入結于舌本；其直者，結于枕骨，上頭，下顏，結于鼻；其支者，為目上網，下結于頄；其支者，從腋后外廉結于肩髃；其支者，入腋下，上出缺盆，上結於完骨；其支者，出缺盆，邪上出于頄。其病小趾支跟腫痛，膕攣，脊反折，項筋急，肩不舉，腋支缺盆中紐痛，不可左右搖。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，名曰仲春痺
也1。
		The Yang meridian of the foot originates at the little toe of the foot, ascends and connects to the ankle, obliquely rises and connects to the knee. Its lower branch follows along the outer side of the foot, connecting to the heel, then upward along the heel, connecting to the popliteal region. Its collateral branch connects externally on the calf, ascends along the inner edge of the popliteal region, combines with the main line in the popliteal area and ascends to connect with the buttocks, then proceeds upward alongside the spine up to the back of the neck; Its branch diverges inward and connects at the base of the tongue; The direct line connects to the occipital bone, ascends to the head, descends along the face, and connects at the nose; Its branch forms the upper eyelid network, descending to connect with the cheeks; Its branch originates from the outer edge behind the armpit and connects at the acromion. Its branch enters beneath the armpit, ascends out of the clavicle region, and upwardly connects to the occipital protuberance; Its branch emerges from the clavicle area, obliquely ascends and exits at the cheeks. Its associated ailments include swelling and pain in the little toe, heel, and ankle; cramping of the popliteal region; spine contortion as if broken; tightness in the neck muscles; inability to raise the shoulders; lateral tension and painful twisting at the armpits and clavicle regions; and restricted movement with inability to move left or right. The treatment involves cauterization with moxibustion and acupuncture, determining the frequency based on symptoms, taking painful points as acupoints. This condition is called Zhongchun Bi (Bing of the Mid-Spring).

1. 也 : 舊脫。

相關討論
	經筋:	
足少陽之筋，起於小指次指，上結外踝，上循脛外廉，結於膝外廉；其支者，別起外輔骨，上走髀，前者結於伏兔之上，後者，結於尻；其直者，上乘䏚季脅，上走腋前廉，繫於膺乳，結於缺盆；直者，上出腋，貫缺盆，出太陽之前，循耳後，上額角，交巔上，下走頷，上結於頄；支者，結於目眥為外維。其病小指次指支轉筋，引膝外轉筋，膝不可屈伸，膕筋急，前引髀，後引尻，即上乘䏚季脅痛，上引缺盆、膺乳、頸維筋急。從左之右，右目不開，上過右角，并蹻脈而行，左絡於右，故傷左角，右足不用，命曰維筋相交。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，名曰孟春痺也。
		The meridian of the Foot-Shaoyang originates at the second toe, ascends and connects externally to the lateral malleolus, then follows along the outer side of the tibia upward, connecting externally on the knee. Its branch originates separately from the lateral fibular bone, ascends toward the thigh; the anterior part connects above the quadriceps femoris, while the posterior part connects at the coccyx. The direct line ascends along the lower ribs and hypochondrium, proceeds upward to the anterior aspect of the armpit, connects with the chest and breasts, and terminates at the clavicle region. The direct line ascends from the armpit, passes through the clavicle region, emerges in front of the Taiyang point, follows behind the ear, ascends to the frontal ridge, crosses at the vertex, descends toward the jaw, and upwardly connects at the cheeks; The branch connects at the outer canthus of the eye, forming an external support. Its associated ailments include cramping and tension in the second toe, pulling with cramps on the outer side of the knee; inability to flex or extend the knee; tightness in the popliteal muscles; anterior pulling toward the thigh, posterior pulling toward the coccyx; pain along the lower ribs and hypochondriac region; upwardly affecting the clavicle, chest, breasts, and neck with taut tendons. When the condition originates from the left and affects the right, the right eye cannot open; it ascends over the right frontal ridge, runs alongside the yao vessels, and crosses to connect with the right side. Therefore, injury to the left frontal region results in paralysis of the right foot; this is termed "Weijin Xiangjiao" (Intersecting Tendons). The treatment involves cauterization and acupuncture with moxibustion, determining the frequency according to symptoms, taking painful points as the acupoints. This is called Mengchun Bi (Bing in Early Spring).

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經筋:	
足陽明之筋，起於中三指，結於跗上，邪外上加於輔骨，上結於膝外廉，直上結於髀樞，上循脅屬脊；其直者，上循骭，結於膝；其支者，結於外輔骨，合少陽；其直者，上循伏兔，上結於髀，聚於陰器，上腹而布，至缺盆而結，上頸，上挾口，合于頄，下結於鼻，上合於太陽。太陽為目上網，陽明為目下網；其支者，從頰結於耳前。其病足中指支脛轉筋，腳跳堅，伏兔轉筋，髀前踵，㿉疝，腹筋急，引缺盆及頰，卒口僻；急者，目不合，熱則筋縱，目不開，頰筋有寒，則急，引頰移口，有熱則筋弛縱，緩不勝收，故僻。治之以馬膏，膏其急者；以白酒和桂，以塗其緩者，以桑鉤鉤之，即以生桑炭置之坎中，高下以坐等。以膏熨急頰，且飲美酒，敢美炙肉，不飲酒者，自強也，為之三拊而已。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，名曰季春痺也。
		The meridian of the Foot-Yangming originates at the middle three toes, connects above the ankle, obliquely extends outward to the fibular bone, upwardly connects externally on the knee, directly ascends and terminates at the hip joint, then proceeds along the flank toward the spine; The direct line ascends following the tibia, connecting at the knee; Its branch connects to the lateral fibular bone and combines with the Shaoyang meridian; The direct line ascends along the quadriceps femoris muscle, upwardly connects at the thigh, converges toward the genital area, extends upward across the abdomen and spreads out, terminates at the clavicle as a connection point, proceeds up to the neck, runs alongside the mouth, combines with the cheeks, descends to connect at the nose, and ascends to merge with the Taiyang meridian. The Taiyang forms the upper eyelid webbing; the Yangming forms the lower eyelid webbing; Its branch originates from the cheeks and connects in front of the ear. Its associated ailments include cramps and tension in the middle toe, lower leg, and feet; stiffness with sudden foot movements; cramping of the quadriceps femoris; anterior thigh tightness extending to the heel; hernia; abdominal muscle spasm; pulling sensations at the clavicle and cheeks; and sudden facial deviation. In cases of excessive tension, the eyes cannot close. When heat is present, the tendons become relaxed and the eyes cannot open. If cold affects the facial muscles, they tighten, pulling the cheeks and causing mouth deviation; when heat causes relaxation and laxity, it becomes weak and unable to control movement, hence resulting in facial asymmetry. Treat with Ma Gao (horse fat ointment), applying it to the tense areas; mix white wine with cassia and apply it to the relaxed areas; use mulberry hooks to secure them, then place green mulberry charcoal in a pit, adjusting its height according to the patient's sitting position. Apply the ointment to massage the tense cheeks, and drink fine wine while eating well-roasted meat. For those who cannot drink alcohol, they should still make an effort; simply perform three gentle compressions as treatment. The treatment involves cauterization using moxibustion and needle therapy, determining the frequency based on symptom awareness, taking painful points as acupuncture sites. This condition is known as Jichun Bi (Late Spring Syndrome).

	經筋:	
足太陰之筋，起於大指之端內側，上結於內踝；其直者，絡於膝內輔骨，上循陰股，結於髀，聚於陰器，上腹結於臍，循腹裏，結於肋，散於胸中；其內者，著於脊。其病足大指支內踝痛，轉筋痛，膝內輔骨痛，陰股引髀而痛，陰器紐痛，上引臍兩脅痛，引膺中脊內痛。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，命曰孟秋痺也。
		The meridian of the Foot-Taiyin originates at the inner side of the tip of the big toe, ascends and connects internally to the medial malleolus; The direct line runs along the medial fibular bone of the knee, ascends through the inner thigh, connects at the hip, converges toward the genitalia, proceeds upward across the abdomen to connect at the navel, follows internally within the abdominal cavity, terminates at the ribs, and spreads into the chest; Its internal branch attaches to the spine. Its associated ailments include pain in the inner side of the big toe and medial malleolus, cramping with pain; pain along the medial fibular bone at the knee; pulling pain from the inner thigh to the hip; twisting pain in the genital area; upwardly causing pain around the navel and both hypochondria; and radiating pain through the chest toward the spine. The treatment involves cauterization combined with acupuncture, determining frequency based on symptom awareness and taking painful points as acupoint locations. This condition is termed Mengqiu Bi (Early Autumn Syndrome).

	經筋:	
足少陰之筋，起於小指之下，并足太陰之筋，邪走內踝之下，結於踵，與太陽之筋合，而上結於內輔之下，并太陰之筋，而上循陰股，結於陰器，循脊內挾膂上至項，結於枕骨，與足太陽之筋合。其病足下轉筋，及所過而結者皆痛及轉筋。病在此者，主癎瘈及痙，在外者不能挽，在內者不能仰。故陽病者，腰反折不能俛，陰病者，不能仰。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸。在內者熨引飲藥，此筋折紐，紐發數甚者死不治，名曰仲秋痺也。
		The meridian of the Foot-Shao Yin originates beneath the little toe, runs parallel to the Foot-Taiyin meridian, obliquely proceeds below the medial malleolus and connects at the heel. It combines with the Taiyang tendons and ascends to connect beneath the medial fibular bone; running alongside the Taiyin tendons upward along the inner thigh, it terminates at the genitalia. Following internally along the spine, it extends up toward the neck, connecting at the occipital bone and merging with the Foot-Taiyang meridian. Its associated ailments include cramping in the lower part of the foot, as well as pain and cramps at all points along its course where it connects. Those afflicted with this condition primarily suffer from epilepsy, convulsions, and spasticity; if the disease affects the exterior pathways, one cannot bend backward; if it affects the interior pathways, one cannot arch forward. Therefore, in cases of Yang pathology, the waist bends backward uncontrollably and one is unable to bend forward; in Yin pathology, one cannot arch upward. The treatment involves cauterization through moxibustion and needling, determining the frequency based on awareness of symptoms, using painful points as acupoints for therapy. For internal conditions, apply heat therapy and take medicinal decoctions. If the tendons are severely fractured or twisted, with frequent episodes of severe twisting, death is inevitable and treatment ineffective; this condition is termed Zhongqiu Bi (Mid-Autumn Syndrome).

	經筋:	
足厥陰之筋，起於大指之上，上結於內踝之前，上循脛，上結內輔之下，上循陰股，結於陰器，絡諸筋。其病足大指支內踝之前痛，內輔痛，陰股痛轉筋，陰器不用，傷於內則不起，傷於寒則陰縮入，傷於熱則縱挺不收，治在行水清陰氣；其病轉筋者，治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，命曰季秋痺也。
		The meridian of the Foot-Jueyin originates above the big toe, ascends to connect in front of the medial malleolus. It proceeds upward along the tibia and connects beneath the medial fibular bone. Continuing up through the inner thigh, it terminates at genitalia and interconnects with other tendons. Its associated ailments include pain at the inner side of the base of the big toe and in front of the medial malleolar region, pain along the medial fibula, cramping pain from the inner thigh; dysfunction of the genitalia. Injury internally may result in inability to stand upright; cold injury causes retraction of the genitals inward; heat injury results in flaccid protrusion with no control. Treatment involves promoting water metabolism and clearing Yin-heat; For cases involving cramping tendons, treatment involves cauterization by moxibustion and puncturing with needles, determining the frequency based on one's awareness of symptoms and taking painful points as sites for acupuncture. This condition is named Jiqiu Bi (Late Autumn Syndrome).

	經筋:	
手太陽之筋，起於小指之上，結於腕，上循臂內廉，結於肘內銳骨之後，彈之應小指之上，入結於腋下；其支者，後走腋後廉，上繞肩胛，循頸出走太陽之前，結於耳後完骨；其支者，入耳中；直者，出耳上，下結於頷，上屬目外眥。其病小指支肘內銳骨後廉痛，循臂陰，入腋下，腋下痛，腋後廉痛，繞肩胛引頸而痛，應耳中鳴痛引頷，目瞑良久乃得視，頸筋急，則為筋瘻頸腫，寒熱在頸者。治在燔鍼劫刺之，以知為數，以痛為輸。其為腫者，復而銳之。本支者，上曲牙，循耳前屬目外眥，上頷結於角，其痛當所過者支轉筋。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，名曰仲夏痺也。
		The meridian of the Hand-Taiyang originates at the tip of the little finger, connects to the wrist, ascends along the inner side of the arm and terminates behind the medial epicondyle of the elbow. When pressed, it responds at the base of the little finger; it proceeds inwardly to connect beneath the armpit; Its branch runs posteriorly along the back of the armpit, upward around the scapula, follows the neck and emerges in front of Taiyang, connecting at the occipital region behind the ear; Its branch enters into the ear canal; The direct line exits above the ear, descends to connect with the jaw, and ascends to attach at the outer canthus of the eyes. Its associated ailments include pain along the little finger and posterior aspect of the medial epicondyle; pain follows down the inner arm, into beneath the armpit causing discomfort there. Pain also occurs at the back of the axilla, radiates around the scapula to pull the neck with accompanying pain, resonating as ringing or pain in the ear canal that extends toward the jaw. The eyes may remain closed for a long time before vision is regained; if neck tendons become taut, it results in tendinous swelling and neck enlargement, accompanied by chills and fever localized in the neck area. The treatment involves cauterizing with moxibustion or acupuncture needles, determining frequency according to symptom awareness and using painful points as acupoint sites. For cases involving swelling, the treatment should be repeated with more precise needling techniques. The branch originates upward from the molars, follows along in front of the ear to connect at the outer canthus, ascends toward the jaw and terminates at the frontal ridge. Pain occurs wherever it passes, causing cramping and tension in those areas. The treatment involves cauterization via moxibustion and piercing with needles, determining frequency based on symptom perception, using painful points as acupuncture sites; this condition is called Zhongxia Bi (Mid-Summer Syndrome).

	經筋:	
手少陽之筋，起於小指次指之端，結於腕，中循臂，結於肘，上繞臑外廉、上肩、走頸，合手太陽；其支者，當曲頰入繫舌本；其支者，上曲牙，循耳前，屬目外眥，上乘頷，結於角。其病當所過者，即支轉筋，舌卷。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，名曰季夏痺也。

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經筋:	
手陽明之筋，起於大指次指之端，結於腕，上循臂，上結於肘外，上臑，結於髃；其支者，繞肩胛，挾脊；直者，從肩髃上頸；其支者，上頰，結於頄；直者，上出手太陽之前，上左角，絡頭，下右頷。其病當所過者，支痛及轉筋，肩不舉，頸不可左右視。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，名曰孟夏痺也。
		The meridian of the Hand-Yangming originates at the tips of the index finger, connects to the wrist and ascends along the arm; it terminates externally on the elbow, proceeds upward through the upper arm and connects at the acromial region. Its branch encircles the scapulae and runs alongside the spine; The direct line originates from the acromial region, ascends to the neck; Its branch proceeds upward along the cheeks and terminates at the malar regions. The direct line ascends in front of the Hand-Taiyang, proceeds to the left frontal ridge, interconnects with the head and descends toward the right jaw. Its associated ailments manifest wherever it passes; symptoms include pain, cramping tension in these areas, inability to raise the shoulder and restricted neck movement with difficulty looking left or right. The treatment involves cauterization along with acupuncture using moxibustion; frequency is determined by symptom awareness, and painful points are used as acupoints. This syndrome is called Mengxia Bi (Early Summer Syndrome).

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經筋:	
手太陰之筋，起於大指之上，循指上行，結於魚後，行寸口外側，上循臂，結肘中，上臑內廉，入腋下，出缺盆，結肩前髃，上結缺盆，下結胸裏，散貫賁，合賁下抵季脅。其病當所過者，支轉筋，痛甚成息賁，脅急吐血。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸。名曰仲冬痺也。
		The meridian of the Hand-Taoyin originates at the base of the thumb, follows along the fingers upward and terminates posterior to the fish-shaped area on the palm. It proceeds externally along the radial side of the wrist, ascends the arm, connects in the cubital region, continues up the inner upper arm, enters beneath the armpit and exits from the clavicle region; it then terminates at the anterior acromial region above the clavicle. Descending further, it connects internally within the chest cavity, spreads through the sternum, combines below the sternum and extends down to the hypochondriac regions. Its associated ailments manifest wherever its course passes, with symptoms including cramping and tension; severe pain may lead to sternal obstruction (xibian), tightness in the hypochondria, and hematemesis. The treatment involves cauterization therapy using moxibustion needles; frequency is determined according to symptom awareness, with painful points used as acupuncture sites. This condition is called Zhongdong Bi (Mid-Winter Syndrome).

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經筋:	
手心主之筋，起於中指，與太陰之筋並行，結於肘內廉，上臂陰，結腋下，下散前後挾脅；其支者，入腋，散胸中，結於臂。其病當所過者，支轉筋前及胸痛息賁。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸，名曰孟冬痺也。
		The meridian of the Heart-Pericardium originates at the middle finger, runs parallel with the Taoyin tendons and terminates along the inner elbow. It ascends internally on the arm to connect beneath the armpits; downwardly it spreads forward and backward alongside the flanks; Its branch enters under the armpit, disperses within the chest cavity and terminates at the arm. Its associated ailments manifest wherever this meridian passes; symptoms include tension with cramping in front, as well as chest pain and sternal obstruction. The treatment involves cauterization acupuncture using moxibustion, with frequency determined by symptom awareness and painful points used as acupoints; this condition is called Mengdong Bi (Early Winter Syndrome).

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	經筋:	
手少陰之筋，起於小指之內側，結於銳骨，上結肘內廉，上入腋，交太陰，挾乳裏，結於胸中，循臂下繫於臍。其病內急心承伏梁，下為肘網。其病當所過者，支轉筋，筋痛。治在燔鍼劫刺，以知為數，以痛為輸。其成伏梁唾血膿者，死不治。經筋之病，寒則反折筋急，熱則筋弛縱不收，陰痿不用。陽急則反折，陰急則俛不伸。焠刺者，刺寒急也，熱則筋縱不收，無用燔鍼，名曰季冬痺也。
		The meridian of the Hand-Shao Yin originates at the inner side of little finger, terminates at medial epicondyle. It ascends to terminate along the inner elbow and enters beneath the armpit where it intersects with Taiyin; running alongside internally within the mammary region, it connects in the chest cavity and follows down the arm toward the navel. Its associated ailments involve internal tension with heart pressure resembling a beam pressing downward; this results in elbow webbing discomfort. Its ailments manifest wherever the meridian passes, presenting as cramping and tendinous pain. The treatment involves cauterization needle therapy using moxibustion with frequency determined by symptom perception, taking painful points as acupunctural sites. Those who develop severe heart pressure (Fu Liang) and cough up blood or pus are beyond treatment and will die. Diseases of the meridian tendons manifest as cold-induced rigidity with tendon tension causing backward bending, or heat-induced relaxation and flaccidity without control, resulting in Yin atrophy and dysfunction. Excessive Yang causes backward bending; excessive Yin results in forward flexion with inability to extend. Cui Ci (a type of acupuncture) is used for cold-induced rigidity. In cases of heat, tendons become flaccid and uncontrolled; moxibustion should not be applied in such conditions. This syndrome is termed Jidong Bi (Late Winter Syndrome).

打開字典
顯示影印本
	經筋:	
足之陽明，手之太陽，筋急則口目為僻，眥急不能卒視，治皆如右方也。
		When the Foot-Yangming and Hand-Taiyang tendons become overly taut, symptoms include facial deviation with eye and mouth misalignment; tightness at the canthi prevents sudden vision. Treatment for these conditions follows the method described above. 

14. 骨度 Bone Measurements

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	骨度:	
黃帝問於伯高曰：脈度言經脈之長短，何以立之？伯高曰：先度其骨節之大小、廣狹、長短，而脈度定矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao, "The Pulse Measurement discusses the lengths of meridians; on what basis are they established?" Bo Gao said, "First measure the size, breadth and narrowness, length and shortness of the bones and joints, and then the pulse measurement is determined."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	骨度:	
黃帝曰：願聞眾人之度。人長七尺五寸者，其骨節之大小長短各幾何？伯高曰：頭之大骨圍，二尺六寸，胸圍四尺五寸。腰圍四尺二寸。髮所覆者顱至項，尺二寸。髮以下至頤，長一尺，君子終折。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to hear about the measurements for ordinary people." For a person seven chi five cun tall, what are the sizes and lengths of each bone and joint? Bo Gao said, "The circumference of the large bones at the head is two chi six cun; the chest circumference is four chi five cun." The waist circumference is four chi two cun. The hair covers from the skull to the nape, one chi two cun. From below the hairline to the chin is one chi; this measurement remains constant for a gentleman.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	骨度:	
結喉以下至缺盆中，長四寸。缺盆以下至𩩲𩨗，長九寸，過則肺大，不滿則肺小。𩩲𩨗以下至天樞，長八寸，過則胃大，不及則胃小。天樞以下至橫骨，長六寸半，過則迴腸廣長，不滿則狹短。橫骨，長六寸半。橫骨上廉以下至內輔之上廉，長一尺八寸。內輔之上廉以下至下廉，長三寸半。內輔下廉，下至內踝，長一尺三寸。內踝以下至地，長三寸。膝膕以下至附屬，長一尺六寸。附屬以下至地，長三寸。故骨圍大則太過，小則不及。
		From the laryngeal prominence downward to the hollow of the clavicle, it is four cun long. From below the hollow of the clavicle to the xiphoid process is nine cun; if it exceeds this, the lung is large; if less than this, the lung is small. From below the xiphoid process to Tian Shu (ST 25) is eight cun; if it exceeds, the stomach is large; if not reaching this length, the stomach is small. From Tian Shu downward to the pubic bone is six cun and a half; if it exceeds, then the transverse colon is broad and long; if not reaching this length, it is narrow and short. The pubic bone measures six cun and a half in length. From the upper margin of the pubic bone downward to the upper margin of Nei Fu (ST 42) is one chi eight cun. From the upper margin to the lower margin of Nei Fu is three cun and a half. From the lower margin of Nei Fu downward to the medial malleolus is one chi three cun. From below the medial malleolus to the ground is three cun. From the popliteal fossa downward to the lateral malleolus (outer ankle) is one chi six cun. From below the lateral malleolus to the ground, it measures three cun. Therefore, if the bone circumference is large, it indicates excess; if small, it indicates deficiency.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	骨度:	
角以下至柱骨，長一尺。行腋中不見者，長四寸。腋以下至季脅，長一尺二寸。季脅以下至髀樞，長六寸，髀樞以下至膝中，長一尺九寸。膝以下至外踝，長一尺六寸。外踝以下至京骨，長三寸。京骨以下至地，長一寸。
		From below the ear to the sternum (columnar bone) is one chi. The part that runs through the armpit and is not visible measures four cun in length. From below the armpit to the lower rib (seasonal flank) is one chi two cun long. From below the lower rib to the hip joint (Bi Shu) is six cun; from Bi Shu downward to the knee center, it measures one chi nine cun. From below the knee to the lateral malleolus is one chi six cun long. From below the lateral malleolus downward to Jing Gu (BL 64) measures three cun. From Jing Gu downward to the ground is one cun long.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	骨度:	
耳後當完骨者，廣九寸。耳前當耳門者，廣一尺三寸。兩顴之間，相去七寸。兩乳之間，廣九寸半。兩髀之間，廣六寸半。
		The width behind the ear, at the level of Wan Gu (the occipital bone), is nine cun. In front of the ear, where Er Men (TE 21) lies, the width measures one chi three cun. The distance between the two cheeks is seven cun. The width between the two nipples is nine cun and a half. The distance between the two hips measures six cun and a half.

打開字典

顯示影印本
	骨度:	
足長一尺二寸，廣四寸半。肩至肘，長一尺七寸；肘至腕，長一尺二寸半。腕至中指本節，長四寸。本節至其末，長四寸半。
		The foot is one chi two cun in length, four cun and a half wide. From the shoulder to the elbow measures one chi seven cun; from the elbow to the wrist is one chi two cun and a half long. From the wrist to the base of the middle finger joint measures four cun. From the base of the joint to its tip is four cun and a half long.

	骨度:	
項髮以下至背骨，長二寸半，膂骨以下至尾骶，二十一節，長三尺，上節長一寸四分分之一，奇分在下，故上七節至于膂骨，九寸八分分之七。此眾人骨之度也，所以立經脈之長短也。是故視其經脈之
		From the hair at the nape downward to the dorsal vertebrae is two cun and a half. From the lumbar vertebrae downward to the sacrum, there are twenty-one segments, measuring three chi in total length. The uppermost segment measures one cun plus one-third of a fen; the remaining fractions accumulate toward the lower part. Therefore, the upper seven segments down to the lumbar vertebrae measure nine cun eight fen and seven-thirds of a fen. These are the standard bone measurements for ordinary people, and they serve as the basis for establishing the lengths of the meridians. Therefore, when examining their meridians,

	骨度:	
在於身也，其見浮而堅，其見明而大者，多血，細而沉者，多氣也。
		those visible on the body that appear floating yet firm, and clearly large, indicate an abundance of blood; those that are fine and deep indicate an abundance of qi. 


15. 五十營 Fifty Circuits


1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五十營:	
黃帝曰：余願聞五十營奈何？歧伯答曰：天周二十八宿，宿三十六分；人氣行一周，千八分，日行二十八宿。人經脈上下左右前後二十八脈，周身十六丈二尺，以應二十八宿。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about the Fifty Encampments. How is this? Qi Bo replied: The heavens have twenty-eight mansions in a full cycle, each mansion divided into thirty-six parts; the human qi travels one full circuit of 1080 divisions, corresponding to the daily movement through the twenty-eight mansions. The human meridians—ascending and descending, left and right, front and back—comprise twenty-eight vessels. They span a total of sixteen zhang and two chi around the body, corresponding to the twenty-eight mansions.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五十營:	
漏水下百刻，以分晝夜。故人一呼脈再動，氣行三寸，一吸，脈亦再動，氣行三寸，呼吸定息，氣行六寸；十息，氣行六尺，日行二分。二百七十息，氣行十六丈二尺，氣行交通於中，一周於身，下水二刻，日行二十五分。五百四十息，氣行再周於身，下水四刻，日行四十分。二千七百息，氣行十周於身，下水二十刻，日行五宿二十分。一萬三千五百息，氣行五十營於身，水下百刻，日行二十八宿，漏水皆盡脈終矣。
		A hundred moments of water dripping from a clepsydra divide day and night. Therefore, with one exhalation, the pulse moves twice; qi travels three cun. With one inhalation, the pulse also moves twice; qi travels another three cun. In a complete breath—exhaling and inhaling—the qi travels six cun; ten breaths move the qi six chi, corresponding to two divisions of the day's movement. Two hundred and seventy breaths move the qi sixteen zhang and two chi, circulating through the body in a full circuit. This takes two moments of water dripping, corresponding to twenty-five divisions of the day's movement. Five hundred and forty breaths allow the qi to circulate twice around the body, taking four moments of water dripping, corresponding to forty divisions of the day's movement. Two thousand seven hundred breaths allow the qi to complete ten full circuits around the body, taking twenty moments of water dripping, corresponding to five mansions and twenty divisions of the day's movement. Twelve thousand seven hundred breaths allow qi to complete fifty circuits around the body. The water drips for a full hundred moments, corresponding to the movement through all twenty-eight mansions; when the dripping water is exhausted, the pulse cycle ends.

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五十營:	
所謂交通者，並行一數也。故五十營備，得盡天地之壽矣，凡行八百一十丈也。
		What is referred to as "circulating communication" means that they proceed simultaneously in one count. Therefore, when the Fifty Circuits are complete, one attains the full span of life granted by Heaven and Earth; in total, it spans eight thousand one hundred zhang. 

16. 營氣 Ying Qi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	營氣:	
黃帝曰：營氣之道，內穀為寶。穀入于胃，乃傳之肺，流溢於中，布散於外，精專者，行於經隧，常營無已，終而復始，是謂天地之紀。故氣從太陰出注手陽明，上行注足陽明，下行至跗上，注大指間，與太陰合；上行抵髀，從脾注心中；循手少陰，出腋下臂，注小指，合手太陽；上行乘腋，出䪼內，注目內眥，上巔，下項，合足太陽；循脊，下尻，下行注小指之端，循足心，注足少陰；上行注腎，從腎注心外，散於胸中；循心主脈，出腋，下臂，出兩筋之間，入掌中，出中指之端，還注小指次指之端，合手少陽；上行注膻中，散於三焦，從三焦注膽，出脅，注足少陽；下行至跗上，復從跗注大指間，合足厥陰，上行至肝，從肝上注肺，上循喉嚨，入頏顙之竅，究於畜門。其支別者，上額，循巔，下項中，循脊入骶，是督脈也；絡陰器，上過毛中，入臍中，上循腹裏，入缺盆，下注肺中，復出太陰。此營氣之所行也，逆順之常也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "The path of nutritive qi considers internal grains as precious." Grains enter the stomach, then are transmitted to the lungs. They overflow within and spread outward. The refined and concentrated portion travels through the meridians, constantly circulating without ceasing, ending only to begin anew. This is called the principle of heaven and earth. Therefore, qi originates from Taiyin, flows to the hand Yangming, ascends to the foot Yangming, descends to the ankle, and reaches between the big toe, where it converges with Taiyin; It then ascends to the hip, flows from the spleen to the heart. Following the hand Shaoyin meridian, it emerges beneath the armpit and down the arm, reaching the little finger to converge with the hand Taiyang; It ascends through the armpit, exits from within the shoulder blade, reaches the inner canthus of the eye, rises to the vertex of the head, descends along the neck, and converges with the foot Taiyang; It follows the spine downward to the buttocks, proceeds down to the tip of the little toe, runs along the sole of the foot, and converges with Zusanli (ST36) on the foot Shaoyin; It ascends to converge at the kidney, from the kidney it reaches the heart externally, and disperses within the chest. Following the heart's governing vessel, it emerges from the armpit, descends the arm, exits between two tendons, enters the palm, reaches the tip of the middle finger, returns to the tips of the little and ring fingers, and converges with hand Shaoyang; It ascends to converge at Zhongwan (CV12), disperses into the triple burner, from the triple burner it reaches the gallbladder, exits along the flank, and converges with foot Shaoyang; It descends to the ankle, then returns from the ankle to between the big toes, converging with foot Jueyin. It ascends to reach the liver, from the liver it proceeds upward to converge at the lungs, follows the throat, enters through the orifices of the chin and forehead, and ultimately terminates at Chumen (CV4). Its collateral branch ascends to the forehead, follows the vertex of the head downward through the neck, runs along the spine into the sacrum; this is the Du meridian; It connects to the genitalia, ascends past the pubic region, enters at the navel, follows upward along the inner abdomen, enters Qubian (LU1), descends into the lungs, and then emerges again from Taiyin. This is the pathway of nutritive qi, the usual pattern of its flow in forward and reverse directions. 

17. 脈度 Pulse Measurements

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脈度:	
黃帝曰：願聞脈度。歧伯答曰：手之六陽，從手至頭，長五尺，五六三丈。手之六陰，從手至胸中，三尺五寸，三六一丈八尺，五六三尺，合二丈一尺。足之六陽，從足上至頭，八尺，六八四丈八尺。足之六陰，從足至胸中，六尺五寸，六六三丈六尺，五六三尺合三丈九尺。蹻脈從足至目，七尺五寸，二七一丈四尺，二五一尺，合一丈五尺。督脈、任脈，各四尺五寸，二四八尺，二五一尺，合九尺。凡都合一十六丈二尺，此氣之大經隧也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about pulse measurements. Qibo replied: The six yang meridians of the hand run from the hands to the head, each measuring five chi in length; five multiplied by six is thirty chi. The six yin meridians of the hand extend from the hands to the chest, each measuring three chi and five cun. Three multiplied by six is eighteen chi, and five multiplied by six is three chi; combined they total twenty-one chi. The six yang meridians of the foot run from the feet up to the head, each measuring eight chi; six multiplied by eight is forty-eight chi. The six yin meridians of foot extend from the feet to the chest, each measuring six chi and five cun; six multiplied by six is thirty-six chi, and five multiplied by six three chi. Combined they total thirty-nine chi. The qiao vessels run from the feet to the eyes, each measuring seven chi and five cun; two multiplied by seven is fourteen chi, and two multiplied by five is ten cun. Combined they total one zhang and five chi. The Du Vessel and the Ren Vessel each measure four chi and five cun; two times four is eight chi, and two times five is ten cun. Together they total nine chi. In total, they sum up to sixteen zhang and two chi; these are the major pathways of qi.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脈度:	
經脈為裏，支而橫者為絡，絡之別者為孫，盛而血者疾誅之，盛者寫之，虛者飲藥以補之。五藏常內閱於上七竅也。故肺氣通於鼻，肺和則鼻能知臭香矣；心氣通於舌，心和則舌能知五味矣；肝氣通於目，肝和則目能辨五色矣；脾氣通於口，脾和則口能知五穀矣；腎氣通於耳，腎和則耳能聞五音矣。五藏不和，則七竅不通；六府不合則留為癰。故邪在府則陽脈不和，陽脈不和則氣留之，氣留之則陽氣盛矣。陽氣太盛，則陰不利，陰脈不利則血留之，血留之則陰氣盛矣。陰氣太盛則陽氣不能榮也，故曰關。陽氣太盛，則陰氣弗能榮也，故曰格。陰陽俱盛，不得相榮，故曰關格。關格者，不得盡期而死也。
		The meridians form the interior pathways; those that branch off and run laterally are called luo vessels. The branches of the luo vessels are termed sun vessels. When there is excessive blood, it should be quickly purged; when there is excess condition, it should be drained; when there is deficiency, herbal medicine should be administered to supplement it. The five zang organs are constantly internally connected with the seven orifices above. Therefore, the qi of the lung communicates with the nose; when the lung is in harmony, the nose can discern odors and fragrances. The qi of the heart communicates with the tongue; when the heart is in harmony, the tongue can perceive the five flavors. The qi of the liver communicates with the eyes; when the liver is in harmony, the eyes can distinguish the five colors. The qi of the spleen communicates with the mouth; when the spleen is in harmony, the mouth can recognize the five grains. The qi of the kidney communicates with the ears; when the kidneys are in harmony, the ears can hear the five sounds. When the five zang organs are disharmonious, the seven orifices become blocked. When the six fu organs do not function properly, stagnation may result in abscesses. Therefore, when pathogenic influences reside in the fu organs, the yang meridians become disharmonious; when the yang meridians are disharmonious, qi becomes stagnant; and when qi is stagnant, the yang qi becomes excessive. When yang qi is excessively abundant, yin becomes obstructed; when the yin meridians are obstructed, blood stagnates; and when blood stagnates, the yin qi becomes excessive. When yin qi is excessively abundant, yang qi cannot nourish properly; therefore it is called "guan." When yang qi is overly excessive, yin qi cannot be nourished properly; thus it is termed "ge." When both yin and yang are excessively abundant and cannot nourish each other, it is called "guange." Those who suffer from guange will not live to their full life span but die prematurely.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脈度:	
黃帝曰：蹻脈安起安止，何氣榮水？歧伯答曰：蹻脈者，少陰之別，起於然骨之後。上內踝之上，直上循陰股，入陰，上循胸裏，入缺盆，上出人迎之前，入頄，屬目內眥，合於太陽，陽蹻而上行，氣并相還，則為濡，目氣不榮，則目不合。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: From where do the qiao vessels originate and terminate, and which qi nourishes them? Qibo replied: The qiao vessels are a branch of the Shaoyin meridian, originating behind the ran gu bone. They ascend above the inner malleolus, run straight up along the yin aspect of the thigh, enter the genital area, then ascend along the chest internally, entering the quan peng (a region near the collarbone), emerging above the renying point in front, entering the cheeks, connecting to the inner canthus of the eye, and converging with the Taiyang meridian. The yang qiao vessel ascends; when their qi merges and circulates together, they nourish the eyes. If this qi does not properly nourish, the eyelids will not close.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脈度:	
黃帝曰：氣獨行五藏，不榮六府，何也？歧伯答曰：氣之不得無行也，如水之流，如日月之行不休，故陰脈榮其藏，陽脈榮其府，如環之無端，莫知其紀，終而復始，其流溢之氣，內溉藏府，外濡腠理。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Why is it that qi flows exclusively through the five zang organs and does not nourish the six fu organs? Qibo replied: Qi cannot remain stagnant; it flows like water and moves continuously, just as the sun and moon never cease their motion. Therefore, yin meridians nourish the zang organs, while yang meridians nourish the fu organs. This circulation is like a ring with no beginning or end, its pattern unknown, endlessly repeating itself. The surplus qi flows inward to moisten the zang and fu organs and outward to nourish the skin and body surface.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	脈度:	
黃帝曰：蹻脈有陰陽，何脈當其數？歧伯曰：男子數其陽，女子數其陰，當數者為陰，其不當數者為絡也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: The qiao vessels have yin and yang aspects; which meridians correspond to their number? Qibo said: For men, count the yang aspect; for women, count the yin aspect. The counted one is considered yin, and the uncounted one belongs to the luo vessels. 

18. 營衛生會 Ying Wei Sheng Hui

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	營衛生會:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：人焉受氣？陰陽焉會？何氣為營？何氣為衛？營安從生？衛于焉會？老壯不同氣，陰陽異位，願聞其會。歧伯答曰：人受氣于穀，穀入于胃，以傳與肺，五藏六府，皆以受氣，其清者為營，濁者為衛，營在脈中，衛在脈外，營週不休，五十而復大會，陰陽相貫，如環無端，衛氣行于陰二十五度，行于陽二十五度，分為晝夜，故氣至陽而起，至陰而止。故曰日中而陽隴，為重陽，夜半而陰隴為重陰，故太陰主內，太陽主外，各行二十五度分為晝夜。夜半為陰隴，夜半後而為陰衰，平旦陰盡而陽受氣矣。日中而陽隴，日西而陽衰，日入陽盡而陰受氣矣。夜半而大會，萬民皆臥，命曰合陰，平旦陰盡而陽受氣，如是無已，與天地同紀。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "From where do people receive vital energy?" Where do Yin and Yang meet? Which qi forms the nutritive essence? Which qi forms the defensive essence? From where does the nutritive essence originate? Where do the defensive essences meet? Old and vigorous people have different qi, Yin and Yang occupy different positions; I would like to hear about their meeting. Qi Bo replied, "People receive vital energy from grains. Grains enter the stomach and are transmitted to the lungs; all five zang organs and six fu organs receive this qi. The clear portion becomes the nutritive essence, while the turbid portion becomes the defensive essence. The nutritive essence resides within the vessels, whereas the defensive essence resides outside the vessels. The nutritive essence circulates without ceasing, completing a full cycle every fifty times, with Yin and Yang interconnecting like a ring without beginning or end. Defensive qi travels through the yin aspect twenty-five times and through the yang aspect twenty-five times, dividing day from night; thus, when the qi reaches the yang aspect, one awakens, and when it reaches the yin aspect, one rests." Therefore, it is said that at noon when yang reaches its peak, it becomes "heavy yang," and at midnight when yin reaches its peak, it becomes "heavy yin." Hence, Taiyin governs the interior while Taiyang governs the exterior; each travels twenty-five times, dividing into day and night. At midnight yin reaches its peak; after midnight, yin begins to wane, and by dawn, yin is exhausted while yang receives the qi. At noon yang reaches its peak; as the sun sets in the west, yang begins to wane; when the sun sets completely, yang is exhausted and yin receives the qi. At midnight there is a great convergence; all the people are asleep, which is called "union with yin." By dawn, yin has been exhausted and yang receives the qi. This cycle continues without end, in harmony with the order of heaven and earth.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	營衛生會:	
黃帝曰：老人之不夜瞑者，何氣使然？少壯之人，不晝瞑者，何氣使然？歧伯答曰：壯者之氣血盛，其肌肉滑，氣道通，營衛之行不失其常，故晝精而夜瞑。老者之氣血衰，其肌肉枯，氣道澀，五藏之氣相搏，其營氣衰少而衛氣內伐，故晝不精，夜不瞑。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Why do elderly people not sleep through the night? What qi causes this?" "Why do vigorous young people not sleep during the day? What qi causes this?" Qi Bo replied, "Vigorous people have abundant qi and blood; their muscles are smooth, the channels of qi are unobstructed, and the circulation of nutritive and defensive essences does not deviate from its normal course. Therefore, they remain alert during the day and sleep through the night." In the elderly, their qi and blood are weakened; their muscles become dry, the channels of qi become sluggish, and the qi of the five zang organs contend with one another. Their nutritive essence is diminished and their defensive essence invades internally, so they remain inattentive during the day and cannot sleep through the night.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	營衛生會:	
黃帝曰：願聞營衛之所行，皆何道從來？歧伯答曰：營出于中焦，衛出于下焦。黃帝曰：願聞三焦之所出。歧伯答曰：上焦出於胃上口，並咽以上，貫膈，而布胸中，走腋，循太陰之分而行，還至陽明，上至舌，下足陽明，常與營俱行于陽二十五度，行于陰亦二十五度一周也。故五十度而復太會于手太陰矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to know about the pathways along which the nutritive and defensive essences travel; from where do they originate?" Qi Bo replied, "The nutritive essence originates in the middle jiao, while the defensive essence originates in the lower jiao." The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to know where the three jiaos originate." Qi Bo replied, "The upper jiao originates at the upper opening of the stomach, runs alongside the pharynx upward, passes through the diaphragm and spreads in the chest, extends to the armpits, follows the distribution of Taiyin, returns to Yangming, ascends to the tongue, descends along the foot Yangming meridian. It constantly travels with the nutritive essence twenty-five times on the yang aspect and twenty-five times on the yin aspect for one full cycle." Thus, after fifty cycles, it reunites at the hand Taiyin.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	營衛生會:	
黃帝曰：人有熱，飲食下胃，其氣未定，汗則出，或出於面，或出於背，或出於身半，其不循衛氣之道而出，何也？歧伯曰：此外傷於風，內開腠理，毛蒸理泄，衛氣走之，固不得循其道，此氣慓悍滑疾，見開而出，故不得從其道，故命曰漏泄。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "When a person has heat, after food and drink enter the stomach, their qi is not yet settled. Sweat emerges—sometimes on the face, sometimes on the back, or only on one side of the body—and does not follow the normal pathway of defensive qi. Why is this so?" Qi Bo said, "This occurs because the person has been externally injured by wind, which internally opens the pores and channels. The hair follicles become heated and the pores open, allowing defensive qi to escape. Therefore, it cannot follow its normal pathway. This type of qi is swift, slippery, and vigorous; when an opening appears, it escapes immediately, hence unable to proceed along its proper route. Thus, this condition is called 'leakage' (Lou Xie)."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	營衛生會:	
黃帝曰：願聞中焦之所出。歧伯答曰：中焦亦並胃中，出上焦之後，此所受氣者，泌糟粕，蒸津液，化其精微，上注于肺脈乃化而為血，以奉生身，莫貴於此，故獨得行於經隧，命曰營氣。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to know the origin of the middle jiao." Qi Bo replied, "The middle jiao is also adjacent to the stomach and originates after the upper jiao. The qi it receives separates dross from essence, steams body fluids, transforms them into refined substances, which ascend to the lung vessels and are transformed into blood, thus nourishing the body. Nothing is more precious than this process; therefore, it alone travels through the meridians and channels, known as nutritive qi."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	營衛生會:	
黃帝曰：夫血之與氣，異名同類。何謂也？歧伯答曰：營衛者，精氣也，血者，神氣也，故血之與氣，異名同類焉。故奪血者無汗，奪汗者無血，故人生有兩死而無兩生。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Blood and qi have different names but belong to the same category." "What does this mean?" Qi Bo replied, "The nutritive and defensive essences are the refined qi; blood is the spiritual qi. Therefore, blood and qi have different names but are of the same category." Therefore, one who has lost excessive amounts of blood will not sweat, and one who has lost excessive sweat will lack blood. Thus, a person may have two causes for death but cannot have two lives.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	營衛生會:	
黃帝曰：願聞下焦之所出。歧伯答曰：下焦者，別回腸，注于膀胱，而滲入焉；故水穀者，常并居于胃中，成糟粕，而俱下于大腸而成下焦，滲而俱下。濟泌別汁，循下焦而滲入膀胱焉。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to know the source of the lower jiao." Qi Bo replied, "The lower jiao separates the ileum, channels fluids into the bladder, and allows them to seep in; Therefore, water and food usually reside together in the stomach, where they are transformed into dross and descend together to the large intestine as part of the lower jiao function, seeping downward collectively. They separate fluids from waste, following the pathway of the lower jiao to seep into the bladder.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	營衛生會:	
黃帝曰：人飲酒，酒亦入胃，穀未熟，而小便獨先下，何也？歧伯答曰：酒者，熟穀之液也。其氣悍以清，故後穀而入，先穀而液出焉。黃帝曰：善。余聞上焦如霧，中焦如漚，下焦如瀆，此之謂也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "When a person drinks alcohol, it also enters the stomach; yet before the food is fully digested, urine is excreted first. Why is this?" Qi Bo replied, "Alcohol is the liquid essence of cooked grains." Its nature is vigorous and clear; therefore, it enters after food but transforms into fluid and exits before the digestion of food. The Yellow Emperor said, "Well said." "I have heard that the upper jiao functions like mist, the middle jiao like fermentation, and the lower jiao like a ditch; this is what you mean." 

19. 四時氣 Qi of the Four Seasons

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	四時氣:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：夫四時之氣，各不同形，百病之起，皆有所生，灸刺之道，何者為定？歧伯答曰：四時之氣，各有所在，灸刺之道，得氣穴為定。故春取經、血脈、分肉之間，甚者，深刺之，間者，淺刺之；夏取盛經孫絡，取分間絕皮膚；秋取經俞。邪在府，取之合；冬取井滎，必深以留之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked 岐伯: "Regarding the qi of the four seasons, each has a different form. The origin of all kinds of illnesses has its own source. In the practice of moxibustion and acupuncture, what is fixed or definite?" Qi Bo answered: "The qi of the four seasons resides in different places. In the practice of moxibution and acupuncture, what determines effectiveness is obtaining the qi at the acupoints." Therefore, in spring, one should take meridians, blood vessels, and the spaces between muscle layers. For severe conditions, deep needling is used; for mild conditions, shallow needling is applied. In summer, one should take the major meridians and collateral vessels, selecting points between the skin layers. In autumn, one should take the shu (transport) points of the meridians. When pathogenic factors reside in the fu organs, select the he (confluence) points. In winter, take the jing and ying points, and needling must be deep with prolonged retention of the needles.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	四時氣:	
溫瘧汗不出，為五十九痏，風㽷膚脹，為五十七痏。取皮膚之血者，盡取之。飧泄補三陰之上，補陰陵泉，皆久留之，熱行乃止。
		For warm malaria with no perspiration, apply 59 moxa cones; for wind-related abdominal distension and skin swelling, apply 57 moxa cones. When blood is to be taken from the skin layer, it should be completely drained. For dysentery with loose stools, tonify the upper parts of the three yin meridians and tonify Yinlingquan. The needles should be retained for a long time; stop when heat sensation spreads.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	四時氣:	
轉筋於陽，治其陽；轉筋於陰，治其陰。皆卒刺之。徒㽷先取環谷下三寸，以鈹鍼鍼之，已刺而筩之，而內之，入而復之，以盡其㽷，必堅。來緩則煩悗，來急則安靜，間日一刺之，㽷盡乃止。飲閉藥，方刺之時徒飲之，方飲無食，方食無飲，無食他食，百三十五日。
		For cramps affecting the yang aspect, treat the yang meridians; For cramps affecting the yin aspect, treat the yin meridians. In both cases, apply quick needling techniques. For abdominal distension without other symptoms, first take the point three cun below Huangu. Insert a bi needle there; after needling, insert a tube over it and push it in, then withdraw slightly to complete the treatment for distension. The insertion must be firm. If the condition progresses slowly, it causes restlessness; if it progresses rapidly, it brings calmness. Treat once every other day with acupuncture; stop when distension is completely resolved. For obstruction due to fluid retention, during the period of acupuncture treatment, drink only water. When drinking, do not eat; when eating, do not drink. Avoid all other foods for 135 days.

著痺不去，久寒不已，卒取其三里。骨為幹。腸中不便，取三里，盛寫之，虛補之。癘風者，素刺其腫上。已刺，以銳鍼鍼其處，按出其惡氣，腫盡乃止。常食方食，無食他食。

For excess conditions, drain it; for deficiency conditions, tonify it. For leprosy or wind-related swellings, needling should be regularly applied to the swollen areas. After needling, use a sharp needle to prick the area and press to expel the harmful qi; stop when all swelling has subsided. During treatment, eat only prescribed food; avoid all other foods.

	四時氣:	
腹中常鳴，氣上衝胸，喘不能久立。邪在大腸，刺肓之原，巨虛上廉、三里。小腹控睪，引腰脊，上衝心。邪在小腸者，連睪系，屬于脊，貫肝肺，絡心系。氣盛則厥逆，上衝腸胃，燻肝，散于肓，結于臍，故取之肓原以散之，刺太陰以予之，取厥陰以下之，取巨虛下廉以去之，按其所過之經以調之。
		There is constant gurgling in the abdomen, qi surging upward to the chest, and shortness of breath making it impossible to stand for long. When pathogenic factors are in the large intestine, needle the source point of Mang (CV-4), Juxu Shanglian (ST-37), and Zusanli (ST-25). The lower abdomen contracts, pulling the testicles, drawing up to the waist and spine, and surging upward toward the heart. When pathogenic factors are located in the small intestine, they connect to the testicular ligaments, belong to the spine, penetrate through the liver and lungs, and network with the heart meridian. When the qi is excessive, it reverses upward, surging into the intestines and stomach, affecting the liver, spreading to the Mang region, and accumulating at the umbilicus. Therefore, needle the source of Mang (CV-4) to disperse it; needle the Taiyin meridian to regulate it; take points on the Jueyin meridian to descend the qi downward; and needle Juxu Xiaolian (ST-39) to eliminate it. Press along the affected meridians to harmonize them.

	四時氣:

		Frequent vomiting, with a bitter taste in the mouth, frequent sighing, a restless feeling in the heart, and fear as if someone is about to arrest them; This indicates pathogenic factors in the gallbladder and reversal of stomach qi. When gallbladder fluid is excreted, it causes a bitter mouth; when stomach qi reverses upward, it leads to vomiting with bitterness. Hence, this condition is called "vomiting bile." Needle Zusanli (ST-26) and points below it to treat the condition. When stomach qi is reversed, needle blood vessels of the Shaoyang meridian to block the gallbladder's reversal. Adjust its deficiency or excess appropriately to eliminate pathogenic factors.

If they are in the upper wǎn (upper part of the epigastrium), needle to suppress and guide them downward; if they are in the lower wǎn (lower part of the epigastrium}}, disperse and eliminate them. Take Zusanli (ST-40) for treatment.
