	四時氣:	
睹其色，察其以，知其散復者，視其目色，以知病之存亡也。一其形，聽其動靜者，持氣口人迎以視其脈，堅且盛且滑者，病日進，脈軟者，病將下，諸經實者，病三日已。氣口候陰，人迎候陽也。
		Observe their complexion, examine the pulse, and know whether the disease is dispersing or recurring. By observing the color of the eyes, one can determine if the illness still exists or has been eliminated. Unify the physical form and observe their movements. Examine the pulse at the Qiguan (radial artery) and Renyang (carotid artery). If the pulse is firm, strong, and slippery, the illness will worsen daily; if the pulse is soft, the disease will subside. When all meridian pulses are full, the condition should resolve within three days. The Qiguan pulse monitors yin conditions, while the Renyang pulse monitors yang conditions. 

20. 五邪 Five Types of Pathogenic Influences

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五邪:	
邪在肺，則病皮膚痛，寒熱，上氣喘，汗出，欬動肩背。取之膺中外喻，背三節五藏之傍，以手疾按之，快然，乃刺之。取之缺盆中以越之。
		If evil lingers in the lungs, it causes pain of the skin and flesh, chills and fever, upward rushing qi with shortness of breath, sweating, and coughing that affects the shoulders and back. Take points on the chest both inside and outside the midline, as well as three vertebrae segments on the back beside the five zang organs. Press quickly with the hand; if it feels pleasant relief, then needle there. Take points in the hollow of the clavicle to relieve the condition.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五邪:	
邪在肝，則兩脅中痛，寒中，惡血在內，行善掣節，時腳腫。取之行間，以引脅下，補三里以溫胃中，取血脈以散惡血；取耳間青脈，以去其掣。
		If evil lingers in the liver, it causes pain between the two hypochondria, coldness within, stagnant blood inside the body, frequent pulling sensations at joints, and occasional swelling of the feet. Take the Xingjian point to draw out the pathogenic influence beneath the hypochondrium, tonify Sanli to warm the stomach internally, and take points along the blood vessels to disperse stagnant blood; Take the blue-colored vessels in the ear area to eliminate the pulling sensation.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五邪:	
邪在脾胃，則病肌肉痛，陽氣有餘，陰氣不足，則熱中善飢；陽氣不足，陰氣有餘，則寒中腸鳴、腹痛；陰陽俱有餘，若俱不足，則有寒有熱，皆調于三里。
		If evil lingers in the spleen and stomach, it causes muscle pain; when yang qi is excessive and yin qi insufficient, there will be internal heat and a tendency to hunger easily; if yang qi is deficient and yin qi excessive, it results in coldness within the body, intestinal rumbling, and abdominal pain; if both yin and yang are excessive or both deficient, there will be alternating coldness and heat, all of which should be regulated at the Sanli point.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五邪:	
邪在腎，則病骨痛，陰痺。陰痺者，按之而不得，腹脹，腰痛，大便難，肩背頸項痛，時眩。取之湧泉、崑崙。視有血者，盡取之。
		If evil lingers in the kidney, it causes bone pain and yin bi (obstruction affecting the lower part of the body). Yin bi is characterized by pain that cannot be pinpointed upon pressing, abdominal distension, lower back pain, difficulty in defecation, and pain in the shoulders, back, neck, and occipital region, with occasional dizziness. Take the Yongquan and Kunlun points for treatment. 儘 If there are blood vessels visible, drain them completely.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五邪:	
邪在心，則病心痛，喜悲時眩仆；視有餘不足而調之其輸也。
		If evil lingers in the heart, it causes chest pain, a tendency toward sadness, and occasional dizziness or fainting; Examine whether there is excess or deficiency and regulate accordingly at its shu points. 

21. 寒熱病 Cold and Heat Disorders

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
皮寒熱者，不可附席，毛髮焦，鼻槁臘。不得汗，取三陽之絡，以補手太陰。肌寒熱者，肌痛，毛髮焦而唇槁臘。不得汗，取三陽于下，以去其血者，補足太陰，以出其汗。
		Those suffering from severe cold and heat should not sit on mats; their hair becomes dry and brittle, and their nose dries up like wax. If sweating does not occur, take the collateral of the Three Yang meridians to tonify the Hand Taiyin meridian. Those with superficial cold and heat experience muscle pain, their hair becomes dry and scorched, and their lips become dry like wax. If sweating does not occur, remove the blood by taking points of the Three Yang meridians below, and tonify the Foot Taiyin to induce perspiration.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
骨寒熱者，病無所安，汗注不休。齒未槁，取其少陰于陰股之絡；齒已槁，死不治。骨厥亦然。骨痺，舉節不用而痛，汗注、煩心。取三陰之經，補之。
		Those with bone coldness and heat feel restless in their illness, and sweat continuously without ceasing. If the teeth have not yet become dry, take points on the Yin leg collaterals of the Shaoyin meridian; If the teeth are already dried up, death is inevitable and treatment will be ineffective. The same applies to bone 厥 (bone 厥). Bone bi syndrome is characterized by inability to move the joints, pain, continuous sweating, and a troubled mind. Take points on the Three Yin meridians and tonify them.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
身有所傷，血出多及中風寒，若有所墮墜，四肢懈惰不收，名曰體惰。取其小腹臍下三結交。三結交者，陽明太陰也，臍下三寸關元也。厥痺者，厥氣上及腹。取陰陽之絡，視主病也，寫陽補陰經也。
		If the body has been injured, with excessive blood loss or affected by wind-cold, or if there was a fall from height, resulting in limp and uncontrolled limbs, it is called "Tidi" (body idleness). Take the three converging points beneath the navel in the lower abdomen. The Three Converging Points refer to the Yangming and Taiyin meridians; three cun below the navel is Guanyuan. Those with jue bi syndrome experience upward-moving qi affecting up to the abdomen. Take points on the Yin and Yang collaterals, identifying those that dominate the disease; drain the Yang and tonify the Yin meridians.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
頸側之動脈人迎。人迎，足陽明也，在嬰筋之前。嬰筋之後，手陽明也，名曰扶突。次脈，足少陽脈也，名曰天牖。次脈，足太陽也，名曰天柱。腋下動脈，臂太陰也，名曰天府。
		The pulsing vessel on the side of the neck is called Renying. Renying belongs to the Foot Yangming meridian and is located in front of the infant's tendon. Behind the infant's tendon lies the Hand Yangming, named Futu. The next vessel is the Foot Shaoyang meridian, called Tianyou. The following vessel belongs to the Foot Taiyang meridian and is named Tianzhu. The pulsing vessel beneath the armpit belongs to the Arm Taiyin meridian and is called Tianshu.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
陽迎頭痛，胸滿不得息，取之人迎。暴瘖氣鞭，取扶突與舌本出血。暴襲氣蒙，耳目不明，取天牖。暴攣癎眩，足不任身，取天柱。暴痺內逆，肝肺相搏，血溢鼻口，取天府。此為天牖五部。
		For headache with yang obstruction, chest fullness, and difficulty breathing, take Renying. In cases of sudden aphonia and qi binding, take Futu and bleed at the base of the tongue. For sudden attacks with qi obstruction causing blurred vision and hearing, take Tianyou. For sudden convulsions, dizziness, and inability to support the body on one's feet, take Tianzhu. In cases of sudden bi syndrome with internal counterflow, where liver and lung qi clash violently causing blood to overflow from the nose and mouth, take Tianshu. These are the Five Regions of Tianyou.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
臂陽明，有入頄遍齒者，名曰大迎。下齒齲，取之臂。惡寒補之，不惡寒寫之。足太陽有入頄遍齒者，名曰角孫。上齒齲，取之在鼻與頄前。方病之時，其脈盛，盛則寫之，虛則補之。一曰取之出鼻外。
		The Arm Yangming meridian has a branch that enters the cheeks and reaches the teeth, named Daying. For decayed lower teeth, take points on the arm. If there is aversion to cold, tonify it; if there is no aversion to cold, drain it. The Foot Taiyang meridian has a branch entering the cheeks and reaching the teeth, named Jiaosun. For decayed upper teeth, take points located at the nose and in front of the cheeks. At the onset of illness, if the pulse is strong, drain it; if weak, tonify it. One method is to take points outside the nose.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
足陽明有挾鼻入于面者，名曰懸顱。屬口，對入繫目本，視有過者取之。損有餘，益不足，反者益其。足太陽有通項入于腦者，正屬目本，名曰眼系。頭目苦痛，取之在項中兩筋間。入腦乃別陰蹻、陽蹻，陰陽相交，陽入陰，陰出陽，交于目銳眥，陽氣盛則瞋目，陰氣盛則瞑目。
		The Foot Yangming meridian has branches that run alongside the nose and enter the face, named Xuanlu. It is connected to the mouth and extends inward toward the root of the eyes; take points where there are abnormalities observed. Reduce excess, supplement deficiency; those with reversal should be supplemented accordingly. The Foot Taiyang meridian extends through the neck into the brain and directly connects to the root of the eye, named Yanxi. For headache and painful eyes, take points located between the two tendons in the neck. The meridian entering the brain then diverges into the Yin Qiao and Yang Qiao vessels. The yin and yang energies intersect: yang enters yin, while yin exits yang, meeting at the inner canthus of the eye. When yang energy is excessive, the eyes open wide in anger; when yin energy is excessive, the eyelids close as if drowsy.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
熱厥取足太陰、少陽，皆留之；寒厥取足陽明、少陰於足，皆留之。舌縱涎下，煩悗，取足少陰。振寒洒洒鼓頷，不得汗出，腹脹煩悗，取手太陰，刺虛者，刺其去也；刺實者，刺其來也。
		For heat 厥 syndrome, take points on the Foot Taiyin and Shaoyang meridians, and retain the needles at these points; For cold 厥 syndrome, take points on the Yangming and Shaoyin meridians of the foot, and retain the needles there. In cases where the tongue hangs loose with drooling saliva and mental distress occurs, take points on the Foot Shaoyin meridian. For chills with shivering, a trembling jaw, inability to sweat, abdominal distension, and mental distress, take points on the Hand Taiyin meridian; needle those with deficiency by draining them. For those with excess conditions, needle at the points where the pathogenic qi arrives.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
春取絡脈，夏取分腠，秋取氣口，冬取經輸。凡此四時，各以時為齊。絡脈治皮膚，分腠治肌肉，氣口治筋脈，經輸治骨髓。五藏，身有五部：伏兔一；腓二，腓者腨也；背三，五藏之輸四；項五。此五部有癰疽者死。
		In spring, take collateral vessels; in summer, take interstitial regions; in autumn, take the Qiguan (mouth of qi); and in winter, take the Jingshu (meridian streams). In general, these four seasons each have their own timing for treatment. Collateral vessels treat the skin and muscles; interstitial regions treat the muscles; Qiguan treats tendons and meridians; Jingshu treats bone marrow. The five zang organs correspond to five regions on the body: Futu is one of them; the second are Fei, which refers to the calf muscles; the third is on the back; the fourth are the five zang's Shu points; the fifth is at the neck. If there are abscesses or carbuncles in these five regions, death will occur.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
病始手臂者，先取手陽明、太陰而汗出；病始頭首者，先取項太陽而汗出；病始足脛者，先取足陽明而汗出。臂太陰可汗出，足陽明可汗出，故取陰而汗出甚者，止之于陽，取陽而汗出甚者，止之於陰。
		When a disease begins in the arms and hands, first take points on the Hand Yangming and Taiyin to induce sweating; when a disease originates in the head or neck, first take points of the Taiyang meridian at the nape to induce perspiration; When a disease begins in the feet and shins, first take points on the Foot Yangming to induce sweating. The Hand Taiyin can induce sweating, as can the Foot Yangming. Therefore, if excessive sweating occurs after taking yin points, stop by using yang points; if excessive sweating results from taking yang points, stop it by using yin points.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱病:	
凡刺之害，中而不去則精泄；不中而去則致氣。精泄則病甚而慪，致氣則生為癰疽也。
		Generally, the harm of acupuncture is that if the needle strikes a point but is not removed in time, vital essence will be depleted; if it does not strike the correct location yet the needle is still removed, qi may become obstructed. If vital essence is depleted, the illness will worsen and cause distress; if qi becomes obstructed, it can lead to abscesses or carbuncles. 

22. 癩狂病 Lai Kuang Bing

1
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
目眥外決於面者，為銳眥；在內近鼻者，為內眥；上為外眥，下為內眥。
		When the blood vessels of the eyes burst outward from the face, it is called Ruiyi. Those that are inside and near the nose are called Neiyi; The upper ones are external Yi, and the lower ones are internal Yi.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
癲疾始生，先不樂，頭重痛，視舉目赤，甚作極，已而煩心。候之於顏。取手太陽、陽明、太陰，血變為止。
		When a person first develops dian disease, they initially feel unhappy, with a heavy and painful head, red eyes when looking up, severe symptoms that reach an extreme point, followed by a feeling of mental distress. Observe it on the face. Treat by taking points from the hand Taiyang, Yangming, and Taiyin meridians; continue until the blood condition changes and stops.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
癲疾始作，而引口啼呼喘悸者，候之手陽明、太陽。左強者，攻其右；右強者，攻其左，血變為止。癲疾始作，先反僵，因而脊痛，候之足太陽、陽明、太陰、手太陽，血變為止。
		When dian disease first occurs, and it causes pulling at the mouth, crying out, panting, and palpitations, observe the hand Yangming and Taiyang meridians. If the left side is stronger, attack its right side; if the right side is stronger, attack its left side, until the blood condition changes and ceases. When dian disease first manifests, the patient initially becomes rigid in reverse and subsequently experiences back pain; observe the foot Taiyang, Yangming, Taiyin, and hand Taiyang meridians until the blood condition changes and subsides.

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
治癲疾者，常與之居，察其所當取之處。病至，視之有過者寫之，置其血於瓠壺之中，至其發時，血獨動矣，不動，灸窮骨二十壯。窮骨者，骶骨也。
		To treat dian disease, one should often stay with the patient and observe the locations that should be selected for treatment. When the disease arrives, observe it; if there is an excess condition, drain it. Place the blood in a gourd vessel. When the time of its onset comes, the blood will move uniquely within the vessel. If it does not move, moxibustion should be applied to Qionggu for twenty cunzhuang. Qionggu refers to the sacrum bone.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
骨癲疾者，顑、齒諸腧、分肉皆滿而骨居，汗出、煩悗，嘔多沃沫，氣下泄，不治。
		In cases of skeletal dian disease, the cheeks, teeth acupoints, and intermuscular regions are all full with bone accumulation. The patient sweats profusely, feels mentally distressed, vomits excessive frothy mucus, experiences downward qi discharge, and cannot be cured.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
筋癲疾者，身倦攣急大，刺項大經之大杼脈，嘔多沃沫，氣下泄，不治。
		In cases of tendinous dian disease, the body becomes weary and greatly stiff with cramps. Acupuncture should be applied to Dazhu pulse on the large meridian at the neck. The patient vomits excessive frothy mucus and experiences downward qi discharge; this condition cannot be cured.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
脈癲疾者，暴仆，四肢之脈皆脹而縱，脈滿，盡刺之出血，不滿，灸之挾項太陽，灸帶脈於腰相去三寸，諸分肉本輸。嘔吐沃沫，氣下泄，不治。癲疾者，疾發如狂者，死不治。
		In cases of meridian dian disease, the patient suddenly collapses, and all pulse channels in the four limbs swell and become relaxed. When the pulses are full, they should be needled to draw blood; if not full, moxibustion is applied on both sides of the Taiyang channel at the back of the neck, along with the Dai meridian three cun away from the waist, as well as at all intermuscular root acupoints. Vomiting frothy mucus and downward qi discharge cannot be cured. In cases of dian disease, if the condition suddenly flares up with symptoms resembling madness, it is fatal and cannot be treated.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
狂始生，先自悲也，喜忘、苦怒、善恐者得之憂飢，治之取手太陽、陽明，血變而止，及取足太陰、陽明。狂始發，少臥不飢，自高賢也，自辯智也，自尊貴也，善罵詈，日夜不休，治之取手陽明太陽太陰舌下少陰，視之盛者，皆取之，不盛，釋之也。
		Kuang disease begins with initial symptoms of spontaneous sadness, forgetfulness, irritability, and excessive fear caused by worry and hunger. To treat it, select points from the hand Taiyang and Yangming meridians until blood condition changes and stops; also take points from the foot Taiyin and Yangming meridians. When kuang disease first manifests, the person sleeps little and has no appetite; they consider themselves superior, wise, and noble. They often curse others without ceasing day or night. To treat this condition, select points from the hand Yangming, Taiyang, Taiyin meridians, as well as the Shaoyin under the tongue. Observe which channels are most active—needling should be performed on those; if they are not active, leave them untreated.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
狂言，驚，善笑，好歌樂，妄行不休者，得之大恐，治之取手陽明太陽太陰。狂，目妄見，耳妄聞，善呼者，少氣之所生也；治之取手太陽太陰陽明，足太陰頭兩顑。
		Kuangyan (mad speech), startle easily, frequent smiling, a fondness for singing and music, and aimless wandering without rest are caused by extreme fear. To treat this condition, take points from the hand Yangming Taiyang Taiyin meridians. Kuang disease causes one to see things with the eyes that are not there, hear sounds with the ears that do not exist, and frequently shout; this arises from a deficiency of qi. To treat it, take points from the hand Taiyang Taiyin Yangming meridians and the two cheeks at the head of the foot Taiyin.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
狂者多食，善見鬼神，善笑而不發於外者，得之有所大喜，治之取足太陰太陽陽明，後取手太陰太陽陽明。狂而新發，未應如此者，先取曲泉左右動脈，及盛者見血，有頃已，不已，以法取之，灸骨骶二十壯。
		Those with kuang disease have a large appetite, often see ghosts and gods, smile frequently without expressing it outwardly, and this condition arises from extreme joy. To treat it, first take points from the foot Taiyinh Taiyang Yangming meridians, then select points from the handtaiyin Taiyang Yangming meridiams. In newly onset cases of kuang disease where symptoms have not yet manifested so severely, first take the left and right moving pulses at Ququan. If they are active, draw blood from them; if it does not improve after a while, treat according to the method and apply moxibustion on the sacrum bone for twenty cunzhuangs.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
風逆，暴四肢腫，身漯漯，唏然時寒，飢則煩，飽則善變，取手大陰表裏，足少陰陽明之徑，肉清取滎，骨清取井、經也。
		Fengni (wind reversal) causes sudden swelling of the four limbs, a slippery body sensation, occasional chills, irritability when hungry, and frequent changes after eating. To treat it, take points on the exterior and interior of the hand Taiyin meridian, as well as along the foot Shaoyin Yangming pathways. If the muscles are clear, select Shuiguan (Shuxu) points; if the bones are clear, choose Jingguan (Jing) or Jingluo (Jingjing) points.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
厥逆為病也，足暴清，胸若將裂，腸若將以刀切之，煩而不能食，脈大小皆澀，暖取足少陰，清取足陽明，清則補之，溫則寫之。厥逆腹脹滿，腸鳴，胸滿不得息，取之下胸二脅，咳而動手者，與背輸，以手按之，立快者是也。
		Jueni, as a disease, causes sudden coldness in the feet, a sensation of the chest about to split open, and an intense feeling as if the intestines are being cut with a knife. The patient feels restless and unable to eat; both large and small pulses become sluggish. When warm, select points on the foot Shaoyin meridian; when cold, choose points on the foot Yangming meridian. If it is cold, tonify; if it is warm, drain. Jueni causes abdominal distension and fullness, intestinal rumbling, chest congestion with difficulty breathing. To treat it, select the two flanks below the chest. If coughing causes movement in the hands, then combine treatment with back acupoints; press on these points by hand, and those that provide immediate relief are correct.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
內閉不得溲，刺足少陰太陽，與抵上以長鍼。氣逆，則取其太陰、陽明、厥陰，甚取少陰、陽明，動者之經也。
		In cases of internal obstruction where urination is impossible, acupuncture should be applied to the foot Shaoyin and Taiyang meridians using long needles at the upper resistance points. For qi reversal, select points from its Taiyin, Yangming, and Jueyin meridians; if severe, also take the Shaoyin and Yangming. Choose the meridian where movement is observed.

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癩狂病:	
少氣，身漯漯也，言吸吸也，骨痠體重，懈惰不能動，補足少陰。短氣息短，不屬，動作氣索，補足少陰，去血絡也。
		Qi deficiency causes a slippery body sensation, labored breathing with shortness of breath, bone soreness and heavy limbs, fatigue, and inability to move; tonify the foot Shaoyin. Shortness of breath with shallow breathing, a sense of disconnection in respiration, and qi depletion during movement should be treated by tonifying the foot Shaoyin meridian and removing blood stasis from the collateral vessels. 

23. 熱病 Febrile Diseases

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
偏枯，身偏不用而痛，言不變，志不亂，病在分腠之間，巨鍼取之，益其不足，損其有餘，乃可復也。
		Bianku is a condition in which the body becomes partially paralyzed and painful, speech remains unaffected, and mental clarity is not disturbed. The illness occurs between the skin layers; treatment should employ large needles to supplement deficiencies and reduce excesses, thereby restoring health.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
痱之為病也，身無痛者，四肢不收；智亂不甚，其言微知，可治；甚則不能言，不可治也。病先起于陽，復入于陰者，先取其陽，後取其陰，浮而取之。
		The condition Fei is characterized by no pain in the body but with the four limbs not functioning properly; mental confusion is mild, and speech is slightly impaired but still somewhat coherent, it can be treated; if the condition worsens to the point where speech is impossible, it cannot be cured. If a disease initially arises in the Yang aspect and then enters the Yin aspect, first treat the Yang, then treat the Yin, using superficial needling techniques.

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
熱病三日，而氣口靜、人迎躁者，取之諸陽，五十九刺，以寫其熱，而出其汗，實其陰，以補其不足者。身熱甚，陰陽皆靜者，勿刺也；其可刺者，急取之，不汗出則泄。所謂勿刺者，有死徵也。
		In a febrile illness on the third day, if the Qi Kou is calm and the Ren Ying is restless, treat all Yang meridians with fifty-nine needle insertions to release the heat and induce sweating, strengthen Yin, and supplement its deficiency. If body fever is severe but both Yang and Yin are calm, do not needle; if needling is indicated, perform it immediately; otherwise, the heat will be released through diarrhea instead of sweating. What is meant by "do not needle" is that there are signs indicating a fatal outcome.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
熱病七日八日，脈口動，喘而短者，急刺之，汗且自出，淺刺手大指間。
		In a febrile illness lasting seven or eight days, if the pulse at the Cuan Kou is active and breathing becomes labored and shallow, needle immediately; sweating will occur naturally. Insert needles superficially between the thumb and index finger of the hand.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
熱病七日八日，脈微小，病者溲血，口中乾，一日半而死。脈代者，一日死。
		In a febrile illness that has lasted seven or eight days, if the pulses are faint and small, the patient urinates blood, and the mouth is dry, death will occur within one and a half days. If the pulse exhibits substitution (pulse stops intermittently), death will occur in one day.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
熱病已得汗出，而脈尚躁，喘且復熱，勿刺膚，喘甚者死。
		In a febrile illness, if sweating has already occurred but the pulse remains restless and breathing is labored with recurring fever, do not needle the skin; if the breathlessness becomes severe, death will follow.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
熱病七日八日，脈不躁，躁不散數，後三日中有汗，三日不汗，四日死。未曾汗者，勿腠刺之。
		In a febrile illness of seven or eight days' duration, if the pulse is not restless and does not become scattered or rapid, sweating will occur in three more days. If no sweat appears within those three days, death will follow on the fourth day. If the patient has never sweated, do not needle between the skin layers.

打開字典

熱病先膚痛，窒鼻充面，取之皮，以第一鍼，五十九，苛軫鼻，索皮于肺，不得，索之火，火者，心也。
		In a febrile disease where the initial symptoms include skin pain, nasal congestion and facial fullness, treat the skin layer using the first type of needle at fifty-nine points. Carefully examine the nose; investigate the skin in relation to the lungs. If no improvement is found, investigate further in terms of Fire, for Fire corresponds to the heart.

打開字典

		In a febrile disease that initially presents with dryness and heat in the body, restlessness, dry lips and mouth with saliva overflowing, treat the skin layer using first-type needles at fifty-nine points; if there is skin swelling, dry mouth, and cold sweating, investigate the pulse in relation to the heart; if no improvement is found, further investigation should be made in terms of Water, for Water corresponds to the kidneys.

		In a febrile disease with excessive dryness in the mouth, increased thirst, frequent startle responses, and inability to rise from lying down, treat the flesh layer using sixth-type needles at fifty-nine points. If the eyelids appear bluish, investigate the flesh in relation to the spleen; if no improvement is observed, further investigation should be conducted in terms of Water (though it says "water," this may refer to a misstatement or confusion with another element), for Wood corresponds to the liver.

		In a febrile disease characterized by a bluish face, headache, and restlessness in the hands and feet, treat between the tendons using fourth-type needles at the Si Ni points; if there is tendon stiffness with sunken eyes, investigate the tendons in relation to the liver; if no improvement occurs, further investigation should be carried out in terms of Metal, for Metal corresponds to the lungs.

		In a febrile disease marked by frequent startles, convulsions and madness, treat the vessels using fourth-type needles, urgently draining excess. If epilepsy-like symptoms appear with loss of body hair, investigate blood in relation to the heart; if ineffective, further investigation should be done in terms of Water, for water corresponds to the kidneys.

		In a febrile disease presenting with body heaviness, bone pain, deafness and a preference for sleep, treat the bones using fourth-type needles at fifty-nine sites to needle the bones; if there is loss of appetite, gnawing of teeth and bluish ears, investigate the bones in relation to the kidneys; if no improvement occurs, investigate further in terms of Earth, for Earth corresponds to the spleen.

		In a febrile disease, if the patient is unaware of pain, has deafness, cannot control their limbs, experiences dry mouth, intense Yang heat, and some Yin coldness, then the heat has reached the marrow; this condition is fatal and untreatable.

		In a febrile disease featuring headache, pain in the temples and cheeks, eye discomfort, pulse pain, frequent nosebleeds, and alternating chills and fever (a 厥 heat illness), treat using third-type needles. Assess whether there is excess or deficiency to address coldness, heat, and hemorrhoids.

		In a febrile disease causing body heaviness and internal intestinal heat, treat using fourth-type needles at their corresponding Shu points and between the lower toes. Investigate Qi in relation to the stomach and pericardium; this is where Qi can be obtained.

		In a febrile disease involving urgent pain around the navel and fullness in the chest and hypochondrium, treat at Yongquan and Yinlingquan using fourth-type needles; needle also at the throat interior.

打開字典

	熱病:	
熱病，而汗且出，及脈順可汗者，取之魚際、太淵、大都、太白。寫之則熱去，補之則汗出，汗出大甚，取內踝上橫脈以止之。
		In a febrile disease in which sweating is about to occur, and the pulse indicates that sweating is appropriate, treat at Yuxi, Taiyuan, Dadu, and Taibai. Drain these points to remove the heat; tonify them to induce sweating. If excessive sweating occurs, needle the horizontal pulse above the inner ankle to stop it.

		In a febrile disease after sweating has occurred but the pulse remains excessively restless, this indicates an extreme condition of Yin deficiency; death will follow. If sweating occurs and the pulse becomes calm, recovery is possible.

	熱病:

		In a febrile disease if the pulse remains strong and restless but no sweat occurs, this indicates an extreme state of Yang; death will follow. If the pulse is strong and restless yet sweating brings calmness to the pulse, recovery is possible.

21
打開字典
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
熱病不可刺者有九：一曰：汗不出，大顴發赤噦者死；二曰：泄而腹滿甚者死；三曰：目不明，熱不已者死；四曰：老人嬰兒熱而腹滿者死；五曰：汗不出嘔下血者死；六曰：舌本爛，熱不已者死；七曰：咳而衄，汗不出，出不至足者死；八曰：髓熱者死；九曰：熱而痙者死。腰折，瘈瘲，齒噤齘也。凡此九者，不可刺也。
		There are nine cases in which a febrile disease should not be needled: first, if no sweat is produced and the large cheeks become red with vomiting blood, death will occur; second, if diarrhea occurs with severe abdominal distension, death will follow; third, if the eyes are dim and the fever does not subside, death will occur; fourth, if elderly people or infants have a fever with abdominal distension, they will die; fifth, if no sweating occurs with vomiting and bloody stools, death will occur; sixth, if the base of the tongue is ulcerated and the fever does not cease, death will follow; seventh, if coughing with nosebleeds occurs, no sweat appears, or sweating does not reach the feet, death will occur; eighth, if heat has reached the bone marrow, death will follow; ninth, if fever is accompanied by spasm or convulsion, death will occur. Yaozhe refers to a condition involving spasms and convulsions with clenched teeth and jaw locking. All of these nine conditions should not be treated by acupuncture.

22
打開字典
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
所謂五十九刺者，兩手外內側各三，凡十二痏。五指間各一，凡八痏，足亦如是。頭入髮一寸傍三分各三，凡六痏。更入髮三寸邊五，凡十痏。耳前後口下者各一，項中一，凡六痏。巔上一，聰會一，髮際一，廉泉一，風池二，天柱二。
		What is referred to as the "fifty-nine needle insertions" includes three points on each of the inner and outer sides of both hands, totaling twelve insertion sites. One point between each of the five fingers, totaling eight insertion sites; the feet are treated in the same manner. Three points on each side at one cun inward from the hairline and three fen to the sides, totaling six insertion sites. Five more points along both sides of the head, three cun inward from the hair, totaling ten insertion sites. One point in front and behind each ear, one below the mouth, and one in the middle of the neck, totaling six needle insertion sites. One at Dian Shang, one at Cong Hui, one at the hairline, one at Lianquan, two at Fengchi, and two at Tianzhu.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	熱病:	
氣滿胸中喘息，取足太陰大趾之端，去爪甲如薤葉，寒則留之，熱則疾之，氣下乃止。
		When Qi is full in the chest causing labored breathing, needle the end of the big toe on the Spleen Meridian (Yinbai), located a distance from the nail like the thickness of a scallion leaf. If there is coldness, retain the needle; if there is heat, remove it quickly. Stop when the Qi descends.

		For sudden and severe pain due to heart hernia, needle the Spleen (Taiyin) and Liver (Jueyin) meridians of the feet; completely drain the blood vessels.

		For pharyngeal obstruction with tongue retraction, dry mouth, mental distress, heart pain, and inner arm pain extending to the head, needle beneath the fingernails of the second and third fingers on the hand, at a distance from their tips like the thickness of leek leaves.

男子如蠱，女子如怚，身體腰脊如解，不欲飲食，先取涌泉見血，視跗上盛者，盡見血也。
		In men, it is as if affected by 蛊 (parasitic or toxic condition), and in women, as if affected by 怚 (a similar pathological state); the body and waist spine feel loosened, with no desire for food or drink. First needle Yongquan until blood appears; examine the dorsum of the foot where the pulse is full, and bleed completely at those points. 

24. 厥病 Jue Bing

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
厥頭痛，面若腫起而煩心，取之足陽明太陰。厥頭痛，頭脈痛，心悲，善泣，視頭動脈反盛者，刺盡去血，後調足厥陰。
		The person has a headache, the face appears swollen and causes mental distress; treat by taking points on the Foot Yangming and Taiyin meridians. In cases of jue headache, head vessel pain, sadness in the heart, and a tendency to weep, if one observes that the pulsating vessels on the head are especially prominent, acupuncture should be used to completely remove the blood; afterward, regulate the Foot Jueyin meridian.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
厥頭痛，貞貞頭重而痛，寫頭上五行，行五，先取手少陰，後取足少陰。厥頭痛，意善忘，按之不得，取頭面左右動脈，後取足太陰。
		In cases of jue headache where the head feels heavy and painful, drain the five lines on the head, five points per line; first take points from the Hand Shaoyin meridian, then those from the Foot Shaoyin meridian. In cases of jue headache with frequent forgetfulness and where pressing does not relieve the condition, take points at the pulsating vessels on both sides of the head and face; afterward, take points from the Foot Taiyin meridian.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
厥頭痛，項先痛，腰脊為應，先取天柱，後取足太陽。厥頭痛，頭痛甚，耳前後脈湧有熱，寫出其血，後取足少陽。
		In cases of jue headache where pain first occurs in the neck and the waist and spine respond accordingly, first take Tianzhu (BL10), then points from the Foot Taiyang meridian. In cases of jue headache with severe head pain, where there is heat and surging vessels before and behind the ears, bleed out the blood; afterward, take points from thefoot Shaoyang meridian.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
真頭痛，頭痛甚，腦盡痛，手足寒至節，死不治。
		Zhen headache is characterized by severe head pain that extends to the entire brain, with coldness in the hands and feet reaching the joints; it is an incurable condition leading to death.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
頭痛不可取於腧者，有所擊墮，惡血在於內，若肉傷，痛未已，可則刺，不可遠取也。頭痛不可刺者，大痺為惡，日作者，可令少愈，不可已。頭半寒痛，先取手少陽陽明，後取足少陽陽明。
		In cases of headache where it is not advisable to take points at the 俞 (shu) acupoints, this may be due to a previous injury from falling or striking, with stagnant blood remaining inside; if there has been soft tissue damage and the pain persists, acupuncture can be used but should not be applied far from the affected area. In cases of headache that cannot be treated with acupuncture, where severe numbness is harmful and symptoms occur daily, the condition may improve slightly but will not be completely cured. For headache with cold pain on one side of the head, first take points from the Hand Shaoyang and Yangming meridians, then those from the Foot Shaoyang and Yangming meridians.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
厥心痛，與背相控，善瘈，如從後觸其心，傴僂者，腎心痛也，先取京骨、崑崙，發狂不已，取然谷。厥心痛，腹脹胸滿，心尤痛甚，胃心痛也，取之大都、大白。
		Jue xintong (厥心痛) is characterized by pain in the heart that extends to and interacts with the back, frequent convulsions, a sensation as if something were pressing against the heart from behind, and hunching of the body; this condition is known as shen xintong (腎心痛). First take Jinggu (BL62) and Kunlun (BL60); if mania persists, then take Rangu (KI2). Jue xintong is characterized by abdominal distension, chest fullness, and especially severe heart pain; this condition is called wei xintong (胃心痛). Treat by taking Dadu (SP2) and Dabai (PC6).

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
厥心痛，痛如以錐鍼刺其心，心痛甚者，脾心痛也，取之然谷、太谿。
		Jue xintong, with pain as if a sharp needle were piercing the heart and severe heartache, is known as pi xintong (脾心痛). Treat by taking Rangu (KI2) and Taixi (KI3).

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
厥心痛，色蒼蒼如死狀，終日不得太息，肝心痛也，取之行間、太衝。
		Jue xintong with a bluish-gray complexion resembling that of death and the inability to take deep breaths all day is known as gan xintong (肝心痛). Treat by taking Xingjian (LR2) and Taichong (LR3).

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
厥心痛，臥若徒居，心痛間，動作，痛益甚，色不變，肺心痛也，取之魚際、太淵。
		Jue xintong where the person lies down as if in solitude, with heart pain that worsens during movement but without change in complexion is known as fei xintong (肺心痛). Treat by taking Yujie (LU10) and Taiyuan (LU9).

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
真心痛，手足清至節，心痛甚，日發夕死，夕發旦死。心痛不可刺者，中有盛聚，不可取於腧。
		Zhen xintong is characterized by coldness in the hands and feet extending to the joints, severe heart pain, and a fatal outcome: if it occurs during the day, death follows at night; if it occurs at night, death follows in the morning. In cases of xintong (heart pain) where acupuncture is not advisable, there may be an excessive accumulation within the body; therefore, it should not be treated at the shu points.

11
打開字典
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
腸中有蟲瘕及蛟蛕，皆不可取以小鍼；心腸痛，憹作痛，腫聚，往來上下行，痛有休止，腹熱喜渴涎出者，是蛟蛕也。以手聚按而堅持之，無令得移，以大鍼刺之，久持之，蟲不動，乃出鍼也。㤣腹憹痛，形中上者。
		Intestinal conditions involving worm masses (chong jia) or parasitic infestations (jiao gui) should not be treated with fine needles; Heart and intestinal pain, intermittent and painful, with swelling and aggregation, moving up and down intermittently, and with periods of relief; if the abdomen feels hot and there is a desire for cold drinks accompanied by excessive saliva production, this condition is known as jiaogui. Use the hand to firmly press and hold the area, preventing it from shifting; insert a large needle and maintain pressure for some time. When the worm no longer moves, remove the needle. Abdominal pain that is intermittent and located in the upper part of the body.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
耳聾無聞，取耳中；耳鳴，取耳前動脈；耳痛不可刺者，耳中有膿，若有乾聆聊，耳無聞也；耳聾取手小指次指爪甲上與肉交者，先取手，後取足；耳鳴取手中指爪甲上，左取右，右取左，先取手，後取足。
		For deafness with no hearing, take points within the ear; Tinnitus: take the pulsating vessel in front of the ear; In cases of ear pain where acupuncture is not advisable, pus may be present within the ear, or there may be a dry obstruction; in such cases, hearing will also be absent. For deafness, take the point at the junction of the nail and flesh on the second finger of the hand; first treat points on the hand, then those on the foot. For tinnitus, take the point at the nail of the middle finger; if the left ear is affected, treat the right hand, and vice versa. First treat points on the hand, then on the foot.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
足髀不可舉，側而取之，在樞合中，以員利鍼，大鍼不可刺。病注下血，取曲泉。
		When the thigh cannot be raised, take points laterally in the hip joint area; use a round and sharp needle. Large needles should not be used for acupuncture here. For diseases involving bloody diarrhea, take Ququan (LR8).

14
打開字典
顯示影印本
	厥病:	
風痺淫礫，病不可已者，足如履冰，時如入湯中，股脛淫礫，煩心頭痛，時嘔時悗，眩已汗出，久則目眩，悲以喜恐，短氣，不樂，不出三年死也。
		In cases of wind-bi syndrome with persistent and severe symptoms, the feet feel as if treading on ice at times and as if immersed in hot water at others; the thighs and shins experience a creeping sensation. Symptoms include mental distress, headache, occasional vomiting and dizziness, sweating after episodes, prolonged dizziness, alternating feelings of sadness and fear, shortness of breath, lack of joy, and death may occur within three years. 

25. 病本 Root of Disease

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病本:	
先病而後逆者，治其本；先逆而後病者，治其本；先寒而後生病者，治其本；先病而後生寒者，治其本；先熱而後生病者，治其本。
		Those who first have an illness and then experience a reversal should be treated at their root. Those who first experience a reversal and then develop an illness should also be treated at their root; Those who first feel cold and then fall ill should be treated at the root; Those who first have an illness but later develop chills should also be treated at the root. Those who first experience heat and then fall ill should also be treated at the root."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病本:	
先泄而後生他病者，治其本，必且調之，乃治其他病。先病而後中滿者，治其標；先病後泄者，治其本；先中滿而後煩心者，治其本。
		Those who first suffer from diarrhea and later develop other illnesses should have their root condition treated. It is necessary to regulate the body first before treating the other diseases. Those who first fall ill and then experience fullness in the middle jiao (abdomen) should be treated at their symptoms; Those who first become ill and later suffer from diarrhea should have their root condition addressed. Those who first experience fullness in the abdomen and then develop a troubled mind (mental distress) should be treated at the root cause.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病本:	
有客氣，有同氣。大小便不利治其標，大小便利，治其本。
		There are external pathogenic influences, as well as internal disharmonies that arise from within. When urination and defecation are obstructed, treat the symptoms; when urination and defecation flow normally, address the root cause.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病本:	
病發而有餘，本而標之，先治其本，後治其標；病發而不足，標而本之，先治其標，後治其本，謹詳察間甚，以意調之，間者並行，甚為獨行；先小大便不利而後生他病者，治其本也。
		When a disease manifests with excess conditions, it involves both the root and the symptoms; first treat the root, then address the symptoms. When a disease manifests with deficiency conditions, it involves the symptoms and then the root; first treat the symptoms, then address the root. Carefully examine whether the condition is mild or severe, and adjust treatment accordingly. For mild cases, both symptom and root treatments can be administered concurrently; for severe cases, they should be treated separately one after another. Those who first experience obstruction in urination and defecation and later develop other diseases should have their root condition treated first.
