27. 周痺 Zhou Bi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	周痺:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：周痺之在身也，上下移徒隨脈，其上下左右相應，間不容空，願聞此痛，在血脈之中邪？將在分肉之間乎？何以致是？其痛之移也，間不及下鍼，其慉痛之時，不及定治，而痛已止矣。何道使然？願聞其故？歧伯答曰：此眾痺也，非周痺也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked 岐伯: "When the condition of zhou bi occurs in the body, it moves up and down with the meridians. Its manifestations above and below, to the left and right, correspond to each other without any gap. I would like to know whether this pain is located within the blood vessels?" Is it located between the muscle layers? What causes this to happen? When the pain moves, there is not enough time to insert a needle; when it causes sudden pain, there is no opportunity for treatment before the pain has already ceased. What principle causes this? I would like to hear an explanation of the reason. Qi Bo answered: "This is a case of zhong bi, not zhou bi."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	周痺:	
黃帝曰：願聞眾痺。歧伯對曰：此各在其處，更發更止，更居更起，以右應左，以左應右，非能周也。更發更休也。黃帝曰：善。刺之奈何？歧伯對曰：刺此者，痛雖已止，必刺其處，勿令復起。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I would like to hear about zhong bi." Qi Bo replied: "Each manifestation of zhong bi occurs in its own location, alternating between onset and cessation, appearing and disappearing alternately. It responds to the right with the left, and to the left with the right; it does not affect the entire body." It alternates between occurring and ceasing. The Yellow Emperor said: "Good." How should it be treated with acupuncture? Qi Bo replied: "When treating this condition, even though the pain may have already ceased, one must still needle the affected area to prevent it from recurring."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	周痺:	
帝曰：善。願聞周痺何如？歧伯對曰：周痺者，在於血脈之中，隨脈以上，隨脈以下，不能左右，各當其所。黃帝曰：刺之奈何？歧伯對曰：痛從上下者，先刺其下以過之，後刺其上以脫之。痛從下上者，先刺其上以過之，後刺其下以脫之。
		The Emperor said: "Good." I would like to hear what zhou bi is like. Qi Bo replied: "Zhou bi occurs within the blood vessels, moving upward and downward along the meridians; it cannot move to the left or right but remains in its respective location." The Yellow Emperor said: "How should this be treated with acupuncture?" Qi Bo replied: "When the pain moves from above downward, first needle the lower part to relieve it, then needle the upper part to release it." When the pain moves from below upward, first needle the upper part to relieve it, then needle its lower part to release it.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	周痺:	
黃帝曰：善。此痛安生？何因而有名？歧伯對曰：風寒濕氣，客於外分肉之間，迫切而為沫，沫得寒則聚，聚則排分肉而分裂也，分裂則痛，痛則神歸之，神歸之則熱，熱則痛解，痛解則厥，厥則他痺發，發則如是。帝曰：善。余已得其意矣。此內不在藏，而外未發於皮，獨居分肉之間，真氣不能周，故名曰周痺。故刺痺者，必先切循其下之六經，視其虛實，及大絡之血結而不通，及虛而脈陷空者而調之，熨而通之。其瘈堅轉引而行之。黃帝曰：善。余已得其意矣，亦得其事也。九者經巽之理，十二經脈陰陽之病也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Good." Where does this pain originate? What is the cause that gives it its name? Qi Bo replied: "Wind, cold, and damp pathogenic factors reside between the outer muscle layers. They press tightly together to form phlegm-like substances; when this phlegm encounters cold, it aggregates. When aggregated, it pushes against the muscle layers, causing them to split apart. This splitting causes pain. When there is pain, the spirit (shen) returns to that location, and with its return comes heat. The resulting heat alleviates the pain, but once the pain subsides, a state of 厥(jue) occurs. With jue, other forms of bi syndrome manifest, appearing in this manner." The Emperor said: "Good." I have already understood its meaning. This condition is not located internally in the zang organs, nor has it manifested externally on the skin; it resides alone between the muscle layers. The true qi cannot circulate fully, hence its name is Zhou bi. Therefore, when needling bi syndrome, one must first palpate and examine the six meridians below it. Observe whether they are deficient or excessive, as well as any blood stasis in the major collateral vessels that is obstructed, or where there is deficiency with a sunken and empty pulse pattern; then regulate these conditions by applying moxibustion to unblock them. Those with spasms, stiffness, or tension should be treated by gently manipulating and guiding the affected areas. The Yellow Emperor said: "Good." I have already grasped its meaning, and I also understand the practical application. The nine types of bi syndrome pertain to the principles of meridian circulation; they are diseases related to the yin and yang aspects of the twelve primary meridians. 

28. 口問 Oral Inquiry

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝閒居，辟左右而問於歧伯曰：余已聞九鍼之經，論陰陽逆順，六經已畢，願得口問。歧伯避席再拜曰：善乎哉問也，此先師之所口傳也。黃帝曰：願聞口傳。歧伯答曰：夫百病之始生也，皆生於風雨寒暑，陰陽喜怒，飲食居處，大驚卒恐。則血氣分離，陰陽破敗，經絡厥絕，脈道不通，陰陽相逆，衛氣稽留，經脈虛空，血氣不次，乃失其常。論不在經者，請道其方。
		The Yellow Emperor, during a quiet time at court, dismissed his attendants and asked 岐伯(Qi Bo) thus: "I have already heard of the nine kinds of acupuncture needles, discussed yin-yang principles in their proper order, and completed my study of the six meridians. I now wish to ask you some questions orally." Qi Bo rose from his seat, bowed twice, and said: "What a good question! This is what was orally transmitted by my predecessors." The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear the oral transmission." Qi Bo replied: "All kinds of diseases originate from exposure to wind, rain, cold and heat, imbalances in yin and yang, emotions such as joy and anger, diet and living conditions, as well as sudden fright or terror." Then the vital energy and blood separate, yin and yang become disrupted, the meridians and collaterals are cut off, the channels of qi circulation become blocked, yin and yang oppose each other, defensive qi lingers in stagnation, the meridian vessels become empty and deficient, and the flow of blood and qi becomes disordered, thus losing its normal state. Regarding theories not found within the classical texts, I shall now explain their methods.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之欠者，何氣使然？歧伯答曰：衛氣晝日行於陽，夜半則行於陰，陰者主夜，夜者臥；陽者主上，陰者主下；故陰氣積於下，陽氣未盡，陽引而上，陰引而下，陰陽相引，故數欠。陽氣盡，陰氣盛，則目瞑；陰氣盡而陽氣盛，則寤矣。寫足少陰，補足太陽。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person yawns, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo replied: "Defensive qi travels through the yang meridians during the day, and at midnight it moves into the yin meridians. Yin governs the night, and during the night one lies down to sleep; Yang governs the upper part of the body, while yin governs the lower part; thus yin qi accumulates below, while yang qi has not yet dissipated. Yang pulls upward, and yin pulls downward; the mutual attraction between yin and yang causes frequent yawning." When yang qi is exhausted and yin qi becomes dominant, the eyes close and one falls asleep; when yin qi is depleted and yang qi prevails again, one awakens. Drain the Foot Shaoyin meridian, and tonify the Foot Taiyang meridian.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之噦者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：穀入于胃，胃氣上注于肺。今有故寒氣與新穀氣，俱還入于胃，新故相亂，真邪相攻，氣並相逆，復出於胃，故為噦。補手太陰，寫足少陰。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person sighs, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "Grains enter the stomach, and the stomach's qi ascends to nourish the lungs. Now, if old cold pathogenic qi and new grain qi both return to the stomach together, they become confused with each other. Genuine and pathological qi attack one another; their opposing forces reverse upward again from the stomach, thus causing sighing." Tonify the Hand Taiyin meridian, drain the Foot Shaoyin meridian.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之唏者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：此陰氣盛而陽氣虛，陰氣疾而陽氣徐，陰氣盛而陽氣絕，故為唏。補足太陽，寫足少陰。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person shivers or chatters their teeth, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "This occurs when yin qi is excessive and yang qi is deficient. Yin moves quickly while yang progresses slowly; when yin becomes overly dominant and yang is nearly exhausted, it results in shivering or teeth-chattering." Tonify the Foot Taiyang meridian, drain the Foot Shaayin meridian.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之振寒者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：寒氣客於皮膚，陰氣盛，陽氣虛，故為振寒寒慄，補諸陽。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person experiences chills and shivering, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "Cold pathogenic qi lodges in the skin; yin is excessive while yang is deficient, hence chills and trembling occur. Tonify all yang meridians."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之噫者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：寒氣客於胃，厥逆從下上散，復出於胃，故為噫。補足太陰陽明，一曰補眉本也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person belches, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "Cold pathogenic qi lodges within the stomach, causing reversal and upward dispersion from below. It then emerges again from the stomach, hence resulting in belching." Tonify both the Foot Taiyin and Foot Yangming meridians; another method says to tonify at the root of the eyebrows.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之嚏者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：陽氣和利，滿於心，出於鼻，故為嚏。補足太陽榮眉本，一曰眉上也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person sneezes, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "When yang qi is harmonious and smooth, it fills the heart and emerges through the nose, thus causing a sneeze." Tonify the Foot Taiyang at the collateral of the root of the eyebrows; another says it is above the eyebrows.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之嚲者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：胃不實則諸脈虛；諸脈虛則筋脈懈惰；筋脈懈惰則行陰用力，氣不能復，故為嚲。因其所在，補分肉間。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person drools, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "If the stomach is not solid and strong, all meridians become deficient; when the meridians are deficient, sinews and vessels become slack and sluggish; when the sinews and vessels are slack and sluggish, excessive exertion in yin activities causes qi to be unable to recover, thus resulting in drooling." According to its location, tonify between the muscle layers.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之哀而泣涕出者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：心者，五藏六府之主也；目者，宗脈之所聚也，上液之道也；口鼻者，氣之門戶也。故悲哀愁憂則心動，心動則五藏六府皆搖，搖則宗脈感，宗脈感則液道開，液道開，故泣涕出焉。液者，所以灌精濡空竅者也，故上液之道開則泣，泣不止則液竭；液竭則精不灌，精不灌則目無所見矣，故命曰奪精。補天柱經俠頸。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person grieves and weeps with tears streaming down, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "The heart is the sovereign of the five zang and six fu organs; the eyes are where the great vessels converge, and they serve as the pathway for bodily fluids to ascend. The mouth and nose are the gates through which qi enters and exits. Therefore, when one experiences sorrow, grief, or worry, the heart is affected; when the heart is disturbed, all five zang and six fu organs are agitated. When they are agitated, the great vessels respond, and when the great vessels respond, the fluid pathways open. Once these pathways open, tears and nasal discharge flow out." Body fluids are what nourish the essence and moisten the hollow orifices. Therefore, when the pathways for ascending fluids open, tears flow; if weeping does not cease, body fluids will be depleted; when body fluids are exhausted, essence cannot nourish the eyes, and when essence fails to nourish them, vision is lost. This condition is called "loss of essence." Tonify at Tianzhu (BL10) and the collateral near the neck.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之太息者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：憂思則心系急，心系急則氣道約，約則不利，故太息以伸出之，補手少陰心主，足少陽留之也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person takes deep sighs or long breaths, what kind of qi causes such behavior?" Qi Bo said: "When one is troubled by worries and thoughts, the heart's connecting vessels become tense. When these vessels are taut, the qi pathways constrict; when they constrict, the flow becomes obstructed. Therefore, deep sighing occurs to release it outward. Tonify the Hand Shaoyin (Heart) and Foot Shaoyang meridians to retain and regulate this condition."

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之涎下者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：飲食者，皆入于胃，胃中有熱則蟲動，蟲動則胃緩，胃緩則廉泉開，故涎下，補足少陰。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person drools involuntarily, what kind of qi causes it?" Qi Bo said: "Food and drink all enter the stomach. When there is heat in the stomach, intestinal worms become active; when these worms are active, the stomach becomes relaxed; when the stomach relaxes, the Lianquan (the orifice controlling saliva) opens, hence causing drooling. Tonify the Foot Shaoyin meridian."

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之耳中鳴者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：耳者，宗脈之所聚也，故胃中空則宗脈虛，虛則下溜，脈有所竭者，故耳鳴，補客主人，手大指爪甲上與肉交者也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person experiences ringing in the ears, what kind of qi causes this?" Qi Bo said: "The ears are where the great vessels congregate. Therefore, when the stomach is empty, these major vessels become deficient; when they are deficient, qi and blood descend downward. When the vessels are exhausted in this way, ringing occurs in the ears. Tonify at Kezhuoren (TE21), which is located at the junction of the flesh and nail bed on the thumb of the hand."

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：人之自齧舌者，何氣使然？此厥逆走上，脈氣輩至也。少陰氣至則齧舌，少陽氣至則齧頰，陽明氣至則齧唇矣。視主病者，則補之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When a person bites their own tongue, what kind of qi causes this?" This is caused by reversal and upward movement of qi, with the meridian energies arriving in disorder. When Shaoyin qi arrives, it causes biting of the tongue; when Shaoyang qi arrives, it leads to biting of the cheek; and when Yangming qi arrives, it results in biting of the lips. Identify which meridian is responsible for the condition, and tonify accordingly.

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
凡此十二邪者，皆奇邪之走空竅者也。故邪之所在，皆為不足。故上氣不足，腦為之不滿，耳為之苦鳴，頭為之苦傾，目為之眩。中氣不足，溲便為之變，腸為之苦鳴。下氣不足，則乃為痿厥心悗。補足外踝下留之。
		All these twelve pathogenic influences are abnormal qi that invade the body's hollow orifices. Therefore, wherever such pathogenic factors reside, there is a deficiency in the body. Thus, when yang qi above is insufficient, the brain becomes inadequately nourished, leading to ringing in the ears; the head feels as if it might tilt forward, and the eyes experience dizziness. When central qi is insufficient, urination and defecation become abnormal, and the intestines produce gurgling sounds. When the lower jiao qi is insufficient, it may lead to atrophy, cold extremities, and a feeling of mental distress in the heart. Tonify below the outer malleolus of the foot and retain the qi there.

15
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	口問:	
黃帝曰：治之奈何？歧伯曰：腎主為欠，取足少陰；肺主為噦，取手太陰、足少陰；唏者，陰與陽絕，故補足太陽，寫足少陰；振寒者，補諸陽；噫者，補足太陰陽明；嚏者，補足太陽眉本；嚲，因其所在，補分肉間；泣出補天柱經俠頸，俠頸者，頭中分也；太息，補手少陰、心主、足少陽，留之；涎下補足少陰；耳鳴補客主人，手大指爪甲上與肉交者；自嚙舌，視主病者，則補之。目眩頭傾，補足外踝下留之；痿厥心悗，刺足大趾間上二寸，留之，一曰足外踝下留之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How should this be treated?" Qi Bo said: "The kidney governs yawning; take points on the Foot Shaoyin. the lungs govern sighing; take points on the Hand Taiyin and Foot Shaoyin; for shivering or teeth-chattering, where yin and yang are separated, therefore tonify the Foot Taiyang and drain the Foot Shaoyin; for chills and shaking, tonify all yang meridians; for belching, tonify the Foot Taiyin and Yangming; for sneezing, tonify the Foot Taiyang at meiben (the root of the eyebrows); for drooling, according to its location, tonify in between the muscle layers; for weeping and tears, tonify Tianzhu (the collateral at the back of the neck); "xianghai" refers to the midline of the head; for deep sighing, tonify Hand Shaoyin, Heart (Shen), and Foot Shaoyang, and retain the needle; for drooling of saliva, tonify the Foot Shaoyin; for tinnitus, tonify Kezhuoren (the point at the junction between the nail and flesh of the thumb); for self-biting of the tongue, identify which meridian is primarily involved in the condition and tonify it accordingly. For dizziness and a sensation that the head might tilt forward, tonify below the outer ankle and retain the needle there; for atrophy with cold extremities and a feeling of mental distress, needle two cun above the space between the big toes and retain the needle. Another method says to needle below the outer ankle and retain it there. 

29. 師傳 Transmission from the Teacher

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：余聞先師，有所心藏，弗著於方，余願聞而藏之，則而行之，上以治民，下以治身，使百姓無病，上下和親，德澤下流，子孫無優，傳於後世，無有終時，可得聞乎？歧伯曰：遠乎哉問也。夫治民與自治，治彼與治此，治小與治大，治國與治家，未有逆而能治之也，夫惟順而已矣。順者，非獨陰陽脈，論氣之逆順也，百姓人民皆欲順其志也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard from my predecessors that there are certain inner teachings, which were not recorded in prescriptions. I wish to hear them and preserve them, follow their principles and put them into practice. Above, they may be used to govern the people; below, to regulate one's own body. Thus, the common people would remain free of illness, superiors and inferiors would live in harmony and affection, virtue and grace would flow downward, descendants would have no worries, and these teachings could be passed on to future generations without end. Might I hear them? Qi Bo said: How profound is your question! Governing the people and governing oneself, treating others and attending to oneself, dealing with small matters and managing great ones, ruling a state and managing a household—none of these can be accomplished successfully by going against their nature; only by following their natural course can they be properly managed. To follow the natural order does not merely refer to the yin-yang meridians or the flow of qi in terms of harmony and disharmony; rather, all common people desire that their wills be followed.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：順之奈何？歧伯曰：入國問俗，入家問諱，上堂問禮，臨病人問所便。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should one follow this natural order? Qi Bo said: When entering a country, inquire about its customs; when visiting a family, ask about their taboos; upon entering the hall, inquire about etiquette; and when attending to a patient, ask what is most convenient for them.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：便病人奈何？歧伯曰：夫中熱消癉，則便寒；寒中之屬，則便熱。胃中熱則消谷，令人懸心善飢。臍以上皮熱，腸中熱，則出黃如糜。臍以下皮寒，胃中寒，則腹脹；腸中寒，則腸鳴飧泄。胃中寒，腸中熱，則脹而且泄，胃中熱，腸中寒，則疾飲，小腹痛脹。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What does it mean to be convenient for the patient? Qi Bo said: When a person suffers from internal heat and consumption, cold is beneficial; those who suffer from cold in the middle jiao find warmth beneficial. When there is heat in the stomach, food is consumed quickly, causing a feeling of emptiness and frequent hunger. If the skin above the navel feels hot, it indicates heat in the intestines, resulting in yellow discharge resembling porridge. If the skin below the navel is cold, and there is coldness in the stomach, it causes abdominal distension; if there is cold in the intestines, it leads to intestinal rumbling and undigested diarrhea. If there is cold in the stomach but heat in the intestines, one will experience distension and diarrhea; if there is heat in the stomach but cold in the intestines, then rapid drinking and pain with distension in the lower abdomen occur.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：胃欲寒飲，腸欲熱飲，兩者相逆，便之奈何？且夫王公大人，血食之君，驕恣從欲輕人，而無能禁之，禁之則逆其志，順之則加其病，便之奈何？治之何先？歧伯曰：人之情，莫不惡死而喜生，告之以其敗，語之以其善，導之以其所便，開之以其所苦，雖有無道之人，惡有不聽者乎？
		The Yellow Emperor asked: If the stomach desires cold drinks but the intestines desire warm ones, and these two tendencies are opposite, how should one proceed? Moreover, those in high positions, such as noble rulers who consume blood sacrifices, are arrogant and indulge their desires while looking down upon others. It is difficult to restrain them; if one restrains them, it goes against their will, but if one follows their wishes, it exacerbates their illness. How should this be handled? Which aspect should be treated first? Qi Bo said: Human nature is such that no one dislikes life and prefers death. Inform them of the consequences of failure, tell them about what is beneficial, guide them toward what suits their condition, and explain to them how they can relieve their suffering. Even among those who are unvirtuous or unreasonable, who would refuse to listen?

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：治之奈何？歧伯曰：春夏先治其標，後治其本；秋冬先治其本，後治其標。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should one treat such conditions? Qi Bo said: In spring and summer, first treat the symptoms, then address the root cause; in autumn and winter, first address the root cause, then treat the symptoms.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：便其相逆者奈何？歧伯曰：便此者，食飲衣服，亦欲適寒溫，寒無淒愴，暑無出汗。食飲者，熱無灼灼，寒無滄滄。寒溫中適，故氣將持，乃不致邪僻也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should one handle cases where conditions are contradictory? Qi Bo said: To accommodate such contradictions, the patient's diet, clothing, and environment should be adjusted to suit their condition in terms of warmth and cold. In cold weather, they should not feel excessively chilled; in hot weather, they should avoid excessive sweating. Regarding food and drink: it should not be so hot as to scorch the body, nor so cold as to cause shivering. When the temperature of food and drink is moderate—neither too hot nor too cold—the vital qi will remain stable, thus preventing the occurrence of pathological conditions.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：本藏以身形肢節䐃肉，候五藏六府之大小焉。今夫王公大人，臨朝即位之君，而問焉，誰可捫循之，而後答乎？歧伯曰：身形肢節者，藏府之蓋也，非面部之閱也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: The fundamental condition of the zang-fu organs can be observed through the body's form, limbs, joints, and musculature. Now, when a noble ruler or sovereign ascends the throne and asks such questions, who is qualified to examine them personally before providing an answer? Qi Bo said: The body's form and limbs are the outer manifestations of the zang-fu organs, not merely reflections visible on the face.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
黃帝曰：五藏之氣，閱於面者，余已知之矣，以肢節知而閱之，奈何？歧伯曰：五藏六府者，肺為之蓋，巨肩陷咽，候見其外。黃帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I already understand how the qi of the five zang organs is reflected on the face, but how can one discern and examine them through the limbs and joints? Qi Bo said: Among the five zang and six fu organs, the lungs serve as their covering. A broad shoulder and a sunken throat are external signs that indicate this condition. The Yellow Emperor said: Well said.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
歧伯曰：五藏六府，心為之主，缺盆為之道，骷骨有余以候𩩲𩨗。黃帝曰：善。
		Qi Bo said: Among the five zang and six Fu organs, the heart is their ruler. The clavicle region serves as its pathway; the bones of the skeleton provide additional signs to observe for the condition of the chest and abdomen. The Yellow Emperor said: Well said.

10
打開字典
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
歧伯曰：肝者，主為將，使之候外，欲知堅固，視目小大。黃帝曰：善。
		Qi Bo said: The liver serves as the general, overseeing external conditions. To determine its strength and firmness, observe the size of the eyes. The Yellow Emperor said: Well said.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
歧伯曰：脾者，主為衛，使之迎糧，視唇舌好惡，以知吉凶。黃帝曰：善。
		Qi Bo said: The spleen serves as the guardian, responsible for receiving nourishment. By observing a person's preference or aversion to the lips and tongue, one can discern auspiciousness or misfortune. The Yellow Emperor said: Well said.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
歧伯曰：腎者，主為外，使之遠聽，視耳好惡，以知其性。黃帝曰：善。願聞六府之候。
		Qi Bo said: The kidneys govern the exterior and enable distant hearing; by observing a person's preference or dislike for their ears, one can understand their nature. The Yellow Emperor said: Well said. I would like to hear about the signs of the six fu organs.

13
打開字典
顯示影印本
	師傳:	
歧伯曰：六府者，胃為之海，龐骸、大頸、張胸，五穀乃容。鼻隧以長，以候大腸。唇厚、人中長，以候小腸。目下果大，其膽乃橫。鼻孔在外，膀胱漏泄。鼻柱中央起，三焦乃約，此所以候六府者也。上下三等，藏安且良矣。
		Qi Bo said: Among the six Fu organs, the stomach is the sea; a broad frame, thick neck, and expanded chest indicate that it can accommodate the five grains. A long nasal passage indicates the condition of the large intestine. Thick lips and a long philtrum indicate the condition of the small intestine. A large area beneath the eyes indicates that the gallbladder is full or enlarged. If the nostrils are prominent on the outside, it suggests a condition of leakage from the bladder. A raised nasal bridge in the center indicates that the triple burner is constricted; these are the signs used to observe the six fu organs. When there is harmony between upper and lower functions, the zang organs remain stable and healthy. 

30. 決氣 Deciding the Six Types of Qi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	決氣:	
黃帝曰：余聞人有精、氣、津、液、血、脈，余意以為一氣耳，今乃辨為六名，余不知其所以然。歧伯曰：兩神相搏，合而成形，常先身生，是謂精。何謂氣？歧伯曰：上焦開發，宣五穀味，熏膚、充身、澤毛，若霧露之溉，是謂氣。何謂津？歧伯曰：腠理發泄，汗出溱溱，是謂津。何謂液？歧伯曰：穀入氣滿，淖澤注于骨，骨屬屈伸，泄澤補益腦髓，皮膚潤澤，是謂液。何謂血？歧伯曰：中焦受氣，取汁變化而赤，是謂血。何謂脈？歧伯曰：壅遏營氣，令無所避，是謂脈。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard that people possess jing, qi, jin, ye, xue, and mai. In my mind, I thought they were merely one kind of qi; now they are distinguished as six names. I do not know the reason for this. Qibo said: When two spirits combine and interact, they form a body; it is usually generated before the physical body, and this is called jing. What is qi? Qibo said: The upper jiao spreads and distributes, conveying the flavors of the five grains; it nourishes the skin, fills the body, and moistens the hair, like mist or dew irrigating, and this is called qi. What is jin? Qibo said: When the pores and interstices release perspiration profusely, this is called jin. What is ye? Qibo said: When grains are ingested and qi is abundant, the moist and lubricating essence flows into the bones; the joints of the bones bend and stretch. This essence nourishes and supplements the brain marrow, moisturizes and glows the skin, and this is called ye. What is xue? Qibo said: The middle jiao receives qi, extracts the essence, and transforms it into redness; this is called xue. What is mai? Qibo said: Mai blocks and channels the nutritive qi, preventing it from escaping anywhere; this is called mai.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	決氣:	
黃帝曰：六氣有，有餘不足，氣之多少，腦髓之虛實，血脈之清濁，何以知之？歧伯曰：精脫者，耳聾；氣脫者，目不明；津脫者，腠理開，汗大泄；液脫者，骨屬屈伸不利，色夭，腦髓消，脛痺，耳數鳴；血脫者，色白，夭然不澤，其脈空虛，此其候也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Regarding the six types of qi, whether they are excessive or insufficient, and how much there is; regarding the emptiness or fullness of brain marrow, and the clarity or turbidity of blood and vessels—how can one know these? Qibo said: When jing is depleted, deafness occurs; when qi is depleted, the eyes become dim; When jin is depleted, pores open and profuse perspiration occurs; When ye is depleted, the joints of bones become stiff and movement difficult, complexion becomes dull, brain marrow atrophies, shins become numb, and ears frequently ring; When xue is depleted, complexion becomes pale, the face appears dull without luster, and the pulse feels empty or weak—these are its signs.

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	決氣:	
黃帝曰：六氣者，貴賤何如？歧伯曰：六氣者，各有部主也，其貴賤善惡，可為常主，然五穀與胃為大海也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Among these six types of qi, which is more important and which less? Qibo said: The six types of qi each have their own functions and roles. Their importance, goodness or badness can be generally determined; however, the five grains and the stomach serve as the great reservoir. 

31. 腸胃 Intestines and Stomach

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	腸胃:	
黃帝問於伯高曰：余願聞六府傳谷者，腸胃之大小長短，受谷之多少奈何？伯高曰：請盡言之，谷所從出入淺深遠近長短之度：唇至齒長九分，口廣二寸半；齒以後至會厭，深三寸半，大容五合；舌重十兩，長七寸，廣二寸半；咽門重十兩，廣一寸半。至胃長一尺六寸，胃紆曲屈，伸之，長二尺六寸，大一尺五寸，徑五寸，大容三斗五升。小腸後附脊，左環回日迭積，其注于回腸者，外附於臍上。回運環十六曲，大二寸半，徑八分分之少半，長三丈三尺。回腸當臍左環，回周葉積而下，回運還反十六曲，大四寸，徑一寸寸之少半，長二丈一尺。廣腸傳脊，以受回腸，左環葉脊上下，辟大八寸，徑二寸寸之大半，長二尺八寸。腸胃所入至所出，長六丈四寸四分，回曲環反，三十二曲也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao: "I wish to learn about the six fu organs' transmission of food. What are the sizes and lengths of the intestines and stomach, and how much food do they receive?" Bo Gao said: "Please allow me to explain in full. Here is the measurement of how food enters and exits, as well as the depth, distance, length, and shortness of these passages: from the lips to the teeth is nine fen long; the mouth is two cun and five fen wide;" from the teeth backward to the epiglottis, it is three cun and five fen deep, with a capacity of five ge; the tongue weighs ten liang, is seven cun long, and two cun and five fen wide; the pharyngeal opening weighs ten liang and is one cun and five fen wide. From there to the stomach is one chi and six cun long. The stomach is tortuously curved; when stretched out, it measures two chi and six cun in length, with an outer diameter of one chi and five cun, an inner diameter of five cun, and a capacity of three dou and five sheng. The small intestine is attached behind the spine; it coils to the left, overlapping in layers like a ring. The part that drains into the large intestine lies externally above the navel. The large intestine has sixteen bends, is two cun and five fen in diameter, eight fen plus a small fraction in inner diameter, and three zhang and three chi long. The large intestine, located to the left of the navel in a ring-like shape, folds and overlaps downward. It has sixteen bends as it returns and turns back; its outer diameter is four cun, inner diameter one cun plus a small fraction, and it measures two zhang and one chi long. The rectum is attached to the spine, receiving contents from the large intestine. It coils to the left and overlaps along the spine above and below; its outer width is eight cun, inner diameter two cun plus a large fraction, and it measures two chi and eight cun long. From where food enters the intestines to where it exits, the total length is six zhang, four cun and four fen. The passage winds and turns in loops, making a total of thirty-two bends. 

32. 平人絕穀 Healthy People Deprived of Food

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	平人絕穀:	
黃帝曰：願聞人之不食，七日而死，何也？伯高曰：臣請言其故。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear an explanation of why a person who does not eat will die after seven days, what is the reason? Bo Gao said: Please allow me to explain the reason.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	平人絕穀:	
胃大一尺五寸，徑五寸，長二尺六寸，橫屈受水谷三斗五升，其中之谷，常留二斗，水一斗五升而滿，上焦泄氣，出其精微，慓悍滑疾，下焦下溉諸腸。
		The stomach is one chi five cun in width, five cun in diameter, and two chi six cun in length. It bends horizontally and can hold three dou five sheng of water and food. Normally, it retains two dou of grain and one dou five sheng of liquid to be full. The upper jiao discharges qi, extracting the refined essence; this essence is swift, agile, and slippery. The lower jiao then irrigates downward into the various intestines.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	平人絕穀:	
小腸大二寸半，徑八分分之少半，長三丈二尺，受谷二斗四升，水六升三合合之大半。
		The small intestine is two cun and a half in width, with a diameter of eight fen minus a little, and three zhang two chi in length. It can hold two dou four sheng of food and six sheng three he plus most of another he of liquid.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	平人絕穀:	
回腸大四寸，徑一寸寸之少半，長二丈一尺，受谷一斗，水七升半。
		The hui cháng (the coiled part of the large intestine) is four cun in width, one cun minus a little in diameter, and two zhang one chi in length. It holds one dou of food and seven sheng half of liquid.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	平人絕穀:	
廣腸大八寸，徑二寸寸之大半，長二尺八寸，受谷九升三合八分合之一。
		The guang cháng (the wide part of the large intestine) has a width of eight cun, a diameter of two cun plus most of another cun, and is two chi eight cun in length. It holds nine sheng three he and one eighth of a he of food.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	平人絕穀:	
腸胃之長，凡五丈八尺四寸，受水谷九斗二升一合合之大半，此腸胃所受水谷之數也。平人則不然，胃滿則腸虛，腸滿則胃虛，更虛更滿，故氣得上下，五藏安定，血脈和利，精神乃居，故神者，水谷之精氣也。故腸胃之中，當留谷二斗，水一斗五升；故平人日再後，後二升半，一日中五升，七日五七三斗五升，而留水谷盡矣；故平人不食飲七日而死者，小谷精氣津液皆盡故也。
		The total length of the intestines and stomach is five zhang eight chi four cun. They can hold nine dou two sheng one he plus most of another he of water and food; this is the amount of water and food that the intestines and stomach can receive. In a healthy person, it is not like this: when the stomach is full, the intestines are empty; when the intestines are full, the stomach is empty. They alternate between emptiness and fullness, so that qi can move upward and downward smoothly. The five zang organs remain stable, blood vessels harmonize and function properly, and spirit resides within. Therefore, spirit is the refined essence of water and food. Therefore, in the intestines and stomach, two dou of grain and one dou and five sheng of liquid should remain; thus, in a healthy person, there are two bowel movements per day, each expelling two sheng and half of liquid; over the course of one day, this amounts to five sheng. Over seven days, it totals five times seven, which is three dou and five sheng, and all retained water and food will be completely expelled; Therefore, a healthy person who does not eat or drink for seven days dies because all the refined essence, body fluids, and moisture from food have been exhausted. 

33. 海論 Treatise on the Seas

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	海論:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：余聞刺法於夫子，夫子之所言，不離於營衛血氣。夫十二經脈者，內屬於府藏，外絡於肢節，夫子乃合之於四海乎。歧伯答曰：人亦有四海，十二經水。經水者，皆注于海，海有東西南北，命曰四海。黃帝曰：以人應之奈何？歧伯曰：人有髓海，有血海，有氣海，有水谷之海，凡此四者，以應四海也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qu Bo, "I have heard from you about acupuncture methods; what you said never departs from the nutritive and defensive qi and blood." The twelve meridians internally connect to the zang-fu organs, externally linking to the limbs and joints. Have you combined them with the four seas? Qu Bo replied, "Human beings also have four seas and twelve meridian waters." The meridian waters all flow into the seas; there are eastern, western, southern, and northern seas, which are called the four seas. The Yellow Emperor said, "How do human beings correspond to them?" Qu Bo said, "Human beings have a marrow sea, a blood sea, a qi sea, and a food-water sea. These four correspond to the four seas."

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	海論:	
黃帝曰：遠乎哉，夫子之合人天地四海也，願聞應之奈何？歧伯答曰：必先明知陰陽表裏滎輸所在，四海定矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How profound is your integration of human beings with heaven and earth's four seas! I would like to hear how they correspond." Qu Bo replied, "One must first clearly know the locations of yin and yang, exterior-interior,荥 (Xing) points, and 输 (Shu) points; then the four seas will be determined."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	海論:	
黃帝曰：定之奈何？歧伯曰：胃者水穀之海，其輸上在氣街，下至三里；衝脈者，為十二經之海，其輸上在於大杼，下出於巨虛之上下廉；膻中者，為氣之海，其輸上在於柱骨之上下，前在於人迎，腦為髓之海，其輸上在於其蓋，下在風府。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How are they determined?" Qu Bo said, "The stomach is the food-water sea; its channels ascend to Qi Jie and descend to San Li." The Chong Vessel is the sea of the twelve meridians; its channels ascend to Da Zhu and descend from Shang Xia Lian at Ju Xu. The Shanzhong is the qi sea; its channels are located above and below the sternum, anteriorly at Renying. The brain is the marrow sea; its channels extend upward to Qigai and downward to Fengfu.

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	海論:	
黃帝曰：凡此四海者，何利何害？何生何敗？歧伯曰：得順者生，得逆者敗；知調者利，不知調者害。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "In general, regarding these four seas, what are their benefits and harms?" "What brings life and what causes failure?" Qu Bo said, "Those who follow the correct flow will live; those who go against it will fail." "Those who know how to regulate them benefit; those who do not know how to regulate them suffer harm."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	海論:	
黃帝曰：四海之逆順奈何？歧伯曰：氣海有餘者，氣滿胸中，悗息面赤；氣海不足，則氣少不足以言。血海有餘，則常想其身大，怫然不知其所病；血海不足，亦常想其身小，狹然不知其所病。水谷之海有餘，則腹滿；水谷之海不足，則飢不受谷食。髓海有餘，則輕勁多力，自過其度；髓海不足，則腦轉耳鳴，脛痠眩冒，目無所見，懈怠安臥。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "What are the inverse and correct flows of the four seas?" Qu Bo said, "When the qi sea is excessive, the chest becomes full with qi; there will be oppression of breath and a red face;" "When the qi sea is insufficient, the qi is weak to the point that one cannot speak properly." "If the blood sea is excessive, a person often feels as if their body has grown larger, becoming irritable and unaware of what illness they have;" "if the blood sea is insufficient, one also often feels as if their body is small, feeling constricted and unaware of what illness they are suffering from." "If the food-water sea is excessive, there will be abdominal fullness;" "if the food-water sea is insufficient, one will feel hungry but cannot accept food." "If the marrow sea is excessive, a person becomes light and strong with great strength, surpassing their normal capacity;" "if the marrow sea is insufficient, there will be dizziness and ringing in the ears, cramping of the shins, vertigo, blurred vision, and a lack of energy leading to lying down and being inactive."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	海論:	
黃帝曰：余已聞逆順，調之奈何？歧伯曰：審守其俞，而調其虛實，無犯其害，順者得復，逆者必敗。黃帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I have already heard about the inverse and correct conditions. How should they be regulated?" Qu Bo said, "Carefully observe and regulate their 俞 (shu) points to adjust the deficiency and excess; do not violate what is harmful. Those who follow this will recover, while those who go against it are bound to fail." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." 

34. 五亂 Five Disorders

1
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五亂:	
黃帝曰：經脈十二者，別為五行，分為四時，何失而亂？何得而治？歧伯曰：五行有序，四時有分，相順則治，相逆則亂。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: The twelve meridians are classified into five elements and divided according to the four seasons; what causes them to become disordered? What brings about their regulation and harmony? Qibo said: The five elements follow an order, the four seasons have their divisions; when they are in harmony with each other, regulation and health result; when they oppose one another, disorder arises.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五亂:	
黃帝曰：何謂相順？歧伯曰：經脈十二者，以應十二月。十二月者，分為四時。四時者，春秋冬夏，其氣各異，營衛相隨，陰陽已知，清濁不相干，如是則順之而治。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is meant by being in harmony? Qibo said: The twelve meridians correspond to the twelve months. The twelve months are divided into four seasons. The four seasons—spring, summer, autumn, and winter—each have distinct qi. The nutritive and defensive energies follow each other accordingly; yin and yang are thus understood. When the pure and turbid do not interfere with one another in this way, harmony is achieved and regulation follows.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五亂:	
黃帝曰：何為逆而亂，歧伯曰：清氣在陰，濁氣在陽，營氣順脈，衛氣逆行，清濁相干，亂於胸中，是謂大悗。故氣亂於心，則煩心密嘿，俛首靜伏；亂於肺，則俛仰喘喝，接手以呼；亂於腸胃，是為霍亂；亂於臂脛，則為四厥；亂於頭，則為厥逆，頭重眩仆。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What constitutes opposition and disorder? Qibo said: When the pure qi resides in yin, and the turbid qi resides in yang; when nutritive qi flows with the meridians but defensive qi moves against them, causing interference between the pure and turbid, confusion arises in the chest. This is called great 郁闷 (yùn mèn). Therefore, when qi becomes disordered in the heart, it causes mental distress and silence; one bows their head and remains still. When disorder occurs in the lungs, one experiences labored breathing and gasping, raising the hands to breathe heavily. Disorder in the intestines and stomach results in huo luan (cholera). When disorder occurs in the arms and legs, it is known as si jue (tetanus or limb coldness). Disorder in the head leads to jue ni (reversal of qi), causing heaviness, dizziness, and fainting.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五亂:	
黃帝曰：五亂者，刺之有道乎？歧伯曰：有道以來，有道以去，審知其道，是謂身寶。黃帝曰：善。願聞其道。歧伯曰：氣在於心者，取之手少陰心主之俞；氣在於肺者，取之手太陰滎，足少陰俞，氣在於腸胃者，取之足太陰陽明，不下者，取之三里，氣在於頭者，取之天柱大杼，不知，取足太陽滎俞；氣在於臂足，取之先去血脈，後取其陽明少陽之滎輸。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: For these five disorders, is there a method for acupuncture treatment? Qibo said: There are methods to bring about regulation and methods to eliminate disorder. Understanding these principles thoroughly is called preserving one's body as a treasure. The Yellow Emperor said: Well said. I would like to hear about these principles. Qibo said: When qi is disordered in the heart, acupuncture should be applied at the shu points of the Hand Shaoyin Heart Meridian. When qi is disordered in the lungs, acupuncture should be applied at Xing points of the Hand Taiyin Meridian and shu points of the Foot Shaoyin Meridian. When qi is disordered in the intestines and stomach, take points on the Foot Taiyin and Yangming meridians; if there is no improvement, take Zusanli (ST36). When qi is disordered in the head, take Tianzhu and Dazhu; if there is still no response, take Xing and shu points of the Bladder Meridian. When qi is disordered in the arms or legs, first remove blood stasis by bleeding therapy, then take Xing and shu (transport) points of the Yangming and Shaoyang meridians.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五亂:	
黃帝曰：補寫奈何？歧伯曰：徐入徐出，謂之導氣。補寫無形，謂之同精。是非有餘不足也，亂氣之相逆也。黃帝曰：允乎哉道，明乎哉論，請著之玉版，命曰治亂也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should tonification and dispersion be applied? Qibo said: Slow insertion and slow withdrawal is called guiding the qi. Tonification and dispersion without visible form are referred to as unifying essence. This is not about excess or deficiency, but rather the reversal of disordered qi. The Yellow Emperor said: How profound is this principle! How clear is this discussion! I shall inscribe it on jade tablets, and name it "Regulating Disorders." 

35. 脹論 Zhang Lun: On Swelling

1
打開字典
顯示影印本
	脹論:	
黃帝曰：脈之應于寸口，如何而脹？歧伯曰：其脈大堅以濇者，脹也。黃帝曰：何以知藏府之脹也。歧伯曰：陰為藏，陽為府。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How does the pulse at the cunkou correspond to swelling?" Qibo said: "A pulse that is large, firm, and 涩 (sè) indicates swelling." The Yellow Emperor asked: "How can one know whether the swelling is in the zang or fu organs?" Qibo said: "Yin corresponds to the zang, and Yang corresponds to the fu."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脹論:	
黃帝曰：夫氣之令人脹也，在于血脈之中耶，藏府之內乎？歧伯曰：三者皆存焉，然非脹之舍也。黃帝曰：願聞脹之舍。歧伯曰：夫脹者，皆在於藏府之外，排藏府而郭胸脅，脹皮膚，故命曰脹。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When qi causes swelling, does it occur within the blood vessels or inside the zang and fu organs?" Qibo said: "All three are present, but they are not the dwelling place of swelling." The Yellow Emperor said: "I would like to hear about the dwelling place of swelling." Qibo said: "Swelling occurs outside the zang and fu organs, spreading around them and encircling the chest and flanks, causing swelling of the skin. Hence it is called 'zhang' (swelling)."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脹論:	
黃帝曰：藏府之在胸脅腹里之內也，若匣匱之藏禁器也，名有次舍，異名而同處，一域之中，其氣各異，願聞其故。黃帝曰：未解其意，再問。歧伯曰：夫胸腹，藏府之郭也。膻中者，心主之宮城也；胃者，太倉也；咽喉、小腸者，傳送也；胃之五竅者，閭里門戶也；廉泉、玉英者，津液之道也。故五藏六府者，各有畔界，其病各有形狀。營氣循脈，衛氣逆為脈脹；衛氣並脈循分為膚脹。三里而寫，近者一下，遠者三下，無問虛實，工在疾寫。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "The zang and fu organs are located within the chest, flanks, and abdomen. They are like forbidden vessels stored in a box or cabinet. Though they have different names and ranks, they occupy the same space. Within one region, their qi is different. I would like to hear the reason for this." The Yellow Emperor said: "I have not yet understood your meaning. Please explain again." Qibo said: "The chest and abdomen are the outer enclosures of the zang and fu organs." Shanzhong is the palace city of the heart, the sovereign organ. The stomach is the Tai Cang (Great Granary). The throat and pharynx, and the small intestine are the channels for transmission. The five orifices of the stomach are like the gates and passages of a neighborhood. Lianquan and Yuying are the pathways for body fluids. Therefore, the five zang organs and six fu organs each have their own boundaries, and their diseases manifest in distinct forms. The nutritive qi follows the vessels, while the defensive qi moving against its normal direction causes vessel swelling; When the defensive qi flows alongside the vessels and spreads outward, it results in skin swelling. Drain at Sanli; for nearby conditions, one needle insertion suffices; for distant ones, three insertions are needed. Regardless of deficiency or excess, the skill lies in rapid drainage.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脹論:	
黃帝曰：願聞脹形。歧伯曰：夫心脹者煩心短氣，臥不安；肺脹者，虛滿而喘咳；肝脹者，脅下滿而痛引小腹；脾脹者，善穢，四肢煩俛，體重不能勝衣，臥不安；腎脹者，腹滿引背央央然，腰髀痛。六府脹，胃脹者，腹滿，胃脘痛，鼻聞焦臭，妨於食，大便難；大腸脹者，腸鳴而痛濯濯，冬日重感於寒，則餐泄不化；小腸脹者，少腹慎脹，引腰而痛；膀胱脹者，少腹滿而氣癃；三焦脹者，氣滿於皮膚中，輕輕然而不堅；膽脹者，脅下痛脹，口中苦，善太息。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I would like to hear about how swelling manifests." Qibo said: "When the heart is affected by swelling, it causes mental distress, shortness of breath, and restless sleep; Lung swelling causes emptiness and fullness in the chest with wheezing and coughing; Liver swelling causes fullness and pain beneath the flanks, radiating to the lower abdomen; Spleen swelling causes a tendency to feel unclean, restlessness in the limbs, heaviness of the body making it difficult to bear clothing, and restless sleep; Kidney swelling causes abdominal fullness that radiates to the back with a sensation of pressure, along with pain in the waist and hips. Swelling of the six fu organs: when the stomach is affected, it causes abdominal fullness, pain in the epigastric region, a smell of scorched odor from the nose, difficulty eating, and hard stools; Large intestine swelling causes gurgling sounds in the intestines with pain and a sensation of dripping. If exposed to cold during winter, it results in undigested diarrhea; Small intestine swelling causes distension beneath the navel with pain radiating to the waist; Bladder swelling causes fullness in the lower abdomen and urinary retention; Sanjiao (Triple Burner) swelling causes qi to accumulate within the skin, resulting in a sensation of mild fullness without firmness; Gallbladder swelling causes pain and distension beneath the flanks, bitterness in the mouth, and frequent sighing.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脹論:	
凡此諸脹者，其道在一，明知逆順，鍼數不失，寫虛補實，神去其室，致邪失正，真不可定，麤之所敗，謂之天命；補虛寫實，神歸其室，久塞其空，謂之良工。
		All these various swellings follow one principle. If the practitioner clearly understands the order of deficiency and excess, does not err in acupuncture techniques, drains where there is excess and tonifies where there is deficiency, then pathogenic influences will leave their dwelling place, while righteous qi is displaced by evil qi. When true harmony cannot be restored, and coarse treatment fails, it is called "tianming" (fate or heavenly decree); Tonifying deficiency and draining excess restores the spirit to its proper dwelling, sealing the void for a long time—this is called an excellent physician.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脹論:	
黃帝曰：脹者焉生？何因而有？歧伯曰：衛氣之在身也，常然並脈，循分肉，行有逆順，陰陽相隨，乃得天和，五藏更始，四時循序，五穀乃化。然後厥氣在下，營衛留止，寒氣逆上，真邪相攻，兩氣相搏，乃合為脹也。黃帝曰：善。何以解惑？歧伯曰：合之於真，三合而得。帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "From where does swelling arise?" What is the cause of its occurrence? Qibo said: "The defensive qi in the body normally flows alongside the vessels, spreading through the muscle layers. It moves according to a proper order, with yin and yang following each other, thus achieving harmony with heaven. The five zang organs renew themselves cyclically, the four seasons proceed in sequence, and the five grains are transformed." Only then does rebellious qi accumulate below, the nutritive and defensive qi stagnate, cold qi rises upward, true and pathogenic influences attack each other, and when these two types of qi clash, they combine to form swelling. The Yellow Emperor said: "Well said." How can one resolve doubts? Qibo said: "Combine it with the true principles; understanding arises through three harmonious unifications." The Emperor said: "Well said."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	脹論:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：脹論言無問虛實，工在疾寫，近者一下，遠者三下，今有其三而不下者，其過焉在？歧伯對曰：此言陷於肉肓，而中氣穴者也。不中氣穴，則氣內閉，鍼不陷肓，則氣不行，上越中肉，則衛氣相亂，陰陽相逐。其於脹也，當寫不寫，氣故不下，三而不下，必更其道，氣下乃止，不下復始，可以萬全，烏有殆者乎？其於脹也，必審其彌，當寫則寫，當補則補，如鼓應桴，惡有不下者乎？
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qibo: "According to the theory of swelling, one should not inquire about deficiency or excess; the skill lies in rapid draining. For nearby conditions, one insertion suffices, and for distant ones, three insertions. But now there are cases where three insertions have been performed yet no improvement is seen—where does the fault lie?" Qibo replied: "This refers to cases where the needle has become embedded in the flesh and fat, failing to reach the central qi points." If the needle does not reach the qi points, then the internal qi becomes blocked. If the needle fails to penetrate the fat layer, the qi will not circulate; if it merely glides over the muscle surface, the defensive qi becomes disordered and yin and yang chase each other in confusion. Regarding swelling, if one should drain but does not do so, the qi remains stagnant and thus does not descend. If three insertions fail to bring improvement, one must change the method; only when the qi descends is treatment complete. If it still does not descend, begin again. With this approach, perfect results can be achieved—how could there ever be danger? Regarding swelling, one must carefully examine the condition. When draining is needed, drain; when tonifying is required, tonify. Just as a drum responds to a mallet, how could there be any case where the swelling does not subside? 

36. 五癃津液別 Five Obstructions and Differentiation of Body Fluids

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：水穀入于口，輸於腸胃，其液別為五，天寒衣薄，則為溺與氣，天熱衣厚則為汗，悲哀氣並則為泣，中熱胃緩則為唾。邪氣內逆，則氣為之閉塞而不行，不行則為水脹，余知其然也，不知其何由生？願聞其道。
		The Yellow Emperor asked 岐伯: "Grains and water enter the mouth, are transported to the intestines and stomach. Their fluids differentiate into five types. When it is cold in the sky and clothing is thin, they become urine and gas; when it is hot in the sky and clothing thick, they become sweat. Sorrow and grief cause qi to congeal, resulting in tears. Internal heat and a relaxed stomach result in saliva." When pathogenic qi invades inward, it causes the normal qi to become obstructed and cease flowing. When the flow ceases, this results in water retention and swelling. I know that this is so, but I do not understand from where it originates? I wish to hear its principle.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
歧伯曰：水穀皆入于口，其味有五，各注其海。津液各走其道，故三焦出氣，以溫肌肉，充皮膚，為其津，其流而不行者為液。
		Qi Bo said: "Water and grains all enter through the mouth, their flavors are five in number, each flowing to its respective 'sea.'" Body fluids and essences each follow their own pathways. Therefore, the triple heater emits qi to warm the muscles, fill the skin, serving as its moisture; that which flows but does not circulate is called essence-fluid (ye).

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
天暑衣厚則腠理開，故汗出，寒留於分肉之間，聚沫則為痛。
		When the weather is hot and clothing thick, the pores open, so sweat emerges. Cold lingers between the muscle layers; when phlegm accumulates, it causes pain.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
天寒則腠理閉，氣濕不行，水下留於膀胱，則為溺與氣。
		When the weather is cold, the pores close, and dampness-qi does not circulate; water remains below in the bladder, thus becoming urine and gas.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
五藏六府，心為之主，耳為之聽，目為之候，肺為之相，肝為之將，脾為之衛，腎為之主外。故五藏六府之津液，盡上滲於目，心悲氣並，則心系急。心系急則肺舉，肺舉則液上溢。夫心系與肺，不能常舉，乍上乍下，故欬而泣出矣。
		The five zang and six fu organs: the heart is their ruler, the ear serves as listener, the eye acts as observer, the lung functions as minister, the liver serves as general, the spleen acts as guard, and the kidney oversees external affairs. Therefore, the body fluids of the five zang and six fu all ascend to seep into the eyes. When the heart feels sorrow and qi converges, the heart's connecting vessels become tense. When the heart's connecting vessels are taut, the lungs rise upward; when the lungs rise, body fluids overflow upward. The heart's connecting vessels and the lungs cannot remain constantly elevated but fluctuate up and down intermittently, hence coughing and tears occur.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
中熱則胃中消穀，消穀則蟲上下作。腸胃充郭，故胃緩，胃緩則氣逆，故唾出。
		Internal heat causes the stomach to consume grains; when grains are consumed, parasites move upward and downward. When the intestines and stomach become distended, the stomach becomes relaxed. When the stomach is relaxed, qi rebels upward, hence saliva emerges.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
五穀之津液，和合而為膏者，內滲入于骨空，補益腦髓，而下流於陰股。
		The body fluids derived from the five grains, when harmonized and transformed into marrow, seep inward into the bone cavities to nourish the brain and spinal marrow, then flow downward to the lower abdomen and thighs.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
陰陽不和，則使液溢而下流於陰，髓液皆減而下，下過度則虛，虛，故腰背痛而脛痠。
		When yin and yang are disharmonious, body fluids overflow and flow downward to the lower parts; marrow and fluids all decrease and descend. When this descent is excessive, it results in deficiency. This deficiency causes back and waist pain as well as cramping in the shins.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五癃津液... :	
陰陽氣道不通，四海閉塞，三焦不寫，津液不化，水穀並行腸胃之中，別於回腸，留於下焦，不得滲膀胱，則下焦脹，水溢則為水脹，此津液五別之逆順也。
		When the yin-yang qi pathways are not unobstructed, the four seas become blocked and the triple heater fails to discharge. Body fluids do not transform; water and grains flow together within the intestines and stomach, separate in the descending colon, remain in the lower jiao, and cannot seep into the bladder. Thus, the lower jiao becomes distended; when water overflows, it results in fluid retention and swelling—this is the inverse or normal course of the five differentiations of body fluids. 

37. 五閱五使 Five Observations and Five Messengers

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五閱五使:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：余聞刺有五官五閱，以觀五氣。五氣者，五藏之使也，五時之副也。願聞其五使當安出？歧伯曰：五官者，五藏之閱也。黃帝曰：願聞其所出，令可為常。歧伯曰：脈出於氣口，色見於明堂，五色更出，以應五時，各如其常，經氣入藏，必當治理。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "I have heard that acupuncture involves five organs and five observations to examine the Five Qis." The Five Qis are the messengers of the Five Zang, and the deputies of the Five Seasons. I would like to hear where these five messengers should emerge from. Qi Bo said, "The Five Officials are the observers of the Five Zang." The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to hear about their locations so that they may be established as a constant standard." Qi Bo said, "The pulse emerges from the Qi Kou; complexion appears in the Mingtang. The five colors alternate and appear to correspond with the Five Seasons, each according to its usual pattern. When the meridian qi enters the Zang, it must be properly regulated."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五閱五使:	
帝曰：善。五色獨決於明堂乎？歧伯曰：五官已辨，闕庭必張，乃立明堂，明堂廣大，蕃蔽見外，方壁高基，引垂居外，五色乃治，平搏廣大，壽中百歲，見此者，刺之必已，如是之人者，血氣有餘，肌肉堅致，故可苦以鍼。
		The Emperor said, "Good." Are the five colors determined solely by the Mingtang? Qi Bo said, "Once the Five Officials have been identified, the Que Ting must be open and clear. Then the Mingtang is established. When the Mingtang is broad and spacious, its outer boundaries are clearly defined; when the walls on all sides are high and solid, and the lower parts extend outward, then the five colors will appear in order. The complexion should be even, full, and expansive—such a person may live to one hundred years of age. When such features are observed, acupuncture treatment is certain to succeed. In people like this, their blood and qi are abundant, and their muscles are firm and dense; therefore, they can endure needle therapy."

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五閱五使:	
黃帝曰：願聞五官。歧伯曰：鼻者，肺之官也；目者，肝之官也；口唇者，脾之官也；舌者，心之官也；耳者，腎之官也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to hear about the Five Officials." Qi Bo said, "The nose is the official of the lung; the eyes are the officials of the liver;" "the mouth and lips are the official of the spleen; 藟 the tongue is the official of the heart; the ears are the officials of the kidney."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五閱五使:	
黃帝曰：以官何候？歧伯曰：以候五藏。故肺病者，喘息鼻張；肝病者，眥青；脾病者，唇黃；心病者，舌捲短，顴赤；腎病者，顴與顏黑。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What signs should be observed in these officials?" Qi Bo said, "To observe the Five Zang." Therefore, those with lung disease will have labored breathing and flaring nostrils; those with liver disease will have blue sclerae; those with spleen disease will have yellow lips; those with heart disease will have a curled and shortened tongue, and red cheeks; those with kidney disease will have blackness on the cheeks and forehead.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五閱五使:	
黃帝曰：五脈安出，五色安見，其常色殆者如何？歧伯曰：五官不辨，闕庭不張，小其明堂，蕃蔽不見，又埤其牆，牆下無基，垂角去外。如是者，雖平常殆，況加疾哉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "From where do the Five Pulses emerge? Where are the Five Colors observed? And what is their normal appearance?" Qi Bo said, "If the Five Officials cannot be distinguished and the Que Ting is not open and clear, then the Mingtang will appear small. The outer boundaries will not be visible; the walls on all sides will be low, with no foundation beneath them, and the corners will recede inward." In such cases, even a normal complexion appears ominous; how much more so when disease is added?

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五閱五使:	
黃帝曰：五色之見於明堂，以觀五藏之氣，左右高下，各有形乎？歧伯曰：藏府之在中也，各以次舍，左右上下，各如其度也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "Do the Five Colors appearing in the Mingtang to observe the qi of the Five Zang have specific forms according to their positions on the left and right, high or low?" Qi Bo said, "The Zang and Fu located in the body each have their respective positions; on the left, right, above, and below, they are arranged according to their proper measurements." 

38. 逆順肥瘦 Normal and Abnormal Flow: Fat and Thin Body Types

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：余聞鍼道于夫子，眾多畢悉矣。夫子之道，應若失，而據未有堅然者也。夫子之問學熟乎，將審察于物而心生之乎？歧伯曰：聖人之為道者，上合于天，下合于地，中合于人事，必有明法，以起度數，法式檢押，乃後可傳焉。故匠人不能釋尺寸而意短長，廢繩墨而起平水也，工人不能置規而為圓，去矩而為方。知用此者，固自然之物，易用之教，逆順之常也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qu Bo, "I have learned acupuncture techniques from you, and I now understand them thoroughly." Your teachings are as elusive as a shadow, yet there is nothing firm or certain to rely upon. Are your inquiries and studies thorough, or do you examine things carefully and then generate understanding from within? Qu Bo said, "The sages who practice the Way harmonize above with Heaven, below with Earth, and in between with human affairs. There must be clear principles to establish measurements and calculations; only when there are established standards and regulations can it then be transmitted." Therefore, a craftsman cannot abandon measurements of inches and feet to guess lengths or discard the plumb line and level to establish horizontality; a worker cannot set aside the compass to draw circles or remove the square to make squares. Knowing how to use these principles means understanding natural things, easily applying teachings, and recognizing the usual patterns of order and disorder.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：願聞自然奈何？歧伯曰：臨深決水，不用功力，而水可竭也。循拙決沖，而經可通也。此言氣之滑澀，血水清濁，行之逆順也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to hear what you mean by 'natural'." Qu Bo said, "When standing at the edge of a deep body of water and deciding to drain it, one does not need to exert effort or labor; yet the water can be drained." Following this method to unblock obstructions, the meridians can then become unimpeded. This refers to the smoothness or stickiness of qi, the clarity or turbidity of blood and fluids, and their directional flow—either in harmony or disorder.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：願聞人之白黑肥瘦小長，各有數乎？歧伯曰：年質壯大，血氣充盈，膚革堅固，因加以邪，刺此者，深而留之，此肥人也。廣肩腋項，肉薄厚皮而黑色，唇臨臨然，其血黑以濁，其氣澀以遲。其為人也，貪于取與，刺此者，深而留之，多益其數也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to know whether people's skin color, whether fair or dark, and their body types—whether fat, thin, short, or tall—are each determined by specific measurements?" Qu Bo said, "When a person is of strong constitution and vigorous age, with abundant qi and blood and firm skin and muscles, if pathogenic factors are added to such a body, acupuncture treatment should be deep and prolonged. This describes a fat person." They have broad shoulders, armpits, and necks; thick flesh and dark skin; their lips appear full and prominent. Their blood is dark and turbid, and their qi is sticky and sluggish. Such people are greedy in taking and giving; when treating them with acupuncture, the needling should be deep and prolonged, and more needles or longer retention times may be added.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：刺瘦人奈何？歧伯曰：瘦人者，皮薄色少，肉廉廉然，薄唇輕言，其血清氣滑，易脫于氣，易損于血，刺此者，淺而疾之。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How should one treat a thin person?" Qu Bo said, "Thin people have thin skin and pale complexions; their flesh is meager and sparse. They have thin lips and speak softly. Their blood is clear and their qi smooth, making them prone to losing qi easily and suffering blood damage readily. When treating such individuals with acupuncture, the insertion should be shallow and swift."

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：刺常人奈何？歧伯曰：視其白黑，各為調之，其端正敦厚者，其血氣和調，刺此者，無失常數也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How should one treat an ordinary person?" Qu Bo said, "Observe their complexion, whether fair or dark; adjust the treatment accordingly. Those who are upright and of solid build have harmonious blood and qi. When treating such people with acupuncture, do not deviate from standard procedures."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：刺壯士真骨者，奈何？歧伯曰：刺壯士真骨，堅肉緩節，監監然，此人重則氣澀血濁，刺此者，深而留之，多益其數；勁則氣滑血清，刺此者，淺而疾之。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How should one treat strong men with solid bones?" Qu Bo said, "When treating strong men with solid bones and firm muscles but loose joints, who appear broad-shouldered and sturdy, their qi tends to be sticky and their blood turbid. When needling such individuals, the insertion should be deep and prolonged; more needles or longer retention may also be added." If they are vigorous with smooth qi and clear blood, acupuncture treatment should involve shallow insertion and swift needling.

7
打開字典
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：刺嬰兒奈何？歧伯曰：嬰兒者，其肉脆，血少氣弱，刺此者，以豪刺，淺刺而疾拔鍼，日再可也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How should one treat infants?" Qu Bo said, "Infants have tender flesh and insufficient blood with weak qi. When needling them, use fine needles; insert shallowly and remove the needle quickly. Needling twice a day is sufficient."

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：臨深決水，奈何？歧伯曰：血清氣濁，疾寫之則氣竭焉。黃帝曰：循拙決沖，奈何？歧伯曰：血濁氣澀，疾寫之，則經可通也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "What does it mean to 'stand at the edge of deep water and decide to drain it'?" Qu Bo said, "When blood is clear but qi is turbid, rapid purging will exhaust the qi." The Yellow Emperor said, "What does it mean to 'follow this method to unblock obstructions'?" Qu Bo said, "When blood is turbid and qi is sluggish, rapid purging can clear the meridians and restore their flow."

9
打開字典
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：脈行之逆順，奈何？歧伯曰：手之三陰，從藏走手；手之三陽，從手走頭；足之三陽，從頭走足；足之三陰，從足走腹。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "What determines the normal or abnormal direction of blood vessel movement?" Qu Bo said, "The three yin meridians of the hand run from the internal organs to the hands; " the three yang meridians of the hand travel from the hands to the head; the three yang meridians of the foot run from the head down to the feet; and the three yin meridians of the foot travel from the feet up to the abdomen.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順肥瘦:	
黃帝曰：少陰之脈獨下行，何也？歧伯曰：不然，夫沖脈者，五藏六府之海也，五藏六府皆稟焉。其上者，出于頏顙，滲諸陽，灌諸精；其下者，注少陰之大絡，出于氣街，循陰股內廉入膕中，伏行骭骨內，下至內踝之後屬而別。其下者，並于少陰之經，滲三陰；其前者，伏行出跗屬，下循跗，入大指間，滲諸絡而溫肌肉。故別絡結則附上不動，不動則厥，厥則寒矣。黃帝曰：何以明之？歧伯曰：以言導之，切而驗之，其非必動，然後仍可明逆順之行也。黃帝曰：窘乎哉！聖人之為道也。明于日月，微于毫釐，其非夫子，孰能道之也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Why does the Shaoyin meridian alone run downward?" Qu Bo said, "Not so. The Chong pulse is the sea of the five zang and six fu organs; all the five zang and six fu receive nourishment from it." Its upper branch emerges at the occipital region of the head, permeates all yang meridians, and nourishes the refined substances. Its lower branch flows into the major collateral of Shaoyin, emerges at Qijie (CV12), follows the inner medial side of the thigh to enter the popliteal region, travels along the inner side of the tibia bone, and descends to behind the medial malleolus where it branches off. Its lower branch runs parallel with the Shaoyin meridian and permeates the three yin meridians; its anterior branch travels along the foot, emerging from the dorsum of the foot and running downward to follow the instep into between the great toe, permeating the collateral vessels and warming the muscles. Therefore, if these collateral branches become obstructed, there will be no movement in the area above the ankle; lack of movement leads to reversal (jue), and reversal results in coldness. The Yellow Emperor said, "How can this be demonstrated?" Qu Bo said, "By using verbal guidance and palpation to verify it. If the obstruction is not resolved, there will certainly be movement; only then can we clearly understand the direction of normal or abnormal flow." The Yellow Emperor said, "How intricate and complex it all is!" The Way practiced by the sages. Is clear as the sun and moon, subtle as a hair's breadth. If not for you, Master, who else could explain it? 

39. 血絡論 Treatise on Blood Vessels (Xue Luo Lun)

1
打開字典
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
黃帝曰：願聞其奇邪而不在經者。歧伯曰：血絡是也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about those extraordinary phenomena that do not appear in the classics. Qibo said: These are the blood vessels.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
黃帝曰：刺血絡而仆者，何也？血出而射者，何也？血少黑而濁者，何也？血出清而半為汁者，何也？拔鍼而腫者，何也？血出若多若少而面色蒼蒼者，何也？拔鍼而面色不變而煩悗者，何也？多出血而不動搖者，何也？願聞其故。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Why do some people faint after needling the blood vessels? Why does the blood spurt out when it is let? Why is the blood scanty, dark and turbid? Why does the blood that comes out appear clear and half like fluid? Why is there swelling after removing the needle? Why does a person's complexion become pale whether the blood let is in large or small quantity? Why, after removing the needle, does one's complexion remain unchanged but feel restless and troubled? Why is there no dizziness or instability even though a large amount of blood has been let? I would like to hear the reason for these phenomena.

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
歧伯曰：脈氣盛而血虛者，刺之則脫氣，脫氣則仆。
		Qibo said: When the pulse energy is abundant but the blood is deficient, needling it will cause the vital energy to escape; when the vital energy escapes, one faints.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
血氣俱盛而陰氣多者，其血滑，刺之則射；陽氣蓄積，久留而不寫者，其血黑以濁，故不能射。
		When both the blood and qi are abundant but yin is excessive, the blood flows smoothly; needling it causes the blood to spurt. When yang energy accumulates and remains stagnant for a long time without being released, the blood becomes dark and turbid; therefore, it does not spurt.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
新飲而液滲于絡，而未合和于血也，故血出而汁別焉；其不新飲者，身中有水，久則為腫。
		When one has recently consumed fluids and the liquid has seeped into the vessels but has not yet blended with the blood, hence when the blood is let, it separates from the fluid; Those who have not recently consumed fluids already have water in their bodies, and over time this causes swelling.

6
打開字典
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
陰氣積于陽，其氣因于絡，故刺之血未出而氣先行，故腫。
		When yin energy accumulates within yang, the qi follows along the vessels; therefore, when needled, the blood has not yet been let but the qi moves first, hence causing swelling.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
陰陽之氣，其新相得而未和合，因而寫之，則陰陽俱脫，表裏相離，故脫色而蒼蒼然。
		When the yin and yang energies have just met but are not yet harmonized, and blood-letting is performed at this time, both yin and yang will be depleted; the exterior and interior separate, hence causing a loss of color and a pale appearance.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
刺之血出多，色不變而煩悗者，刺絡而虛經，虛經之屬于陰者，陰脫，故煩悗。
		When needling causes a large amount of blood to be let but the complexion remains unchanged and restlessness occurs, it is because the vessels were needled while the meridians became deficient. When the deficiency affects those meridians associated with yin, yin depletion results, hence causing restlessness and unease.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
陰陽相得而合為痺者，此為內溢于經，外注于絡。如是者，陰陽俱有餘，雖多出血而弗能虛也。
		When yin and yang harmonize but combine to form bi syndrome (bi being obstruction or blockage), this is because the energy overflows internally into the meridians and externally into the vessels. In such cases, both yin and yang are in excess; therefore, even though a large amount of blood is let, the body does not become deficient.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
黃帝曰：相之奈何？歧伯曰：血脈者，盛堅橫以赤，上下無常處，小者如鍼，大者如筋，則而寫之萬全也，故無失數矣。失數而反，各如其度。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How can one observe these conditions? Qibo said: Blood vessels that are full, firm, swollen and red, with no fixed location either above or below; the small ones are like a needle, the large ones like tendons—when such conditions are identified and blood is let accordingly, it ensures complete safety. Therefore, there will be no failure in the technique. If one fails to follow the proper method and acts contrary to it, each condition will manifest according to its own measure.

11
打開字典
顯示影印本
	血絡論:	
黃帝曰：鍼入而肉著者，何也？歧伯曰：熱氣因于鍼，則鍼熱，熱則內著于鍼，故堅焉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Why does the flesh stick to the needle when it is inserted? Qibo said: When heat energy follows the needle, the needle becomes hot; when it is hot, the flesh adheres to the needle internally, hence becoming firm. ф

40. 陰陽清濁 Yin Yang Clarity and Turbidity

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽清濁:	
黃帝曰：余聞十二經脈，以應十二經水者，其五色各異，清濁不同，人之血氣若一，應之奈何？歧伯曰：人之血氣，苟能若一，則天下為一矣，惡有亂者乎？黃帝曰：余問一人，非問天下之眾。歧伯曰：夫一人者，亦有亂氣，天下之象，亦有亂人，其合為一耳。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard that the twelve meridians correspond to the twelve rivers, and their five colors are different, their clarity and turbidity vary. If a person's blood and qi are uniform, how can they correspond? Qibo said: If a person's blood and qi could indeed be uniform, then the whole world would become one; how could there possibly be disorder? The Yellow Emperor said: I am asking about a single person, not about all the people in the world. Qibo said: A single person also has disordered qi; just as in the world there are disorderly people. They are essentially one and the same.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽清濁:	
黃帝曰：願聞人氣之清濁。歧伯曰：受穀者濁，受氣者清。清者注陰，濁者注陽。濁而清者，上出於咽，清而濁者，則下行。清濁相干，命曰亂氣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about the clarity and turbidity of a person's qi. Qibo said: Qi that is derived from food is turbid, while qi that is received directly is clear. Clear qi flows into the yin meridians, and turbid qi flows into the yang meridians. Turbid yet clear substances rise upward to exit through the throat; clear yet turbid substances descend downward. When clarity and turbidity interfere with each other, it is called disordered qi.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽清濁:	
黃帝曰：夫陰清而陽濁，濁者有清，清者有濁，清濁別之奈何？歧伯曰：氣之大別，清者上注于肺，濁者下走于胃。胃之清氣，上出于口；肺之濁氣，下注于經，內積于海。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Yin is clear and yang is turbid, yet the turbid contains clarity and the clear contains turbidity. How can one distinguish between clarity and turbidity? Qibo said: The great distinction of qi is that the clear part rises to the lungs, while the turbid part descends into the stomach. The clear qi from the stomach ascends and exits through the mouth; the turbid qi from the lungs descends into the meridians, accumulating internally in the sea.

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	陰陽清濁:	
黃帝曰：諸陽皆濁，何陽濁甚乎？歧伯曰：手太陽獨受陽之濁，手太陰獨受陰之清；其清者上走空竅，其濁者下行諸經。諸陰皆清，足太陰獨受其濁。
		The Yellow Emperor said: All yang is turbid; which yang is most turbid? Qibo said: The hand taiyang meridian alone receives the most turbid yang, while the hand taiyin meridian alone receives the clearest yin; the clear part ascends to the hollow orifices, and the turbid part descends through the various meridians. All yin is clear, yet the foot taiyin meridian alone bears its turbidity.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽清濁:	
黃帝曰：治之奈何？歧伯曰：清者其氣滑，濁者其氣澀，此氣之常也。故刺陰者，深而留之；刺陽者，淺而疾之；清濁相干者，以數調之也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: How should this be treated? Qibo said: Clear qi is smooth, turbid qi is rough; this is the normal state of qi. Therefore, when needling yin meridians, one should insert deeply and retain the needle for a long time; when needling yang meridians, one should pierce shallowly and withdraw quickly; for cases where clarity and turbidity interfere, regulate them with appropriate techniques. 

41. 陰陽繫日月 Yin Yang Xi Ri Yue

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽繫日... :	
黃帝曰：余聞天為陽，地為陰，日為陽，月為陰，其合之於人，奈何？歧伯曰：腰以上為天，腰以下為地，故天為陽，地為陰，故足之十二經脈，以應十二月，月生於水，故在下者為陰；手之十指，以應十日，日主火，故在上者為陽。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard that Heaven is Yang, Earth is Yin, the Sun is Yang, and the Moon is Yin. How do these correspond to human beings? Qibo said: The area above the waist corresponds to Heaven, and the area below the waist corresponds to Earth. Therefore, Heaven is Yang and Earth is Yin. Hence, the twelve meridians of the lower limbs correspond to the twelve months. Since the Moon originates from water, those located downward are Yin; the ten fingers of the hands correspond to the ten days. The Sun is associated with fire, so those located upward are Yang.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	陰陽繫日... :	
黃帝曰：合之於脈，奈何？歧伯曰：寅者，正月之生陽也，主左足之少陽；未者，六月，主右足之少陽。卯者，二月，主左足之太陽；午者，五月，主右足之太陽。辰者，三月，主左足之陽明；巳者，四月，主右足之陽明。此兩陽合於前，故曰陽明。申者，七月之生陰也，主右足之少陰；丑者，十二月，主左足之少陰；酉者，八月，主右足之太陰；子者，十一月，主左足之太陰；戌者，九月，主右足之厥陰；亥者，十月，主左足之厥陰；此兩陰交盡，故曰厥陽。
		The Yellow Emperor said: How do these principles apply to the pulse? Qibo said: Yin, which represents the initial Yang of the first month, governs the Shaoyang meridian of the left foot; Wei, representing June, governs the Shaoyang Meridian of the right foot. Mao, representing February, governs the Taiyang meridian of the left foot; Wu, representing May, governs the Taiyang Meridian of the right foot. Chen, representing March, governs the Yangming meridian of the left foot; Si, representing April, governs the Yangming Meridian of the right foot. This is where two Yang forces converge in front; hence it is called Yangming. Shen, representing the initial Yin of July, governs the Shaoyin meridian of the right foot; Chou, representing December, governs the Shaoyin Meridian of the left foot; You, representing August, governs the Taiyin meridian of the right foot; 戌者，九月，主右足之地氣； You, representing September, governs the Earth Qi of the right foot; 亥者，十月，主左足之地氣。 Hai, representing October, governs the Earth Qi of the left foot. Xu, representing September, governs the Jueyin meridian of the right foot; Hai, representing October, governs the Jueyin Meridian of the left foot; This is where two Yin forces converge completely; hence it is called Jueyin.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽繫日... :	
甲主左手之少陽；己主右手之少陽；乙主左手之太陽，戊主右手之太陽；丙主左手之陽明，丁主右手之陽明，此兩火并合，故為陽明。庚主右手之少陰，癸主左手之少陰，辛主右手之太陰，壬主左手之太陰。
		Jia governs the Shaoyang of the left hand; Ji governs the Shaoyang of the right hand; Yi governs the Taiyang of the left hand, and Wu governs the Taiyang of the right hand; Bing governs the Yangming of the left hand, and Ding governs the Yangming of the right hand. These two Fire elements combine; hence they are called Yangming. Geng governs the Shaoyin of the right hand, and Gui governs the Shaoyin of the left hand. Xin governs the Taiyin of the right hand, and Ren governs the Taiyin of the left hand.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽繫日... :	
故足之陽者，陰中之少陽也；足之陰者，陰中之太陰也。手之陽者，陽中之太陽也；手之陰者，陽中之少陰也。腰以上者為陽，腰以下者為陰。
		Therefore, the Yang in the feet is the Shaoyang within Yin; the Yin in the feet is the Taiyin within Yin. The Yang in the hands is the Taiyang within Yang; the Yin in the hands is the Shaoyin within Yang. The area above the waist is Yang, and the area below the waist is Yin.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽繫日... :	
其於五藏也，心為陽中之太陽，肺為陰中之少陰，肝為陰中之少陽，脾為陰中之至陰，腎為陰中之太陰。
		In relation to the five zang organs, the heart is Taiyang within Yang, the lungs are Shaoyin within Yin, the liver is Shaoyang within Yin, the spleen is Zhuyin (extreme Yin) within Yin, and the kidneys are Taiyin within Yin.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽繫日... :	
黃帝曰：以治之奈何？歧伯曰：正月二月三月，人氣在左，無刺左足之陽；四月五月六月，人氣在右，無刺右足之陽，七月八月九月，人氣在右，無刺右足之陰，十月十一月十二月，人氣在左，無刺左足之陰。
		The Yellow Emperor said: How should one treat these conditions? Qibo said: In the first, second, and third months, human Qi resides on the left side; therefore, one should not needle the Yang meridians of the left foot; in the fourth, fifth, and sixth months, human Qi resides on the right side; therefore, one should not needl e the Yang meridians of the right foot. In the seventh, eighth, and ninth months, human Qi also resides on the right side; thus, one should not needle the Yin meridians of the right food. In the tenth, eleventh, and twelfth months, human Qi resides on t he left side; therefore, one shou ld not needle the Yin meridians o f the left foot.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽繫日... :	
黃帝曰：五行以東方為甲乙木主春。春者，蒼色，主肝，肝者，足厥陰也。今乃以甲為左手之少陽，不合於數，何也？歧伯曰：此天地之陰陽也，非四時五行之以次行也。且夫陰陽者，有名而無形，故數之可十，離之可百，散之可千，推之可萬，此之謂也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: The Five Elements regard the East as governed by Jia and Yi, which belong to Wood and preside over spring. Spring is associated with the green color and governs the liver; the liver corresponds to the Jueyin of the foot. Now, however, Jia is said to govern the Shaoyang of the left hand—this does not conform to the numerical system. Why is this so? Qibo said: This refers to the Yin and Yang of Heaven and Earth, not the sequential operation of the Four Seasons and Five Elements. Moreover, Yin and Yang have names but no physical forms. Therefore, they can be counted in tens, separated into hundreds, dispersed into thousands, and extended to ten thousand—this is what it means. 

42. 病傳 Disease Transmission

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
黃帝曰：余受九鍼于夫子，而私覽于諸方，或有導引行氣，喬摩、灸、熨、刺、焫、飲藥之一者，可獨守耶，將盡行之乎？歧伯曰：諸方者，眾人之方也，非一人之所盡行也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I received the Nine Needles from my teacher, and have privately examined various methods. Some of them involve guiding and leading qi circulation, stretching massage, moxibustion, steaming, acupuncture, cautery, or taking medicinal decoctions. Should one method be used alone, or should all these methods be applied completely? Qibo said: These various methods are for the general population; they cannot all be practiced by a single individual.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
黃帝曰：此乃所謂守一勿失，萬物畢者也。今余已聞陰陽之要，虛實之理，傾移之過，可治之屬，願聞病之變化，淫傳絕敗而不可治者，可得聞乎？歧伯曰：要乎哉問也，昭乎其如日醒，窘乎其如夜瞑，能被而服之，神與俱成，畢將服之，神自得之，生神之理，可著於竹帛，不可傳於子孫。
		The Yellow Emperor said: This is what is meant by "holding to one method without deviation, and thus attaining the completion of all things." Now I have heard the essentials of yin and yang, the principles of deficiency and excess, the nature of imbalances, and the conditions that can be treated. I would like to hear about diseases whose patterns change, spread uncontrollably, or reach a state of irreversible decline where they cannot be cured—may I learn about such cases? Qibo said: What a crucial question this is! As clear as waking in the daylight, yet as perplexing as being plunged into darkness. If one can accept and follow it, then spirit and health will be achieved together. When fully embraced, the spirit shall naturally attain harmony. The principles of cultivating life and spirit may be recorded on bamboo slips or silk scrolls, but they cannot be passed down to descendants.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
黃帝曰：何謂日醒？歧伯曰：明於陰陽，如惑之解，如醉之醒。黃帝曰：何謂夜瞑？歧伯曰：瘖乎其無聲，漠乎其無形，折毛發理，正氣橫傾，淫邪泮衍，血脈傳溜，大氣入藏，腹痛下淫，可以致死，不可以致生。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is meant by "waking in the daylight"? Qibo said: Understanding yin and yang clearly, as if dispelling confusion or awakening from intoxication. The Yellow Emperor asked: What is meant by "being plunged into darkness"? Qibo said: It is silent as if mute, formless and indistinct. The fine hairs are affected; the vital qi becomes imbalanced and flows sideways. Pathogenic influences spread widely, blood vessels become infiltrated, great qi enters the internal organs, abdominal pain occurs with downward discharge. Such conditions can lead to death but not to life.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
黃帝曰：大氣入藏，奈何？歧伯曰：病先發於心，一日而之肺，三日而之肝，五日而之脾，三日不已，死。冬夜半，夏日中。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What should be done when great qi enters the internal organs? Qibo said: The disease first originates in the heart, then reaches the lungs after one day, the liver after three days, and the spleen after five days. If it does not stop within another three days, death will occur. Winter at midnight; summer at noon.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
病先發於肺，三日而之肝，一日而之脾，五日而之胃，十日不已，死。冬日入，夏日出。
		Disease first originates in the lungs, then reaches the liver after three days, the spleen after one day more, and the stomach after five additional days. If it does not cease within ten days, death will occur. In winter, it enters; in summer, it exits.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
病先發於肝，三日而之脾，五日而之胃，三日而之腎，三日不已，死。冬日入，夏蚤食。
		Disease first originates in the liver, then reaches the spleen after three days, the stomach after five more days, and the kidneys after another three days. If it does not subside within ten days total, death will occur. In winter, it enters during the day; in summer, before breakfast.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
病先發於脾，一日而之胃，二日而之腎，三日而之膂膀胱，十日不已，死。冬人定，夏晏食。
		Disease first originates in the spleen, then reaches the stomach after one day, the kidneys after two more days, and the loins and bladder after another three days. If it persists beyond ten days total, death will result. In winter at the hour of Ren Ding; in summer during late mealtime.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
病先發於胃，五日而之腎，三日而之膂膀胱，五日而上之心，二日不已，死，冬夜半，夏日昳。
		Disease first originates in the stomach, then reaches the kidneys after five days, the loins and bladder after three more days, and ascends to the heart after another five days. If it continues beyond two additional days, death will occur—this occurs at midnight in winter and during late afternoon in summer.

9
打開字典

顯示影印本
	病傳:	
病先發於腎，三日而之膂膀胱，三日而上之心，三日而之小腸，三日不已，死。冬大晨，夏晏晡。
		Disease first originates in the kidneys, then reaches the loins and bladder after three days, ascends to the heart after three more days, then proceeds to the small intestine after another three days. If this condition persists for a further three days without ceasing, death will occur. In winter at early morning; in summer during late afternoon.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
