	病傳:	
病先發於膀胱，五日而之腎，一日而之小腸，一日而之心，二日不已，死。冬雞鳴，夏下晡。
		Disease first originates in the bladder, then reaches the kidneys after five day, the small intestine after one more day, and the heart after another day. If it does not abate within two additional days, death will result. In winter at cockcrow; in summer during late afternoon hours.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	病傳:	
諸病以次相傳，如是者，皆有死期，不可刺也；間一藏及二、三、四藏者，乃可刺也。
		All diseases that transmit to the next organ in this sequential manner, as described above, have a fixed time for death and should not be treated with acupuncture. If there is an interval of one organ or two, three, or four organs in the transmission sequence, then acupuncture may be used for treatment. 

43. 淫邪發夢 Dreams Caused by Licentious and Pathogenic Influences

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	淫邪發夢:	
黃帝曰：願聞淫邪泮衍，奈何？歧伯曰：正邪從外襲內，而未有定舍，反淫于藏，不得定處，與營衛俱行，而與魂魄飛揚，使人臥不得安而喜夢；氣淫于府，則有餘于外，不足于內；氣淫于藏，則有餘于內，不足于外。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about licentiousness and evil spreading widely—how is this? Qi Bo said: Righteous and pathogenic influences attack from the exterior into the interior, but have not yet settled in a fixed location. They instead spread internally to the zang organs, unable to settle in one place. Accompanying the ying and wei qi as they circulate throughout the body, they also cause the hun (ethereal soul) and po (corporeal soul) to become agitated and restless, making people unable to sleep peacefully and prone to having dreams; When the qi spreads into the fu organs, there will be an excess in the exterior and a deficiency in the interior. When the qi spreads to the zang organs, there will be an abundance within and insufficiency without.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	淫邪發夢:	
黃帝曰：有餘不足，有形乎？歧伯曰：陰氣盛，則夢涉大水而恐懼；陽氣盛，則夢大火而燔焫；陰陽俱盛，則夢相殺。上盛則夢飛，下盛則夢墮；甚飢則夢取，甚飽則夢予；肝氣盛，則夢怒，肺氣盛，則夢恐懼、哭泣、飛揚；心氣盛，則夢善笑恐畏；脾氣盛，則夢歌樂，身體重不舉；腎氣盛，則夢腰脊兩解不屬。凡此十二盛者，至而寫之，立已。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Are these conditions of excess and deficiency manifested in tangible forms? Qi Bo said: When yin qi is excessive, one dreams of crossing great waters and feels fear; When yang qi is excessive, one dreams about a big fire burning fiercely; When both yin and yang are excessive, one dreams of mutual killing. Excess in the upper part causes dreams of flying; excess in the lower part causes dreams of falling; Extreme hunger causes dreams of taking, while extreme fullness causes dreams of giving; When liver qi is excessive, one has dreams of anger. When lung qi is excessive, one experiences dreams of fear, weeping, and flying; When heart qi is excessive, one dreams with frequent smiling yet also feels fear and dread; When spleen qi is excessive, one dreams of singing and rejoicing while feeling the body heavy and unable to move it; When kidney qi is excessive, one dreams that the waist and spine are split into two parts no longer connected. All these twelve conditions of excess, when they occur, should be purged; their symptoms will immediately cease.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	淫邪發夢:	
厥氣客於心，則夢見丘山煙火；客於肺，則夢飛揚，見金鐵之奇物；客於肝，則夢山林樹木；客於脾，則夢見丘陵大澤，壞屋風雨；客于腎，則夢臨淵，沒居水中；客于膀胱，則夢遊行；客于胃，則夢飲食；客于大腸，則夢田野；客于小腸，則夢聚邑沖衢；客于膽，則夢鬥訟自刳；客于陰器，則夢接內；客于項，則夢斬首；客于脛，則夢行走而不能前，及居深地窌苑中；客于股肱，則夢禮節拜起；客于胞䐈，則夢溲便。凡此十五不足者，至而補之立已也。
		When rebellious qi lodges in the heart, one dreams of hills and mountains with smoke and fire; When it lodges in the lungs, one dreams of flying and sees strange objects made of metal or iron; When it lodges in the liver, one dreams of forests and trees; When it lodges in the spleen, one dreams of hills, marshes, ruined houses, wind, and rain; When it lodges in the kidneys, one dreams of standing at the edge of a deep pool or living submerged underwater; When it lodges in the bladder, one dreams of traveling and wandering; When it lodges in the stomach, one dreams of eating and drinking; When it lodges in the large intestine, one dreams of fields; When it lodges in the small intestine, one dreams of crowded towns and busy roads; When it lodges in the gallbladder, one dreams of fighting, litigation, or self-inflicted wounds; When it lodges in the genital organs, one dreams of sexual intercourse; When it lodges in the neck, one dreams of beheading; When it lodges in the shins, one dreams of walking but being unable to move forward, or of staying deep underground in a well or garden; When it lodges in the thighs and arms, one dreams of performing rituals, bowing, and paying respects; When it lodges in the urinary bladder or lower abdomen, one dreams of urinating or defecating. All these fifteen conditions of deficiency, when they occur and are treated with tonification, will immediately cease. 

44. 順氣一日分為四時 Dividing One Day into Four Seasons According to the Flow of Qi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：夫百病之所始生者，必起於燥溫寒暑風雨陰陽喜怒飲食居處，氣合而有形，得藏而有名，余知其然也。夫百病者，多以旦慧晝安，夕加夜甚，何也？歧伯曰：四時之氣使然。
		The Yellow Emperor said: All kinds of diseases originate from dryness, warmth, cold, heat, wind, rain, yin and yang, joy and anger, diet, and living conditions. When these factors combine with the vital energy (qi), they take form; when they affect the internal organs, they acquire names. I understand this to be true. All kinds of illnesses often manifest as mild in the morning, stable during the day, worsen in the evening, and become severe at night. Why is this so? Qibo said: This is caused by the qi of the four seasons.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：願聞四時之氣。歧伯曰：春生，夏長，秋收，冬藏，是氣之常也，人亦應之，以一日分為四時，朝則為春，日中為夏，日入為秋，夜半為冬。朝則人氣始生，病氣衰，故旦慧；日中人氣長，長則勝邪，故安；夕則人氣始衰，邪氣始生，故加；夜半人氣入藏，邪氣獨居於身，故甚也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about the qi of the four seasons. Qibo said: In spring, things sprout; in summer, they grow; in autumn, they are harvested; and in winter, they are stored. This is the normal pattern of qi, and human beings correspond to it as well. One day can be divided into four seasons accordingly: morning corresponds to spring, midday to summer, evening to autumn, and midnight to winter. In the morning, human qi begins to rise while pathogenic qi declines; therefore, symptoms are mild at dawn. At midday, human qi is strong and vigorous enough to overcome pathogens, hence the condition remains stable. In the evening, human qi begins to decline while pathogenic qi starts to rise; therefore, symptoms worsen. At midnight, human qi retreats inward for storage, leaving the body vulnerable as pathogenic qi remains alone within; thus, symptoms become severe.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：有時有反者何也？歧伯曰：是不應四時之氣，藏獨主其病者，是必以藏氣之所不勝時者甚，以其所勝時者起也。黃帝曰：治之奈何？歧伯曰：順天之時，而病可與期。順者為工，逆者為麤。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Are there cases where this pattern is reversed? Why? Qibo said: This occurs when the condition does not correspond to the qi of the four seasons, but rather a particular zang organ is primarily responsible for the illness. In such cases, symptoms become severe during the season that the affected zang organ cannot withstand and improve during the season it can overcome. The Yellow Emperor asked: How should one treat this? Qibo said: Follow the timing of heaven, and the illness can be anticipated and treated accordingly. Those who follow this principle are skilled physicians; those who go against it are unskilled.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：善，余聞刺有五變，以主五輸。願聞其數。歧伯曰：人有五藏，五藏有五變。五變有五輸，故五五二十五輸，以應五時。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Well spoken. I have heard that there are five variations in acupuncture, corresponding to the Five Transport Points (Wu Shu). I would like to hear about them. Qibo said: Humans have five zang organs, and each of the five zang organs has five variations. Each variation corresponds to one of the Five Transport Points; therefore, there are twenty-five transport points in total, corresponding to the five seasons.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：願聞五變。歧伯曰：肝為牡藏，其色青，其時春，其音角，其味酸，其日甲乙；心為牡藏，其色赤，其時夏，其日丙丁，其音徵，其味苦；脾為牝藏，其色黃，其時長夏，其日戊己，其音宮，其味甘；肺為牝藏，其色白，其音商，其時徵，其日庚辛，其味辛；腎為牝藏，其色黑，其時冬，其日壬癸，其音羽，其味鹹。是為五變。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about these five variations. Qibo said: The liver is a yin zang organ, with the color green, corresponding to spring, the musical note Jiao, the taste sour, and the days Jiaye; The heart is a yin zang organ. Its color is red, it corresponds to summer, the days Bingding, the musical note Zhi, and the taste bitter; The spleen is a yin zang organ with the color yellow, corresponding to late summer, the days Wuyi, the musical note Gong, and the taste sweet; The lungs are a yin zang organ; their color is white, corresponding to the musical note Shang and the season of autumn, the days Gengxin, and the taste pungent; The kidneys are a yin zang organ: their color is black, they correspond to winter, the days Renkui, the musical note Yu, and the taste salty. These constitute the five variations.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：以主五輸奈何？藏主冬，冬刺井；色主春，春刺滎；時主夏，夏刺輸；音主長夏，長夏刺經；味主秋，秋刺合。是謂五變，以主五輸。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How do these correspond to the Five Transport Points? Zang organs are associated with winter; acupuncture should be performed at the Jing points during winter. Color is associated with spring; acupuncture should be performed at Ying points in spring. Seasons are associated with summer; acupuncture should be performed at Shu points during summer. Musical notes correspond to late summer; acupuncture should be applied at Jing points in late summer. Taste corresponds to autumn; acupuncture should be performed at He points during autumn. This is called the five variations, which correspond to the Five Transport Points.

7
打開字典
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：諸原安和，以致五輸。歧伯曰：原獨不應五時，以經合之，以應其數，故六六三十六輸。
		The Yellow Emperor said: The various Yuan points are harmonious and lead to the Five Transport Points. Qibo said: The Yuan points do not individually correspond to the five seasons, but they are combined with meridians and used in accordance with their numbers; therefore, there are six times six, totaling thirty-six transport points.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	順氣一日... :	
黃帝曰：何謂藏主冬，時主夏，音主長夏，味主秋，色主春。願聞其故。歧伯曰：病在藏者，取之井；病變於色者，取之滎；病時間時甚者，取之輸；病變於音者，取之經；經滿而血者，病在胃；及以飲食不節得病者，取之於合，故命曰味主合。是謂五變也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is meant by the zang organs being associated with winter, seasons with summer, musical notes with late summer, taste with autumn, and color with spring? I would like to understand the reason behind this. Qibo said: When illness is located in a zang organ, treat it at the Jing point; When disease manifests as changes in color, treat it at Ying points; When the illness worsens with seasonal changes, treat it at Shu points; When disease manifests as changes in tone or voice, treat it at Jing points; When the meridians are full and there is blood stagnation, the illness lies in the stomach. And for illnesses caused by immoderate diet, treat at He points; thus it is said that taste governs the He points. This is what is meant by the five variations. 

45. 外揣 External Inference

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	外揣:	
黃帝曰：余聞九鍼九篇，余親受其調，頗得其意。夫九鍼者，始於一而終於九，然未得其要道也。夫九鍼者，小之則無內，大之則無外，深不可為下，高不可為蓋，恍惚無竅，流溢無極，佘知其合於天道人事四時之變也，然余願雜之毫毛，渾束為一，可乎？歧伯曰：明乎哉問也，非獨鍼道焉，夫治國亦然。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard of the Nine Needles in nine chapters, and I personally received their principles; I have gained a fair understanding of their meaning. The Nine Needles begin with one and end with nine, yet the essential principles remain elusive. The Nine Needles, when minimized, have no interior; when maximized, have no exterior. Their depth cannot be measured downward, and their height cannot be capped upward. They are indistinct and without openings, flowing endlessly with no limit. I know they harmonize with the principles of Heaven, human affairs, and seasonal changes. Yet I wish to blend them as fine as a hair strand, merging them into one—would that be possible? Qibo said: What an insightful question! It is not only in the art of acupuncture, but also in governing a nation that this principle applies.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	外揣:	
黃帝曰：余願聞鍼道，非國事也。歧伯曰：夫治國者，夫惟道焉，非道，何可小大深淺，雜合而為一乎。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I wish to hear about the principles of acupuncture, not matters of state affairs. Qibo said: Governing a nation relies solely on the Dao. Without the Dao, how could one blend the small and large, the deep and shallow into one?

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	外揣:	
黃帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：日與月焉，水與鏡焉，鼓與響焉。夫日月之明，不失其影，水鏡之察，不失其形，鼓響之應，不後其聲，動搖則應和，盡得其情。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I wish to hear it in full. Qibo said: The sun and the moon, water and a mirror, a drum and its echo—these are examples. The brightness of the sun and moon never loses its shadow; the clarity of water and a mirror never fails to reflect form. The response of a drum's echo does not lag behind its sound—when moved, it resonates in harmony, fully capturing the situation.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	外揣:	
黃帝曰：窘乎哉！昭昭之明不可蔽，其不可蔽，不失陰陽也。合而察之，切而驗之，見而得之，若清水明鏡之不失其形也。五音不彰，五色不明，五藏波蕩，若是則內外相襲，若鼓之應桴，響之應聲，影之似形。故遠者，司外揣內，近者，司內揣外，是謂陰陽之極，天地之蓋，請藏之靈蘭之室，弗敢使泄也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: How profound! Bright clarity cannot be obscured. That it cannot be obscured is because it does not deviate from the principles of yin and yang. When combined and examined, when closely tested, when observed and understood—it is like clear water or a bright mirror that never fails to reflect its form. When the five tones are not distinct, when the five colors are unclear, and when the five zang organs become agitated—under such circumstances, internal and external conditions influence each other, just as a drum responds to its mallet, an echo follows sound, or a shadow resembles its form. Therefore, for distant conditions, one observes the exterior to infer the interior; for nearby conditions, one examines the interior to understand the exterior. This is called reaching the extremes of yin and yang, encompassing the principles of Heaven and Earth. Please preserve this knowledge in the Linglan Chamber, and do not allow it to be revealed. 

46. 五變 Five Transformations

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝問於少俞曰：余聞百疾之始期也，必生於風雨寒暑，循毫毛而入腠理，或復還，或留止，或為風腫汗出，或為消癉，或為寒熱，或為留痺，或為積聚。奇邪淫溢，不可勝數，願聞其故。夫同時得病，或病此，或病彼，意者天之為人生風乎，何其異也？少俞曰：夫天之生風者，非以私百姓也，其行公平正直，犯者得之，避者得無殆，非求人而人自犯之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Shao Yu, "I have heard that the initial stage of all kinds of diseases must originate from wind, rain, cold, or heat. They enter through the hair follicles and penetrate into the skin pores. Some may return, some may remain, some cause swelling with perspiration, some lead to consumption, some result in chills and fever, some produce lingering arthralgia, and others form accumulations." Abnormal pathogenic influences overflow excessively; they are too numerous to count. I wish to hear the reason behind this. When people contract illnesses at the same time, some fall ill with one condition while others suffer from another. Could it be that Heaven produces wind for human beings? Why is there such a difference? Shao Yu said, "The wind produced by Heaven is not intended to harm the common people. It moves fairly and uprightly; those who are exposed to it contract illness, while those who avoid it remain unharmed. It does not seek out people, but rather people expose themselves to it."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：一時遇風，同時得病，其病各異，願聞其故。少俞曰：善乎其問！請論以比匠人。匠人磨斧斤，礪刀削斷材木。木之陰陽，尚有堅脆，堅者不入，脆者皮弛，至其交節，而缺斤斧焉。夫一木之中，堅脆不同，堅者則剛，脆者易傷，況其材木之不同，皮之厚薄，汁之多少，而各異耶。夫木之蚤花先生葉者，遇春霜烈風，則花落而葉萎；久曝大旱，則脆木薄皮者，枝條汁少而葉萎；久陰淫雨，則薄皮多汁者，皮漉而淺；卒風暴起，則剛脆之木，枝折杌傷；秋霜疾風，則剛脆之木，根搖而葉落。凡此五者，各有所傷，況於人乎！
		The Yellow Emperor said, "When people encounter wind at the same time and contract illness simultaneously, their illnesses differ. I wish to hear the explanation for this." Shao Yu said, "What a good question!" Please allow me to explain by comparing it to the work of a craftsman. A craftsman sharpens his axes and chisels, and polishes knives to cut through wood. Even the yin and yang aspects of a tree have differences in hardness and softness. The hard parts cannot be cut easily, while the soft parts become loose and flaky; at their joints and knots, the axes and chisels may break or wear out. Within a single tree, hardness and softness differ; the hard parts are tough, while the soft ones are easily damaged. How much more so when considering different types of wood, varying bark thicknesses, differing amounts of sap, which all lead to differences! A tree that flowers and puts out leaves early in the season will have its blossoms fall and its leaves wither when it encounters severe spring frosts or strong winds. After prolonged exposure to intense drought, trees with brittle wood and thin bark will have branches with little sap and wilted leaves; Prolonged overcast weather and continuous rain will cause trees with thin bark and abundant sap to have their bark softened and weakened. A sudden storm arising will break the branches of tough yet brittle trees, causing damage to their trunks. Autumn frosts and strong winds will cause even sturdy but brittle trees to have their roots shaken and leaves fall. All these five situations result in different types of damage; how much more so is this the case for human beings!

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：以人應木，奈何？少俞答曰：木之所傷也，皆傷其枝。枝之剛脆而堅，未成傷也。人之有常病也，亦因其骨節皮膚腠理之不堅固者，邪之所舍也，故常為病也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "If we compare people to trees, then what is the analogy?" Shao Yu replied, "When a tree is injured, it is always its branches that are harmed." If the branch is tough and not brittle or hardened, then no injury occurs. When a person suffers from recurring illnesses, it is because their bones, joints, skin, and interstitial tissues are not firm. These become the dwelling places for pathogenic influences, hence they frequently fall ill.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：人之善病風厥漉汗者，何以候之？少俞答曰：內不堅，腠理疏，則善病風。黃帝曰：何以候肉之不堅也？少俞答曰：膕肉不堅，而無分理。理者麤理，麤理而皮不致者，腠理疏。此言其渾然者。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "For people who are prone to illnesses such as wind syncope and excessive perspiration, how can we observe or diagnose this?" Shao Yu replied, "If the interior is not firm and the skin pores are loose, one tends to easily contract wind-related illnesses." The Yellow Emperor said, "How can we observe whether the flesh is not firm?" Shao Yu replied, "If the muscles are not firm and lack clear texture or structure." Those with coarse muscle fibers, where the skin is not tightly knit, have loose interstitial pores. This refers to their overall condition as a whole.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：人之善病消癉者，何以候之？少俞答曰：五藏皆柔弱者，善病消癉。黃帝曰：何以知五藏之柔弱也？少俞答曰：夫柔弱者，必有剛強，剛強多怒，柔者易傷也。黃帝曰：何以候柔弱之與剛強？少俞答曰：此人薄皮膚，而目堅固以深者，長沖直腸，其心剛，剛則多怒，怒則氣上逆，胸中蓄積，血氣逆留，髖皮充肌，血脈不行，轉而為熱，熱則消肌膚，故為消癉。此言其人暴剛而肌肉弱者也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "For people who are prone to wasting and consumption diseases, how can we observe this?" Shao Yu replied, "Those whose five zang organs are all weak tend to easily develop wasting and consumption diseases." The Yellow Emperor said, "How can one know that the five zang organs are weak?" Shao Yu replied, "Those who are inherently weak must have counterparts that are strong and firm. The strong and firm ones tend to generate anger frequently; the weak ones are easily injured." The Yellow Emperor said, "How can one observe or determine those who are weak versus those who are strong and firm?" Shao Yu replied, "A person with thin skin and firm, deep-set eyes has a long, straight colon. Such individuals have strong hearts; when the heart is strong, it tends to generate frequent anger. Anger causes qi to rise upward, leading to accumulation in the chest. The blood and qi become stagnant and retained, filling the muscles beneath the skin, obstructing the circulation of blood vessels. This stagnation transforms into heat, and this heat consumes the flesh and skin, hence resulting in wasting disease." This refers to those individuals who are outwardly strong but have weak muscles.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：人之善病寒熱者，何以候之？少俞答曰：小骨弱肉者，善病寒熱。黃帝曰：何以候骨之小大，肉之堅脆，色之不一也？少俞答曰：顴骨者，骨之本也。顴大則骨大，顴小則骨小。皮膚薄而其肉無䐃，其臂懦懦然，其地色殆然，不與其天同色，污然獨異，此其候也。然後臂薄者，其髓不滿，故善病寒熱也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "For people prone to chills and fever, how can we observe this?" Shao Yu replied, "Those with small bones and weak muscles are prone to diseases of chills and fever." The Yellow Emperor said, "How can one observe the size of bones, the firmness or brittleness of muscles, and variations in complexion?" Shao Yu replied, "The zygomatic bone is the foundation of the bones." A large zygomatic bone indicates a generally large skeletal structure; a small zygomatic bone suggests a generally small skeletal structure. If the skin is thin and the muscles lack fullness, if the arms feel soft and flaccid, and if the complexion appears dull or pale, differing from the natural healthy hue of the heavens, appearing sullied and distinctly different—this is the sign. Therefore, those with thin arms have insufficient marrow, which is why they are prone to chills and fever.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：何以候人之善病痺者？少俞答曰：麤理而肉不堅者，善病痺。黃帝曰：痺之高下有處乎？少俞答曰：欲知其高下者，各視其部。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How can one observe whether a person is prone to arthralgia or rheumatic conditions?" Shao Yu replied, "Those with coarse muscle texture and weak flesh are prone to arthralgic diseases." The Yellow Emperor said, "Do rheumatic conditions occur in specific locations or areas of the body?" Shao Yu replied, "To determine where a rheumatic condition occurs—whether high or low—one should examine its corresponding region."

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：人之善病腸中積聚者，何以候之？少俞答曰：皮膚薄而不澤，肉不堅而淖澤。如此，則腸胃惡，惡則邪氣留止，積聚乃傷脾胃之間，寒溫不次，邪氣稍至。蓄積留止，大聚乃起。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "For people who are prone to accumulations in the intestines, how can we observe this?" Shao Yu replied, "Those with thin and dry skin, and flesh that is not firm but soft and moist." In such cases, the intestines and stomach are in poor condition. When this is so, pathogenic influences tend to linger and accumulate, forming masses between the spleen and stomach. If cold and heat imbalances occur irregularly, pathogenic qi gradually takes hold. When these accumulations remain stagnant for a long time, large masses will eventually form.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五變:	
黃帝曰：余聞病形，已知之矣！願聞其時。少俞答曰：先立其年，以知其時。時高則起，時下則殆，雖不陷下，當年有沖道，其病必起，是謂因形而生病，五變之紀也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I have heard about the forms of diseases and now understand them!" I would like to hear about their timing. Shao Yu replied, "First establish the year, in order to know its time." When the timing is high, illness arises; when it is low, danger occurs. Even if there is no sinking or decline, during that year if there is a channel of influence (chong dao), disease will certainly arise. This is called illness arising due to physical constitution—this is part of the five transformations' record. 

47. 本藏 Inner Zang

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：人之血氣精神者，所以奉生而周於性命者也；經脈者，所以行血氣而營陰陽、濡筋骨，利關節者也；衛氣者，所以溫分肉，充皮膚，肥腠理，司開闔者也；志意者，所以御精神，收魂魄，適寒溫，和喜怒者也。是故血和則經脈流行，營復陰陽，筋骨勁強，關節清利矣；衛氣和則分肉解利，皮膚調柔，腠理致密矣；志意和則精神專直，魂魄不散，悔怒不起，五藏不受邪矣；寒溫和則六府化穀，風痺不作，經脈通利，肢節得安矣，此人之常平也。五藏者，所以藏精神血氣魂魄者也；六府者，所以化水穀而行津液者也。此人之所以具受於天也，無愚智賢不肖，無以相倚也。然有其獨盡天壽，而無邪僻之病，百年不衰，雖犯風雨卒寒大暑，猶有弗能害也；有其不離屏蔽室內，無怵惕之恐，然猶不免於病，何也？願聞其故。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "Human blood and qi as well as spirit are what sustain life and circulate throughout the vital essence. The meridians are what carry blood and qi, nourish yin and yang, moisten the tendons and bones, and facilitate joint movement. The defensive qi is what warms the muscle layers, fills the skin, strengthens the interstices of the body surface, and regulates opening and closing. Will and intention are what govern spirit and mind, collect the soul and spiritual essence, regulate responses to cold and warmth, and harmonize joy and anger. Therefore, when the blood is in harmony, the meridians flow smoothly, nourishing yin and yang; the tendons and bones become strong and supple, and the joints clear and flexible. When defensive qi is in harmony, the muscle layers are relaxed and smooth, the skin soft and supple, and the interstices dense and tightly sealed. When will and intention are in harmony, spirit and mind remain focused and upright; the soul and spiritual essence do not disperse, anger and regret do not arise, and the five zang organs are not affected by pathogenic influences. When the regulation of cold and warmth is in harmony, the six fu organs transform food properly; wind-bi syndrome does not arise, the meridians remain unobstructed, and limbs and joints are at ease—this is the normal state of a person. The five zang organs are where spirit, mind, blood, qi, soul, and spiritual essence are stored; the six fu organs are what transform water and food and circulate body fluids. These functions are what a person receives from Heaven; regardless of whether one is wise or foolish, virtuous or unworthy, all depend equally on them. Yet some individuals alone attain the full span of Heaven's allotted years, free from pathological deviations; they remain vigorous for a hundred years and are not weakened. Even if exposed suddenly to wind and rain or extreme cold and heat, these conditions still cannot harm them. Others may never leave the shelter of their rooms, free from fear or anxiety, and yet they still cannot avoid illness. Why is this so? I wish to hear your explanation.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
歧伯對曰：窘乎哉問也。五藏者，所以參天地，副陰陽，而運四時，化五節者也；五藏者，固有小大、高下、堅脆、端正、偏傾者，六府亦有小大、長短、厚薄、結直、緩急。凡此二十五者，各不同，或善或惡，或吉或凶，請言其方。
		Qi Bo replied, "What a profound question this is." The five zang organs correspond to Heaven and Earth, mirror yin and yang, regulate the four seasons, and transform the five seasonal phases; the five zang organs naturally vary in size, height, firmness or fragility, regularity or irregularity; the six fu organs also differ in size, length, thickness, tightness or looseness. All these twenty-five characteristics are different; some are good and others bad, some auspicious and others inauspicious. Please explain their principles.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
心小則安，邪弗能傷，易傷以憂；心大則憂，不能傷，易傷於邪。心高則滿於肺中，悗而善忘，難開以言；心下，則藏外，易傷於寒，易恐以言。心堅，則藏安守固；心脆則善病消癉熱中。心端正，則和利難傷；心偏傾則操持不一，無守司也。
		If the heart is small, a person will be tranquil; pathogenic influences cannot harm them, but they are easily affected by worry; if the heart is large, one tends to worry, yet cannot be harmed by it, but is more susceptible to pathogenic influences. lahoma If the heart is high, it fills the lungs, causing mental confusion and forgetfulness; such a person finds it difficult to express themselves in speech. If the heart is low, its position is externalized, making one susceptible to cold and easily frightened by words. If the heart is firm, then the zang organs remain stable and secure; if the heart is brittle, one is prone to diseases of consumption, internal heat, and diabetes. If the heart is properly aligned, harmony and smooth function are maintained, making it difficult for illness to occur; if the heart is tilted or misaligned, one's willpower becomes inconsistent, and there is no stability in governance.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
肺小，則少飲，不病喘喝；肺大則多飲，善病胸痺、喉痺、逆氣。肺高，則上氣，肩息咳；肺下則居賁迫肺，善脅下痛。肺堅則不病，咳上氣；肺脆，則苦病消痺易傷。肺端正，則和利難傷；肺偏傾，則胸偏痛也。
		If the lungs are small, a person drinks little and does not suffer from asthma or coughing; if the lungs are large, one drinks excessively and is prone to diseases such as chest obstruction, throat obstruction, and upward-rebellious qi. If the lungs are high, a person experiences shortness of breath, labored breathing with shoulder elevation, and coughing; if the lungs are low, they press upon the diaphragm, causing frequent pain beneath the ribs. If the lungs are firm, one does not fall ill easily but may still experience coughing and shortness of breath; if the lungs are brittle, one is vulnerable to diseases involving consumption and obstruction, with a high susceptibility to injury. If the lungs are properly aligned, harmony and function remain smooth, making it difficult for disease to occur; if the lungs are tilted or misaligned, pain will be felt on one side of the chest.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
肺小則藏安，無脅下之病；肝大則逼胃迫咽，迫咽則苦膈中，且脅下痛。肝高，則上支賁切，脅挽為息賁；肝下則逼胃脅下空，脅下空則易受邪。肝堅則藏安難傷；肝脆則善病消痺，易傷。肝端正，則和利難傷；肝偏傾，則脅下痛也。
		If the lungs are small, the zang organs remain at peace, and there is no illness beneath the ribs; if the liver is large, it presses upon the stomach and throat; when the throat is pressed, one experiences discomfort in the epigastrium and pain beneath the ribs. If the liver is high, it supports the diaphragm forcefully, causing tension in the flanks and resulting in a condition known as "xi ben" (diaphragmatic constriction); if the liver is low, it presses on the stomach and leaves a void beneath the ribs; when there is an emptiness beneath the ribs, one becomes easily affected by pathogenic influences. If the liver is firm, the zang organs remain secure and are difficult to injure; if the liver is brittle, one easily contracts diseases of consumption and obstruction and is vulnerable to injury. If the liver is properly aligned, harmony remains smooth and it is difficult for disease to occur; If the liver is properly aligned, harmony remains smooth and disease is difficult to occur; if the liver is tilted or misaligned, it causes pain beneath the ribs.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
脾小，則藏安，難傷於邪也；脾大，則苦湊䏚而痛，不能疾行。脾高，則䏚引季脅而痛；脾下則下歸於大腸，下加於大腸，則藏苦受邪。脾堅，則藏安難傷；脾脆，則善病消痺易傷。脾端正，則和利難傷；脾偏傾，則善滿善脹也。
		If the spleen is small, the zang organs are at peace and difficult to be harmed by pathogenic influences; if the spleen is large, one suffers from pain in the abdomen and is unable to walk quickly. If the spleen is high, it pulls on the lower ribs and causes pain; if the spleen is low, it descends toward the large intestine; when pressure is added to the large intestine, the zang organs suffer from pathogenic influences. If the spleen is firm, the zang organs are secure and difficult to injure; If the spleen is firm, the zang organs are secure and difficult to injure; if the spleen is brittle, one easily suffers from diseases of consumption and obstruction. If the spleen is properly aligned, harmony remains unimpaired and disease is difficult to arise; if the spleen is tilted or misaligned, a person tends to experience frequent fullness and distension.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
腎小，則藏安難傷；腎大，則善病腰痛，不可以俛仰，易傷以邪。腎高，則苦背膂痛，不可以俛仰；腎下則腰尻痛，不可以俛仰，為狐疝。腎堅，則不病腰背痛；腎脆，則善病消癉，易傷。腎端正，則和利難傷；腎偏傾，則苦腰尻痛也。凡此二十五變者，人之所苦常病。
		If the kidneys are small, the zang organs will remain at peace and be difficult to injure; if the kidneys are large, one is prone to lower back pain, unable to bend forward or backward easily, and vulnerable to pathogenic influences. If the kidneys are high, one suffers from persistent pain in the upper back and shoulders, making it difficult to bend forward or backward; if the kidneys are low, lower back and buttock pain occur, preventing one from bending forward or backward, a condition known as "hu shen" (inguinal hernia). If the kidneys are firm, one will not suffer from lower back and shoulder pain; if the kidneys are brittle, one easily develops diseases of consumption or diabetes and is vulnerable to injury. If the kidneys are properly aligned, harmony remains stable and disease is difficult to occur; if the kidneys are tilted or misaligned, persistent pain in the lower back and buttocks will result. All these twenty-five variations are common ailments that people often suffer from.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
黃帝曰：何以知其然也？歧伯曰：赤色小理者，心小；麤理者，心大。無𩩲𩨗者，心高；𩩲𩨗小、短、舉者，心下。𩩲𩨗長者，心下堅；𩩲𩨗弱小以薄者，心脆。𩩲𩨗直下不舉者，心端正；𩩲𩨗倚一方者，心偏傾也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "How can one know this to be the case?" Qi Bo said: "A person with a reddish complexion and fine skin texture has small heart organs; a coarse skin texture indicates a large heart." Those without prominent cheekbones have high-positioned hearts; those with small, short, and elevated cheekbones have low-positioned hearts. Those with long cheekbones have firm, well-anchored hearts; those with weak, small, and thin cheekbones have brittle hearts. Those whose cheekbones extend straight downward without elevation have properly aligned hearts; those with cheekbones leaning to one side have tilted or misaligned hearts.

打開字典
顯示相似段落

	本藏:	
白色小理者，肺小；麤理者，肺大。巨肩反膺陷喉者，肺高；合腋張脅者，肺下。好肩背厚者，肺堅；肩背薄者，肺脆。背膺厚者，肺端正；脅偏疏者，肺偏傾也。
		Those with a pale complexion and fine skin texture have small lungs; those with coarse skin texture have large lungs. Those with broad shoulders, a sunken chest, and a receding throat have high-positioned lungs; those whose armpits are close together and whose ribs protrude outward have low-positioned lungs. Those with broad, thick shoulders and backs have firm lungs; those with thin shoulders and backs have brittle lungs. Those with thick upper backs and chests have properly aligned lungs; those whose ribs are uneven or loosely spaced have tilted or misaligned lungs.

打開字典
顯示相似段落

	本藏:	
青色小理者，肝小；麤理者，肝大。廣胸反骹者，肝高；合脅兔骹者，肝下。胸脅好者，肝堅；脅骨弱者，肝脆。膺腹好相得者，肝端正；脅骨偏舉者，肝偏傾也。
		Those with a bluish complexion and fine skin texture have small livers; those with coarse skin texture have large livers. Those with broad chests and prominent lower ribs have high-positioned livers; those whose ribs are tightly drawn together and who have narrow lower ribs (like a rabbit) have low-positioned livers. Those with well-developed chests and flanks have firm livers; those with weak rib bones have brittle livers. Those whose upper chest and abdomen are well-proportioned and harmonious in form have properly aligned livers; those with unevenly raised ribs have tilted or misaligned livers.

Those with upturned lips have high-positioned spleens; those whose lower lip hangs loose or sags downward have low-positioned spleens. Those with firm lips have firm spleens; those with large but soft lips have brittle spleens. Those whose upper and lower lips are well-formed and proportionate have properly aligned spleens; those with uneven or tilted lips have tilted or misaligned spleens.

打開字典
顯示相似段落

	本藏:	
黑色小理者，腎小；麤理者，腎大。高耳者，腎高；耳後陷者，腎下。耳堅者，腎堅；耳薄不堅者，腎脆。耳好前居牙車者，腎端正；耳偏高者，腎偏傾也。凡此諸變者，持則安，減則病也。
		Those with a dark complexion and fine skin texture have small kidneys; those with coarse skin texture have large kidneys. Those with high ears have high-positioned kidneys; those whose ears are set low and sunken behind the head have low-positioned kidneys. Those with firm ears have firm kidneys; those with thin and soft ears have brittle kidneys. Those whose ears are well-formed, positioned forward in front of the jawbone, have properly aligned kidneys; those with unevenly high ears have tilted or misaligned kidneys. All these variations, when balanced and stable, bring health; but if diminished or imbalanced, they lead to illness.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
帝曰：善。然非余之所問也，願聞人之有不可病者，至盡天壽，雖有深擾大恐，怵惕之志，猶不能減也，甚寒大熱，不能傷也；其有不離屏蔽室內，又無怵惕之恐，然不免於病者，何也？願聞其故。歧伯曰：五藏六府，邪之舍也，請言其故。五藏皆小者，少病，苦憔心，大愁擾；五藏皆大者，緩於事，難使以擾。五藏皆高者，好高舉措；五藏皆下者，好出人下。五藏皆堅者，無病；五藏皆脆者，不離於病。五藏皆端正者，和利得人心；五藏皆偏傾者，邪心而善盜，不可以為人平，反復言語也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." However, this is not what I wish to ask about. I would like to know if there are people who cannot fall ill and can live out their full life span according to Heaven's decree; even when greatly disturbed or deeply frightened by great fears, their anxious thoughts still cannot diminish them, nor can extreme cold or intense heat harm them; yet there are those who never leave the safety of their homes, and experience no fear or anxiety, yet still cannot avoid illness. Why is this so?" I would like to hear your explanation. Qi Bo said: "The five zang and six fu organs are the dwellings of pathogenic influences; please allow me to explain the reasons." Those whose five zang organs are all small suffer from few illnesses, but they often feel weary-hearted and experience great worry and disturbance; those whose five zang organs are large tend to be slow in their dealings with matters and difficult to disturb or agitate. Those whose five zang organs all have a high position like to take bold actions; those whose five zang organs all are in low positions tend to prefer being beneath others. Those whose five zang organs are firm do not suffer from illness; those whose five zang organs are brittle cannot escape illness. Those whose five zang organs are properly aligned and upright enjoy harmony, benefit from favorable conditions, and gain the favor of others; those whose five zang organs are uneven or tilted have deviant minds, are prone to theft, and cannot be trusted as upright people; they tend to speak inconsistently.

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
黃帝曰：願聞六府之應。歧伯答曰：肺合大腸，大腸者，皮其應；心合小腸，小腸者，脈其應；肝合膽，膽者，筋其應；脾合胃，胃者，肉其應；腎合三焦膀胱，三焦膀胱者，腠理毫毛其應。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I would like to hear about the correlations of the six fu organs." Qi Bo replied: "The lung is paired with the large intestine; the large intestine corresponds to the skin. the heart is paired with the small intestine, and the small intestine corresponds to the vessels (blood vessels); the liver is paired with the gallbladder; the gallbladder corresponds to tendons; the spleen is paired with the stomach, and the stomach corresponds to flesh (muscle); the kidney is paired with the triple burner and bladder; the triple burner and bladder correspond to the interstices of the skin, hair follicles, and fine hairs.

15
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
黃帝曰：應之奈何？歧伯曰：肺應皮。皮厚者，大腸厚，皮薄者，大腸薄；皮緩，腹裏大者，大腸大而長；皮急者，大腸急而短；皮滑者，大腸直；皮肉不相離者，大腸結。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How do these correlations manifest?" Qi Bo said: "The lung corresponds to the skin." Those with thick skin have a thick large intestine; those with thin skin have a thin large intestine; those whose skin is loose and who have a broad abdomen possess a large and long large intestine; those with taut skin have a tense and short large intestine; those with smooth skin have a straight large intestine; those whose skin and flesh are tightly adhered together have a constricted or knotted large intestine.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
心應脈，皮厚者，脈厚，脈厚者，小腸厚；皮薄者，脈薄，脈薄者，小腸薄；皮緩者，脈緩，脈緩者，小腸大而長；皮薄而脈沖小者，小腸小而短。諸陽經脈皆多紆屈者，小腸結。
		The heart corresponds to the vessels. Those with thick skin have thick vessels, and those with thick vessels have a thick small intestine; those with thin skin have thin vessels, and those with thin vessels have a thin small intestine; those with loose skin have relaxed vessels, and those with relaxed vessels possess a large and long small intestine; those whose skin is thin but the vessels are taut and narrow have a small and short small intestine. Those with all yang meridians and vessels that are excessively coiled or twisted have a constricted small intestine.

17
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
脾應肉，肉䐃堅大者，胃厚；肉䐃么者，胃薄。肉䐃小而么者，胃不堅；肉膕不稱身者，胃下，胃下者，下管約不利。肉脾不堅者，胃緩；肉䐃無小裏累者，胃急。肉䐃多少裏累者，胃結，胃結者，上管約不利也。
		The spleen corresponds to flesh. Those whose flesh on the cheeks is firm and large possess a thick stomach; those with small or thin cheek flesh have a thin stomach. Those whose cheek flesh is small and thin possess an unfirm stomach; those whose body's flesh does not proportionally match their physique have a low-positioned stomach, and when the stomach is low, the lower passages become constricted and obstructed. Those with soft or weak facial flesh have a relaxed stomach; those whose cheek flesh lacks small inner layers possess a tense stomach. Those with excessive layers of cheek flesh have a constricted stomach; when the stomach is constricted, the upper passages become obstructed and unsmooth.

18
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
肝應爪，爪厚色黃者，膽厚；爪薄色紅者，膽薄；爪堅色青者，膽急；爪濡色赤者，膽緩；爪直色白無約者，膽直；爪惡色黑多紋者，膽結也。
		The liver corresponds to the nails. Those with thick yellow-colored nails have a thick gallbladder; those with thin red-colored nails have a thin gallbladder; those with firm blue-colored nails possess a tense gallbladder; those with soft red-colored nails have a relaxed gallbladder; those with straight white-colored nails without any constriction possess a straight gallbladder; those whose nails are poor in quality, black in color, and have many lines or patterns have a constricted gallbladder.

19
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
腎應骨，密理厚皮者，三焦膀胱厚；麤理薄皮者，三焦膀胱薄。疏腠理者，三焦膀胱緩；皮急而無毫毛者，三焦膀胱急。毫毛美而麤者，三焦膀胱直，稀毫毛者，三焦膀胱結也。
		The kidney corresponds to the bones. Those with fine skin texture and thick skin possess a thick triple burner and bladder; those with coarse skin texture and thin skin have a thin triple burner and bladder. Those with loose interstitial spaces in the skin possess a relaxed triple burner and bladder; those whose skin is taut and lacks fine hairs have a tense triple burner and bladder. Those with beautiful, coarse fine hairs possess a straight triple burner and bladder; those with sparse fine hairs have a constricted triple burner and bladder.

20
打開字典
顯示影印本
	本藏:	
黃帝曰：厚薄美惡，皆有形，願聞其所病。歧伯答曰：視其外應，以知其內藏，則知所病矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Thickness, thinness, beauty, or ugliness all manifest in physical form; I would like to hear what illnesses they may cause." Qi Bo replied: "By observing their external manifestations, one can understand the internal zang organs and thus know what illnesses they suffer from." 

48. 禁服 Prohibited Prescriptions

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	禁服:	
雷公問於黃帝曰：細子得受業，通于九鍼六十篇，旦暮勤服之，近者編絕，久者簡垢，然尚諷誦弗置，未盡解於意矣。「外揣」言渾束為一，未知所謂也。夫大則無外，小則無內，大小無極，高下無度，束之奈何？士之才力，或有厚薄，智慮褊淺，不能博大深奧，自強於學若細子。細子恐其散於後世，絕於子孫，敢問約之奈何？黃帝曰：善乎哉問也。此先師之所禁，坐私傳之也，割臂歃血之盟也，子若欲得之，何不齋乎。
		Lei Gong asked the Yellow Emperor, "I have received instruction in the art and mastered the Nine Needles and sixty chapters. From morning to night I diligently serve it; recently some of the scrolls are broken, and those kept for a long time are stained with dirt. Yet I still recite them without ceasing, but I have not yet fully understood their meaning." The term "Wai Chuai" speaks of uniting all things into one, but the exact meaning is still unclear to me. When something is great, there is nothing outside it; when small, there is nothing inside it. Greatness and smallness have no extremes, height and depth no measure—how can one bind or contain such things? A scholar's talent and ability may vary in depth; some have narrow minds and shallow insight, unable to grasp broad and profound knowledge. Yet they strive to study as diligently as myself. I fear that these teachings may be lost in later generations, severed from descendants; I dare ask how they might be preserved? The Yellow Emperor said: What a good question this is. This is what our predecessors forbade; it must not be privately transmitted. It is a solemn oath sealed with blood drawn from the arm. If you wish to obtain this knowledge, why not purify yourself through fasting?

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	禁服:	
雷公再拜而起曰：請聞命於是也，乃齋宿三日而請曰：敢問今日正陽，細子願以受盟。黃帝乃與俱入齋室，割臂歃血，黃帝親祝曰：今日正陽，歃血傳方，有敢背此言者，反受其殃。雷公再拜曰：細子受之。黃帝乃左握其手，右授之書曰：慎之慎之，吾為子言之，凡刺之理，經脈為始，營其所行，知其度量，內刺五藏，外刺六府，審察衛氣，為百病母，調其虛實，虛實乃止，寫其血絡，血盡不殆矣。
		Lei Gong bowed twice and rose, saying: "I will follow your instruction on this matter." He then fasted for three days and requested, "May I ask today is the Day of Zhengyang; I, a humble student, wish to receive the oath." The Yellow Emperor then entered the fasting chamber with him, cut his arm and drank the blood. The Yellow Emperor personally made an oath: "Today is the Day of Zhengyang. By drinking this blood, we transmit these teachings. Whoever dares to betray this vow shall suffer misfortune in return." Lei Gong bowed twice again and said: "I, a humble student, accept the oath." The Yellow Emperor then held his left hand with his own, and with his right he handed him the book, saying: "Be cautious, be cautious. I shall speak to you now. In general, the principles of acupuncture begin with the meridians; one must understand their pathways and know their measurements. Internally, they relate to the five zang organs; externally, to the six fu organs. Carefully examine the Wei Qi, for it is the root of all diseases. Regulate its deficiency or excess—when this balance is achieved, illness will cease. Drain the blood vessels; when the blood is fully released, there shall be no danger."

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	禁服:	
雷公曰：此皆細子之所以通，未知其所約也。黃帝曰：夫約方者，猶約囊也，囊滿而弗約，則輸泄，方成弗約，則神與弗俱。雷公曰：願為下材者，勿滿而約之。黃帝曰：未滿而知約之以為工，不可以為天下師。
		Lei Gong said: "All of this I have already understood; what I do not yet know is how to summarize and preserve it." The Yellow Emperor said: "To summarize a prescription is like tying a bag. If the bag is full but not tied, it will spill; if a formula is complete but not summarized, its essence and spirit will be lost." Lei Gong said: "I wish to serve as the inferior material; please do not fill me completely but summarize it for me." The Yellow Emperor said: "One who has not yet been filled with knowledge, yet pretends to understand and summarize in order to appear skilled, cannot be a teacher for the world."

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	禁服:	
雷公曰：願聞為工。黃帝曰：寸口主中，人迎主外，兩者相應，俱往俱來，若引繩大小齊等。春夏人迎微大，秋冬寸口微大，如是者，名曰平人。
		Lei Gong said: "I wish to hear what it means to become a true practitioner." The Yellow Emperor said: "The cun Kou governs the interior, while the Ren Ying governs the exterior. When both correspond to each other, they move together in harmony, like pulling a rope with equal tension on both sides." In spring and summer the Ren Ying pulse is slightly larger; in autumn and winter, the Cun Kou pulse is slightly larger. One who exhibits this pattern is called a healthy person.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	禁服:	
人迎大一倍於寸口，病在足少陽，一倍而躁，在手少陽。人迎二倍，病在足太陽，二倍而躁，病在手太陽。人迎三倍，病在足陽明，三倍而躁，病在手陽明。盛則為熱，虛則為寒，緊則為痛痺，代則乍甚乍間。盛則寫之，虛則補之，緊痛則取之分肉，代則取血絡，且飲藥，陷下則灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之，名曰經刺。人迎四倍者，且大且數，名曰溢陽，溢陽為外格，死不治。必審按其本末，察其寒熱，以驗其藏府之病。
		If the Ren Ying pulse is one and a half times larger than the Cun Kou, illness resides in the foot Shaoyang meridian; if it is twice as large and agitated, the illness is in the hand Shaoyang. If the Ren Ying pulse is two times larger than the Cun Kou pulse, illness resides in the foot Taiyang meridian; if it doubles and becomes agitated, the illness lies in the hand Taiyang. If the Ren Ying pulse is three times larger than the Cun Kou puls, the illness resides in the foot Yangming meridian; if it triples and becomes agitated, then the illness lies in the hand Yangming. A strong pulse indicates heat; a weak one indicates cold. A tense pulse signifies pain or arthralgia, while an intermittent pulse suggests alternating severity and remission of symptoms. When the pulse is strong, drain it; when weak, tonify it. For tension and pain, needle at the flesh between muscles. When the pulse is intermittent, treat the blood vessels and also take medicine. If there is depression or deficiency, use moxibustion. If neither excessive nor deficient, follow the meridian for treatment—this is called Jing Ci (meridian acupuncture). If the Ren Ying pulse becomes four times larger, both large and rapid, it is called Yi Yang. Yi Yang signifies an external blockage; this condition is fatal and cannot be treated. One must carefully examine the root and branch, observe cold or heat symptoms, to verify illness in the zang-fu organs.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	禁服:	
寸口大於人迎一倍，病在足厥陰，一倍而躁，在手心主。寸口二倍，病在足少陰，二倍而躁，在手少陰。寸口三倍，病在足太陰，三倍而躁，在手太陰。盛則脹滿，寒中，食不化，虛則熱中、出糜、少氣、溺色變，緊則痛痺，代則乍痛乍止。盛則寫之，虛則補之，緊則先刺而後灸之，代則取血絡，而後調之，陷下則徒灸之，陷下者，脈血結於中，中有著血，血寒，故宜灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。寸口四倍者，名曰內關，內關者，且大且數，死不治。必審察其本末之寒溫，以驗其藏府之病。
		lords If the Cun Kou pulse is one and a half times greater than the Ren Ying, illness resides in the foot Jueyin meridian; if it increases by one and a half times and becomes agitated, illness lies in the hand Jueyin (Heart-Pericardium). If the Cun Kou pulse doubles that of the Ren Ying, illness is in the foot Shaoyin meridian; if it becomes twice as large and agitated, illness resides in the hand Shaoyin. If the Cun Kou pulse triples that of the Ren Ying pulse, the illness is located in the foot Taiyin meridian; if tripled and agitated, it resides in the hand Taiyin. A strong pulse indicates distension and fullness, cold in the middle jiao with undigested food; a weak pulse signifies heat in the middle jiao, loose stools, shortness of breath, and changes in urine color. A tense pulse means pain or arthralgia; an intermittent pulse indicates alternating episodes of pain and remission. When the pulse is excessive, drain it; if deficient, tonify it. When tense, first needle and then apply moxibustion; when intermittent, treat the blood vessels first and then regulate them. If there is depression and deficiency, use only moxibustion—this occurs when blood stagnates in the interior, where stagnant blood becomes cold, thus moxibustion is appropriate. When neither excessive nor deficient, treat according to the meridian. If the Cun Kou pulse becomes four times greater than normal, it is called Nei Guan. When Nei Guan occurs, with a large and rapid pulse, this condition is fatal and untreatable. One must carefully examine the cold or warm nature of its root and branch to verify illness in the zang and fu organs.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	禁服:	
通其營輸，乃可傳於大數。大數曰：盛則徒寫之，虛則徒補之，緊則灸刺，且飲藥，陷下則徒灸之，不盛不虛，以經取之。所謂經治者，飲藥，亦曰灸刺，脈急則引，脈大以弱，則欲安靜，用力無勞也。
		Only by understanding the circulation of nutritive energy can one truly grasp the great principles. The great principle states: When excessive, drain alone; when deficient, tonify alone. For tension, apply both moxibustion and acupuncture, along with medicine. In cases of depression or deficiency, use only moxiburnation. If neither excessive nor deficient, treat by following the meridian. What is meant by Jing Zhi (meridian treatment) includes taking medicine, as well as moxibustion and needling. When the pulse is tense, it should be relaxed; when large yet weak, one should remain calm and not exert effort to avoid fatigue. 

49. 五色 Five Colors

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公問於黃帝曰：五色獨決於明堂乎？小子未知其所謂也。黃帝曰：明堂者，鼻也；闕者，眉間也；庭者，顏也；蕃者，頰側也；蔽者，耳門也。其間欲方大，去之十步，皆見於外，如是者壽，必中百歲。
		Lei Gong asked the Yellow Emperor: "Do the five colors uniquely determine themselves in Mingtang?" I, your humble servant, do not yet know what is meant by that. The Yellow Emperor said: "Mingtang refers to the nose; the Que refers to the space between the eyebrows;" Ting refers to the forehead; Fan refers to the sides of the cheeks; Bi refers to the ear gates." If the space among them is square and large, from ten paces away one can clearly see it from outside; such a person will live long, certainly reaching a hundred years of age.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公曰：五言之辨，奈何？黃帝曰：明堂骨高以起，平以直，五藏次於中央，六府挾其兩側，首面上於闕庭，王宮在於下極，五藏安於胸中，真色以致，病色不見，明堂潤澤以清，五官惡得無辨乎？
		Lei Gong said: "How should the distinctions among these five statements be understood?" The Yellow Emperor said: "If the Mingtang bone is high and prominent, level and upright, then the five zang organs are arranged in the center, while the six fu organs flank both sides. The forehead rises up to Que Ting (the space between eyebrows and forehead), the Wang Gong (palace of kings) lies at the lower extremity. The five zang organs rest securely within the chest; their true colors manifest clearly, while signs of disease do not appear. When Mingtang is moist, lustrous, and clear, how could the five facial features fail to be distinguishable?"

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公曰：其不辨者，可得聞乎？黃帝曰：五色之見也，各出其色部。部骨陷者，必不免於病矣。其色部乘襲者，雖病甚，不死矣。
		Lei Gong said: "Might I hear about those who cannot make such distinctions?" The Yellow Emperor said: "When the five colors appear, each emerges from its respective color region." If the bone of a color region is sunken, one cannot escape illness. When the colors in their respective regions overlap and succeed each other, even if the disease is severe, death will not occur.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公曰：官五色奈何？黃帝曰：青黑為痛，黃赤為熱，白為寒，是謂五官。
		Lei Gong said: "How should one observe the five colors?" The Yellow Emperor said: "Blue-black indicates pain, yellow-red signifies heat, white denotes cold. This is what is called the five facial features."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公曰：病之益甚，與其方衰，如何？黃帝曰：外內皆在焉。切其脈口，滑小緊以沉者，病益甚，在中；人迎氣大緊以浮者，其病益甚，在外。其脈口浮滑者，病日進；人迎沉而滑者，病日損。其脈口滑以沉者，病日進，在內；其人迎脈滑盛以浮者，其病日進，在外。脈之浮沉及人迎與寸口氣小大等者，病難已；病之在藏，沉而大者，易已，小為逆；病在府，浮而大者，其病易已。人迎盛堅者，傷於寒，氣口盛堅者，傷於食。
		Lei Gong said: "How can one discern whether a disease is worsening or beginning to subside?" The Yellow Emperor said: "Both external and internal conditions are involved." If one examines the pulse at the mouth of the cun, finding it slippery, small, tight, and deep, then the disease is worsening internally; If the Qi at Renying is greatly taut and floating, the disease is worsening externally. If the pulse at the mouth is floating and slippery, the illness progresses daily; if the Renying pulse is deep and slippery, the disease diminishes day by day. If the mouth pulse is slippery and deep, the illness progresses daily from within; if the Renying pulse is slippery, strong, and floating, then the disease progresses daily externally. When the depth of the pulse, as well as the size of the Qi at Renying and Cunkou, are equal, the disease will be difficult to cure; if a disease resides in the zang organs, when the pulse is deep and large, it is easy to cure, but if small, it indicates an adverse condition; if a disease resides in the fu organs, when the pulse is floating and large, the illness will be easy to cure. If Renying is strong and firm, it indicates injury from cold; if Qikou is strong and firm, it signifies injury from overeating.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公曰：以色言食之間甚，奈何？黃帝曰：其色麤以明，沉夭者為甚，其色上行者，病益甚；其色下行，如雲徹散者，病方已。五色各有藏部，有外部有內部也。色從外部走內部者，其病從外走內；其色從內走外者，其病從內走外。病生於內者，先治其陰，後治其陽，反者益甚。其病生於陽者，先治其外，後治其內，反者益甚。其脈滑大，以代而長者，病從外來，目有所見，志有所惡，此陽氣之並也，可變而已。
		Lei Gong said: "How can one determine the severity of food-related illness through facial color?" The Yellow Emperor said: "If the complexion is coarse and bright, but with a dark hue, it indicates severity. If the color moves upward, the disease becomes more severe;" if the color descends like clouds dispersing, then the illness is beginning to subside. The five colors each correspond to specific regions of the zang organs; these regions have both external and internal aspects. When color moves from an external region into an internal one, the disease progresses inward from outside; when color moves outward from an internal region, the illness spreads outward from within. If a disease originates internally, one should first treat its yin aspect and then address its yang aspect; doing the reverse will worsen the condition. If a disease originates in the yang, one should first treat the exterior and then address the interior; doing the reverse will make it worse. If the pulse is slippery and large, with a substituting and prolonged rhythm, then the disease has come from outside. The patient may have seen something unusual or harbored aversion in their mind; this indicates an excess of yang energy, which can be transformed and resolved.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公曰：小子聞風者，百病之始也；厥逆者，寒濕之起也，別之奈何？黃帝曰：常候闕中，薄澤為風，沖濁為痺。在地為厥。此其常也；各以其色言其病。
		Lei Gong said: "I have heard that wind is the beginning of all diseases; and that jue ni (reversal and obstruction) arises from cold and dampness. How can these be distinguished?" The Yellow Emperor said: "Normally, observe the Que region (between eyebrows and forehead). A thin and moist appearance indicates wind; a surging and turbid condition indicates bi syndrome." On the earth aspect it is jue. This is the general rule; each disease can be understood according to its color.

8
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公曰：人不病卒死，何以知之？黃帝曰：大氣入于藏府者，不病而卒死矣。雷公曰：病小愈而卒死者，何以知之？黃帝曰：赤色出兩顴，大如拇指者，病雖小愈，必卒死。黑色出於庭，大如拇指，必不病而卒死。
		Lei Gong said: "How can one know if a person suddenly dies without having been ill?" The Yellow Emperor said: "When the great qi enters the zang and fu organs, one may die suddenly without showing symptoms of illness." Lei Gong said: "How can one know when a person who has slightly recovered from an illness dies suddenly?" The Yellow Emperor said: "If red color appears on both cheeks, large as the thumb, even if the illness has slightly improved, sudden death is inevitable." If black color emerges from Ting (the forehead), large as the thumb, one will die suddenly without having been ill.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
雷公再拜曰：善哉！其死有期乎？黃帝曰：察色以言其時。雷公曰：善乎！願卒聞之。黃帝曰：庭者，首面也；闕上者，咽喉也；闕中者，肺也；下極者，心也；直下者，肝也；肝左者，膽也；下者，脾也；方上者，胃也；中央者，大腸也；挾大腸者，腎也；當腎者，臍也；面王以上者，小腸也，面王以下者，膀胱子處也；顴者，肩也；顴後者，臂也；臂下者，手也；目內眥上者，膺乳也；挾繩而上者，背也；循牙車以下者，股也；中央者，膝也；膝以下者，脛也；當脛以下者，足也；巨分者，股裏也；巨屈者，膝臏也。此五藏六府肢節之部也，各有部分。有部分，用陰和陽，用陽和陰，當明部分，萬舉萬當。能別左右，是謂大道；男女異位，故曰陰陽。審察澤夭，謂之良工。
		Lei Gong bowed twice and said: "How excellent!" Is there a time limit for their death? The Yellow Emperor said: "Observe the complexion to determine the timing." Lei Gong said: "How good!" I wish to hear it in full. The Yellow Emperor said: "Ting refers to the upper part of the face; the area above Que is the throat; the middle of Que corresponds to the lungs; the lower extremity is the heart; directly below that is the liver; to the left of the liver is the gallbladder; below that is the spleen; the square area above is the stomach; the central region corresponds to the large intestine; flanking the large intestine are the kidneys; the area corresponding to the kidneys is the navel; above Mianwang (face king) is the small intestine, and below Mianwang is the bladder region. The cheeks correspond to the shoulders; behind the cheeks is the arms; below the arms are the hands; the upper inner canthus of the eyes corresponds to the chest and breasts; those flanking the rope-like lines upward correspond to the back; following down from the jawbone corresponds to the thighs; the central part is the knees; below the knees are the shins; corresponding to below the shins is the feet; Ju Fen corresponds to the inner thighs; Ju Qu refers to the kneecap. These are the regions corresponding to the five zang, six fu organs, and limbs' joints; each has its own designated area. With these designated areas, one uses yin to harmonize yang and yang to harmonize yin. When the distinctions of regions are clearly understood, every action will be appropriate. To distinguish between left and right is called the great principle; men and women occupy different positions, hence they are called yin and yang. To carefully examine luster and signs of decline is to be a skilled physician.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
沉濁為內，浮澤為外。黃赤為風，青黑為痛，白為寒，黃而膏潤為膿，赤甚者為血痛，甚為攣，寒甚為皮不仁。五色各見其部，察其浮沉，以知淺深；察其澤夭，以觀成敗；察其散搏，以知遠近；視色上下，以知病處；積神於心，以知往今。故相氣不微，不知是非，屬意勿去，乃知新故。色明不麤，沉天為甚，不明不澤，其病不甚。其色散，駒駒然，未有聚；其病散而氣痛，聚未成也。
		A deep, turbid complexion indicates internal conditions; a floating, moist one signifies external ones. Yellow and red indicate wind; blue-black indicates pain; white signifies cold. Yellow with a greasy, moist appearance suggests pus. An intense red color indicates blood-related pain; extreme redness leads to cramping. Extreme cold results in numbness of the skin. When the five colors each appear in their respective regions, observe their depth and shallowness to determine the severity; examine their luster or signs of decline to assess whether the condition is improving or worsening; observe their dispersion or aggregation to determine how distant or near the disease lies; look at whether the color moves upward or downward to know where the illness resides; Accumulate spiritual insight in the mind to understand past and present conditions. Therefore, if one observes qi without subtlety, one cannot discern right from wrong; maintain focused attention without distraction, and then one will understand the new or old nature of a condition. If the color is clear but not coarse, with deep darkness indicating severity; if it is unclear and lacks luster, then the illness is not serious. When the color disperses in a scattered manner, appearing like small horses running about without gathering; the disease is dispersed and causes qi pain, as it has not yet aggregated.
