打開字典
顯示影印本
	五色:	
腎乘心，心先病，腎為應，色皆如是。
		When the kidney affects the heart, the heart becomes ill first; the kidney follows in response, and the complexion will appear as described above.

12
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五色:	
男子色在于面王，為小腹痛；下為卵痛；其圜直為莖痛，高為本，下為首，狐疝㿉陰之屬也。女子在于面王，為膀胱子處之病，散為痛，搏為聚，方員左右，各如其色形。其隨而下至胝，為淫，有潤如膏狀，為暴食不潔。
		In men, if the color appears at Mianwang (the central facial region), it indicates pain in the lower abdomen; below that is pain in the testicles; a round and straight appearance indicates pain along the penis shaft. The higher part is its base, the lower part its head; these belong to conditions such as huxian (inguinal hernia) or yin swelling. In women, if the color appears in Mianwang, it indicates illness in the bladder and pelvic region. A dispersed appearance signifies pain; a concentrated one suggests aggregation. The shape—square or round—and whether on the left or right side each corresponds to its specific color and form. When it follows downward to the zhi (soles of the feet), it indicates leucorrhea; if moist and greasy like fat, it suggests overeating or unclean dietary habits.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五色:	
左為左，右為右。其色有邪，聚散而不端，面色所指者也。色者，青黑赤白黃，皆端滿有別鄉。別鄉赤者，其色赤，大如榆莢，在面王為不日。其色上銳，首空上向，下銳下向，在左右如法。以五色命藏，青為肝，赤為心，白為肺，黃為脾，黑為腎。肝合筋，心合脈，肺合皮，脾合肉，腎合骨也。
		Left corresponds to left, right to right. If the color appears abnormal, with aggregation and dispersion in an irregular manner, it indicates the location pointed out by the complexion. Colors—blue, black, red, white, yellow—are all distinct and full in their respective directions. зло If the color is red in its designated direction, it appears as a red patch large like a walnut pod; when located at Mianwang (central facial region), this indicates an imminent illness. The color is sharp on top, with the upper end hollow and pointing upward, and sharp at the bottom, pointing downward; it appears similarly to the left and right according to this pattern. The five colors correspond to the internal organs: blue corresponds to the liver, red to the heart, white to the lungs, yellow to the spleen, and black to the kidneys. The liver corresponds to tendons, the heart to blood vessels, the lungs to skin, the spleen to flesh, and the kidneys to bones. 

50. 論勇 On Bravery

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論勇:	
黃帝問於少俞曰：有人於此，并行并立，其年之長少等也，衣之厚薄均也，卒然遇烈風暴雨，或病或不病，或皆病，或皆不病，其故何也？少俞曰：帝問何急？黃帝曰：願盡聞之。少俞曰：春青風夏陽風，秋涼風，冬寒風。凡此四時之風者，其所病各不同形。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Shao Yu, "Suppose there are some people here who walk and stand together; their ages are equal in seniority or juniority, the thickness of their clothes is the same. Suddenly they encounter a violent wind and heavy rain, yet some fall ill while others do not, some all fall ill, and some all remain healthy. What is the reason for this?" Shao Yu said, "What is it that Your Majesty urgently wishes to know?" The Yellow Emperor said, "I wish to hear the full explanation." Shao Yu said, "In spring there is a green wind; in summer, a yang wind; in autumn, a cool wind; and in winter, a cold wind." All these winds of the four seasons cause illnesses that manifest differently.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	論勇:	
黃帝曰：四時之風，病人如何？少俞曰：黃色薄皮弱肉者，不勝春之虛風；白色薄皮弱肉者，不勝夏之虛風；青色薄皮弱肉，不勝秋之虛風；赤色薄皮弱肉，不勝冬之虛風也。黃帝曰：黑色不病乎？少俞曰：黑色而皮厚肉堅，固不傷於四時之風；其皮薄而肉不堅，色不一者，長夏至而有虛風者，病矣。其皮厚而肌肉堅者，長夏至而有虛風，不病矣。其皮厚而肌肉堅者，必重感於寒，外內皆然，乃病。黃帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How do the winds of the four seasons affect people's health?" Shao Yu said: "Those with yellowish thin skin and weak flesh cannot withstand the empty wind of spring; " those with white, thin skin and weak flesh cannot endure the empty wind of summer; those with greenish, thin skin and weak flesh can't withstand the empty wind of autumn; and those with reddish, thin skin and weak flesh are unable to endure the empty wind of winter." The Yellow Emperor said: "What about those with black complexions? Are they not affected?" Shao Yu said, "Those who are dark-skinned but have thick skin and firm flesh are naturally unaffected by the winds of the four seasons; " those with thin skin, weak flesh, and inconsistent coloring will fall ill when in late summer they encounter an empty wind." Those who have thick skin and strong muscles, even if they encounter the empty winds of late summer, will not fall ill. Those with thick skin and firm muscles must suffer a severe cold to become ill; this applies both externally and internally, resulting in illness. The Yellow Emperor said: "Well said."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論勇:	
黃帝曰：夫人之忍痛與不忍痛者，非勇怯之分也。夫勇士之不忍痛者，見難則前，見痛則止；夫怯士之忍痛者，聞難則恐，遇痛不動。夫勇士之忍痛者，見難不恐，遇痛不動；夫怯士之不忍痛者，見難與痛，目轉面盻，恐不能言，失氣，惊，顏色變化，乍死乍生。余見其然也，不知其何由，願聞其故。少俞曰：夫忍痛與不忍痛者，皮膚之薄厚，肌肉之堅脆，緩急之分也，非勇怯之謂也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Whether a person can endure pain or not is not the distinction between bravery and cowardice. A brave man who cannot endure pain will advance in the face of danger but stop when he feels pain; a timid person who can endure pain, upon hearing of difficulty becomes fearful yet remains unmoved by pain. A brave man who endures pain does not fear danger and remains unmoved when encountering pain; a timid person who cannot endure pain, when faced with danger and pain, turns his eyes away, glances around anxiously, is too frightened to speak, loses breath, becomes startled, changes color, and appears alternately dead and alive. I have observed this phenomenon but do not know its cause; I wish to hear your explanation. Shao Yu said: "Whether one can endure pain or not depends on the thickness of the skin, the firmness or fragility of the muscles, and their physical constitution; it is not a matter of bravery or cowardice."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論勇:	
黃帝曰：願聞勇怯之所由然。少俞曰：勇士者，目深以固，長沖直揚，三焦理橫，其心端直，其肝大以堅，其膽滿以傍，怒則氣盛而胸張，肝舉而膽橫，眥裂而目揚，毛起而面蒼，此勇士之由然者也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to learn what causes one to be brave or timid." Shao Yu said: "A brave man has deep and firm eyes, a long and upright nose with a straight bridge, the three burners (sanjiao) arranged horizontally. His heart is upright, his liver large and firm, and his gallbladder full and broad. When angry, his qi becomes abundant and his chest expands; his liver rises and his gallbladder spreads out; his eyelids split open and his eyes lift upward; his hair stands on end and his face turns pale. This is the nature of a brave man."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論勇:	
黃帝曰：願聞怯士之所由然。少俞曰：怯士者，目大而不減，陰陽相失，其焦理，縱𩩲𩨗短而小，肝系緩，其膽不滿而縱，腸胃挺，脅下空，雖方大怒，氣不能滿其胸，肝肺雖舉，氣衰復下，故不能久怒，此怯士之所由然者也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear about what causes one to be timid." Shao Yu said: "A timid person has large eyes but lacks depth; the yin and yang are out of balance. The channels of his three burners are disordered, with a long and narrow nose that is short and small. His liver tendons are loose, his gallbladder is not full and hangs loosely, his intestines and stomach protrude, and there is emptiness beneath his flanks. Even when greatly angered, the qi cannot fill his chest; although his liver and lungs may rise, the qi weakens and sinks again, so he cannot remain angry for long. This is what causes a person to be timid."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論勇:	
黃帝曰：怯士之得酒，怒不避勇士者，何藏使然？少俞曰：酒者，水谷之精，熟谷之液也，其氣慓悍，其入于胃中，則胃脹，氣上逆，滿於胸中，肝浮膽橫，當是之時，固比于勇士，氣衰則悔。與勇士同類，不知避之，名曰酒悖也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "When a timid man drinks alcohol, he becomes angry and dares not avoid even the bravest warriors. Which organ causes this change?" Shao Yu said: "Alcohol is the essence of water and grain, the liquid extracted from cooked grains. Its qi is fierce and aggressive. When it enters the stomach, the stomach becomes distended and the qi rises upward, filling the chest; the liver floats up and the gallbladder spreads outward. At this time, he naturally compares to a brave warrior. However, when the qi declines, regret follows." He becomes similar to a brave man and does not know how to avoid confrontation; this condition is called "jiu bei" (alcoholic confusion). 

51. 背腧 Back 俞 Points

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	背腧:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：願聞五藏之腧，出於背者。歧伯曰：背中大腧，在杼骨之端，肺腧在三焦之間，心腧在五焦之間，膈腧在七焦之間，肝腧在九焦之間，脾腧在十一焦之間，腎腧在十四焦之間。皆挾脊相去三寸所，則欲得而驗之，按其處，應在中而痛解，乃其輸也。灸之則可刺之則不可。氣盛則寫之，虛則補之。以火補者，毋吹其火，須自滅也；以火寫之，疾吹其火，傳其艾，須其火滅也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qu Bo, "I would like to hear about the 俞 points of the five zang organs that emerge on the back." Qu Bo said: "The major 俞 points on the back are located at the end of the scapula. The lung 俞 is between the third and fourth vertebrae, the heart 俞 is between the fifth and sixth vertebrae, the diaphragm 俞 is between the seventh and eighth vertebrae, the liver 俞 is between the ninth and tenth vertebrae, the spleen 俞 is between the eleventh and twelfth vertebrae, and the kidney 俞 is between the thirteenth and fourteenth vertebrae." They are all located three cun away from the spine. If one wishes to locate and verify them, press on these points; if there is a response in the middle with relief of pain, then that point is indeed its 俞. They can be treated with moxibustion but should not be needled. If the qi is excessive, it should be drained; if deficient, it should be supplemented. For fire supplementation therapy, do not blow out the flame; let it extinguish naturally. For fire drainage therapy, blow vigorously on the flame, transfer the moxa quickly, and wait for the fire to go out. 


52. 衛氣 Wei Qi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣:	
黃帝曰：五藏者，所以藏精神魂魄者也；六府者，所以受水谷而行化物者也。其氣內干五藏，而外絡肢節。其浮氣之不循經者，為衛氣；其精氣之行於經者，為營氣。陰陽相隨，外內相貫，如環之無端。亭亭淳淳乎，孰能竊之。然其分別陰陽，皆有標本虛實所離之處。能別陰陽十二經者，知病之所生；候虛實之所在者，能得病之高下；知六府之氣街者，能知解結契紹於門戶；能知虛石之堅軟者，知補寫之所在；能知六經標本者，可以無惑於天下。
		The Yellow Emperor said: The five zang organs are where spirit, mind, soul, and vital essence are stored. The six fu organs are where water and grains are received and transformed into substances. Their qi internally affects the five zang organs, and externally connects with the limbs and joints. The floating qi that does not follow the meridians is called wei qi; the refined qi that flows along the meridians is known as ying qi. Yin and yang follow each other, with the exterior and interior interconnecting like a ring without an end. Tingting chunchun hu, who could possibly steal it? However, in distinguishing yin and yang, there are always places where the root and branch, deficiency and excess become separated. Those who can distinguish the twelve meridians of yin and yang know the origin of diseases; those who observe where deficiency and excess are located can determine the severity of a disease; those who understand the qi streets of the six fu organs can know how to resolve knots and connect with the gates and passages; Those who can discern the hardness or softness of deficiency and excess know where to apply tonification or reduction; Those who understand the root and branch aspects of the six meridians can remain unconfused in all matters under heaven.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣:	
歧伯曰：博哉！聖帝之論。臣請盡意悉言之。足太陽之本，在限以上五寸中，標在兩絡命門。命門者，目也。足少陽之本，在竅陰之間，標在窗籠之前。窗籠者，耳也。足少陰之本，在內踝下上三寸中，標在背輸與舌下兩脈也。足厥陰之本，在行間上五寸所，標在背腧也。足陽明之本，在厲兌，標在人迎，頰挾頏顙也。足太陰之本，在中封前上四寸之中，標在背腧與舌本也。
		Qibo said: How vast! the discourse of the sage emperor. I humbly request to fully express my thoughts and speak in detail. The root of the Foot Taiyang meridian is located five cun above the orbit, while its branch extends to both the luo points and the Mingmen. Mingmen refers to the eyes. The root of the Foot Shaoyang meridian lies between the qiaoyin, while its branch is located in front of the chuanglong. Chuanglong refers to the ears. The root of the Foot Shaoyin meridian is located three cun above the medial malleolus, while its branch lies at the back shu points and in the two vessels beneath the tongue. The root of the Foot Jueyin meridian is located about five cun above Xingjian, while its branch lies in the back shu points. The root of the Foot Yangming meridian is at Liedui, while its branch lies in Renying and extends along the cheeks, below the chin, and to the forehead. The root of the Foot Taiyin meridian lies four cun above Zhongfeng in front, while its branch is found at the back shu points and at the base of the tongue.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣:	
手太陽之本，在外踝之後，標在命門之上一寸也。手少陽之本，在小指次指之間上二寸，標在耳後上角下外眥也。手陽明之本，在肘骨中，上至別陽，標在顏下合鉗上也。手太陰之本，在寸口之中，標在腋內動也。手少陰之本，在銳骨之端，標在背腧也。手心主之本，在掌後兩筋之間二寸中，標在腋下下三寸也。
		The root of the Hand Taiyang meridian lies behind the lateral malleolus, while its branching point is one cun above Mingmen. The root of the Hand Shaoyang meridian lies two cun above between the little finger and the ring finger, while its branch is at the upper corner behind the ear and below the lateral canthus. The root of the Hand Yangming meridian lies in the elbow bone, extending upward to Bieyang; its branch is located beneath the face and above Heqian. The root of the Hand Taiyin meridian lies in the cunkou, while its branch is felt as a pulsation inside the armpit. The root of the Hand Shaoyin meridian lies at the tip of the xuegu, while its branch is in the back shu points. The root of the Hand Jueyin (Heart-Cover) meridian lies two cun between the two tendons at the back of the palm, while its branch is three cun below the armpit.

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	衛氣:	
凡候此者，下虛則厥，下盛則熱；上虛則眩，上盛則熱痛。故石者，絕而止之，虛者，引而起之。
		Generally, when observing these points, deficiency below leads to coldness (jue), while excess below causes heat; deficiency above causes dizziness, and excess above results in heat and pain. Therefore, for excess conditions, one must drain and stop it; for deficiency conditions, one should guide and stimulate it to rise.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	衛氣:	
請言氣街，胸氣有街，腹氣有街，頭氣有街，脛氣有街。故氣在頭者，止之於腦；氣在胸者，止之膺與背腧；氣在腹者，止之背腧，與沖脈於臍左右之動脈者；氣在脛者，止之於氣街，與承山踝上以下。取此者，用毫鍼，必先按而在久應於手，乃刺而予之。所治者，頭痛眩仆，腹痛中滿暴脹，及有新積。痛可移者，易已也；積不痛，難已也。
		Please allow me to speak about the qi streets: there are streets for chest qi, abdominal qi, head qi, and leg (shin) qi. Therefore, when qi is located in the head, it should be regulated at the brain; when qi resides in the chest, it should be controlled at the ying region and the back shu points; when qi is located in the abdomen, it should be regulated through the back shu points, as well as at the chong meridian's pulsation points on either side of the navel; when qi is in the shins, it should be regulated at its street (meridian pathway), as well as between Chengshan and below or above the ankle. When treating these points, use a fine needle; one must first press on them and wait until there is a prolonged response in the hand before needling and administering treatment. The conditions treated include headache, dizziness with fainting, abdominal pain, fullness in the middle jiao, sudden distension, and newly formed accumulations. Pain that can be shifted is easily resolved; accumulations without pain are difficult to resolve.


53. 論痛 On Pain

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論痛:	
黃帝問于少俞曰：筋骨之強弱，肌肉之堅脆，皮膚之厚薄，腠理之疏密，各不同，其于鍼石火焫之痛何如？腸胃之厚薄堅脆亦不等，其於毒藥何如？願盡聞之。少俞曰：人之骨強、筋弱、肉緩、皮膚厚者，耐痛，其于鍼石之痛火焫亦然。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Shao Yu, "The strength or weakness of sinews and bones, the firmness or brittleness of muscles, the thickness or thinness of skin, and the looseness or tightness of pores and interstices vary from person to person. How do these differences affect their response to pain caused by acupuncture, moxibustion, and cauterization?" The thickness or thinness, firmness or brittleness of the intestines and stomach also differ; how do these differences affect their reaction to toxic medicines? I wish to hear a complete explanation. Shao Yu said, "A person whose bones are strong, sinews weak, muscles loose, and skin thick can endure pain; this is also true for the pain caused by acupuncture, stone therapy, or cauterization."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論痛:	
黃帝曰：其耐火焫者，何以知之？少俞答曰：加以黑色而美骨者，耐火焫。黃帝曰：其不耐鍼石之痛者，何以知之？少俞曰：堅肉薄皮者，不耐鍼石之痛，於火焫亦然。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How do you know that a person can endure cauterization?" Shao Yu replied, "Those who have dark complexions and beautiful bones are able to endure cauterization." The Yellow Emperor said, "How do you know that someone cannot endure the pain of acupuncture and stone therapy?" Shao Yu said, "Those with firm muscles and thin skin cannot endure the pain of acupuncture or stone therapy; this is also true for cauterization."

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	論痛:	
黃帝曰：人之病，或同時而傷，或易已，或難已，其故何如？少俞曰：同時而傷，其身多熱者，易已；多寒者，難已。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "When people fall ill, some injuries occur simultaneously, some recover quickly, and others are difficult to cure. What is the reason for this?" Shao Yu said, "When injuries occur simultaneously in a person whose body has much heat, recovery is easier;" those with much coldness find it difficult to recover.

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	論痛:	
黃帝曰：人之勝毒，何以知之？少俞曰：胃厚、色黑、大骨及肥者，皆勝毒；故其瘦而薄胃者，皆不勝毒也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How do you know whether a person can withstand toxic medicines?" Shao Yu said, "Those with thick stomachs, dark complexions, large bones, and who are fat can all withstand toxic medicines;" therefore, those who are thin and have a thin stomach cannot withstand toxins. 

54. 天年 Heavenly Years

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	天年:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：願聞人之始生，何氣築為基，何立而為楯，何失而死，何得而生？歧伯曰：以母為基，以父為楯；失神者死，得神者生也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "I would like to know: when a person is first born, what kind of qi forms the foundation? What establishes itself as a shield? What loss causes death? And what gain brings life?" Qi Bo said: "The mother's essence forms the foundation, and the father's essence serves as a shield; those who lose their Shen die, while those who retain their Shen live."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	天年:	
黃帝曰：何者為神？歧伯曰：血氣已和，營衛已通，五藏已成，神氣舍心，魂魄畢具，乃成為人。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "What is this 'Shen'?" Qi Bo said: "When the blood and qi are in harmony, the nutritive and defensive qi have become interconnected, the five zang organs have fully formed, the Shen-qi resides in the heart, and both Hun (ethereal soul) and Po (corporeal soul) are complete, then a person is formed."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	天年:	
黃帝曰：人之壽夭各不同，或夭壽，或卒死，或病久，願聞其道。歧伯曰：五藏堅固，血脈和調，肌肉解利，皮膚致密，營衛之行，不失其常，呼吸微徐，氣以度行，六府化穀，津液布揚，各如其常，故能長久。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "People's longevity and shortness of life vary; some die young, others suddenly, or suffer from prolonged illness. I would like to hear the principles behind this." Qi Bo said: "When the five zang organs are strong and solid, the blood vessels are in harmony and regulation, the muscles are supple and flexible, the skin is dense and tight, the nutritive and defensive qi circulate without deviation from their normal patterns, breathing is subtle and even, the qi flows at an appropriate rate, the six fu organs transform grain into essence, body fluids are distributed properly, and all functions proceed as they should—thus one can live a long life."

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	天年:	
黃帝曰：人之壽百歲而死，何以致之？歧伯曰：使道隧以長，基牆高以方，通調營衛，三部三里起，骨高肉滿，百歲乃得終。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "What causes a person to live for a hundred years and then die?" Qi Bo said: "When the meridians remain unobstructed and long, the foundational energy is strong and stable, the nutritive and defensive systems are well-regulated, the three parts of the body and the Sanli points are active, the bones are robust and the muscles full—only then can one reach a hundred years before death."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	天年:	
黃帝曰：其氣之盛衰，以至其死，可得聞乎？歧伯曰：人生十歲，五藏始定，血氣已通，其氣在下，故好走；二十歲，血氣始盛肌肉方長，故好趨；三十歲，五藏大定，肌肉堅固，血脈盛滿，故好步；四十歲，五藏六府十二經脈，皆大盛以平定，腠理始疏，滎貨頹落，髮頗斑白，平盛不搖，故好坐；五十歲，肝氣始衰，肝葉始薄，膽汁始減，目始不明；六十歲，心氣始衰，若憂悲，血氣懈惰，故好臥；七十歲，脾氣虛，皮膚枯；八十歲，肺氣衰，魄離，故言善誤；九十歲，腎氣焦，四藏經脈空虛；百歲，五藏皆虛，神氣皆去，形骸獨居而終矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Can you tell me about how the waxing and waning of qi leads to death?" Qi Bo said: "At ten years old, the five zang organs begin to stabilize, and blood and qi have become interconnected. The qi resides in the lower part of the body, so one enjoys running; at twenty years old, blood and qi begin to be abundant, and muscles are growing strong, hence one likes to move quickly; at thirty years old, the five zang have become well established, muscles are firm, and blood vessels are full and strong, so one prefers to walk steadily; at forty years old, the five zang and six fu organs as well as the twelve meridians are all strong and stable. The interstitial tissues begin to loosen, body fluids decline, hair turns gray at the temples, and although vitality remains balanced without fluctuation, one prefers sitting; at fifty years old, liver qi begins to decline, the liver's function becomes weakened, bile decreases, and vision starts to become impaired; at sixty years old, heart qi begins to wane, one becomes prone to sorrow and grief, blood and qi become sluggish, hence a preference for lying down; at seventy years old, spleen qi is weakened, and the skin becomes dry and withered; at eighty years old, lung qi declines, Po (corporeal soul) begins to detach, hence speech is often inaccurate or mistaken; at ninety years old, kidney essence becomes scorched and depleted, the four zang organs' meridians become empty and deficient; at one hundred years old, all five zang organs are depleted, Shen-qi has departed, the physical body remains alone, and thus life ends."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	天年:	
黃帝曰：其不能終壽而死者，何如？歧伯曰：其五藏皆不堅，使道不長，空外以張，喘息暴疾；又卑基牆薄，脈少血，其肉不石，數中風寒，血氣虛，脈不通，真邪相攻，亂而相引，故中壽而盡也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "What about those who do not live to a full old age but die prematurely? What is the reason?" Qi Bo said: "When all five zang organs are not strong, the meridians do not remain unobstructed or long, and there is an outward emptiness that spreads; this leads to sudden shortness of breath and rapid breathing; furthermore, when the foundational energy is weak and unstable, the pulse is deficient in blood, the muscles are not firm, one frequently suffers from wind-cold invasion, blood and qi become depleted, meridians fail to communicate properly, true essence and pathogenic factors attack each other, creating chaos and mutual attraction—thus life ends at a middle age." 

55. 逆順 Reversal and Smooth Flow

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順:	
黃帝問于伯高曰：余聞氣有逆順，脈有盛衰，刺有大約，可得聞乎？伯高曰：氣之逆順者，所以應天地陰陽四時五行也；脈之盛衰者，所以候血氣之虛實有餘不足；刺之大約者，必明知病之可刺，與其未可刺，與其已不可刺也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao, "I have heard that qi has patterns of reversal and smooth flow, the pulse has conditions of excess and deficiency, and there are general principles for acupuncture. May I hear about them?" Bo Gao said: "The reversal or smooth flow of qi corresponds to the yin and yang, four seasons, and five elements of heaven and earth." "The excess or deficiency in the pulse indicates the condition of blood and qi—whether they are strong or weak, excessive or insufficient." "The general principles for acupuncture require one to clearly know when a condition is suitable for needling, when it is not yet suitable, and when it has already become unsuitable."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順:	
黃帝曰：候之奈何？伯高曰：兵法曰無迎逢逢之氣，無擊堂堂之陣。刺法曰：無刺熇熇之熱，無刺漉漉之汗，無刺渾渾之脈，無刺病與脈相逆者。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "How should one observe these conditions?" Bo Gao said: "According to the art of war, one must not oppose a chaotic force, nor attack an orderly formation." "The method of acupuncture says: Do not needle a condition with intense heat, do not needle profuse sweating, do not needle a confused pulse, and do not needle when the disease and the pulse are in opposition."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	逆順:	
黃帝曰：候其可刺奈何？伯高曰：上工，刺其未生者也；其次，刺其未盛者也；其次，刺其已衰者也。下工，刺其方襲者也；與其形之盛者也；與其病之與脈相逆者也。故曰：方其盛也，勿敢毀傷，刺其已衰，事必大昌。故曰：上工治未病，不治已病，此之謂也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "How should one determine that a condition is suitable for acupuncture?" Bo Gao said: "A superior physician treats the disease before it arises;" "the next level, treats when the condition is not yet severe;" "and the lowest level, treats after the condition has already begun to decline." An inferior physician needles when the disease is just beginning to attack;" "or when the physical condition appears strong;" "or when the disease and pulse are in opposition." Therefore it is said: "When a condition is at its peak, one must not dare to harm or injure; needling after the decline has begun will surely bring great success." Therefore it is said: "A superior physician treats disease before it occurs, and does not treat after the disease has already manifested. This is what is meant by that statement." 

56. 五味 Five Flavors

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	五味:	
黃帝曰：願聞穀氣有五味，其入五藏，分別奈何？伯高曰：胃者，五藏六府之海也，水穀皆入于胃，五藏六府，皆稟氣於胃。五味各走其所喜，穀味酸，先走肝，穀味苦，先走心，穀味甘，先走脾，穀味辛，先走肺，穀味咸，先走腎。穀氣津液已行，營衛大通，乃化糟粕，以次傳下。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear about how the five flavors of grain qi enter the five zang organs, and in what way they are differentiated? Bo Gao said: The stomach is the sea of the five zang and six fu organs; both water and grain enter the stomach, and all the five zang and six fu receive their qi from the stomach. The five flavors each go to the organs they favor. If the grain flavor is sour, it first goes to the liver; if the grain flavor is bitter, it first goes to the heart; if the grain flavor is sweet, it first goes to the spleen; if the grain flavor is pungent, it first goes to the lungs; and if the grain flavor is salty, it first goes to the kidneys. Once the qi of grains and body fluids have circulated, and the nutritive and defensive qi are well communicated, then the dregs will be transformed and transmitted downward in order.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味:	
黃帝曰：營衛之行奈何？伯高曰：穀始入于胃，其精微者，先出於胃之兩焦，以溉五藏，別出兩行，營衛之道。其大氣之搏而不行者，積於胸中，命曰氣海，出於肺，循咽喉，故呼則出，吸則入。天地之精氣，其大數常出三入一，故穀不入，半日則氣衰，一日則氣少矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: How do the nutritive and defensive qis circulate? Bo Gao said: When grain first enters the stomach, its refined essence first emerges from the two regions of the stomach's upper and lower parts to nourish the five zang organs. It then branches out into two pathways, forming the routes for nutritive and defensive qi. The great qi that is condensed but does not circulate accumulates in the chest, and this is called the "qi sea." It emerges from the lungs and follows the throat and pharynx; therefore, it exits during exhalation and enters during inhalation. The refined qi of heaven and earth generally circulates in a ratio of three parts out for every one part in. Therefore, if grain does not enter the body, after half a day the qi becomes weakened, and after one full day it diminishes significantly.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味:	
黃帝曰：穀之五味，可得聞乎？伯高曰：請盡言之。五穀：糠米甘，麻酸，大豆咸，麥苦，黃黍辛。五果：棗甘，李酸，栗咸，杏苦，桃辛。五畜：牛甘，犬酸，豬咸，羊苦，雞辛。五菜：葵甘，韭酸，藿咸，薤苦，蔥辛。
		The Yellow Emperor said: May I hear about the five flavors of grains? Bo Gao said: Please allow me to explain in full. The five grains: millet is sweet, hemp is sour, soybeans are salty, wheat is bitter, and yellow broomcorn millet is pungent. The five fruits: dates are sweet, plums are sour, chestnuts are salty, apricots are bitter, and peaches are pungent. The five meats: beef is sweet, dog meat is sour, pork is salty, mutton is bitter, and chicken is pungent. The five vegetables: kui (Chinesekale) is sweet, leeks are sour, huo (legume leaves) are salty, xie (leek-like plant) is bitter, and green onions are pungent.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味:	
五色：黃色宜甘，青色宜酸，黑色宜咸，赤色宜苦，白色宜辛。凡此五者，各有所宜。五宜所言五色者，脾病者，宜食糠米飯，牛肉棗葵；心病者，宜食麥羊肉杏薤；腎病者，宜食大豆黃卷豬肉栗藿；肝病者，宜食麻犬肉李韭；肺病者，宜食黃黍雞肉桃蔥。
		The five colors: yellow corresponds to sweetness, green (or blue) corresponds to sourness, black corresponds to saltiness, red corresponds to bitterness, and white corresponds to pungency. All these five categories each have their appropriate correspondences. The "five appropriateness" mentioned in relation to the five colors: those with spleen disease should eat millet rice, beef, dates, and kui. Those with heart disease should eat wheat, mutton, apricots, and xie. Those with kidney disease should eat soybeans, huangjuan (yellow sprouts), pork, chestnuts, and huo. Those with liver disease should eat hemp, dog meat, plums, and leeks. Those with lung disease should eat yellow broomcorn millet, chicken meat, peaches, and green onions.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五味:	
五禁：肝病禁辛，心病禁咸，脾病禁酸，腎病禁甘，肺病禁苦。
		The five prohibitions: those with liver disease should avoid pungent flavors; those with heart disease should avoid salty flavors; those with spleen disease should avoid sour flavors; those with kidney disease should avoid sweet flavors; and those with lung disease should avoid bitter flavors.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味:	
肝色青，宜食甘，糠米飯、牛肉、棗、葵皆甘。心色赤，宜食酸，犬肉、麻、李、韭皆酸。脾黃色，宜食咸，大豆、豬肉、栗、藿皆咸。肺白色，宜食苦，麥、羊肉、杏、薤皆苦。腎色黑，宜食辛，黃黍、雞肉、桃、蔥皆辛。
		The liver is associated with the green color, and it benefits from sweet flavors; millet rice, beef, dates and kui are all sweet. The heart is associated with the red color, and it benefits from sour flavors; dog meat, hemp, plums, and leeks are all sour. The spleen is associated with the yellow color, and it benefits from salty flavors; soybeans, pork, chestnuts, and legume leaves are all salty. The lungs are associated with the white color, and they benefit from bitter flavors; wheat, mutton, apricots and xie are all bitter. The kidneys are associated with the black color, and they benefit from pungent flavors; yellow broomcorn millet, mutton, peaches and green onions are all pungent. 

57. 水脹 Water Retention

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	水脹:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：水與膚脹、鼓脹、腸覃、石瘕、石水，何以別之？歧伯答曰：水始起也，目窠上微腫，如新臥起之狀，其頸脈動，時咳，陰股間寒，足脛腫，腹乃大，其水已成矣。以手按其腹，隨手而起，如裹水之狀，此其候也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked 岐伯: "How can we distinguish between water retention, furzhang, guchang, changtan, shijia, and shishui?" Qi Bo replied: "When water retention first begins, the upper part of the eye sockets becomes slightly swollen, as if one has just woken up from lying down. The pulse in the neck is active; coughing occurs occasionally; there is coldness between the yin and thigh areas; the shins swell; and then the abdomen becomes large. At this point, water retention has already developed." If one presses on the abdomen with the hand, it rises again immediately as if wrapped in water; this is its diagnostic sign.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	水脹:	
黃帝曰：膚脹何以候之？歧伯曰：膚脹者，寒氣客於皮膚之間，鼕鼕然不堅，腹大，身盡腫，皮厚，按其腹，窅而不起，腹色不變，此其候也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How can furzhang be diagnosed?" Qi Bo said: "Furzhang occurs when cold qi lodges between the skin and flesh. It feels soft and not firm to the touch, with a large abdomen and swelling all over the body. The skin is thick; pressing on the abdomen leaves an indentation that does not immediately rebound, and the color of the abdomen remains unchanged. This is its diagnostic sign."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	水脹:	
鼓脹何如？歧伯曰：腹脹身皆大，大與膚脹等也，色蒼黃，腹筋起，此其候也。
		What about guchang? Qi Bo said: "In guchang, the abdomen swells and the whole body becomes large; this enlargement is similar to that of furzhang. The complexion turns bluish-yellow, and abdominal veins become prominent. This is its diagnostic sign."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	水脹:	
腸覃何如？歧伯曰：寒氣客於腸外，與衛氣相搏，氣不得榮，因有所繫，癖而內著，惡氣乃起，瘜肉乃生。其始生也，大如雞卵，稍以益大，至其成，如懷子之狀，久者離歲，按之則堅，推之則移，月事以時下，此其候也。
		What about changtan? Qi Bo said: "Cold qi lodges outside the intestines and combines with defensive qi. The qi cannot nourish properly, so it becomes obstructed and adheres internally; harmful qi then arises, leading to the formation of a tumor-like growth." When it first forms, it is as large as a chicken's egg; gradually, it grows larger. When fully developed, it resembles the size of a pregnant womb. In long-standing cases, it may persist for years. Pressing on it feels firm, and pushing it causes it to shift slightly. Menstruation occurs at regular intervals. This is its diagnostic sign.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	水脹:	
石瘕何如？歧伯曰：石瘕生於胞中，寒氣客於子門，子門閉塞，氣不得通，惡血當寫不寫，衄以留止，日以益大，狀如懷子，月事不以時下，皆生於女子，可導而下。
		What about shijia? Qi Bo said: "Shijia originates in the uterus. Cold qi lodges at the birth gate, which becomes blocked and prevents the flow of qi. Stagnant blood that should be discharged is not expelled; it accumulates and remains stagnant, growing larger day by day. It resembles a pregnant womb, and menstruation does not occur at regular intervals. These conditions arise in women and can be treated to facilitate discharge."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	水脹:	
黃帝曰：膚脹鼓脹，可刺邪？歧伯曰：先寫其脹之血絡，後調其經，刺去其血血絡也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Can furzhang and guchang be treated with acupuncture?" Qi Bo said: "First, drain the blood vessels associated with swelling; then regulate the meridians. This means needling to remove the stagnant blood from those vessels." 

58. 賊風 Harmful Winds

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	賊風:	
黃帝曰：夫子言賊風邪氣之傷人也，令人病焉，今有其不離屏蔽，不出室穴之中，卒然病者，非不離賊風邪氣，其故何也？歧伯曰：此皆嘗有所傷於濕氣，藏於血脈之中，分肉之間，久留而不去。若有所墮墜，惡血在內而不去，卒然喜怒不節，飲食不適，寒溫不時，腠理閉而不通。其開而遇風寒，則血氣凝結，與故邪相襲，則為寒痺。其有熱則汗出，汗出則受風，雖不遇賊風邪氣，必有因加而發焉。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Master, you say that the harmful winds and evil qi injure people, causing illness. Now there are those who do not leave their shelters, remaining within their rooms or caves, yet suddenly fall ill. This is not because they have escaped from harmful winds and evil qi; what then is the reason?" Qibo said: "This is because they have previously been injured by dampness, which has lodged within their blood vessels and between the muscle layers, remaining there for a long time without departing." If they have experienced a fall or injury, causing stagnant blood to remain inside without being expelled, and suddenly experience uncontrolled joy or anger, improper diet, or irregular exposure to cold and warmth, the pores and channels will become closed and obstructed. When their body opens up and encounters wind-cold, the blood and qi will congeal; when this combines with previous pathogenic factors, it results in cold bi syndrome. If there is heat present, perspiration occurs; when sweat emerges, it becomes susceptible to wind. Even without encountering harmful winds or evil qi, illness will inevitably arise as a result.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	賊風:	
黃帝曰：今夫子之所言者，皆病人之所自知也。其毋所遇邪氣，又毋怵惕之所志，卒然而病者，其故何也？唯有因鬼神之事乎？歧伯曰：此亦有故邪留而未發，因而志有所惡，及有所慕，血氣內亂，兩氣相搏。其所從來者微，視之不見，聽而不聞，故似鬼神。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Now what you have spoken of, Master, are all things that patients themselves can perceive." If they have not encountered pathogenic qi and there is no record of fear or alarm, yet illness suddenly occurs, what then is the cause? Is it only because of ghosts and spirits? Qibo said: "This is also due to lingering pathogenic factors that have not yet manifested. When the mind harbors aversion or longing, the blood and qi become internally disordered, leading to a clash between two types of qi." Its origin is subtle; it cannot be seen with the eyes or heard with the ears, hence it appears as if caused by ghosts and spirits.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	賊風:	
黃帝曰：其祝而已者，其故何也？歧伯曰：先巫者，因知百病之勝，先知其病之所從生者，可祝而已也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "In those cases where merely offering prayers brings about recovery, what is the reason?" Qibo said: "The early shamans understood the effective treatments for all kinds of illnesses. By knowing beforehand where a disease originates, one can effect recovery merely through prayers." 

59. 衛氣失常 Abnormal Wei Qi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：衛氣之留於腹中，搐積不行，菀蘊不得常所，使人支脅胃中滿，喘呼逆息者，何以去之？伯高曰：其氣積於胸中者，上取之，積於腹中者，下取之，上下皆滿者，旁取之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: When Wei Qi lingers in the abdomen, it becomes stagnant and does not circulate. It accumulates and stagnates without reaching its usual location, causing a person to experience fullness in the chest, flanks, and stomach, as well as shortness of breath and labored breathing. How can this be eliminated? Bo Gao said: If the Qi accumulates in the chest, it should be treated by taking from above; if it accumulates in the abdomen, treat by taking from below; and if both upper and lower regions are full, then take from the sides.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：取之奈何？伯高對曰：積於上，寫人迎、天突、喉中；積於下者，寫三里與氣街；上下皆滿者，上下取之，與季脅之下一寸；重者，雞足取之。診視其脈大而弦急，及絕不至者，及腹皮急甚者，不可刺也。黃帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How exactly should one take it? Bo Gao replied: If the accumulation is above, drain at Renying, Tiantu, and in the throat; if it accumulates below, drain Sanli and Qijie; if both upper and lower regions are filled, take from above and below, as well as one cun beneath the Ji Xie. For severe cases, treat at Jizu. If the pulse is large and taut or severed and absent, or if the abdominal skin is extremely tense, acupuncture should not be performed. The Yellow Emperor said: Good.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝問於伯高曰：何以知皮肉氣血筋骨之病也？伯高曰：色起兩眉薄澤者，病在皮；唇色青黃赤白黑者，病在肌肉；營氣濡然者，病在血氣；目色青黃赤白黑者，病在筋；耳焦枯受塵垢，病在骨。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao, How can one know the diseases of skin, flesh, Qi and blood, tendons, and bones? Bo Gao said: If the color rises between the two eyebrows with a thin and lusterless appearance, the disease is in the skin; if the lip color appears blue, yellow, red, white, or black, the disease lies in the muscles; if Ying Qi is moist and sluggish, the disease resides in the blood and Qi; if the eye color appears blue, yellow, red white or black, the disease is in the tendons; if the ears are dry and shriveled with dust and dirt, the disease lies in the bones.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：病形何如，取之奈何？伯高曰：夫百病變化，不可勝數，然皮有部，肉有柱，血氣有輸，骨有屬。黃帝曰：願聞其故。伯高曰：皮之部，輸於四末；肉之柱，有臂脛諸陽分肉之間，與足少陰分間；血氣之輸，輸於諸絡，氣血留居，則盛而起，筋部無陰無陽，無左無右，候病所在；骨之屬者，骨空之所以受益而益腦髓者也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What do these disease appearances look like? How should they be treated? Bo Gao said: Many diseases undergo transformations, and their variations are too numerous to count. However, the skin has its regions, the flesh has its pillars, the blood and Qi have their channels, and the bones have their affiliations. The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear an explanation of this. Bo Gao said: The regions of the skin transmit to the four extremities; the pillars of flesh are located between the arms and shins in the Yang regions, as well as between the foot Shaoyin meridian; the channels of blood and Qi transmit through the collateral vessels. When Qi and blood stagnate, they become excessive and manifest visibly; the regions of tendons have neither Yin nor Yang, neither left nor right—observe where the disease is located; the affiliations of bones refer to the bone cavities through which benefits are received and brain marrow is nourished.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：取之奈何？伯高曰：夫病變化，浮沉深淺，不可勝究，各在其處，病間者淺之，甚者深之，間者小之，甚者眾之，隨變而調氣，故曰上工。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should one treat these conditions? Bo Gao said: Diseases undergo transformations, with floating or sinking, deep or shallow conditions that are too numerous to exhaustively investigate. Each condition resides in its own location. For mild diseases, treat superficially; for severe ones, penetrate deeply. For minor ailments, use few needles; for serious ones, employ many. Adjust the Qi according to changes—this is what is called a superior physician.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝問于伯高曰：人之肥瘦大小溫寒，有老壯少小，別之奈何？伯高對曰：人年五十已上為老，二十已上為壯，十八已上為少，六歲已上為小。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao: How can one distinguish people according to their fatness, thinness, size, warmth or coldness, and whether they are old, strong, young, or small? Bo Gao replied: A person who is fifty years old or older is considered old; one who is twenty or older is strong; one who is eighteen or older is young; and a child six years old or older is small.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：何以度知其肥瘦？伯高曰：人有肥、有膏、有肉。黃帝曰：別此奈何？伯高曰：膕肉堅，皮滿者，肥。膕肉不堅，皮緩者，膏。皮肉不相離者，肉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How can one determine the degree of fatness or thinness? Bo Gao said: People may be fatty, greasy, or muscular. The Yellow Emperor asked: How can these be distinguished? Bo Gao said: If the thigh and body muscles are firm, and the skin is full, the person is fat. If the thigh and body meat are not firm, and the skin is loose, the person has greasy tissue. If the skin and flesh do not separate easily, it is muscular.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：身之寒溫何如？伯高曰：膏者，其肉淖而麤理者，身寒，細理者，身熱。脂者，其肉堅，細理者熱，麤理者寒。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How can one determine a person's body warmth or coldness? Bo Gao said: In those with greasy tissue, if the flesh is soft and has coarse texture, their body tends to be cold; if it has a fine texture, their body tends to be warm. In those with fatty tissue, if the flesh is firm and has a fine texture, they tend to be warm; if it has coarse texture, they tend to be cold.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：其肥瘦大小奈何？伯高曰：膏者，多氣而皮縱緩，故能縱腹垂腴。肉者，身體容大。脂者，其身收小。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How do fatness, thinness, large size, and small size differ? Bo Gao said: Those with greasy tissue have abundant Qi but loose skin, hence they tend to have a sagging abdomen and hanging fat. Those with muscular flesh have larger body frames. Those with fatty tissue tend to have compact, smaller bodies.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：三者之氣血多少何如？伯高曰：膏者，多氣，多氣者，熱，熱者耐寒。肉者，多血則充形，充形則平。脂者，其血清，氣滑少，故不能大。此別於眾人者也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How do the amounts of Qi and blood differ among these three types? Bo Gao said: Those with greasy tissue have more Qi. Those with abundant Qi tend to be warm, and those who are warm can withstand cold. Those with muscular flesh possess ample blood, which fills the body form; when the form is well filled, the condition is balanced and harmonious. Those with fatty tissue have clear blood and smooth but scant Qi, hence their bodies do not grow large. These are the distinctions among different types of people.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：眾人奈何？伯高曰：眾人皮肉脂膏，不能相加也，血與氣，不能相多，故其形不小不大，各自稱其身，命曰眾人。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What about ordinary people? Bo Gao said: Ordinary people have skin, flesh, fat, and greasy tissue that do not excessively accumulate. Their blood and Qi are neither excessive nor deficient; therefore, their bodies are neither too small nor too large, each proportionate to their own form. Such individuals are called ordinary people.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣失常:	
黃帝曰：善。治之奈何？伯高曰：必先別其三形，血之多少，氣之清濁，而後調之，治無失常經。是故膏人縱腹垂腴，肉人者，上下容大，脂人者，雖脂不能大者。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Good. How should they be treated? Bo Gao said: One must first distinguish the three body types, determine the amount of blood and the clarity or turbidity of Qi, and then regulate accordingly. Treatment should not deviate from the normal meridians. Therefore, those with greasy tissue tend to have sagging abdomens and hanging fat; those with muscular flesh appear large both above and below; while those with fatty tissue may be fat but do not become overly large. 

60. 玉版 Jade Tablet

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：余以小鍼為細物也，夫子乃言上合之于天，下合之于地，中合之于人，余以為過鍼之意矣，願聞其故。歧伯曰：何物大于天乎？夫大于鍼者，惟五兵者焉，五兵者死之備也，非生之具。且夫人者，天地之鎮也，其不可不參乎？夫治民者，亦唯鍼焉。夫鍼之與五兵，其孰小乎？
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I regarded the small needle as a minor thing, yet you have spoken of its correspondence with Heaven above, Earth below, and Man in between. I believe this to be an exaggeration regarding the meaning of the needle; I wish to hear your explanation." Qibo said: "What thing is greater than Heaven?" Those things larger than the needle are only the five weapons; the five weapons are preparations for death, not instruments of life. Moreover, man is the pivot of Heaven and Earth; how could he not be considered? To govern the people also depends solely on the needle. Between the needle and the five weapons, which is smaller?

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：病之生時，有喜怒不測，飲食不節，陰氣不足，陽氣有餘，營氣不行，乃發為癰疽。陰陽不通，兩熱相搏，乃化為濃，小鍼能取之乎？歧伯曰：聖人不能使化者為之，邪不可留也。故兩軍相當，旗幟相望，白刃陳於中野者，此非一日之謀也。能使其民令行，禁止士卒無白刃之難者，非一日之教也，須臾之得也。夫至使身被癰疽之病，膿血之聚者，不亦離道遠乎？夫癰疽之生，膿血之成也，不從天下，不從地出，積微之所生也，故聖人自治於未有形也，愚者遭其已成也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "When a disease arises, it is often due to unexpected joy or anger, immoderate diet, deficiency of Yin qi, excess of Yang qi, and stagnation of the nutritive qi; these conditions may give rise to abscesses." When Yin and Yang are not in communication, two sources of heat combine, transforming into pus—can the small needle remove it? Qibo said: "Sages cannot make transformation occur by force; evils must not be allowed to remain." Therefore, when two armies face each other, their banners visible from afar, and sharp weapons are displayed in the open field, this is not a plan made within one day. To be able to command the people so that orders are carried out and prohibitions observed, preventing soldiers from encountering the danger of sharp weapons—this is not achieved in a single day's instruction, but through momentary success. To allow one's body to be afflicted with abscesses and the accumulation of pus and blood—does this not deviate far from the Dao? The formation of abscesses and the gathering of pus and blood do not descend from Heaven nor arise from Earth; they are products of accumulated minute imbalances. Therefore, sages regulate themselves before any visible signs appear, while the foolish encounter them after they have already formed.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：其已形，不予遭，膿已成，不予見；為之奈何？歧伯曰：膿已成，十死一生，故聖人弗使已成，而明為良方，著之竹帛，使能者踵而傳之後世，無有終時者，為其不予遭也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "When a condition has already taken form, one does not encounter it; when pus has already formed, one does not see it;" What should be done about this? Qibo said: "When pus has already formed, there is one chance of survival out of ten; therefore, sages do not allow it to reach this stage. They clearly formulate effective prescriptions and record them on bamboo slips and silk, enabling those with ability to follow and transmit these teachings to future generations without end, precisely because they wish to prevent people from encountering such conditions."

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：其已有膿血而後遭乎？不導之以小鍼治乎？歧伯曰：以小治小者，其功小，以大治大者，多害，故其已成膿血者，其唯砭石鈹鋒之所取也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Is it only after pus and blood have formed that one encounters this condition?" Is it not treated by guiding with the small needle? Qibo said: "Using a small method to treat a minor condition yields a small effect, while using an aggressive approach for a major condition often causes harm. Therefore, when pus and blood have already formed, it can only be addressed by the scraping stone or sharp instruments."

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：多害者其不可全乎？歧伯曰：其在逆順焉。黃帝曰：願聞逆順。歧伯曰：以為傷者，其白眼青，黑眼小，是一逆也；內藥而嘔者，是二逆也；腹痛渴甚，是三逆也；肩項中不便，是四逆也；音嘶色脫，是五逆也。除此五者，為順矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Is a condition that causes much harm not curable at all?" Qibo said: "It depends on whether the condition is in harmony or disharmony." The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear about harmony and disharmony." lòng Qibo said: "When injury is present, the white of the eye appears blueish and the black part becomes small; this constitutes one sign of disharmony;" when internal medicine causes vomiting, this constitutes a second sign of disharmony; intense abdominal pain and extreme thirst constitute the third sign of disharmony; discomfort in the shoulders and neck constitutes the fourth sign of disharmony; a hoarse voice and a pale complexion constitute the fifth sign of disharmony. Beyond these five signs, it is considered harmonious.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：諸病皆有逆順，可得聞乎？歧伯曰：腹脹、身熱、脈大，是一逆也；腹鳴而滿，四肢清泄，其脈大，是二逆也；衄而不止，脈大，是三逆也；咳而溲血脫形，其脈小勁，是四逆也；咳脫形，身熱，脈小以疾，是謂五逆也。如是者，不過十五日而死矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "All diseases have signs of harmony and disharmony—may I hear about them?" Qibo said: "Abdominal distension, body heat, and a large pulse constitute one sign of disharmony;" rumbling in the abdomen with fullness, cold extremities with diarrhea, and a large pulse constitutes the second sign of disharmony; epistaxis that does not stop, along with a large pulse, constitutes the third sign of disharmony; coughing with hematuria and emaciation, accompanied by a small but taut pulse, constitutes the fourth sign of disharmony; coughing with emaciation, body heat, and a small yet rapid pulse constitute the fifth sign of disharmon. Those who exhibit such signs will not live beyond fifteen days.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
其腹大脹，四末清，脫形，泄甚，是一逆也；腹脹便血，其脈大，時絕，是二逆也；咳溲血，形肉脫，脈搏，是三逆也；嘔血，胸滿引背，脈小而疾，是四逆也；咳嘔，腹脹且飧泄，其脈絕，是五逆也。如是者，不及一時而死矣。工不察此者而刺之，是謂逆治。
		When the abdomen is greatly distended, the extremities are cold, emaciation occurs, and diarrhea is severe, this constitutes one sign of disharmon. abdominal distension with bloody stools and a large pulse that intermittently ceases constitutes the second sign of disharmo. coughing up blood, emaciation of body and flesh, and a forceful pulse constitute the third sign of dis. vomiting blood, chest fullness radiating to the back, and a small yet rapid pulse constitutes the fourth sign of di. coughing with vomiting, abdominal distension, and loose stools, accompanied by a pulse that has ceased, constitutes the fifth sign of d. Those who exhibit such signs will die before one shi (four hours) has passed. If a physician fails to observe these signs and proceeds with acupuncture, this is called treatment against the Dao.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：夫子之言鍼甚駿，以配天地，上數天文，下度地紀，內別五藏，外次六府，經脈二十八會，盡有周紀。能殺生人，不能起死者，子能反之乎？歧伯曰：能殺生人，不能起死者也。黃帝曰：余聞之，則為不仁，然願聞其道，弗行於人。歧伯曰：是明道也，其必然也，其如刀劍之可以殺人，如飲酒使人醉也，雖勿診，猶可知矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Your words about the needle are most profound, matching Heaven and Earth. You have counted the celestial phenomena above, measured the terrestrial principles below, distinguished internally among the five zang organs, arranged externally the six fu organs, and mapped the twenty-eight convergences of meridians—completely encompassing a complete cycle." It can bring about death in the living but cannot revive the dead; can you reverse this? Qibo said: "It can cause the living to die, but it cannot bring the dead back to life." The Yellow Emperor said: "I have heard this and find it unkind, yet I wish to understand its principles so that they may not be applied to people." Qibo said: "This is a clear principle, an inevitable outcome. It is like how swords and knives can kill people, or how drinking alcohol makes one drunk; even without diagnosis, it remains evident."

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：人之所受氣者，谷也。谷之所注者，胃也。胃者，水谷氣血之海也。海之所行云氣者，天下也。胃之所出氣血者，經隧也。而隧者，五藏六府之大絡也，迎而奪之而已矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear the full explanation." Qibo said: "What people receive as vital energy is from food grains." The place where the grain's essence converges is the stomach. The stomach is the sea of water, grains, qi, and blood. Where the clouds and vapors from the sea circulate is throughout the world. The pathways through which the stomach's qi and blood emerge are the meridians. And these pathways are the major networks connecting the five zang and six fu organs; it is merely a matter of intercepting and dispersing them.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	玉版:	
黃帝曰：上下有數乎？歧伯曰：迎之五里，中道而止，五至而已，五往而藏之氣盡矣，故五五二十五，而竭其輸矣，此所謂奪其天氣者也，非能絕其命而傾其壽者也。黃帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：闚門而刺之者，死於家中；入門而刺之者，死於堂上。黃帝曰：善乎方，明哉道，請著之玉版，以為重寶，傳之後世，以為刺禁，令民勿敢犯也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Is there a numerical pattern for this circulation above and below?" Qibo said: "If one intercepts at the Wuli point, it stops halfway; five pulses are sufficient. After five repetitions, the qi of the zang organs is exhausted. Thus, five times five makes twenty-five, and this drains their energy completely. This is what is called 'depriving them of heavenly qi,' but it does not mean one can end a life or shorten its span." The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear the complete explanation." Qibo said: "One who enters through a side door and applies acupuncture will die within their home; one who enters the main gate and administers acupuncture will die on the hall floor. The Yellow Emperor said: "Excellent is this method, clear indeed is the Dao. Please inscribe it on jade tablets as a precious treasure to be passed down through generations, serving as an acupuncture prohibition so that people dare not violate it." 

61. 五禁 Five Taboos

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五禁:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：余聞刺有五禁，何謂五禁？歧伯曰：禁其不可刺也。黃帝曰：余聞刺有五奪。歧伯曰：無寫其不可奪者也。黃帝曰：余聞刺有五過。歧伯曰：補寫無過其度。黃帝曰：余聞刺有五逆。歧伯曰：病與脈相逆，命曰五逆。黃帝曰：余聞刺有九宜。歧伯曰：明知九鍼之論，是謂九誼。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "I have heard that there are five taboos in acupuncture; what are these five taboos?" Qi Bo said: The five taboos refer to situations where needling should not be performed. The Yellow Emperor said, "I have heard of the Five Deprivations in acupuncture." Qi Bo said: One must not drain what cannot be deprived. The Yellow Emperor said, "I have heard of the five mistakes in acupuncture." Qi Bo said: The mistake lies in overdoing either tonification or drainage. The Yellow Emperor said, "I have heard of the Five Reversals in acupuncture." Qi Bo said: When the disease and the pulse are contrary to each other, it is called the Five Reversals. The Yellow Emperor said, "I have heard of the Nine Appropriateness in acupuncture." Qi Bo said: Fully understanding the theories of the nine types of needles is called the Nine Appropriatenesses.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五禁:	
黃帝曰：何謂五禁，願聞其不可刺之時。歧伯曰：甲乙日自乘，無刺實，無發蒙於耳內。丙丁日自乘，無振埃於肩喉廉泉。戊己日自乘四季，無刺腹，去爪寫水。庚辛日自乘，無刺關節於股膝。壬癸日自乘，無刺足脛，是謂五禁。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What are the Five Taboos? I wish to hear about the times when needling should not be performed." Qi Bo said: On Jia and Yi days, when Wood is dominant, do not needle for excess conditions, nor should one unblock obstructions in the ear. On Bing and Ding days, when Fire is dominant, one must not remove blockages from the shoulders, throat, or Lianquan (CV23). On Wu and Ji days, when Earth is dominant in all four seasons, one must not needle the abdomen, nor remove water by scraping the nails. On Geng and Xin days, when Metal is dominant, do not needling joints in the thighs and knees. On Ren and Gui days, when Water is dominant, one must not needle the shins of the feet; this is called the Five Taboos.

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	五禁:	
黃帝曰：何謂五奪？歧伯曰：形肉已奪，是一奪也；大奪血之後，是二奪也；大汗出之後，是三奪也；大泄之後，是四奪也；新產及大血之後，是五奪也。此皆不可寫。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "What are the Five Deprivations?" Qi Bo said: When the body's flesh and muscles have already been depleted, this is one form of deprivation. After a great loss of blood, this constitutes the second form of deprivation; After profuse perspiration, this is the third form of deprivation; After a severe purgation, this is the fourth form of deprivation; After childbirth or after a major hemorrhage, this is the fifth form of deprivation. In all these cases, drainage should not be performed.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五禁:	
黃帝曰：何謂五逆？歧伯曰：熱病脈靜，汗已出，脈盛躁，是一逆也；病泄，脈洪大，是二逆也；著痺不移䐃肉破，身熱，脈偏絕，是三逆也；淫而奪形、身熱，色夭然白，乃後下血衄，血衄篤重，是謂四逆也；寒熱奪形，脈堅搏，是謂五逆也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "What are the Five Reversals?" Qi Bo said: In a febrile disease, if the pulse is calm and perspiration has already occurred, but the pulse becomes full and restless, this constitutes one reversal; In a case of diarrhea with a large, surging pulse, this is the second reversal; When fixed arthralgia does not move and causes muscle atrophy, with body fever and a pulse that is cut off on one side, this constitutes the third reversal; When excessive sexual activity leads to physical depletion and body fever, with a complexion that is abnormally pale, followed by hematemesis or epistaxis, and the bleeding becomes severe, this is called the fourth reversal; When alternating chills and fever lead to physical depletion, with a pulse that is firm and forceful, this is known as the fifth reversal. 



62. 動輸 Movement and Transmission

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	動輸:	
黃帝曰：經脈十二，而手太陰、足少陰、陽明，獨動不休，何也？歧伯曰：是明胃脈也。胃為五藏六府之海，其清氣上注于肺，肺氣從太陰而行之，其行也，以息往來，故人一呼，脈再動，一吸脈亦再動，呼吸不已，故動而不止。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: There are twelve meridians, yet the Taiyin of the hand, Shaoyin and Yangming of the foot alone remain active without ceasing. Why is this so? Qibo said: This indicates that the stomach meridian is clear. The stomach is the sea of the five zang and six fu organs. Its clear qi ascends to infuse the lungs, and lung qi follows the Taiyin meridian in its course. The movement of this qi corresponds with respiration; therefore, with each exhalation, the pulse moves twice, and with each inhalation, it also moves twice. Since breathing does not cease, the movement of the pulse never stops.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	動輸:	
黃帝曰：氣之過於寸口也，上十焉息，下八焉伏，何道從還？不知其極。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: When qi passes through the cunkou, it rises ten units during exhalation and descends eight units during inhalation. By what path does it return? Its ultimate course is unknown.

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	動輸:	
歧伯曰：氣之離藏也，卒然如弓弩之發，如水之下岸，上於魚以及衰，其餘氣衰散以逆上，故其行微。
		Qibo said: When qi departs from the zang organs, it suddenly surges forth like a bowstring released or water rushing down a bank. It rises to the yu gate and then gradually declines; its residual qi disperses and flows upward in reverse, hence its movement becomes subtle.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	動輸:	
黃帝曰：足之陽明，何因而動？歧伯曰：胃氣上注于肺，其悍氣上沖頭者，循咽，上走空竅，循眼系，入絡腦，出顑，下客主人，循牙車，合陽明，并下人迎，此胃氣別走於陽明者也。故陰陽上下，其動也若一。故陽病而陽脈小者，為逆；陰病而陰脈大者，為逆。故陰陽俱靜俱動，若引繩相傾者病。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What causes the Yangming of the foot to move? Qibo said: Stomach qi ascends to the lungs. Its more vigorous portion surges upward toward the head, follows the throat, and moves up into the hollow orifices; it travels along the optic nerve, enters the network of vessels in the brain, exits at the cheeks, descends to the guest host (a point on the face), runs along the jawbone, converges with the Yangming meridian, and proceeds downward alongside the Renying vessel. This is the stomach qi that branches off toward the Yangming. Therefore, whether yin or yang, above or below, their movement is as one. Thus, if the yang meridians are diseased but the yang pulse is weak, it is a sign of disharmony; if the yin meridians are diseased yet the yin pulse is strong, this also indicates disharmony. Therefore, when both yin and yang are either excessively static or excessively active, like a rope being pulled in opposite directions, it indicates illness.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	動輸:	
黃帝曰：足少陰何因而動？歧伯曰：沖脈者，十二經之海也，與少陰之大絡，起於腎下，出於氣街，循陰股內廉，邪入膕中，循脛骨內廉，并少陰之經，下入內踝之後。入足下，其別者，邪入踝，出屬附上，入大指之間，注諸絡，以溫足脛，此脈之常動者也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What causes the Shaoyin of the foot to move? Qibo said: The Chong meridian is the sea of the twelve regular meridians. It forms a major collateral with the Shaoyin, originating below the kidneys, emerging at the qijie (a region in the lower abdomen), running along the inner side of the thigh, obliquely entering the popliteal fossa, following the medial aspect of the tibia, and proceeding alongside the Shaoyin meridian downward to enter behind the medial malleolus. It enters beneath the foot; its collateral branch obliquely enters the ankle, emerges at the fushang (a point above the inner malleolus), and enters between the big toe. It infuses into the collateral vessels to warm the feet and shins—this is a pulse that normally remains active.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	動輸:	
黃帝曰：營衛之行也，上下相貫，如環之無端，今有其卒然遇邪風，及逢大寒，手足懈惰，其脈陰陽之道，相輸之會，行相失也，氣何由還？歧伯曰：夫四末陰陽之會者，此氣之尤絡也；四街者，氣之徑路也。故絡絕則徑通，四末解則氣從合，相輸如環。黃帝曰：善。此所謂如環無端，莫知其紀，終而復始，此之謂也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: The movement of ying and wei qi flows upward and downward, interconnecting like a ring with no beginning or end. Now, if someone suddenly encounters pathogenic winds or is exposed to intense cold, their hands and feet become weak and sluggish. At the junctions where yin and yang meridians intersect and transmit energy, these pathways lose coordination—how then can qi return? Qibo said: The extremities are where yin and yang converge; this is the most important network of vessels for qi. The four streets are the direct pathways through which qi travels. Therefore, if a collateral is cut off, the direct pathway opens; when the extremities loosen up, qi follows and converges again, transmitting in a circular manner like a ring. The Yellow Emperor said: Well said. This is what is meant by a cycle without beginning or end, whose order no one can fully comprehend—ending and then beginning anew. This is precisely what it means. 

63. 五味論 Treatise on the Five Flavors

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味論:	
黃帝問於少俞曰：五味入于口也，各有所走，各有所病，酸走筋，多食之，令人癃；鹹走血，多食之，令人渴；辛走氣，多食之，令人洞心；苦走骨，多食之，令人變嘔；甘走肉，多食之，令人挽心。余知其然也，不知其何由？願聞其故。少俞答曰：酸入于胃，其氣澀以收，上之兩焦，弗能出入也，不出即留於胃中，胃中和溫，則下注膀胱，膀胱之胞薄以懦，得酸則縮綣，約而不通，水道不行，故癃。陰者，積筋之所終也，故酸入而走筋矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Shao Yu, "When the five flavors enter the mouth, each has its own destination and causes specific illnesses. Sourness goes to the sinews; excessive consumption of sour foods causes difficulty in urination." Saltiness goes to the blood; excessive consumption of salty foods causes thirst. Pungency goes to the qi; excessive consumption of pungent foods causes a burning sensation in the heart. Bitterness goes to the bones; excessive consumption of bitter foods causes vomiting and discomfort. Sweetness goes to the flesh; excessive consumption of sweet foods causes a feeling of heaviness in the heart. I know this is so, but I do not understand why it is so. I would like to hear the reason behind it. Shao Yu replied, "Sourness enters the stomach. Its qi is astringent and constricting; it ascends to both upper and middle jiao, but cannot move in or out. If it does not exit, it remains in the stomach. When the stomach is harmonious and warm, sourness descends into the bladder. The bladder's lining is thin and delicate; when affected by sourness, it contracts and becomes constricted, leading to obstruction and inability to pass urine, thus causing difficulty in urination." Yin is the final destination of accumulated sinews; therefore, sourness enters and moves toward the sinews.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味論:	
黃帝曰：鹹走血，多食之，令人渴，何也？少俞曰：鹹入于胃；其氣上走中焦，注于脈，則血氣走之，血與鹹相得，則凝，凝則胃中汁注之，注之則胃中竭，竭則咽路焦，故舌本干而善渴。血脈者，中焦之道也，故鹹入而走血矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Saltiness goes to the blood. Excessive consumption causes thirst; why is this so?" Shao Yu said, "When saltiness enters the stomach," its qi ascends to the middle jiao and enters the vessels, thus drawing blood and qi toward it. When blood combines with saltiness, it becomes stagnant; stagnation causes bodily fluids from the stomach to be directed there. As a result, the stomach's fluids are depleted, leading to dryness in the throat passage, hence the tongue root dries out and one experiences frequent thirst. The blood vessels are the pathways of the middle jiao; therefore, saltiness enters and moves toward the blood.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味論:	
黃帝曰：辛走氣，多食之，令人洞心，何也？少俞曰：辛入于胃，其氣走於上焦，上焦者，受氣而營諸陽者也，薑韭之氣熏之，營衛之氣，不時受之，久留心下，故洞心。辛與氣俱行，故辛入而與汗俱出。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Pungency goes to qi. Excessive consumption causes a burning sensation in the heart; why is this so?" Shao Yu said, "When pungency enters the stomach, its qi moves upward to the upper jiao. The upper jiao receives qi and nourishes all yang aspects. When pungent substances such as ginger or leeks enter, their qi permeates; if the nutritive and defensive qi cannot accommodate them in a timely manner, they remain for a long time beneath the heart, thus causing a burning sensation in the chest." Pungency moves together with qi; therefore, when pungent substances enter, they are expelled along with perspiration.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味論:	
黃帝曰：苦走骨，多食之，令人變嘔，何也？少俞曰：苦入于胃，五穀之氣，皆不能勝苦，苦入下脘，三焦之道，皆閉而不通，故變嘔。齒者，骨之所終也，故苦入而走骨，故入而復出，知其走骨也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Bitterness goes to the bones. Excessive consumption causes vomiting and discomfort; why is this so?" Shao Yu said, "When bitterness enters the stomach, the qi of the five grains cannot overcome it. Bitterness descends to the lower wǎn (a region in the abdomen), and all pathways of the triple burner become closed and obstructed, hence vomiting and discomfort occur." "The teeth are where bone terminates; therefore, bitterness enters and moves toward the bones. Hence, when it enters and then exits again, one can know that it has affected the bones."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五味論:	
黃帝曰：甘走肉，多食之。令人悗心，何也？少俞曰：甘入于胃，其氣弱小，不能上至於上焦，而與谷留於胃中者，令人柔潤者也，胃柔則緩，緩則蟲動，蟲動則令人悗心。其氣外通於肉，故甘走肉。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Sweetness goes to flesh; excessive consumption," causes a feeling of oppression in the heart; why is that so? Shao Yu said, "When sweetness enters the stomach, its energy is weak and cannot ascend to the upper jiao; instead, it remains in the stomach along with food. This causes a softening and moistening effect on the body. When the stomach becomes too soft and relaxed, internal parasites become active, and their movement causes a feeling of oppression or discomfort in the heart." Its energy communicates outward to the flesh; therefore, sweetness moves toward the flesh. 


64. 陰陽二十五人 Yin Yang Ershiwu Ren

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
黃帝曰：余聞陰陽之人何如？伯高曰：天地之間，六合之內，不離於五，人亦應之。故五五二十五人之政，而陰陽之人不與焉。其態又不合於眾者五，余已知之矣。願聞二十五人之形，血氣之所生，別而以候，從外知內，何如？歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也，此先師之秘也，雖伯高猶不能明之也。黃帝避席遵循而卻曰：余聞之得其人弗教，是謂重失，得而泄之，天將厭之，余願得而明之，金櫃藏之，不敢揚之。歧伯曰：先立五形金木水火土，別其五色，異其五形之人，而二十五人具矣。黃帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：慎之慎之，臣請言之。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard of people belonging to yin and yang; what are they like? Bo Gao said: Between Heaven and Earth, within the six directions, nothing is separated from the Five Elements; human beings also correspond to them. Therefore, there are twenty-five types of people according to the five combinations of the Five Elements, and those belonging to yin and yang are not included among them. There are also five whose appearances do not conform to the common types; I already know about them. I wish to hear about the forms of these twenty-five people, how their vital energy and blood are generated, distinguishing them in order to observe signs; by examining external appearances to understand internal conditions—how would that be? Qibo said: What a thorough question this is! This is the secret of our predecessors; even Bo Gao cannot fully understand it. The Yellow Emperor rose from his seat, followed the proper etiquette, and stepped back while saying: If I have heard this knowledge but do not teach it to the right person, that would be a great loss; if I obtain it and reveal it recklessly, Heaven will resent me. I wish to acquire and understand it clearly, store it in a golden cabinet for safekeeping, and never dare to publicize it. Qibo said: First establish the five forms—metal, wood, water, fire, and earth—distinguish their five colors, differentiate people according to these five forms, and thus all twenty-five types of people are complete. The Yellow Emperor said: I wish to hear the rest in full. Qibo said: Be cautious, be cautious; allow me to explain.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
木形之人，比于上角似於蒼帝，其為人蒼色，小頭，長面大肩背直身小，手足好。有才，勞心少力多憂，勞於事，能春夏不能秋冬感而病生。足厥陰，佗佗然，大角之人比於左足少陽，少陽之上遺遺然。左角之人比於右足少陽，少陽之下隨隨然。鈦角之人，比於右足少陽，少陽之上推推然。判角之人比於左足少陽，少陽之下枯枯然。
		Those with the Wood form are comparable to Shangjiao and resemble the Emperor Cang; they have a bluish complexion, small heads, long faces, broad shoulders, straight backs, slender bodies, and well-formed hands and feet. They are talented, use their minds rather than physical strength, often worry, and are burdened by affairs; they thrive in spring and summer but struggle in autumn and winter, becoming susceptible to illness when affected. Their Liver Meridian (Zu Jueyin) is prominent, appearing broad and open; those with the Grand Horn type are comparable to the Left Foot Shaoyang, and above the Shaoyang they appear graceful and elegant. Those of the Left Horn type correspond to the Right Foot Shaoyang; below the Shaoyang, they appear relaxed and unhurried. Those of the Tiaijiao type correspond to the Right Foot Shaoyan; above the Shaoyang, they are characterized by a pushing or advancing manner. Those of the Panjiao type correspond to the Left Foot Shaoyan; below the Shaoyang, their appearance is dry and withered.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
火形之人，比於上徵，似於赤帝。其為人赤色廣䏖脫面小頭，好肩背，髀腹小手足，行安地疾心，行搖肩背肉滿。有氣輕財少信多慮，見事明好顏，急心不壽暴死。能春夏不能秋冬，秋冬感而病生，手少陰核核然。質徵之人，比於左手太陽，太陽之上，肌肌然，少徵之人比於右手太陽，太陽之下慆慆然，右徵之人比於右手太陽，太陽之上鮫鮫然。質判之人，比於左手太陽，太陽之下支支頤頤然。
		Those with the Fire form are comparable to Shangzheng and resemble Emperor Chi. They have a reddish complexion, broad shoulders and chest, flat face, small head, well-developed shoulders and back, narrow hips and abdomen, slender hands and feet; they walk firmly on the ground with quick steps, their hearts are active, and when walking, they sway their shoulders and backs while having full muscles. They possess abundant vital energy, are light with wealth, have little trustworthiness, often worry excessively, perceive matters clearly, enjoy a good appearance, but their hearts are hasty and they do not live long, often dying suddenly. They thrive in spring and summer, but struggle in autumn and winter; when affected during these seasons, illness arises. Their Heart Meridian (Shou Shaoyin) is characterized by a firm or solid condition. Those of the Zhi Zheng type correspond to the Left Hand Taiyang; above the Taiyang, they appear well-muscled. Those of the Shaozheng type correspond to the Right Hand Taiyang; below the Taiyang, their condition is marked by a relaxed or drifting manner. Those of the Youzheng type correspond to the Right-hand Taiyang; above it, they are characterized as jiao jiao (possibly meaning "like a fish" or with a smooth and flowing quality). Those of the Zhipan type correspond to the Left-hand Taiyang; below it, their condition is described as zhi zhi yi yi (possibly indicating a weak or drooping posture).

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
土形之人，比於上宮，似於上古黃帝，其為人黃色圓面、大頭、美肩背、大腹、美股脛、小手足、多肉、上下相稱行安地，舉足浮。安心，好利人不喜權勢，善附人也。能秋冬不能春夏，春夏感而病生，足太陰，敦敦然。大宮之人比於左足陽明，陽明之上婉婉然。加宮之人，比於左足陽明，陽明之下坎坎然。少宮之人，比於右足陽明，陽明之上，樞樞然。左宮之人，比於右足陽明，陽明之下，兀兀然。
		Those with the Earth form are comparable to Shanggan and resemble the ancient Yellow Emperor. They have a yellow complexion, round faces, large heads, well-developed shoulders and backs, broad abdomens, full thighs and calves, small hands and feet, abundant flesh, their upper and lower bodies are proportionate; they walk steadily on the ground, lifting their feet lightly as if floating. They have a calm mind, enjoy benefiting others, do not like power or authority, and are good at attaching themselves to others. They thrive in autumn and winter but struggle in spring and summer; when affected during these seasons, disease arises. Their Spleen Meridian (Zu Taiyin) is marked by a solid or steady condition. Those of the Dagong type correspond to the Left-foot Yangming; above it, their appearance is graceful and elegant. Those of the Jiagong type correspond to the left foot Yangming; below it, they appear hesitant or cautious. Those of the Shaogong type correspond to the right foot Yangming; above it, they exhibit a rotating or flexible manner. Those of the Zuogong type correspond to the Right-foot Yangming; below it, their posture is upright and prominent.

	陰陽二十... :	
金形之人比於上商，似於白帝，其為人方面白色、小頭、小肩背小腹、小手足如骨發踵外，骨輕。身清廉，急心靜悍，善為吏，能秋冬，不能春夏，春夏感而病生。手太陰敦敦然，釱商之人比於左手陽明，陽明之上，廉廉然。右商之人，比於左手陽明，陽明之下脫脫然。左商之人比於右手陽明，陽明之上監監然。少商之人，比於右手陽明，陽明之下，嚴嚴然。
		Those with the Metal form are comparable to Shangshang and resemble Emperor Bai. They have square faces, white complexions, small heads, narrow shoulders and backs, small abdomens, small hands and feet; their bones extend outward from the heels as if emerging, and their skeletons appear light. Their bodies are clean and upright; they have quick minds yet calm dispositions, are good at serving as officials, thrive in autumn and winter, but struggle in spring and summer. When affected during these seasons, illness occurs. Their Lung Meridian (Shou Taiyin) is solid and steady; those of the Dianshang type correspond to the left-hand Yangming, above which they appear upright and clean. Those of the Youshang type correspond to the left hand Yangming; below it, their appearance is loose or detached. Those of the Zuoshang type correspond to the right-hand Yangming; above it, they appear observant and vigilant. Those of the Shaoshang type correspond to the Right-Hand Yangming; below it, their demeanor is solemn and strict.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
水形之人，比於上羽，似於黑帝，其為人，黑色面不平，大頭廉頤，小肩大腹動手足，發行搖身下尻長，背延延然。不敬畏善欺紹人，戮死。能秋冬不能春夏，春夏感而病生。足少陰汗汗然。大羽之人，比於右足太陽，太陽之上，頰頰然。少羽之人，比於左足太陽，太陽之下潔潔然。眾之為人，比於右足太陽，太陽之下潔潔然。桎之為人，比於左足太陽，太陽之上安安然。是故五形之人二十五變者，眾之所以相欺者是也。
		Those with the Water form are comparable to Shangyu and resemble Emperor Hei. They have dark complexions, uneven faces, large heads with sharp chins, narrow shoulders, broad abdomens; they move their hands and feet frequently, walk while swaying their bodies, have long lower buttocks, and their backs appear elongated and drooping. They show no reverence or fear, are good at deceiving others, and may meet a violent death. They thrive in autumn and winter but cannot endure spring and summer; when affected by these seasons, illness arises. Their Kidney Meridian (Zu Shaoyin) is marked by perspiration or a moist condition. Those of the Dayu type correspond to the Right-foot Taiyang; above it, their cheeks appear prominent and full. Those of the Shaoyu type correspond to the Left-foot Taiyang; below it, they are marked by a clean or pure condition. Those of the Zhongyu type correspond to the Right-foot Taiyin; below it, they also exhibit a clean and pure condition. Those of the Zhiyu type correspond to the Left-foottaiyang; above it, their demeanor is calm and composed. Therefore, these twenty-five variations of the five forms are what cause people to deceive one another.

7
打開字典
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
黃帝曰：得其形，不得其色何如？歧伯曰：形勝色，色勝形者，至其勝時年加，感則病行，失則憂矣。形色相得者，富貴大樂。黃帝曰：其形色相勝之時，年加可知乎？歧伯曰：凡年忌下上之人，大忌常加七歲，十六歲、二十五歲、三十四歲、四十三歲、五十二歲、六十一歲皆人之大忌，不可不自安也，感則病行，失則憂矣，當此之時，無為姦事，是謂年忌。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: If one knows their form but not their color, how would that be? Qibo said: When the form prevails over the color, or when the color prevails over the form, at their respective dominant times and years, if affected by external influences, illness will occur; if not properly managed, worry will follow. When form and color harmonize with each other, one attains wealth, honor, and great happiness. The Yellow Emperor asked: When the form and color are in opposition to each other, can we know when the years of influence will add up? Qibo said: Generally, for those with superior or inferior forms and colors, the most critical years are when seven is added to their age. The major critical years occur at ages 7, 16, 25, 34, 43, 52, and 61; these are significant times that one must not neglect. If affected during these periods, illness will arise; if neglected, worry follows. At such times, one should refrain from engaging in improper or harmful actions—this is known as the year's taboos (nianji).

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
黃帝曰：夫子之言脈之上下，血氣之候似知形氣，奈何？歧伯曰：足陽明之上血氣盛則髯美長，血少氣多則髯短，故氣少血多則髯少，血氣皆少則無髯。兩吻多畫，足陽明之下血氣盛則下毛美長至胸，血多氣少則下毛美短至臍，行則善高舉足，足趾少肉足善寒，血少氣多則肉而善瘃，血氣皆少則無毛有則稀、枯悴，善痿厥，足痺。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Your words about the rise and fall of meridians, and the signs of vital energy and blood seem to resemble knowledge of form and qi. How is this so? Qibo said: When the vital energy and blood in the Foot Yangming Meridian are abundant, the beard is beautiful and long; when there is little blood but much qi, the beard is short. Therefore, if there is less qi but more blood, the beard will be sparse; if both blood and qi are deficient, then no beard will grow at all. Those with prominent lips and many lines; when the vital energy and blood of the Foot Yangming are abundant, body hair grows beautifully and long down to the chest. When there is more blood but less qi, the body hair is beautiful yet short, reaching only to the navel; such individuals walk by lifting their feet high, have thin flesh on their toes, cold feet, and are prone to chilblains when there is little blood and much qi. If both blood and qi are insufficient, then no body hair will grow or it will be sparse and dry; they are likely to suffer from atrophy, cold extremities, and foot numbness.

	陰陽二十... :	
足少陽之上，氣血盛則通髯美長，血多氣少則通髯美短，血少氣多則少髯，血氣皆少則無鬚，感於寒濕則善痺。骨痛爪枯也。足少陽之下，血氣盛則脛毛美長，外踝肥；血多氣少則脛毛美短，外踝皮堅而厚，血少氣多則胻毛少，外踝皮薄而軟，血氣皆少則無毛，外踝瘦無肉。
		When the vital energy and blood in the upper part of the Foot Shaoyang Meridian are abundant, one has a full and long beard; when there is more blood but less energy, the beard is short yet beautiful. When there is little blood but more qi, the beard becomes sparse; if both blood and energy are deficient, no beard will grow at all; in such cases, exposure to cold and dampness easily leads to numbness and stiffness (bi syndrome). It also results in bone pain and dry nails. sluts When the vital energy and blood at the lower part of the Foot Shaoyang are abundant, the leg hair is beautiful and long, and the outer ankle is well-developed. When there is more blood and less qi, the leg hair will be short yet beautiful, with thick and firm skin on the outer ankle. When there is less blood but more qi, there will be little leg hair, and the skin on the outer ankle becomes thin and soft. If both blood and energy are insufficient, no body hair grows at all, and the outer ankles appear lean and lacking in flesh.

	陰陽二十... :	
足太陽之上，血氣盛則美眉，眉有毫毛血多氣少則惡眉，面多少理，血少氣多則面多肉，血氣和則美色，足太陽之下，血氣盛則跟肉滿，踵堅，氣少血多則瘦，跟空，血氣皆少則善轉筋，踵下痛。
		When the vital energy and blood on the upper part of the Foot Taiyang are abundant, one has beautiful eyebrows with fine hair. When there is more blood than qi, the eyebrows appear coarse or unattractive, and the face shows few wrinkles. When there is less blood and more qi, the face appears fleshy. When blood and energy are in harmony, the complexion becomes attractive. On the lower part of the Foot Taiyang Meridian, when vital energy and blood are abundant, the heel flesh is full and the soles are firm; if there is little qi but much blood, one will be thin with hollow heels. If both blood and energy are lacking, it leads to frequent muscle cramps and pain beneath the soles of the feet.

	陰陽二十... :	
手陽明之上，血氣盛則髭美。血少氣多則髭惡，血氣皆少則無髭。手陽明之下血氣盛則腋下毛美，手魚肉以溫，氣血皆少則手瘦以寒。
		When the vital energy and blood above the Hand Yangming Meridian are abundant, then one has beautiful mustaches. If there is little blood but excessive qi, the mustache will be coarse or unattractive; if both blood and vital energy are deficient, then no mustache grows at all. When the vital energy and blood below the Hand Yangming Meridian are strong, the armpit hair is beautiful, the fleshy part of the palm (shouyu) remains warm; if both qi and blood are deficient, the hands become thin and cold.

	陰陽二十... :	
手少陰之上，血氣盛則眉美以長，耳色美，血氣皆少則耳焦惡色。手少陽之下，血氣盛則手卷多肉以溫，血氣皆少則寒以瘦，氣少血多則瘦以多脈。
		When the vital energy and blood along the upper part of the Hand Shaoyin Meridian are abundant, eyebrows become beautiful and long, with attractive ear coloration. When both qi and blood are insufficient, ears appear dry and have an unattractive hue. When the vital energy and blood beneath the Hand Shaoyang Meridian are strong, one's hands have abundant flesh and remain warm. When both qi and blood deficiency occur, the hands become cold and thin; if there is more blood but insufficient qi, then the hands appear thin with prominent veins.

	陰陽二十... :	
手太陽之上，血氣盛則有多鬚，面多肉以平，血氣皆少則面瘦惡色。手太陽之下，血氣盛則掌肉充滿，血氣皆少則掌瘦以寒。
		When the vital energy and blood are strong in the upper part of the Hand Taiyang Meridian, one has abundant mustache hair, a full face with smooth features. When both blood and qi are lacking, the face becomes thin and displays an unattractive complexion. When the vital energy and blood flow abundantly in the lower part of the Hand Taiyang meridian, the palm is full with ample flesh. When both qi and xue are insufficient, the palms become thin and cold to the touch.
