打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
黃帝曰：二十五人者，刺之有約乎？歧伯曰：美眉者，足太陽之脈，氣血多，惡眉者，血氣少，其肥而澤者，血氣有餘，肥而不澤者，氣有餘，血不足，瘦而無澤者，氣血俱不足，審察其形氣有餘不足而調之，可以知逆順矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: For these twenty-five types of individuals, is there a general principle for acupuncture treatment? Qibo said: Those with beautiful eyebrows have the Foot Taiyang meridian with abundant qi and blood; those with coarse or unattractive eyebrows have deficient qi and blood. If someone is plump and lustrous, it indicates an excess of both qi and blood. A person who is fat but not lustrous has excessive qi but insufficient blood. One who is thin and lacks luster suffers from a deficiency in both qi and blood. By carefully examining the form and condition to determine whether there is an excess or deficiency, one can adjust accordingly; this allows for understanding of what is harmonious (shun) or disharmonious (ni).

15
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	陰陽二十... :	
黃帝曰：刺其諸陰陽奈何？歧伯曰：按其寸口人迎，以調陰陽，切循其經絡之凝澀，結而不通者，此於身皆為痛痺，甚則不行，故凝澀，凝澀者，致氣以溫之血和乃止。其結絡者，脈結血不和，決之乃行，故曰：氣有餘於上者，導而下之，氣不足於上者，推而休之，其稽留不至者，因而迎之，必明於經隧，乃能持之，寒與熱爭者，導而行之，其宛陳血不結者，則而予之，必先明知二十五人則血氣之所在，左右上下，刺約畢也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should acupuncture be applied to the various yin and yang meridians? Qibo said: Examine the Cun Kou and Ren Ying to regulate yin and yang. Carefully palpate along the meridians for stagnation or obstruction; where there is a knot that does not flow, it manifests as pain and stiffness in the body, and if severe, movement becomes impossible. Therefore, when there is stagnation, guide qi with warmth until blood harmonizes and the condition resolves. Those with obstructed collaterals have stagnant meridians and disharmonious blood; by resolving the obstruction, the flow is restored. Therefore it is said: When there is excess qi in the upper part, guide it downward; when there is insufficient qi above, support and allow it to rest. For those where qi lingers without reaching its destination, meet it head-on to facilitate movement. One must clearly understand the meridian pathways to properly manage such conditions. In cases of conflict between cold and heat, guide the flow accordingly. If stagnant blood has not yet formed a clot, treat it gently with appropriate methods. It is essential first to fully comprehend the twenty-five types in order to know where qi and blood reside—whether on the left or right, above or below—and thus acupuncture can be applied effectively. 


65. 五音五味 Five Tones and Five Flavors

	五音五味:	
右徵與少徵，調右手太陽上。左商與左徵，調左手陽明上。少徵與大宮，調左手陽明上。右角與大角，調右手少陽下。大徵與少徵，調左手太陽上。眾羽與少羽，調右足太陽下。少商與右商調右手太陽下。桎羽與眾羽，調右足太陽下。少宮與大宮，調右足陽明下。判角與少角，調右足少陽下。釱商與上商，調右足陽明下。釱商與上角，調左足太陽下。
		Youzheng yu Shaozheng, diao youshou taiyang shang. Zuo shang yu zuo zheng, diao zuoshou yangming shang. Shaozheng yu Dagong, diao zuoshou Yangming shang. Youjiao yu Dajiao, diao youshou Shaoyang xia. Dazheng yu Shaozheng diao zuoshou Taiyang shang. Zhongyu yu Shaoyu, diao youzu Taiyang xia. Shaoshang yu Yushang, diao youshou Taiyang xia. Zhiyu yu Zhongyu, diao youzu taiyang xia. Shaogong yu Dagong, diao youzu yangming xia. Panjiao yu Shaojiao, diao youzu Shaoyang xia. Dianshang yu Shangshang, diao youzu Yangming xia. Dianshang yu shangjiao, diao zuozu taiyang xia.

上徵與右徵同穀麥、畜羊、果杏，手少陰藏心，色赤味苦，時夏。上羽與大羽，同穀大豆，畜彘，果栗，足少陰藏腎，色黑味鹹，時冬。上宮與大宮同穀稷，畜牛，果棗，足太陰藏脾，色黃味甘，時季夏。上商與右商同穀黍，畜雞，果桃，手太陰藏肺，色白味辛，時秋。上角與大角，同穀麻、畜犬、果李，足厥陰藏肝，色青味酸，時春。

打開字典
顯示影印本
	五音五味:	
黃帝曰：婦人無鬚者，無血氣乎？歧伯曰：沖脈任脈皆起於胞中，上循背裏，為經絡之海，其浮而外者，循腹右上行，會於咽喉，別而絡唇口，血氣盛則充膚熱肉，血獨盛者澹滲皮膚，生毫毛。今婦人之生有餘於氣，不足於血以其數脫血也，沖任之脈，不榮口唇，故鬚不生焉。
		Huangdi said: Women who have no beard, do they lack blood and qi? Qibo said: The Chong meridian and Ren meridian both originate in the uterus, ascend along the back internally, serving as the sea of meridians and collaterals. Those that float externally follow the right side of the abdomen upward, converging at the throat; they branch off to network with the lips and mouth. When blood and qi are abundant, they fill the skin and warm the flesh. If only blood is abundant, it diffuses through the skin, producing fine hair. Now women are born with excess qi but insufficient blood, because they frequently lose blood. Therefore, the Chong and Ren meridians do not nourish the lips and mouth; hence, beards do not grow.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五音五味:	
黃帝曰：士人有傷於陰，陰氣絕而不起，陰不用，然其鬚不去，其故何也？宦者獨去何也？願聞其故。歧伯曰：宦者去其宗筋，傷其沖脈，血寫不復，皮膚內結，唇口不榮故鬚不生。
		Huangdi said: Some men suffer injury to their yin, and the yin qi is cut off so that it does not rise; the yin becomes inactive. Yet their beards do not fall out. What is the reason for this? Why do eunuchs uniquely lose their beards, then? I wish to hear the reason. Qibo said: Eunuchs have their seminal vessels removed, which injures the Chong meridian. Blood is lost and does not return; the skin becomes internally constricted, and the lips and mouth are not nourished, so beards do not grow.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五音五味:	
黃帝曰：其有天宦者，未嘗被傷，不脫於血，然其鬚不生其故何也？歧伯曰：此天之所不足也，其任沖不盛、宗筋不成，有氣無血，唇口不榮，故鬚不生。
		Huangdi said: Are there those who are naturally eunuchs, never having been injured and not losing blood, yet their beards still do not grow? What is the reason for that? Qibo said: This is a natural insufficiency. Their Ren and Chong meridians are not strong, their seminal vessels do not develop properly; they have qi but lack blood, so the lips and mouth remain un-nourished, hence beards do not grow.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五音五味:	
黃帝曰：善乎哉！聖人之通萬物也，若日月之光影，音聲鼓響，聞其聲而知其形，其非夫子，孰能明萬物之精。是故聖人，視其顏色黃赤者，多熱氣，青白者少熱氣，黑色者多血少氣，美眉者，太陽多血；通髯極須者，少陽多血，美鬚者陽明多血，此其時然也。
		Huangdi said: Well spoken! Sages understand all things, just as the light and shadow of the sun and moon, or the sound and echo of a drum. Hearing its sound, they know its form. If not for you, Master, who could illuminate the essence of all things? Therefore, sages observe that those with yellow or red complexions have much heat qi; those with blue or white complexions have little heat qi. Those with dark complexions have abundant blood but deficient qi. Those with well-formed eyebrows have the Taiyang meridian with abundant blood. Those with thick beards and long whiskers have much blood in the Shaoyang meridian; those with handsome beards have abundant blood in the Yangming meridian. This is how it naturally is.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	五音五味:	
夫人之常數，太陽常多血少氣，少陽常多氣少血，陽明常多血多氣，厥陰常多氣少血，少陰常多血少氣，太陰常多血少氣，此天之常數也。
		In the normal pattern of human beings, the Taiyang meridian usually has abundant blood but deficient qi; the Shaoyang meridian usually has abundant qi but deficient blood. The Yangming meridian typically has both abundant blood and qi. The jueyin meridian commonly has abundant qi but scarce blood. The Shaoyin meridian often has abundant blood but insufficient qi, while the Taiyin meridian generally has ample blood but lacks qi. This is the natural pattern established by Heaven. 

66. 百病始生 Origin of a Hundred Illnesses

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	百病始生:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：夫百病之始生也，皆生於風雨寒暑，清濕喜怒，喜怒不節則傷藏，風雨則傷上，清濕則傷下。三部之氣所傷異類，願聞其會，歧伯曰：三部之氣各不同或起於陰或起於陽請言其方，喜怒不節則傷藏，藏傷則病起於陰也，清濕襲虛，則病起於下，風雨襲虛，則病起於上，是謂三部，至於其淫泆，不可勝數。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "When all kinds of illnesses first arise, they are all caused by wind and rain, cold and heat, dampness and emotional states such as joy and anger. If one's emotions of joy and anger are not regulated properly, they will injure the internal organs; wind and rain injure the upper part of the body, while cold and dampness injure the lower part." The injuries caused by the three types of qi are different in nature. I would like to hear your explanation, said the Yellow Emperor. Qi Bo replied: The three types of qi differ from each other; some originate from yin and others from yang. Please allow me to explain their characteristics. If joy and anger are not properly regulated, they injure the internal organs, and when the internal organs are injured, illness originates from the yin aspect. When cold dampness takes advantage of a weakened state, illness arises from the lower part; when wind and rain take advantage of a weakened condition, illness arises from the upper part. This is called the three divisions. As for their further spread and effects, they cannot be fully counted.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	百病始生:	
黃帝曰：余固不能數，故問先師願卒聞其道，歧伯曰：風雨寒熱不得虛，邪不能獨傷人。卒然逢疾風暴雨而不病者，蓋無虛，故邪不能獨傷人。此必因虛邪之風，與其身形，兩虛相得，乃客其形。兩實相逢，眾人肉堅，其中於虛邪也因於天時，與其身形，參以虛實，大病乃成，氣有定舍，因處為名，上下中外，分為三員。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I naturally cannot count them all, so I ask my revered teacher to please complete the explanation of this principle." Qi Bo replied, "Wind, rain, cold, and heat cannot cause illness without a deficiency; pathogenic factors cannot harm a person alone. Those who suddenly encounter strong winds or heavy rain but do not fall ill are so because they have no deficiency; thus, pathogenic factors cannot harm them alone. This must occur when a person's body is already in a state of deficiency and encounters external pathogenic wind; the deficiency within the body and the external pathogen combine, allowing the pathogen to take hold. When both the body and external environment are strong, a person's flesh is firm, and thus they can resist the invasion of pathogenic factors. However, when one encounters seasonal influences along with their own bodily condition, combining these three aspects—seasonal conditions, internal state, and strength or deficiency—a major illness may develop. The qi has fixed locations where it resides; names are given according to these locations. These locations in terms of upper, lower, inner, and outer parts are divided into three categories.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
相關討論
	百病始生:	
是故虛邪之中人也，始於皮膚，皮膚緩則腠理開，開則邪從毛髮入，入則抵深，深則毛髮立，毛髮立則淅然，故皮膚痛。留而不去，則傳舍於絡脈，在絡之時，痛於肌肉，故痛之時息，大經乃代，留而不去，傳舍於經，在經之時，洒淅喜驚。留而不去，傳舍於輸，在輸之時，六經不通四肢，則肢節痛，腰脊乃強，留而不去，傳舍於伏衝之脈，在伏衝之時體重身痛，留而不去，傳舍於腸胃，在腸胃之時，賁響腹脹，多寒則腸鳴飱泄，食不化，多熱則溏出糜。留而不去，傳舍於腸胃之外，募原之間，留著於脈，稽留而不去，息而成積，或著孫脈，或著絡脈，或著經脈，或著輸脈，或著於伏衝之脈，或著於膂筋，或著於腸胃之募原，上連於緩筋，邪氣淫泆，不可勝論。
		Therefore, when external pathogenic factors invade the body, they first affect the skin and muscles. When the skin is loose, the interstices between tissues open; once opened, pathogens enter through the hair follicles. As they penetrate deeper, the hairs stand on end, causing a shivering sensation, hence pain in the skin. If the pathogenic factors remain and do not leave, they will transfer to the collateral vessels. When pathogens reside in the collaterals, pain occurs in the muscles; thus, during episodes of pain, there is a sensation of relief, after which the major meridians take over. If the pathogens still remain without leaving, they then transfer into the main meridians. When residing in the main meridians, one experiences chills and easily becomes startled. If the pathogenic factors still remain and do not depart, they transfer to the shu points. When residing at the shu points, the six meridians become obstructed, affecting the limbs; thus, joint pain occurs, and the lower back and spine become stiff. If the pathogens continue without leaving, they move into the fu chong vessels. During this stage, one experiences heaviness in the body and generalized pain. If still not resolved, the pathogenic factors transfer to the intestines and stomach. When residing there, symptoms include abdominal distension with gurgling sounds; if cold predominates, intestinal rumbling and watery diarrhea occur, along with undigested food; if heat is predominant, loose stools mixed with mucus appear. If the pathogenic factors continue to remain and do not depart, they settle outside the intestines and stomach, in the region between the mu points and yuan qi. They adhere to the vessels and linger without leaving; over time, they accumulate into masses. These may attach to the collateral vessels, or to the connecting vessels, or to the main meridians, or to the shu points, or to the fu chong vessels, or to the muscles of the back, or to the mu yuan area between the intestines and stomach; they extend upward and connect with the tendons. The spread of pathogenic qi is extensive and cannot be fully described.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	百病始生:	
黃帝曰：願盡聞其所由然。歧伯曰：其著孫絡之脈而成積者，其積往來上下，臂小孫絡之居也，浮而緩，不能句積而止之，故往來移行腸胃之間，水湊滲注灌，濯濯有音，有寒則䐜䐜滿雷引，故時切痛，其著於陽明之經則挾臍而居，飽食則益大，飢則益小。其著於緩筋也，似陽明之積，飽食則痛，飢則安。其著於腸胃之募原也，痛而外連於緩筋，飽食則安，飢則痛。其著於伏沖之脈者，揣之應手而動，發手則熱氣下於兩股，如湯沃之狀。其著於膂筋，在腸後者飢則積見，飽則積不見，按之不得。其著於輸之脈者，閉塞不通，津液不下，孔竅干壅，此邪氣之從外入內，從上下也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I wish to hear a complete explanation of how these conditions arise." Qi Bo replied: "When pathogenic factors adhere to the fine collateral vessels and form accumulations, these masses move up and down because they reside in the minor collaterals. The qi is superficial and slow-moving; it cannot effectively restrain or localize the accumulation, so the mass moves back and forth between the intestines and stomach. Fluids gather and seep through, producing a sound like flowing water. When cold is involved, there is a feeling of fullness with rumbling sounds that resonate like thunder, hence intermittent sharp pain occurs. If the pathogenic factors adhere to the Yangming meridian, they reside near the navel; after eating, the mass becomes larger, and when hungry, it diminishes somewhat." When pathogenic factors adhere to weak tendons, the condition resembles an accumulation on the Yangming meridian; pain occurs after eating, but subsides when hungry. When pathogenic factors adhere to the mu yuan region between the intestines and stomach, pain occurs with an outward connection to weak tendons; after eating, one feels relief, but experiences pain when hungry. When pathogenic factors adhere to the fu chong vessels, pressing on them will elicit a response with movement; when one releases pressure, heat rises down both thighs, as if scalded by hot water. When pathogenic factors adhere to the back muscles and tendons, particularly in the area behind the intestines, the accumulation becomes evident when hungry but disappears after eating; pressing on it will not reveal anything. When pathogenic factors adhere to the shu vessels, they block and obstruct the flow; body fluids cannot descend, leading to dryness and obstruction in the orifices. This represents how external pathogenic influences enter from outside into the interior of the body and move downward.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	百病始生:	
黃帝曰：積之始生，至其已成，奈何？歧伯曰：積之始生，得寒乃生，厥乃成積也，黃帝曰：其成積奈何？歧伯曰：厥氣生足悗，悗生脛寒，脛寒則血脈凝澀，血脈凝澀則寒氣上入于腸胃，入于腸胃則䐜脹，䐜脹則腸外之汁沫迫聚不得散，日以成積。卒然多食飲，則腸滿，起居不節，用力過度，則絡脈傷，陽絡傷則血外溢，血外溢則衄血，陰絡傷則血內溢，血內溢則後血。腸胃之絡傷則血溢於腸外，腸外有寒，汁沫與血相搏，則併合凝聚不得散，而積成矣。卒然中外於寒，若內傷於憂怒，則氣上逆，氣上逆則六俞不通，溫氣不行，凝血蘊裏而不散，津液澀滲，著而不去，而積皆成矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "How does an accumulation begin to form until it is fully developed?" Qi Bo replied, "The initial formation of an accumulation occurs when cold is involved; it becomes a solidified mass through stagnation. The Yellow Emperor asked, "How exactly does this accumulation develop?" Qi Bo replied: "Stagnant qi gives rise to foot numbness; numbness leads to cold in the shins. When the shins become cold, blood and vessels congeal and stagnate. As the blood and vessels stagnate, cold qi rises upward into the intestines and stomach. Once it enters there, distension occurs. With this distension, the fluid secretions outside the intestines gather together and cannot disperse; over time, these form an accumulation." If one suddenly consumes excessive food and drink, the intestines become full. If daily activities are not regulated properly and physical exertion is excessive, the collateral vessels will be injured. When yang collaterals are injured, blood escapes outward, resulting in nosebleeds; when yin collaterals are injured, the blood flows inward, leading to rectal bleeding. When the collateral vessels of the intestines and stomach are injured, blood overflows into the area outside the intestines. If cold is present in this external region, fluid secretions mix with the blood; they combine and coagulate without dispersing, thus forming an accumulation. If one is suddenly exposed to external cold or internally affected by sorrow and anger, the qi will rise upward against its normal direction. When qi rises abnormally, the six 俞 (shu) points become obstructed; warm qi cannot circulate properly. Blood stagnates within and does not disperse, while body fluids become stagnant and seep improperly, adhering to tissues without leaving—thus, accumulations are formed.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	百病始生:	
黃帝曰：其生於陰者，奈何？歧伯曰：憂思傷心，重寒傷肺，忿怒傷肝，醉以入房，汗出當風傷脾，用力過度，若入房汗出洛，則傷腎，此內外三部之所生病者也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "What is the nature of accumulations that originate from yin?" Qi Bo replied: "Excessive worry and thought injure the heart; severe cold injures the lungs; anger and resentment injure the liver; becoming drunk and engaging in sexual activity, or sweating and then being exposed to wind, injures the spleen. Excessive physical exertion or engaging in sexual activity while perspiring heavily injures the kidneys. These are the internal illnesses caused by the three divisions of yin."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	百病始生:	
黃帝曰：善治之奈何？歧伯答曰：察其所痛，以知其應，有餘不足，當補則補，當寫則寫，毋逆天時，是謂至治。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "What is the proper method for treating these conditions?" Qi Bo replied: "Examine the location of pain to understand its corresponding pattern. Determine whether there is excess or deficiency; when appropriate, tonify what is deficient and drain what is excessive. Do not act contrary to the natural rhythms of heaven and time. This is called supreme treatment." 

67. 行鍼 Needle Insertion

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	行鍼:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：余聞九鍼於夫子，而行之於百姓，百姓之血氣，各不同形，或神動而氣先鍼行；或氣與鍼相逢；或鍼已出，氣獨行；或數刺乃知；或發鍼而氣逆；或數刺病益劇。凡此六者，各不同形，願聞其方。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qu Bo, "I have learned about the nine kinds of needles from you and applied them to the common people. The blood and qi of the common people differ in form; some are so responsive that their vital energy moves before the needle is even inserted." or the qi meets with the needle; or after the needle has been removed, the qi continues to flow on its own; or it takes several insertions before one becomes aware of it; or when the needle is inserted, the qi flows in a reversed direction; or after several insertions, the illness becomes more severe. All these six situations differ in form; I would like to hear your explanation of their treatment methods.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	行鍼:	
歧伯曰：重陽之人，其神易動，其氣易往也。黃帝曰：何謂重陽之人？歧伯曰：重陽之人，熇熇高高，言語善疾，舉足善高，心肺之藏氣有餘，陽氣滑盛而揚，故神動而氣先行。
		Qu Bo said, "A person with excessive Yang has a spirit that is easily stirred and qi that moves readily." The Yellow Emperor asked, "What is meant by a person of excessive Yang?" Qu Bo said, "A person of excessive Yang is hot-tempered and tall in stature. Their speech is quick, their steps are high, and the heart and lungs have an excess of energy. The Yang qi is smooth and abundant, thus causing the spirit to be stirred and the qi to move before the needle is inserted."

3
打開字典
顯示影印本
	行鍼:	
黃帝曰：重陽之人而神不先行者，何也？歧伯曰：此人頗有陰者也。黃帝曰：何以知其頗有陰也。歧伯曰：多陽者，多喜；多陰者，多怒，數怒者，易解，故曰頗有陰。其陰陽之離合難，故其神不能先行也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "For a person of excessive Yang whose spirit does not precede the movement of the qi, why is that?" Qu Bo said, "This person has somewhat Yin characteristics." The Yellow Emperor asked, "How can one know that they have somewhat Yin characteristics?" Qu Bo said, "Those with excessive Yang tend to be cheerful; those with excessive Yin tend to become angry easily. Frequent anger is easily resolved, hence it is said they have somewhat Yin characteristics." Because the separation and union of their Yin and Yang are difficult, their spirit cannot move ahead first.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	行鍼:	
黃帝曰：其氣與鍼相逢，奈何？歧伯曰：陰陽和調，而血氣淖澤滑利，故鍼入而氣出，疾而相逢也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What is meant by 'the qi meets with the needle'?" Qu Bo said, "When Yin and Yang are in harmony and the blood and qi are moist, smooth, and flowing freely, the needle enters and the qi emerges quickly, thus meeting with the needle."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	行鍼:	
黃帝曰：鍼已出而氣獨行者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：其陰氣多而陽氣少，陰氣沉而陽氣浮者內藏，故鍼已出，氣乃隨其後，故獨行也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "In the case where after the needle has been withdrawn the qi continues to move on its own, what kind of qi causes this?" Qu Bo said, "This occurs when the Yin qi is abundant and the Yang qi is deficient. The Yin qi sinks while the Yang qi floats inwardly stored; therefore, after the needle has been removed the qi follows behind it, hence moving on its own."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	行鍼:	
黃帝曰：數刺乃知，何氣使然？歧伯曰：此人之多陰而少陽，其氣沉而氣往難，故數刺乃知也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "In the case where one becomes aware only after several insertions, what kind of qi causes this?" Qu Bo said, "This person has excessive Yin and deficient Yang. Their qi is sinking and difficult to move forward; therefore, one becomes aware of it only after several insertions."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	行鍼:	
黃帝曰：鍼入而氣逆者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：其氣逆與其數刺病益甚者，非陰陽之氣，浮沉之勢也。此皆麤之所敗，工之所失，其形氣無過焉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "In the case where inserting the needle causes the qi to flow in a reversed direction, what kind of qi is responsible for this?" Qu Bo said, "The cases where the qi flows in reverse and where repeated insertions worsen the illness are not due to Yin-Yang energies or the tendencies of floating and sinking." These situations all result from the practitioner's failure and mistakes, but there is nothing wrong with the patient's physical form or qi. 

68. 上膈 Upper Obstruction

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	上膈:	
黃帝曰：氣為上膈者，食飲入而還出，余已知之矣。蟲為下膈。下膈者，食焠時乃出，余未得其意，願卒聞之。歧伯曰：喜怒不適，食飲不節，寒溫不時，則寒汁流於腸中。流於腸中則蟲寒，蟲寒則積聚，守於下管，則腸胃充郭，衛氣不營，邪氣居之。人食則蟲上食，蟲上食則下管虛，下管虛則邪氣勝之，積聚以留，留則癰成，癰成則下管約。其癰在管內者，即而痛深，其癰在外者，則癰外而痛浮，癰上皮熱。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "When qi causes upper obstruction, food and drink are taken in but immediately expelled again; I already know this." Parasites cause lower obstruction. Those with lower obstruction vomit food only after it has been digested; I have not yet grasped the meaning, and I wish to hear a complete explanation. Qibo said: "When joy and anger are not properly regulated, when food and drink consumption is not moderate, and when one does not observe proper timing for cold or warmth, then cold fluids flow into the intestines." When these fluids flow into the intestines, they chill the parasites; when the parasites are chilled, they congregate and gather, lodging in the lower passages. This causes the intestines and stomach to become distended like a shell, preventing Wei qi from circulating properly, allowing pathogenic qi to reside there. When a person eats, the parasites ascend to feed; when they ascend to feed, the lower passages become empty. When the lower passages are empty, pathogenic qi prevails there, leading to accumulation and stagnation. Stagnation leads to abscess formation, and once an abscess forms, the lower passage becomes constricted. If the abscess is inside the passage, it causes deep and immediate pain; if the abscess is outside, then the abscess appears externally with superficial pain, and the skin above feels hot.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	上膈:	
黃帝曰：刺之奈何？歧伯曰：微按其癰，視氣所行，先淺刺其傍，稍內益深，逐而刺之，毋過三行，察其沈浮，以為深淺。已刺必熨，令熱入中，日使熱內，邪氣益衰，大癰乃潰。伍以參禁，以除其內，恬憺無為，乃能行氣，後以咸苦，化谷乃下矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How should one treat this condition?" Qibo said: "Gently press on the abscess, observe the flow of qi, and first make shallow needle insertions at its sides. Gradually advance deeper with each insertion, following the direction of the condition, but do not exceed three passes. Examine whether it is sinking or floating to determine the depth." After needling, apply heat therapy to ensure warmth penetrates inward. Each day encourage internal warming so that pathogenic qi gradually weakens, and the large abscess will then rupture. Combine this with dietary restrictions to eliminate internal stagnation; maintain a tranquil and effortless state in order to regulate qi. Afterwards, use salty and bitter substances to transform food, allowing it to descend properly. 

69. 憂恚無言 Sudden Anxiety and Loss of Voice


1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	憂恚無言:	
黃帝問於少師曰：人之卒然憂恚，而言無音者，何道之塞？何氣出行？使音不彰？願聞其方。少師答曰：咽喉者，水谷之道也。喉嚨者，氣之所以上下者也。會厭者，聲音之戶也。口唇者，聲音之扇也。舌者，聲音之機也。懸壅垂者，聲音之關者。頏顙者，分氣之所泄也。橫骨者，神氣所使主發舌者也。故人之鼻洞涕出不收者，頏顙不開，分氣失也。是故厭小而疾薄，則發氣疾，其開闔利，其出氣易，其厭大而厚，則開闔難，其氣出遲，故重言也。人卒然無音者，寒氣客於厭，則厭不能發，發不能下，至其開闔不致，故無音。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Master Shao Shi: "When a person suddenly becomes anxious and agitated, yet speaks without voice, what passage is blocked?" What qi has departed? Causing the voice to be inaudible? I wish to hear your explanation. Master Shao Shi replied: "The throat and pharynx are the pathways for water and food." "The larynx is where qi moves up and down." 立志 The epiglottis is the gate of voice. The lips are the shutter of voice. The tongue is the mechanism of voice. The uvula is the gate of voice. The chin and forehead are where qi disperses. The horizontal bone is the master controlled by spirit and qi, which mainly governs the tongue. Therefore, when a person's nose runs with mucus that cannot be controlled, it is because the chin and forehead are not open, resulting in a loss of proper qi distribution. Therefore, if the epiglottis is small and thin, qi will be emitted quickly; its opening and closing are smooth, and exhalation is easy. If the epiglottis is large and thick, then opening and closing become difficult, and exhalation becomes slow, hence speech sounds heavy. When a person suddenly loses their voice, it is because cold qi lodges in the epiglottis; thus, the epiglottis cannot open, and when it does open, it cannot descend properly. As a result, its opening and closing functions are impaired, hence there is no voice.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	憂恚無言:	
黃帝曰：刺之奈何？歧伯曰：足之少陰，上係於舌，絡於橫骨，終於會厭。兩寫其血脈，濁氣乃避。會厭之脈，上絡任脈，取之天突，其厭乃發也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "How should one treat this with acupuncture?" Qibo said: "The foot's Shaoyin meridian ascends to connect with the tongue, connects with the horizontal bone, and terminates at the epiglottis." Drain both the blood vessels; then turbid qi will disperse. The meridian of the epiglottis ascends and connects with the Conception Vessel; take the point Tian Tu, then the epiglottis will open.



70. 寒熱 Cold and Heat

1
打開字典
顯示影印本
	寒熱:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：寒熱瘰癧在於頸腋者，皆何氣使生？歧伯曰：此皆鼠瘻寒熱之毒氣也，留於脈而不去者也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked 岐 ber: "Cold and heat swellings in the neck and armpits—what kind of qi causes them to arise?" Qi Bo said: "These are all toxic qi of cold and heat swellings, which remain in the vessels without dispersing."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱:	
黃帝曰：去之奈何？歧伯曰：鼠瘻之本，皆在於藏，其末上出於頸腋之間，其浮於脈中，而未內著於肌肉，而外為膿血者，易去也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "How can they be dispelled?" Qi Bo said: "The root of swellings all lies in the zang organs, and their manifestation appears between the neck and armpit. When they float within the vessels but have not yet deeply adhered to the muscles, forming pus and blood externally, it is easy to remove them."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱:	
黃帝曰：去之奈何？歧伯曰：請從其本引其末，可使衰去，而絕其寒熱。審按其道以予之，徐往徐來以去之，其小如麥者，一刺知，三刺而已。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "How can they be removed?" Qi Bo said: "Please follow their root and draw out their manifestation; this can cause them to weaken and dissipate, thus eliminating the cold and heat." Carefully examine their path to treat them, proceeding slowly and gently to remove the condition. If it is small as a grain of wheat, one needle insertion will be known effective; three insertions will suffice.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	寒熱:	
黃帝曰：決其生死奈何？歧伯曰：反其目視之，其中有赤脈，上下貫瞳子，見一脈，一歲死；見一脈半，一歲半死；見二脈，二歲死；見二脈半，二歲半死；見三脈，三歲而死。見赤脈不下貫瞳子，可治也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "How can we determine life and death in such cases?" Qi Bo said: "Turn the patient's eyes upward to look at them. If there are red vessels inside, running vertically through the pupil—seeing one such vessel indicates death within a year; seeing one and a half vessels means death in one and a half years; seeing two vessels, death within two years; seeing two and a half vessels, death in two and a half years; seeing three vessels, death within three years." If the red vessel does not extend downward through the pupil, it is treatable. 


71. 邪客 Xie Ke

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝問于伯高曰：夫邪氣之客人也，或令人目不瞑不臥出者，何氣使然？伯高曰：五穀入于胃也，其糟粕津液宗氣，分為三隧。故宗氣積於胸中，出於喉嚨，以貫心脈，而行呼吸焉。營氣者，泌其津液，注之於脈，化以為血，以榮四末，內注五藏六府，以應刻數焉。節氣者，出其悍氣之慓疾，而先行於四末分肉皮膚之間，而不休者也。晝日行於陽，夜行於陰，常從足少陰之分間，行於五藏六府，今厥氣客於五藏六府，則衛氣獨衛其外，行於陽，不得入于陰。行於陽則陽氣盛，陽氣盛則陽橋陷，不得入于陰，陰虛，故目不瞑。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao, "When pathogenic qi invades the body, sometimes it causes people to have difficulty closing their eyes and not sleep. What kind of qi causes this?" Bo Gao replied, "When the five grains enter the stomach, their dregs, body fluids, and vital qi are divided into three channels." Therefore, the vital qi accumulates in the chest, emerges from the throat, passes through the heart and vessels, and thus regulates respiration. The nutritive qi extracts body fluids, infuses them into the vessels, transforms them into blood, nourishes the extremities, and internally flows to the five zang and six fu organs in accordance with precise temporal divisions. The defensive qi, being vigorous and swift-moving, travels ahead in the spaces between the extremities, muscles, and skin without ceasing. During the day, it travels along the yang meridians; at night, it moves through the yin meridians. It constantly follows the pathways of the foot shaoyin meridian and circulates among the five zang and six fu viscera. Now, if this qi becomes obstructed in the five zang or six fu organs, then defensive qi remains on the exterior, travels along the yang channels, and cannot enter into the yin channels. When it remains in the yang, the yang qi becomes excessive; when yang qi is excessive, the yin cannot receive it. The defensive qi cannot enter into yin, causing yin deficiency, hence the eyes do not close and sleep does not occur.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝曰：善。治之奈何？伯高曰：補其不足，寫其有餘，調其虛實，以通其道，而去其邪。飲以半夏湯一劑，陰陽已通，其臥立至。黃帝曰：善。此所謂決瀆壅塞，經絡大通，陰陽和得者也。願聞其方。伯高曰：其湯方以流水千里以外者八升，揚之萬遍，取其清五升，煮之，炊以葦薪火，沸置秫米一升，治半夏五合，徐炊，令竭為一升半，去其滓，飲汁一小杯，日三稍益，以知為度，故其病新發者，復杯則臥，汗出則已矣。久者，三飲而已也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Well said." How should this be treated? Bo Gao replied, "Supplement the deficiency, drain the excess, regulate the balance between deficiency and excess, clear the pathways, and remove the pathogenic factors." Administer one dose of Banxia Decoction. Once yin and yang are harmonized, sleep will return immediately. The Yellow Emperor said, "Well said." This is what is meant by clearing obstructions and blockages, greatly unblocking the meridians and collaterals, thus achieving harmony between yin and yang. I would like to hear about its formula. Bo Gao said, "The formula for this decoction uses eight sheng of flowing water from a thousand li away. Stir it ten thousand times and take five sheng of the clear portion to cook. Use fire made from reed wood to bring it to a boil. Add one sheng of red millet and five ge of processed Banxia (Pinellia). Cook slowly until reduced to one sheng and a half, then strain out the residue. Drink a small cup; take three times daily, gradually increasing the amount as appropriate, using awareness of effects as the measure. Therefore, for those who have recently contracted the illness, drinking another cup will induce sleep, and sweating will signify recovery." For chronic cases, three cups are sufficient.

打開字典

顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝問於伯高曰：願聞人之肢節以應天地奈何？伯高答曰：天圓地方，人頭圓足方以應之。天有日月，人有兩目；地有九州，人有九竅；天有風雨，人有喜怒；天有雷電，人有聲音；天有四時，人有四肢；天有五音，人有五藏；天有六律，人有六府；天有冬夏，人有寒熱；天有十曰，人有手十指；辰有十二，人有足十指，莖垂以應之，女子不足二節，以抱人形；天有陰陽，人有夫妻；歲有三百六十五日，人有三百六十節；地有高山，人有肩膝；地有深谷，人有腋膕；地有十二經水，人有十二經脈；地有泉脈，人有衛氣；地有草蓂，人有毫毛；天有晝夜，人有臥起；天有列星，人有牙齒；地有小山，人有小節；地有山石，人有高骨；地有林木，人有募筋；地有聚邑，人有膕肉；歲有十二月，人有十二節；地有四時不生草，人有無子。此人與天地相應者也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Bo Gao: "I would like to know how the limbs and joints of a person correspond with heaven and earth." Bo Gao replied, "Heaven is round and earth is square; accordingly, the human head is round and the feet are square to correspond with them." Heaven has the sun and moon; humans have two eyes. Earth has nine provinces; humans have nine orifices. Heaven has wind and rain; humans experience joy and anger. Heaven has thunder and lightning; humans have voice and sound. Heaven has four seasons; humans have four limbs. Heaven has five tones; humans have five zang organs. Heaven has six pitch pipes; humans have six fu organs. Heaven has winter and summer; humans experience cold and heat. Heaven has ten days; humans have ten fingers on their hands. The zodiac has twelve divisions; humans have ten toes on their feet, and the penis corresponds to this. Women lack two segments but embrace the human form. Heaven has yin and yang; humans have husbands and wives. A year has 365 days; a human body has 360 joints. Earth has high mountains; humans have shoulders and knees. Earth has deep valleys; humans have armpits and popliteal fossae. Earth has twelve major rivers; humans have twelve meridians. Earth has springs and underground water channels; humans have defensive qi. Earth has grasses and herbs; humans have fine body hair. Heaven has day and night; humans have sleeping and waking. Heaven has constellations; humans have teeth. Earth has small hills; humans have minor joints. Earth has mountains and rocks; humans have prominent bones. Earth has forests and trees; humans have tendons and muscles. Earth has clusters of towns; humans have soft flesh in the popliteal region. A year has twelve months; a human body has twelve joints corresponding to them. Earth has four seasons in which grass does not grow; humans have infertility. This is how the human body corresponds with heaven and earth.

	邪客:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：余願聞持鍼之數，內鍼之理，縱舍之意，扞皮開腠理，奈何？脈之屈折，出入之處，焉至而出，焉至而止，焉至而徐，焉至而疾，焉至而入，六府之輸於身者，余願盡聞，少序別離之處，離而入陰，別而入陽，此何道而從行，願盡聞其方。歧伯曰：帝之所問，鍼道畢矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo: "I wish to learn about the techniques of holding the needle, the principles of inserting it, the meaning of advancing and retreating, how to lift the skin and open the pores, what should be done?" The vessels have bends and turns, with points of entry and exit. At which locations do they emerge? At which do they stop? Where do they proceed slowly, where quickly, and where do they enter? Regarding the six fu organs' pathways in the body, I wish to hear fully about them. Please explain their sequence, how they diverge and separate—where they branch into yin and where they diverge into yang—and through which channels they travel. I wish to know all of this completely. Qi Bo said, "What the Emperor has asked encompasses the entire art of acupuncture."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：手太陰之脈，出於大指之端，內屈，循白肉際，至本節之後太淵，留以澹，外屈，上於本節下，內屈，與陰諸絡會於魚際，數脈並注，其氣滑利，伏行壅骨之下，外屈，出於寸口而行，上至於肘內廉，入于大筋之下，內屈，上行臑陰，入腋下，內屈，走肺。此順行逆數之屈折也。心主之脈，出於中指之端，內屈，循中指內廉以上，留於掌中，伏行兩骨之間，外屈，出兩筋之間，骨肉之際，其氣滑利，上二寸，外屈，出行兩筋之間，上至肘內廉，入于小筋之下，留兩骨之會，上入于胸中，內絡於心脈。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I wish to hear it in full." Qi Bo said: "The Taiyin meridian of the hand originates at the tip of the thumb, turns inward, follows along the junction between the white flesh and the red skin, reaching behind the first metacarpophalangeal joint at Taiyuan. It lingers briefly before turning outward again, ascending below this joint, then turning inward once more to converge with the yin collateral vessels at Yuji. Several vessels merge here; their qi is smooth and flowing, moving along beneath the prominent bone. Turning outward, it emerges at Cun Kou (the radial artery) and proceeds upward to the inner side of the elbow, entering beneath a large tendon. It turns inward again, ascends along the inner upper arm, enters under the armpit, turns inward once more, and reaches the lung." This is an example of the meridian's natural course with its twists and bends. The Jueyin meridian of pericardium originates at the tip of the middle finger, turns inward along its medial side upward, lingers in the palm. Its qi flows smoothly between two bones, then turns outward to emerge between two tendons and the junction of bone and flesh. It ascends two cun, turns outward again, travels between two tendons, reaches the inner side of the elbow above, enters beneath a small tendon, lingers at the meeting point of two bones, and ascends into the chest where it internally connects with the heart vessels.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝曰：手少陰之脈，獨無俞，何也？歧伯曰：少陰，心脈也。心者，五藏六府之大主也，精神之所舍也，其藏堅固，邪弗能容也。容之則心傷，心傷則神去，神去則死矣。故諸邪之在於心者，皆在於心之包絡。包絡者，心主之脈也，故獨無俞焉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Why is it that only the Shaoyin meridian of the hand has no shu points?" Qi Bo replied, "Shaoyin is the heart vessel." The heart is the supreme master of the five zang and six fu, the dwelling place of spirit and mind. Its organ is strong and solid; pathogenic influences cannot take hold within it. If such influences were to invade, the heart would be harmed; if the heart is harmed, the spirit departs; and when the spirit departs, death occurs. Therefore, any pathogenic influences that affect the heart actually reside in its pericardium. The pericardium is the vessel of the pericardium, hence it alone has no shu points.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝曰：少陰獨無俞者，不病乎？歧伯曰：其外經病而藏不病，故獨取其經於掌後銳骨之端。其餘脈出入屈折，其行之徐疾，皆如手少陰心主之脈行也。故本俞者，皆因其氣之虛實疾徐以取之，是謂因沖而寫，因衰而補，如是者，邪氣得去，真氣堅固，是謂因天之序。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "If Shaoyin alone lacks shu points, does that mean it is immune to illness?" Qi Bo replied: "Illness may affect its exterior meridian but not the zang itself; therefore, for treatment one should exclusively take points on this meridian at the sharp bone's end behind the palm." The other vessels, in terms of their points of emergence and exit, twists and turns, as well as the speed or slowness of their flow, all follow patterns similar to those of the Shaoyin heart vessel. Therefore, when selecting shu points for treatment, one should take into account the deficiency or excess of its qi and its rapidity or slowness. This is called draining according to the surge of pathogenic influences and supplementing during their decline. In this way, pathogenic factors are expelled and true qi remains strong—this is known as following the order of heaven.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝曰：持鍼縱舍奈何？歧伯曰：必先明知十二經脈之本末，皮膚之寒熱，脈之盛衰滑澀。其脈滑而盛者，病日進；虛而細者，久以持；大以澀者，為痛痺。陰陽如一者，病難治。其本末尚熱者，病尚在；其熱以衰者，其病亦去矣。持其尺，察其肉之堅脆，大小滑澀，寒溫燥濕。因視目之五色，以知五藏，而決死生。視其血脈，察其色，以知其寒熱痛痺。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How should one manipulate the needle in advancing and retreating?" Qi Bo replied, "One must first clearly understand the origins and terminations of the twelve meridians, the coldness or heat of the skin, as well as whether the pulse is strong, weak, smooth, or rough." If the pulse is smooth and full, the illness is progressing daily. If it is deficient and fine, the condition has persisted for a long time. If it is large yet rough, it indicates pain and bi syndrome (bi represents obstruction or stagnation). When yin and yang are equally balanced, the illness becomes difficult to treat. If the origins and terminations still feel warm, the disease is still present. When the warmth has diminished, the illness will also depart. Grasp the cun-kou (radial artery), examine the firmness or softness of the flesh, its size, smoothness or roughness, and whether it is cold, warm, dry, or moist. By observing the five colors of the eyes, one can discern the condition of the five zang organs and determine life or death. Examine the blood vessels and observe their color to know whether there is coldness, heat, pain, or bi syndrome.

		The Yellow Emperor said: "Regarding the manipulation of the needle in advancing and retreating, I have not yet grasped its meaning." Qi Bo replied: "The method of holding the needle requires uprightness and correctness, as well as calmness and stability." First discern deficiency or excess before proceeding with rapid or slow manipulation. The left hand holds the bone, while the right hand follows along it. Do not grasp the flesh tightly. When draining, hold the needle upright and straight; when supplementing, ensure the skin is closed properly. Assist with needles to guide qi so that pathogenic influences disperse and true qi can remain in its proper place.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	邪客:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：人有八虛，各何以候？歧伯答曰：以候五藏。黃帝曰：候之奈何？歧伯曰：肺心有邪，其氣留於兩肘；肝有邪，其氣流於兩腋；脾有邪，其氣留於兩髀；腎有邪，其氣留於兩膕。凡此八虛者，皆機關之室，真氣之所過，血絡之所遊。邪氣惡血，固不得住留。住留則傷筋絡骨節；機關不得屈伸，故痀攣也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo: "There are eight virtual locations in a person. What do they indicate?" Qi Bo replied, "They are used to observe the five zang organs." The Yellow Emperor asked: "How should they be observed?" Qi Bo replied, "When the lung and heart are affected by pathogenic influences, their qi lingers at both elbows;" "When the liver is affected, its qi flows to both armpits;" "When the spleen is affected, its qi lingers at both thighs;" "When the kidney is affected, its qi remains in both popliteal regions." All these eight virtual locations are chambers of the joints and passages through which true qi flows, as well as where blood vessels travel. Pathogenic influences and stagnant blood cannot remain there for long. If they do linger, they will injure the tendons, vessels, bones, and joints; the joints will lose their ability to bend and stretch, hence causing stiffness and cramping.


		72. 通天 Tongtian

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
黃帝問於少師曰：余嘗聞人有陰陽，何謂陰人？何謂陽人？少師曰：天地之間，六合之內，不離於五，人亦應之，非徒一陰一陽而已也，而略言耳，口弗能遍明也。黃帝曰：願略聞其意，有賢人聖人，心能備而行之乎？少師曰：蓋有太陰之人，少陰之人，太陽之人，少陽之人，陰陽和平之人。凡五人者，其態不同，其筋骨氣血各不等。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Master Shao Shi, "I have often heard that people possess yin and yang; what is meant by a 'yin person'?" What is meant by a "yang person"? Master Shao Shi said, "Between Heaven and Earth, within the six directions, nothing is separated from the Five Elements. People also correspond to them; it is not merely one yin and one yang, but a general statement, for words cannot fully explain all aspects." The Yellow Emperor said, "I wish to briefly hear the meaning. Are there virtuous and sage people who can fully understand and practice it?" Master Shao Shi said, "There are those of great yin, those of lesser yin, those of great yang, those of lesser yang, and those with a balanced harmony of yin and yang." These five types of people have different appearances, and their sinews, bones, qi, and blood are each unequal.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
黃帝曰：其不等者，可得聞乎？少師曰：太陰之人，貪而不仁，下齊湛湛，好內而惡出，心和而不發，不務於時，動而後之，此太陰之人也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "May I hear about these differences?" Master Shao Shi said, "Those of great yin are greedy and unkind; they appear solemn and deep, preferring inwardness while disliking outward action. Their minds are harmonious but not expressive, and they do not strive to act in accordance with the times. They move only after being prompted—these are people of great yin."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
少陰之人，小貪而賊心，見人有亡，常若有得，好傷好害，見人有榮，乃反慍怒，心疾而無恩，此少陰之人也。
		Those of lesser yin are slightly greedy and possess a malicious heart. When they see others losing something, they feel as if they have gained; they enjoy causing harm and injury. Upon seeing others honored, they become resentful and angry. Their hearts are quick to anger and devoid of kindness—these are people of lesser yin."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
太陽之人，居處於於，好言大事，無能而虛說，志發乎四野，舉措不顧是非，為事如常自用，事雖敗，而常無悔，此太陽之人也。
		Those of great yang dwell in a manner that is ostentatious; they like to speak grandly about major matters, lacking ability yet making empty claims. Their ambitions extend as far as the four corners of the world. In their actions and conduct, they disregard right or wrong, doing things according to their own will regardless of consequences. Even when their endeavors fail, they never regret it—these are people of great yang."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
少陽之人，諟諦好自責，有小小官，則高自宜，好為外交，而不內附，此少陽之人也。
		Those of lesser yang are meticulous and prone to self-reproach. When holding a minor official position, they overrate themselves; they prefer external dealings but do not attach themselves internally—these are people of lesser yang."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
陰陽和平之人，居處安靜，無為懼懼，無為欣欣，婉然從物，或與不爭，與時變化，尊則謙謙，譚而不治，是謂至治。
		Those with a balanced harmony of yin yang reside in tranquility; they do not act out of fear or excitement. They gently follow the course of things, yielding without contention, and adapt to changes in time. When honored, they remain humble; when speaking, they do so without seeking control—this is called the highest state of order."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
古之善用鍼艾者，視人五態，乃治之。盛者寫之，虛者補之。
		Ancient practitioners skilled in acupuncture and moxibustion would observe a person's five constitutions before treating them. Those with excess should be drained; those with deficiency should be supplemented.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
黃帝曰：治人之五態奈何？少師曰：太陰之人，多陰而無陽，其陰血濁，其衛氣澀，陰陽不和，緩筋而厚皮，不之疾寫，不能移之。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "How should one treat the five constitutions of people?" Master Shao Shi said, "Those of great yin have excessive yin and deficient yang. Their yin blood is turbid, their defensive qi sluggish; yin and yang are disharmonious. They possess relaxed sinews and thick skin. If not promptly drained, their condition cannot be transformed."

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
少陰之人，多陰少陽，小胃而大腸，六府不調，其陽明脈小，而太陽脈大，必審調之，其血易脫，其氣易敗也。
		Those of lesser yin have more yin and less yang; they possess a small stomach and large intestines, with the six fu organs out of harmony. Their Yangming meridian is weak while their Taiyang meridian is strong. One must carefully regulate this condition, for their blood is easily depleted and their qi easily weakened.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
太陽之人，多陽而少陰，必謹調之，無脫其陰，而寫其陽。陽重脫者易狂，陰陽皆脫者，暴死，不知人也。
		Those of great yang have excessive yang and deficient yin; one must carefully regulate them, preserving their yin while draining their yang. Those who suffer excessive depletion of yang are prone to mania; those in whom both yin and yang have been depleted will die suddenly, losing consciousness.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
少陽之人，多陽少陰，經小而絡大，血在中而氣外，實陰而虛陽。獨寫其絡脈，則強氣脫而疾，中氣不足，病不起也。
		Those of lesser yang possess excessive yang and deficient yin. Their main meridians are small while their collateral vessels are large; blood resides internally while qi is externalized, resulting in a condition where the yin is solid but the yang is weak. If one drains only their collateral vessels, strong qi will be depleted too quickly; middle qi becomes insufficient and the illness cannot recover.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
陰陽和平之人，其陰陽之氣和，血脈調，謹診其陰陽，視其邪正，安容儀，審有餘不足，盛則寫之，虛則補之，不盛不虛，以經取之，此所以調陰陽，別五態之人者也。
		Those with a balanced harmony of ying and yang possess harmonious yin-yang qi, regulated blood vessels. One must carefully diagnose their yin and yang, observe whether the condition is deviant or normal, examine their demeanor, determine if there is excess or deficiency. If excessive, drain it; if deficient, supplement it. When neither excessively full nor deficient, treat according to the meridians—this is how one regulates yin and yang and distinguishes among the five constitutional types of people.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
黃帝曰：夫五態之人者，相與毋故，卒然新會，未知其行也，何以別之？少師答曰：眾人之屬，不知五態之人者，故五五二十五人，而五態之人不與焉。五態之人，尤不合於眾者也。黃帝曰：別五態之人，奈何？少師曰：太陰之人，其狀黮黮然黑色，念然下意，臨臨然長大，膕然未僂，此太陰之人也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Regarding these five constitutional types of people, when they unexpectedly meet for the first time and their dispositions are unknown, how can one distinguish them?" Master Shao Shi replied, "Among ordinary people who do not understand the five constitutional types, there are twenty-five categories in total, but those of the five constitutions are excluded from this classification." Those of the five constitutions especially do not conform to the general population. The Yellow Emperor said, "How can one distinguish among the five constitutional types?" Master Shao Shi said, "Those of great yin appear dark and sallow in complexion; they are quiet and reserved, tall and imposing in stature, yet their posture is relaxed but not hunched—these are people of great ying."

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
少陰之人，其狀清然竊然，固以陰賊，立而躁嶮，行而似伏，此少陰之人也。
		Those of lesser yin appear clear yet furtive, firm in their secretive and malicious nature. When standing, they are restless; when walking, they seem to crouch—these are people of lesser yi."

15
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
太陽之人，其狀軒軒儲儲，反身折膕，此太陽之人也。
		Those of great yang appear proud and expansive, often bending backward with their knees flexed—these are people of greater yang."

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
少陽之人，其狀立則好仰，行則好搖，其兩臂兩肘，則常出於背，此少陽之人也。
		Those of lesser yang, when standing, prefer to hold their heads high; when walking, they tend to sway. Their two arms and elbows often protrude behind them—these are people of minor yang."

17
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	通天:	
陰陽和平之人，其狀委委然，隨隨然，顒顒然，愉愉然，䁢䁢然，豆豆然，眾人皆曰君子，此陰陽和平之人也。
		Those with a balanced harmony of yang and yin appear graceful, composed, dignified, pleasant, bright-eyed, and refined. Everyone calls them gentlemen—these are people with harmonious balance between ying and yang." 


73. 官能 Qualifications of Practitioners

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：余聞九鍼於夫子，眾多矣不可勝數，餘推而論之，以為一紀。余司誦之，子聽其理，非則語余，請正其道，令可久傳後世無患，得其人乃傳，非其人勿言。歧伯稽首再拜曰：請聽聖王之道。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qu Bo, "I have heard from you about the Nine Needles; they are numerous and cannot be counted. I have considered them carefully and summarized them into one system." I shall recite them, and you should listen to their principles. If there is anything incorrect, please tell me so that I may correct the doctrine, ensuring it can be passed down for a long time without harm to future generations. It should only be transmitted to those who are worthy; if someone is unworthy, do not speak of it. Qu Bo bowed respectfully twice and said, "Please allow me to listen to the teachings of the Sage King."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
黃帝曰：用鍼之理，必知形氣之所在，左右上下，陰陽表裏，血氣多少，行之逆順，出入之合，謀伐有過。知解結，知補虛寫實，上下氣門，明通於四海。審其所在，寒熱淋露以輸異處，審於調氣，明於經隧，左右肢絡，盡知其會。寒與熱爭，能合而調之，虛與實鄰，知決而通之，左右不調，把而行之，明於逆順，乃知可治，陰陽不奇，故知起時。審於本末，察其寒熱，得邪所在，萬刺不殆。知官九鍼，刺道畢矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "To understand the principles of using needles, one must know where the form and vital energy are located—on the left or right, above or below; the yin-yang relationships between exterior and interior; the amount of blood and qi; their direction of flow, whether it is normal or reversed; and how they enter and exit in harmony. One should plan carefully to correct any excesses." One must know how to resolve obstructions, how to tonify deficiencies and drain excesses, understand the openings for qi above and below, and have clear knowledge that extends throughout the four seas. Carefully determine their locations, and regulate cold and heat by directing the flow to different areas. Be thorough in regulating qi, understand clearly the meridians and channels, as well as the collateral networks of the left and right limbs, and fully know all their junctions. When cold and heat contend, one must be able to harmonize and regulate them. When deficiency is adjacent to excess, one should know how to resolve and unblock it. If the left and right sides are disharmonious, grasp and guide the flow accordingly. Understanding what is normal or reversed allows one to know if treatment is possible. When yin and yang are not in conflict, one can thus discern the time when symptoms will subside. Carefully examine the root and branch, observe cold or heat conditions, identify where pathogenic factors reside, and then acupuncture can be applied effectively without danger. Understanding the functions of the Nine Needles means one has fully grasped the principles of needling.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
明於五俞徐疾所在，屈伸出入，皆有條理。言陰與陽，合於五行，五藏六府，亦有所藏，四時八風，盡有陰陽。各得其位，合於明堂，各處色部，五藏六府。察其所痛，左右上下，知其寒溫，何經所在。審皮膚之寒溫滑澀，知其所苦，膈有上下，知其氣所在。先得其道，稀而疏之，稍深以留，故能徐入之。大熱在上，推而下之；從上下者，引而去之；視前痛者，常先取之。大寒在外，留而補之；入于中者，從合寫之。鍼所不為，灸之所宜。上氣不足，推而揚之；下氣不足，積而從之；陰陽皆虛，火自當之。厥而寒甚，骨廉陷下，寒過於膝，下陵三里。陰絡所過，得之留止，寒入于中，推而行之；經陷下者，火則當之；結絡堅緊，火所治之。不知所苦，兩蹻之下，男陰女陽，良工所禁，鍼論畢矣。
		Be clear about where the Five Shu points are located in terms of slow or rapid flow, and understand how to flexibly manipulate insertion and withdrawal; all should be done with order and method. Speaking of yin and yang, they correspond to the Five Elements; the five zang organs and six fu organs also have their respective stores. The four seasons and eight winds all embody yin and yang. Each has its proper position, corresponding to the Hall of Brightness (mingtang), with each organ located in its respective color and area on the face: the five zang and six fu organs. Examine where the pain is, whether it occurs on the left or right side, above or below; know its coldness or warmth, and determine which meridian it belongs to. Carefully observe the skin's coldness, warmth, smoothness, or roughness; know what troubles the patient. Understand the upper and lower regions of the diaphragm to determine where the qi resides. First obtain the correct method, then insert the needle gently and sparsely; gradually deepen it to retain it, thus allowing a slow insertion. If intense heat is located above, push it downward; When the condition originates from below and rises upward, guide it away. For visible pain, always treat it first. If severe cold is present externally, retain the needle and tonify it; When pathogenic factors have entered internally, follow the method of draining at the He points. Conditions for which acupuncture is ineffective are suitable for moxibustion. If the upper qi is insufficient, push and lift it upward; If the lower qi is insufficient, accumulate and follow its flow. When both yin and yang are deficient, fire (moxibustion) should be applied directly. łoż If there is cold extremity with severe coldness, the bones and sinews sink downward; if the cold extends beyond the knees, treat Xiaoling Sanli (ST36). When cold pathogenic factors affect the yin meridians and become lodged there, if they penetrate inward, push them to move along their course; If the meridian is depressed or deficient, fire (moxibustions) should be applied. When collaterals are knotted and tightly bound, it is treated with fire (moxibustion). If one does not know the source of their suffering, it lies beneath the two Yao vessels; in men, it relates to yin, and in women, to yang. A skilled physician must avoid treating these areas—thus concludes the discussion on acupuncture theory.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
用鍼之服，必有法則，上視天光，下司八正，以辟奇邪，而觀百姓，審於虛實，無犯其邪。是得天之靈，遇歲之虛，救而不勝，反受其殃，故曰必知天忌，乃言鍼意。
		The practice of using acupuncture must follow certain principles. Look upward to the celestial phenomena, and downward to the eight cardinal directions, in order to ward off unusual pathogenic influences. Observe the people carefully, discerning deficiency and excess, and avoid offending any external pathogens. If one fails to understand the subtle forces of heaven and encounters a year marked by deficiency, attempting treatment may not succeed but instead bring harm. Therefore it is said: One must certainly know the heavenly taboos before speaking about acupuncture intent.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
法於往古，驗於來今，觀於窈冥，通於無窮。麤之所不見，良工之所貴。莫知其形，若神髣彿。
		Follow the methods of ancient times, verify them in present practice, observe the profound and subtle, and understand what is boundless. What an ordinary person cannot see is precisely what a skilled physician values highly. Its form remains unseen, as if it were a spirit appearing faintly and indistinctly.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
邪氣之中人也，洒淅動形；正邪之中人也，微先見於色，不知於其身，若有若無，若亡若存，有形無形，莫知其情。是故上工之取氣，乃救其萌芽；下工守其已成，因敗其形。
		When pathogenic qi invades the body, it causes shivering and trembling of the form; When righteous and pathogenic qi interact in the body, subtle changes first appear on the complexion; one may not be aware of it within their own body. It is as if present or absent, as though lost yet existing—its form appears and disappears, and no one truly knows its nature. Therefore, a superior physician who takes qi treats the condition at its initial stage; An inferior physician waits until the disease has fully formed and then causes further damage to the body.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
是故工之用鍼也，知氣之所在，而守其門戶，明於調氣，補寫所在，徐疾之意，所取之處。寫必用員，切而轉之，其氣乃行，疾而徐出，邪氣乃出，伸而迎之，遙大其穴，氣出乃疾。補必用方，外引其皮，令當其門，左引其樞，右推其膚，微旋而徐推之，必端以正，安以靜，堅心無解，欲微以留，氣下而疾出之，推其皮，蓋其外門，真氣乃存。用鍼之要，無忘其神。
		Therefore, when a skilled physician uses acupuncture needles, they know where the qi is located and guard its entry and exit points. They understand how to regulate qi, determine which points need tonification or drainage, apply slow or rapid techniques, and select appropriate locations for treatment. When draining, the needle must be rounded; press and rotate it gently so that qi can flow. Withdraw quickly but slowly to expel pathogenic factors. Extend the insertion point to meet them, widen the acupuncture site, and the qi will exit rapidly. When tonifying, the needle must be square in shape. Gently stretch the skin outward to align with the opening; pull left at its pivot and push right on the skin. Rotate slightly and slowly advance it, keeping the body upright and calm. Maintain a focused mind without distraction, apply slight retention, allow qi to descend, then withdraw quickly. Push back the skin and close the external opening so that true qi remains within. The key to using acupuncture is never to forget its spiritual essence.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	官能:	
雷公問於黃帝曰：鍼論曰：得其人乃傳，非其人勿言，何以知其可傳？黃帝曰：各得其人，任之其能，故能明其事。雷公曰：願聞官能奈何？黃帝曰：明目者，可使視色；聰耳者，可使聽音；捷疾辭語者，可使傳論；語徐而安靜，手巧而心審諦者，可使行鍼艾，理血氣而調諸逆順，察陰陽而兼諸方。緩節柔筋而心和調者，可使導引行氣；疾毒言語輕人者，可使唾癰呪病；爪苦手毒，為事善傷者，可使按積抑痺。各得其能，方乃可行，其名乃彰。不得其人，其功不成，其師無名。故曰：得其人乃言，非其人勿傳，此之謂也。手毒者，可使試按龜，置龜於器下，而按其上，五十日而死矣，手甘者，復生如故也。
		Lei Gong asked the Yellow Emperor, "The acupuncture theory says: 'Transmit it only to those who are worthy; do not speak of it if someone is unworthy.' How can one know whether a person is suitable for transmission?" The Yellow Emperor said, "Each person must be found who is worthy and entrusted with the ability; only then can they understand its principles." Lei Gong said, "I would like to hear about what constitutes a qualified practitioner." The Yellow Emperor said: One who has sharp eyesight may be entrusted with observing complexion; One who has keen hearing may be entrusted with listening to sounds; One who is quick and articulate in speech may be entrusted with conveying theories; One who speaks slowly and calmly, has nimble hands and a careful mind may be entrusted to perform acupuncture and moxibustion, regulate blood and qi, adjust imbalances in flow, discern yin and yang relationships, and integrate various therapeutic approaches. One who moves slowly with supple tendons and maintains a harmonious mind may be entrusted to guide qi through qigong or breathing exercises; One who speaks harshly, uses violent language, and looks down on others may be used for spitting at abscesses or reciting incantations to treat illness; One who has strong fingers and hands, and is skilled in causing injury through touch may be entrusted with pressing on masses or suppressing numbness. Each person must find their own ability; only then can the method be applied, and their name will become renowned. If one does not find a worthy person to transmit it to, the achievement cannot succeed, and the teacher remains unknown. Therefore it is said: "Speak of it only when you find a worthy person; do not transmit it if the person is unworthy." This is what that means. One with strong, forceful hands may be entrusted to test pressure by pressing on a turtle. Place the turtle beneath an object and press from above; after fifty days, it will die. One whose touch is gentle and kind, however, can restore the turtle to its original condition. 

74. 論疾診尺 On Diagnosing Illness by Examining the Cun and Chi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論疾診尺:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：余欲無視色持脈，獨調其尺，以言其病，從外知內，為之奈何？歧伯曰：審其尺之緩急小大滑澀，肉之堅脆，而病形定矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "I wish to diagnose illness without observing the patient's complexion or taking his pulse, but by examining only the cun and chi regions of the wrist. How can I understand internal conditions from external signs?" Qi Bo said, "By carefully examining the cun and chi for signs of tension or relaxation, size, smoothness or roughness, as well as the firmness or softness of the flesh, the nature of the illness can be determined."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論疾診尺:	
視人之目窠上微癰，如新臥起狀，其頸脈動，時咳，按其手足上，窅而不起者，風水膚脹也。
		When a person's eye sockets appear slightly swollen, as if just waking up from lying down, the neck veins are pulsating, coughing occurs occasionally, and pressing on their hands and feet reveals deep depressions that do not quickly rebound, this indicates 风水肤胀 (wind-water skin swelling).

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論疾診尺:	
尺膚滑，其淖澤者，風也。尺肉弱者，解併，安臥脫肉者，寒熱，不治。尺膚滑而澤脂者，風也。尺膚澀者，風痺也。尺膚麤如枯魚之鱗者，水泆飲也。尺膚熱甚，脈盛躁者，病溫也，其脈甚而滑者，病且出也。尺膚寒，其脈小者，泄、少氣。尺膚炬然，先熱後寒者，寒熱也；尺膚先寒，久大之而熱者，亦寒熱也。
		If the cun and chi areas of the wrist feel smooth and moist with a glossy texture, it indicates wind-related illness. If the flesh in the cun and chi is weak, it indicates 解併 (loose joints). If a person lies down with emaciated flesh, it signifies alternating chills and fever, and such condition is incurable. If the cun and chi skin feels smooth and greasy, it indicates a wind-related illness. If the cun and chi feel rough or dry to the touch, it is indicative of wind-bi syndrome (wind arthralgia). If the skin over the cun and chi feels rough like the scales of a dried-up fish, it indicates water retention or fluid accumulation. If the cun and chi are very warm to the touch, and the pulse is strong and restless, it indicates a febrile disease. If the pulse is especially forceful and slippery, the illness will soon manifest or break out. If the cun and chi area feels cold to the touch and the pulse is weak, it indicates diarrhea and deficiency of vital energy (qi). If the skin over the cun region suddenly feels hot at first and then turns cold, this is a sign of alternating chills and fever. If the skin over the cun area initially feels cold but gradually becomes hot after some time, it is also a sign of alternating chills and fevers.

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	論疾診尺:	
肘所獨熱者，腰以上熱；手所獨熱者，腰以下熱。肘前獨熱者，膺前熱；肘後獨熱者，肩背熱。臂中獨熱者，腰腹熱；肘後麤以下三四寸熱者，腸中有蟲。掌中熱者，腹中熱；掌中寒者，腹中寒。魚上白肉有青血脈者，胃中有寒。
		If only the elbow region is warm to the touch, this indicates heat in the area above the waist. If only the hands are uniquely warm to the touch, it indicates heat below the waist. If the area in front of the elbow is uniquely warm, this indicates heat in front of the chest (the region of the sternum). If only the back of the elbow feels warm to the touch, it signifies heat in the shoulders and back. If the middle part of the arm is uniquely warm, it indicates heat in the waist and abdomen. lógica If the area behind the elbow, roughly three to four cun below it, feels rough and warm, there are parasites in the intestines. If the palm is warm, this indicates internal heat in the abdomen. If the palm feels cold, it signifies internal coldness in the abdomen. If there are bluish blood vessels on the white flesh at the base of the thumb (the "fish" area), it indicates coldness within the stomach.

尺炬然熱，人迎大者，當奪血；尺堅大，脈小甚，少氣，免有加，立死。
		If the cun and chi region is intensely hot, and the renying pulse is large or prominent, blood loss should be expected; If the cun and chi flesh feels firm and full but the pulse is extremely weak, it indicates deficiency of vital energy. If additional symptoms arise suddenly, death will occur immediately.

目赤色者病在心，白在肺，青在肝，黃在脾，黑在腎。黃色不可名者，病在胸中。
		Redness in the eyes indicates a condition affecting the heart; whiteness indicates involvement of the lungs; greenish coloration relates to the liver; yellow suggests the spleen is affected; and blackness points to an issue with the kidneys. If the yellow discoloration cannot be clearly identified or localized, the disease lies in the chest region.

		lógica When diagnosing eye pain, if red blood vessels run from above downward, it indicates Taiyang syndrome; If the red blood vessels extend upward from below, it indicates Yangming syndrome; If the red veins run inward from the outer part of the eye, it is a sign of Shaoyang syndrome.

診寒熱，赤脈上下至瞳子，見一脈一歲死；見一脈半，一歲半死；見二脈，二歲死；見二脈半，二歲半死；見三脈，三歲死。
		When diagnosing alternating chills and fever (cold-heat), if red blood vessels extend from above to below reaching the pupil, and one such vessel is observed, death will occur within a year; if one and a half such vessels are seen, death will occur in one and a half years; if two such vessels appear, death will occur within two years; if two and a half of these vessels are observed, death occurs in two and a half years; if three such vessels appear, the person will die within three years.

		When diagnosing toothache caused by dental caries, press along the yang meridians. If there is an excess condition, it will feel uniquely warm: if on the left side, warmth appears on the left; if on the right, warmth occurs on the right; if in the upper part, the upper area feels warm; and if in the lower part, the lower region feels warm.

診血脈者，多赤多熱，多青多痛，多黑為久痺，多赤、多黑、多青皆見者，寒熱。
		When diagnosing blood vessels, excessive redness indicates heat; excessive bluish color suggests pain; and excessive blackness signifies a long-standing bi syndrome (bi is a term for obstruction or stagnation). If all three—red, black, and blue—are prominently present, it indicates alternating chills and fever.

		If the body aches and the complexion is slightly yellow, with yellowish tooth plaque and yellowing of the fingernails or toenails, it indicates jaundice. If a person lies down comfortably with urine that is yellowish-red in color and the pulse is small and rough, it indicates lack of appetite.

人病，其寸口之脈，與人迎之脈小大等，及其浮沉等者，病難已也。
		When a person is ill, if the cunkou pulse and the renying (carotid) pulse are equal in size and depth—neither being more prominent than the other—the illness will be difficult to resolve.

		If a woman's Shaoyin meridian pulse on her hand is markedly active, she is pregnant.
