打開字典
顯示影印本
	論疾診尺:	
嬰兒病，其頭毛皆逆上者必死。耳間青脈起者掣痛。大便赤瓣飧泄，脈小者，手足寒，難已；飧泄，脈小，手足溫，泄易也。
		In an infant with illness, if all the hair on the head stands up in the opposite direction, it indicates certain death. If blue or green veins appear around the ears, it indicates cramping pain (spasmodic pain). If there is red-streaked diarrhea with undigested food and the pulse is weak, this indicates cold hands and feet, making recovery difficult; if the diarrhea consists of undigested food, the pulse is small but the hands and feet are warm, then the condition will resolve easily.

15
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論疾診尺:	
四時之變，寒暑之勝，重陰必陽，重陽必陰；故陰主寒，陽主熱，故寒甚則熱，熱甚則寒，故曰寒生熱，熱生寒，此陰陽之變也。
		The changes of the four seasons, the dominance of cold or heat, follow a pattern: excessive yin must transform into yang, and excessive yang must transform into yin; thus, yin governs cold and yang governs heat. Therefore, when cold becomes excessive it transforms into heat, and when heat is extreme it turns to cold. Hence it is said that cold gives rise to heat and heat gives rise to cold; this is the transformation of yin and yang.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	論疾診尺:	
故曰：冬傷於寒，春生病熱；春傷於風，夏生飧泄腸僻，夏傷於暑，秋生痎瘧；秋傷於濕，冬生咳嗽。是謂四時之序也。
		Therefore, it is said: "Injury from cold in winter leads to febrile illness in spring;" "injury from wind in spring results in diarrhea and intestinal disorders in summer; injury from heat in summer leads to alternating chills and fever in autumn;" "injury from dampness in autumn causes coughing in winter." This is called the sequence of the four seasons. 


75. 刺節真邪 Needling Jie: True and Evil Qi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝問于歧伯曰：余聞刺有五衛，奈何？歧伯曰：固有五衛，一曰振埃，二曰發蒙，三曰去爪，四曰徹衣，五曰解惑。黃帝曰：夫子言五衛，余未知其意。歧伯曰：振埃者，刺外經去陽病也；發蒙者，刺府俞，去府病也；去爪者，刺關節肢絡也；徹衣者，盡刺諸陽之奇俞也；解惑者，盡知調陰陽，補寫有餘不足，相傾移也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qu Bo, "I have heard that there are five types of guards in acupuncture; what are they?" Qu Bo said, "Indeed there are five types of guards: the first is Zhen'ai, the second is Famen, the third is Quzhua, the fourth is Cheyi, and the fifth is Jiehuo." The Yellow Emperor said, "You have spoken of the five types of guards, but I do not yet understand their meanings." Qu Bo said, "Zhen'ai refers to needling the exterior meridians to treat yang disorders; " "Famen means needling the fu shu (organ 俞 points) to eliminate diseases of the fu organs;" "Quzhua refers to needling joints and collateral vessels of the limbs;" "Cheyi means thoroughly needling all the extraordinary shu points of the yang meridians;" "Jiehuo refers to fully understanding how to regulate yin and yang, supplementing where there is deficiency and draining where there is excess, thus restoring balance."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：刺衛言振埃，夫子乃言刺外經，去陽病，余不知其所謂也。願卒聞之。歧伯曰：振埃者，陽氣大逆，上滿於胸中，憤瞋肩息，大氣逆上，喘喝坐伏，病惡埃煙，飼不得息，請言振埃，尚疾於振埃。黃帝曰：善。取之何如？歧伯曰：取之天容。黃帝曰：其咳上氣窮拙胸痛者，取之奈何？歧伯曰：取之廉泉。黃帝曰：取之有數乎？歧伯曰：取天容者，無過一里，取廉泉者，血變而止。帝曰：善哉。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Regarding the guard Zhen'ai in acupuncture, you have mentioned needling the exterior meridians and treating yang disorders. I do not understand what this means." "I would like to hear a complete explanation of it." Qu Bo said, "Zhen'ai refers to a condition where yang qi is greatly reversed and accumulates in the chest. The patient becomes angry and breathes with his shoulders raised; strong qi surges upward, causing labored breathing and coughing so severe that he must sit or lie down. He dislikes dust and smoke, and cannot breathe properly when eating. I will now explain Zhen'ai—its treatment is even more urgent than merely removing dust." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." "How should it be treated?" Qu Bo said, "Needle the Tianrong point." The Yellow Emperor asked, "For those who cough with upward rushing qi, feel breathless, and experience chest pain, how should they be treated?" Qu Bo said, "Needle the Lianquan point." The Yellow Emperor asked, "Is there a specific number of insertions for needling?" Qu Bo said, "When needling Tianrong, do not exceed one cun. When needling Lianquan, stop when the blood changes." The Emperor said, "Good indeed."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：刺衛言發蒙，餘不得其意。夫發蒙者，耳無所聞，目無所見，夫子乃言刺府俞，去府病，何輸使然，願聞其故。歧伯曰：妙乎哉問也。此刺之大約，鍼之極也，神明之類也，口說書卷，猶不能及也，請言發蒙耳，尚疾於發蒙也。黃帝曰：善。願卒聞之。歧伯曰：刺此者，必於日中，刺其聽宮，中其眸子，聲聞於耳，此其輸也。黃帝曰：善。何謂聲聞於耳？歧伯曰：刺邪以手堅按其兩鼻竅，而疾偃其聲，必應於鍼也。黃帝曰：善。此所謂弗見為之，而無目視，見而取之，神明相得者也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Regarding the guard Famen in acupuncture, I do not understand its meaning." "You mention Famen as a condition in which the ears hear nothing and the eyes see nothing. Yet you say that needling the fu shu points eliminates diseases of the fu organs. Which specific 俞 points cause this, and I wish to learn the reason behind it." Qu Bo said, "What a profound question you have asked." "This is the general principle of acupuncture, the utmost in needle techniques, and akin to divine wisdom. Even spoken words or written records cannot fully convey it. I will now explain Famen—its treatment is even more critical than merely addressing Famen itself." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." "I would like to hear the complete explanation." Qu Bo said, "When needling for this condition, it must be done at noon. Needle the Tinggong point and aim precisely at the pupil; the sound will reach the ear. This is its 俞 (shu) point." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." "What is meant by 'the sound reaching the ear'?" Qu Bo said, "When needling to expel pathogenic factors, firmly press both nasal passages with your hand and quickly suppress the sound; it will surely respond to the needle." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." "This is what is meant by acting without seeing and without visual observation—yet achieving success through the harmony of spiritual insight."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：刺衛言去爪，夫子乃言刺關節肢絡，願卒聞之。歧伯曰：腰脊者，身之大關節也；肢脛者，人之管以趨翔也；莖垂者，身中之機，陰精之候，津液之道也。故飲食不節，喜怒不時，津液內溢，乃下留於睪，血道不通，日大不休，俛仰不便，趨翔不能。此病榮然有水，不上不下，鈹石所取，形不可匿，常不得蔽，故命曰去爪。帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Regarding the guard Quzhua in acupuncture, you mentioned needling joints and collateral vessels of limbs. I would like to hear a full explanation." Qu Bo said, "The waist and spine are the major joints of the body; " "Limbs and shins are the conduits through which a person moves and walks;" "The genitalia are the pivot of the body, an indicator of yin essence, and the passage for bodily fluids." Therefore, if one indulges in eating and drinking without restraint, experiences joy and anger at irregular times, and internal body fluids overflow, they will accumulate downward into the testicles. When the blood pathways become obstructed and this condition persists without ceasing, it becomes difficult to bend or straighten the torso, and walking or moving becomes impossible." "This condition appears as if there is water in the body, neither ascending nor descending. It can be treated with moxibustion and stone therapy. The physical symptoms are evident and cannot be concealed; it is therefore called Quzhua." The Emperor said, "Good."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：刺衛言徹衣，夫子乃言盡刺諸陽之奇俞，未有常處也。願卒聞之。歧伯曰：是陽氣有餘，而陰氣不足，陰氣不足則內熱，陽氣有餘則外熱，內熱相搏，熱於懷炭，外畏綿帛近，不可近身，又不可近席。腠理閉塞，則汗不出，舌焦唇槁，腊乾益燥，飲食不讓美惡。黃帝曰：善。取之奈何？歧伯曰：取之於其天府大杼三痕，又刺中膂，以去其熱，補足手太陰，以去其汗，熱去汗稀，疾於徹衣。黃帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Regarding the guard Cheyi in acupuncture, you mentioned thoroughly needling all extraordinary shu points of yang meridians, which do not have fixed locations." "I would like to hear a complete explanation." Qu Bo said, "This condition arises when yang qi is excessive and yin qi is insufficient. When yin deficiency causes internal heat and excess yang produces external heat, the two combine to create intense internal heat, as if holding hot charcoal in one's arms. The person fears contact with soft fabrics or anything close; they cannot tolerate being near their body or even lying on a mat." "The pores and channels become blocked, so sweat cannot be produced. The tongue becomes dry and cracked, the lips wither, the skin dries further, and one no longer discriminates between good or bad food." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." "How should this condition be treated?" Qu Bo said, "Needle the Tianfu and Dazhu points on both sides, as well as three shu points. Also needle Zhonglu to eliminate the heat. Tonify the foot and hand Taiyin meridians to reduce sweating. Once the heat is dispelled and sweating decreases, the condition will improve more rapidly than with Cheyi." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：刺衛言解惑，夫子乃言盡知調陰陽，補寫有餘不足，相傾移也，惑何以解之？歧伯曰：大風在身，血脈偏虛，虛者不足，實者有餘，輕重不得，傾側宛伏，不知東西，不知南北，乍上乍下，乍反乍復，顛倒無常，甚於迷惑。黃帝曰：善。取之奈何？歧伯曰：寫其有餘，補其不足，陰陽平復，用鍼若此，疾於解惑。黃帝曰：善。請藏之靈蘭之室，不敢妄出也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "Regarding the guard Jiehuo in acupuncture, you said it means fully understanding how to regulate yin yang, supplement where there is deficiency and drain where there is excess so as to restore balance. How exactly does this dispel confusion?" Qu Bo said, "When a great wind pathogen resides in the body and causes uneven deficiency of blood vessels—where there is deficiency, it is insufficient; where there is excess, it is overabundant—the condition becomes unbalanced. The person experiences dizziness and disorientation, unable to distinguish directions or maintain stability, shifting unpredictably between up and down, reversal and restoration. This state is chaotic and more severe than mere confusion." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." "How should this condition be treated?" Qu Bo said, "Drain the excess and tonify the deficiency. When yin and yang are balanced and restored, using acupuncture in this way will resolve the condition more effectively than merely dispelling confusion." The Yellow Emperor said, "Good." "Please preserve this knowledge in the Linglan Hall; I dare not let it be carelessly disclosed."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：余聞刺有五邪，何謂五邪？歧伯曰：病有持癰者，有容大者，有狹小者，有熱者，有寒者，是謂五邪。黃帝曰：刺五邪奈何？歧伯曰：凡刺五邪之方，不過五章，癉熱消滅，腫聚散亡，寒痺益溫，小者益陽；大者必去，請道其方。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I have heard that acupuncture involves five types of evils. What are these five evils?" Qu Bo said, "Diseases include those that are persistent and abscess-like, those with large swellings, those with narrow or small swellings, those caused by heat, and those caused by cold. These are called the five evils." The Yellow Emperor asked, "How should one needle these five types of evils?" Qu Bo said, "Generally, the method for needling these five evils does not exceed five principles: eliminate abscesses and heat to make them disappear; disperse swellings so they vanish; warm cold and numb conditions to improve them; and strengthen yang in cases of small or weak symptoms;" "Large swellings must be removed. Please explain the method."

8
打開字典
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
凡刺癰邪，無迎隴，易俗移性。不得膿，脆道更行，去其鄉，不安處所乃散亡，諸陰陽過癰者，取之其輸寫之。
		"When needling abscesses, do not resist their nature; instead, adapt to them and change their course according to their characteristics." "If pus cannot be drained, change the method and redirect it away from its original location. When it no longer finds a suitable place to settle, it will disperse and vanish. For all abscesses caused by excessive yin or yang, needle their 俞 (shu) points for draining."

9
打開字典
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
凡刺大邪，日以小，泄奪其有餘，乃益虛。剽其通，鍼其邪，肌肉親視之，毋有反其真，刺諸陽分肉間。
		"When needling large evils, treat them gradually to reduce their size. Drain and remove the excess; this will help restore balance by reducing excessive conditions." "Follow their pathways, needle the evils directly. Observe the muscles closely; do not oppose their true nature. Insert needles between the flesh of yang meridians."

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
凡刺小邪，日以大，補其不足，乃無害。視其所在，迎之界，遠近盡至，其不得外侵而行之，乃自費，刺分肉間。
		"When needling small evils, treat them progressively to strengthen and expand their resolution. Tonify what is deficient, and there will be no harm." "Observe where the condition resides. Meet it at its boundary; whether near or far, ensure complete resolution. If it cannot be expelled outward, then it will dissipate on its own. Needle between the flesh layers."

11
打開字典
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
凡刺熱邪，越而蒼，出遊不歸，乃無病。為開通，辟門戶，使邪得出，病乃已。
		"When needling heat evils, expel them thoroughly so they become pale and retreat. If they leave the body without returning, then there will be no illness." "Open pathways and clear the gates to allow evils to exit; when this is done, the disease will cease."

12
打開字典
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
凡刺寒邪，日以溫，徐往徐來，致其神。門戶已閉，氣不分，虛實得調，其氣存也。
		"When needling cold evils, gradually warm them. Proceed slowly and gently to restore their normal function and harmonize the spirit." "When the pathways are sealed, qi does not disperse; when deficiency and excess are properly regulated, the qi remains in harmony."

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：官鍼奈何？歧伯曰：刺癰者，用鈹鍼；刺大者，用鋒鍼；刺小者，用員利鍼；刺熱者，用纔鍼；刺寒者，用毫鍼也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What is meant by 'officer needles'?" Qu Bo said, "For abscesses, use moxa needles; " "for large evils, use sharp needles;" "for small evils, use round and sharp needles;" "for heat conditions, use fine needles;" "and for cold conditions, use filiform needles."

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
請言解論，與天地相應，與四時相副，人參天地，故可為解。下有漸洳，上生葦蒲，此所以知形氣之多少也。陰陽者，寒暑也，熱則滋雨而在上，根莖少汁，人氣在外，皮膚緩，腠理開，血氣減，汗大泄，皮淖澤。寒則地凍水冰，人氣在中，皮膚致，腠理閉，汗不出，血氣強，肉堅澀。當是之時，善行水者，不能往冰，善穿地者，不能擊凍，善用鍼者，亦不能取四厥，血脈凝結，堅搏不往來者，亦未可即柔。故行水者，必待天溫，冰釋凍解，而水可行，地可穿也。人脈猶是也。治厥者，必先熨調和其經，掌與腋，肘與腳，項與脊以調之，火氣已通，血脈乃行。然後視其病，脈淖澤者，刺而平之；堅緊者，破而散之，氣下乃止，此所謂以解結者也。
		"I will now explain the theory in detail. It corresponds with heaven and earth, aligns with the four seasons, and since humans are patterned after heaven and earth, this understanding can be achieved." "Just as below there is gradually moist ground and above grow reeds and papyrus, this illustrates how one may discern the quantity of form and qi." "Yin and yang correspond to cold and heat. When there is heat, it brings rain above; the roots and stems of plants contain less juice. In humans, qi resides in the exterior, the skin becomes loose, pores open, blood and qi decrease, sweat flows profusely, and the skin feels moist and slippery." "When it is cold, the ground freezes and water turns to ice. In humans, qi remains internally; the skin becomes tight, pores close, sweat does not emerge, blood and qi become strong, and muscles feel firm and dry." "At such times, even those skilled in swimming cannot cross the ice; those adept at digging cannot break through frozen ground. Even a skilled acupuncturist cannot treat four jue (extremity coldness) when blood vessels are congealed and qi is stagnant—such conditions cannot be immediately softened." "Therefore, those who travel on water must wait for warmer weather when the ice melts and frost thaws before they can proceed; only then is it possible to swim or dig through the earth." "The same principle applies to human meridians. " "To treat jue (cold extremities), one must first warm and harmonize the meridians—between the palm and armpit, elbow and foot, neck and spine. Once warmth is restored and qi flows through, blood circulation will resume." "Then examine the condition: if the pulse feels moist and slippery, needle to balance it; " "if it is firm and tense, disperse it by needling until the qi descends and stops. This is what is called resolving a knot."

15
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
用鍼之類，在於調氣，氣積於胃，以通營衛，各行其道。宗氣留於海，其下者，注于氣街，其上者，走於息道。故厥在於足，宗氣不下，脈中之血，凝而留止，弗之火調，弗能取之。
		"The use of needles lies in regulating qi, which accumulates in the stomach to communicate ying and wei qi, each following its own pathway." "Zongqi (primordial qi) accumulates in the qihai. The part that flows downward reaches the qi street, while the part that moves upward travels through the respiratory passages." "Therefore, when jue occurs in the feet, zongqi does not descend; blood within the vessels becomes stagnant and stops. Without warming and regulating with fire therapy, it cannot be treated."

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
用鍼者，必先察其經絡之實虛，切而循之，按而彈之，視其應動者，乃後取之而下之。六經調者，謂之不病，雖病，謂之自已也。一經上實下虛而不通者，此必有橫絡盛加於大經，令之不通，視而寫之，此所謂解結也。
		"One who uses needles must first examine the deficiency or excess of the meridians and collaterals, palpate and trace them, press and gently tap them. Observe where there is a response or movement; only then should needling be performed to release it." "When the six meridians are in harmony, one is considered free of illness; even if symptoms appear, they may resolve on their own." "If one meridian is full above and deficient below, yet blocked, it must be due to a collateral vessel that has become overly strong and obstructs the major meridian. Observe this condition and needle to drain it; this is what is called resolving a blockage."

17
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
上寒下熱，先刺其項太陽，久留之，已刺則熨項與肩胛，令熱下合乃止，此所謂推而上之者也。上熱下寒，視其虛脈而陷之於經絡者，取之，氣下乃止，此所謂引而下之者也。
		"For conditions with cold above and heat below, first needle the Taiyang points on the neck and leave the needles in place for a long time. After needling, apply warmth to the neck and scapular region until the heat moves downward and harmonizes—this is what is called 'pushing upward.'" "For conditions with heat above and cold below, identify the deficient pulse that has sunk into the meridians or collaterals and needle it. Continue until the qi moves downward—this is what is called 'drawing downward.'"

18
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
大熱遍身，狂而妄見妄聞妄言，視足陽明及大絡取之，虛者補之，血而實者寫之。因其偃臥，居其頭前，以兩手四指挾按頸動脈，久持之，卷而切，推下至缺盆中，而復止如前，熱去乃止，此所謂推而散之者也。
		"For intense heat affecting the whole body, with mania and delusions of sight, hearing, and speech, examine the Yangming meridian on the foot and major collateral vessels for treatment. Tonify where there is deficiency; drain where blood is excessive." "Have the patient lie on their back, position yourself in front of their head, and use both hands with four fingers to grasp and press the carotid artery. Hold it firmly for a prolonged time, then gently compress and push downward toward the clavicle area before releasing as before. Repeat until the heat subsides—this is what is called dispersing by pushing."

19
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
黃帝曰：有一脈生數十病者，或痛，或癰，或熱，或寒，或癢，或痺，或不仁，變化無窮，其故何也？歧伯曰：此皆邪氣之所生也。黃帝曰：余聞氣者，有真氣，有正氣，有邪氣。何謂真氣？歧伯曰：真氣者，所受於天，與穀氣并而充身也。正氣者，正風也，從一方來，非實風，又非虛風也。邪氣者，虛風之賊傷人也，其中人也深，不能自去。正風者，其中人也淺，合而自去，其氣來柔弱，不能勝真氣，故自去。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "There is a single meridian that gives rise to many diseases—some cause pain, others abscesses, heat, cold, itching, numbness, or insensitivity. The changes are endless; what causes this?" Qu Bo said, "All these conditions arise from pathogenic evils." The Yellow Emperor said, "I have heard that qi includes true qi, righteous qi, and evil qi. " "What is meant by true qi?" Qu Bo said, "True qi is the vital energy received from heaven and combines with grain qi to fill and nourish the body." "Righteous qi refers to normal wind, which comes from a single direction. It is neither excessive nor deficient wind." "Evil qi refers to harmful winds that injure people; when they invade the body, their penetration is deep and they cannot leave on their own." "Normal wind, when it enters the body, does so superficially and departs on its own. Its nature is gentle and weak; it cannot overcome true qi, hence it leaves by itself."

20
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
虛邪之中人也，洒晰動形，起毫毛而發腠理。其入深，內搏於骨，則為骨痺；搏於筋，則為筋攣；搏於脈中，則為血閉，不通則為癰。搏於肉，與衛氣相搏，陽勝者，則為熱，陰勝者，則為寒。寒則真氣去，去則虛，虛則寒搏於皮膚之間。其氣外發，腠理開，毫毛搖，氣往來行，則為痒。留而不去，則痺。衛氣不行，則為不仁。
		"When deficient evils invade the body, they cause a shivering sensation and movement of the form, raising the hair and opening the pores. " "If their penetration is deep, and they strike inward at the bones, this results in bone numbness; " "if they strike the tendons, it leads to tendon spasm; " "if they strike within the vessels, this causes blood stasis. When the flow is obstructed, abscesses form." "If they strike the flesh and contend with defensive qi, when yang prevails, it results in heat; when yin prevails, it causes cold." "When there is cold, true qi departs. When true qi departs, deficiency arises; and when there is deficiency, the cold lingers between the skin and flesh." "When this qi moves outward, the pores open, the hair stands on end, and the qi flows in and out—this results in itching." "If it lingers without departing, it leads to numbness (bi)." "If defensive qi does not circulate properly, it results in insensitivity or numbness."

21
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
虛邪偏容於身半，其入深，內居榮衛，榮衛稍衰，則真氣去，邪氣獨留，發為偏枯。其邪氣淺者，脈偏痛。
		"If deficient evils partially invade one side of the body and penetrate deeply, they reside internally within the nutritive and defensive qi. When nutritive and defensive qi gradually weaken, true qi departs and evil qi remains alone, giving rise to hemiplegia." "If the pathogenic influence is superficial, it causes lateral pain in the meridians. "

22
打開字典
顯示影印本
	刺節真邪:	
虛邪之入于身也深，寒與熱相搏，久留而內著，寒勝其熱，則骨疼肉枯；熱勝其寒，則爛肉腐肌為膿，內傷骨，內傷骨為骨蝕。有所疾前筋，筋屈不得伸，邪氣居其間而不反，發為筋溜。有所結，氣歸之，衛氣留之，不得反，津液久留，合而為腸溜。久者，數歲乃成，以手按之柔，已有所結，氣歸之，津液留之，邪氣中之，凝結日以易甚，連以聚居，為昔瘤。以手按之堅，有所結，深中骨，氣因於骨，骨與氣并，日以益大，則為骨疽。有所結，中於肉，宗氣歸之，邪留而不去，有熱則化而為膿，無熱則為肉疽。凡此數氣者，其發無常處，而有常名也。
		"When deficient evils deeply invade the body, cold and heat contend with each other. If they remain for a long time and settle internally, when cold prevails over heat, it causes bone pain and flesh atrophy; " "when heat prevails over cold, the flesh rots and muscles decay into pus. Internally it injures the bones; when the bones are injured internally, this is called bone erosion." "If there is a previous injury to the tendons and the tendons remain contracted without being able to stretch, pathogenic evils dwell between them and do not retreat—this gives rise to tendon leakage (jin liu)." "If there is a stagnation, qi gathers there and defensive qi lingers, unable to return. When body fluids remain for a long time, they combine to form intestinal leakage (chang liu)." "If the condition persists for a long time, it may take several years to develop. When pressed by hand, it feels soft; however, once there is stagnation, qi accumulates there and body fluids remain. Pathogenic evils strike this area, causing gradual coagulation that becomes increasingly severe over time. Eventually, they congregate and form what is known as a tumor (xi lou)." "If pressed by hand it feels hard, and there is stagnation deeply affecting the bones, with qi adhering to the bone—when bone and qi combine, they gradually grow larger; this condition is known as bone carbuncle (gu ju)." "If there is stagnation that affects the flesh, primary qi gathers there. If pathogenic evils remain without departing, when heat arises it transforms into pus; if no heat develops, it becomes a flesh carbuncle (rou ju)." "All these various conditions arise without fixed locations but have established names." 

76. 衛氣行 Wei Qi Xing

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣行:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：願聞衛氣之行，出入之合，何如？歧伯曰：歲有十二月，日有十二辰，子午為經，卯酉為緯。天周二十八宿，而一面七星，四七二十八星。房昴為緯，虛張為經。是故房至畢為陽，昴至心為陰。陽主晝，陰主夜。故衛氣之行，一日一夜五十周於身，晝日行於陽二十五周，夜行於陰二十五周，周於五藏1。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "I would like to hear about the circulation of Wei qi, its entry and exit, and how they combine. How is this?" Qi Bo said: "A year has twelve months, a day has twelve shichen. Zi and Wu form the meridians, Mao and You form the latitudes." The heavens encircle twenty-eight lodges; one side has seven stars, four times seven making twenty-eight stars. Fang and Mao form the latitudes, Xu and Zhang form the meridians. Therefore, from Fang to Bi is Yang, and from Mao to Xin is Yin. Yang governs the day, and Yin governs the night. Therefore, the circulation of Wei qi completes fifty cycles in the body in one day and night. During the day it travels through Yang twenty-five times, and at night it travels through Yin twenty-five times, completing a cycle every five years.

1. 藏 : 原作「歲」。
2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣行:	
是故平旦陰盡，陽氣出於目，目張則氣上行於頭，循項下足太陽，循背下至小趾之端。其散者，別於目銳眥，下手太陽，下至手小指之間外側。其散者，別於目銳眥，下足少陽，注小趾次趾之間。以上循手少陽之分側，下至小指之間。別者以上至耳前，合於頷脈，注足陽明以下行，至跗上，入五趾之間。其散者，從耳下下手陽明，入大指之間，入掌中。其至於足也，入足心，出內踝，下行陰分，復合於目，故為一周。
		Therefore, at dawn Yin is exhausted and Yang qi emerges from the eyes. When the eyes open, the qi ascends to the head, follows the neck down to the Taiyang meridian of the feet, and runs along the back down to the tips of the little toes. The dispersed part separates from the sharp corner of the eyes, goes down along the Taiyang meridian on the hands, and reaches the outer side between the little fingers of the hands. The dispersed part separates from the corner of the eyes, descends along the Shaoyang meridian on the feet, and flows to between the little toe and the second toe. It ascends along the lateral aspect of the Shaoyang meridian in the hands, descending to between the small fingers. The divergent branch ascends to in front of the ear, converges with the submental vessels, flows into the Yangming meridian on the feet downward, reaches above the ankle, and enters between the five toes. The dispersed part descends from below the ear along the Yangming meridian in the hands, enters between the thumb and index finger, and reaches into the palm. When it reaches the feet, it enters the sole of the foot, exits at the inner ankle, descends through the Yin aspect, and reunites with the eyes; thus completing one cycle.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣行:	
是故日行一舍，人氣行一周與十分身之八；日行二舍，人氣行三周於身與十分身之六；日行三舍，人氣行於身五周與十分身之四；日行四舍，人氣行於身七周與十分身之二；日行五舍，人氣行於身九周；日行六舍，人氣行於身十周與十分身之八；日行七舍，人氣行於身十二周在身與十分身之六；日行十四舍，人氣二十五周於身有奇分與十分身之二，陽盡於陰，陰受氣矣。其始入于陰，常從足少陰注于腎，腎注于心，心注于肺，肺注于肝，肝注于脾，脾復注于腎為周。是故夜行一舍，人氣行於陰藏一周與十分藏之八，亦如陽行之二十五周，而復合於目。陰陽一日一夜，合有奇分十分身之四，與十分藏之二，是故人之所以臥起之時，有早晏者，奇分不盡故也。
		Therefore, as the sun travels one shed (a measure of distance), human qi completes one full circulation and eight-tenths of another in the body; when the sun travels two sheds, human qi circulates three times through the body plus six-tenths of a cycle; when the sun travels three sheds, human qi completes five cycles in the body and four-tenths of another cycle; when the sun travels four sheds, human qi circulates seven times through the body and two-tenths of a further cycle; when the sun travels five sheds, human qi completes nine cycles in the body; when the sun travels six sheds, human qi circulates ten times through the body and eight-tenths of an additional cycle; when the sun travels seven sheds, human qi completes twelve cycles in the body plus six-tenths more; when the sun travels fourteen sheds, human qi completes twenty-five cycles in the body with some remainder and two-tenths more; then Yang is exhausted into Yin, and Yin receives the qi. When it first enters Yin, it commonly begins from the Shaoyin meridian on the foot and flows into the kidney; the kidney flows into the heart, the heart into the lungs, the lungs into the liver, the liver into the spleen, and the spleen returns to the kidney, thus completing a cycle. Therefore, when the sun travels one shed at night, human qi circulates once through the Yin organs and eight-tenths more, just as in the twenty-five cycles of Yang, then reunites with the eyes. In one day and night, Yin and Yang together combine with some remainder of four-tenths in the body and two-tenths in the organs. Therefore, people have different times for lying down and waking up—some early, some late—because these remainders are not exhausted.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣行:	
黃帝曰：衛氣之在於身也，上下往來不以期，候氣而刺之，奈何？伯高曰：分有多少，日有長短，春秋冬夏，各有分理，然後常以平旦為紀，以夜盡為始。是故一日一夜，水下百刻，二十五刻者，半日之度也，常如是毋已，日入而止，隨日之長短，各以為紀而刺之。謹候其時，病可與期，失時反候者，百病不治。故曰：刺實者，刺其來也，刺虛者，刺其去也。此言氣存亡之時，以候虛實而刺之，是故謹候氣之所在而刺之，是謂逢時。在於三陽，必候其氣在於陽而刺之，病在於三陰，必候其氣在陰分而刺之。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "When Wei qi resides in the body, it moves up and down without fixed timing. How should one wait for its arrival to needle it?" Bo Gao said: "The distribution has more or less, days have long and short durations. Spring, autumn, winter, and summer each have their own patterns. Therefore, one should always take dawn as the reference point and midnight as the starting time." Therefore, in one day and night, water flows for a hundred ke (units of time), with twenty-five ke representing half a day's measure. This remains constant without change; it stops at sunset, following the length or shortness of each day, using these as reference points for needling accordingly. Carefully await its timing; then illness can be anticipated and treated at the right time. If one misses the timing or misjudges the signs, a hundred illnesses will remain untreatable. Therefore it is said: "When needling for excess conditions, needle when the qi arrives; when needling for deficiency conditions, needle when the qi departs." This means that one should await the time when the qi exists or departs, to determine deficiency or excess and needle accordingly. Therefore, carefully awaiting where the qi resides before needling is called encountering the right timing. If illness lies in the three Yang meridians, one must await when the qi is in Yang before needling; if illness lies in the three Yin meridians, one must wait until the qi resides in the Yin aspect before needling.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	衛氣行:	
水下一刻，人氣在太陽；水下二刻，人氣在少陽；水下三刻，人氣在陽明；水下四刻，人氣在陰分。水下五刻，人氣在太陽；水下六刻，人氣在少陽；水下七刻，人氣在陽明；水下八刻，人氣在陰分。水下九刻，人氣在太陽；水下十刻，人氣在少陽；水下十一刻，人氣在陽明；水下十二刻，人氣在陰分。水下十三刻，人氣在太陽；水下十四刻，人氣在少陽；水下十五刻，人氣在陽明；水下十六刻，人氣在陰分。水下十七刻，人氣在太陽；水下十八刻，人氣在少陽；水下十九刻，人氣在陽明；水下二十刻，人氣在陰分。水下二十一刻，人氣在太陽；水下二十二刻，人氣在少陽；水下二十三刻，人氣在陽明；水下二十四刻，人氣在陰分。水下二十五刻，人氣在太陽，此半日之度也。從房至畢一十四舍水下五十刻，日行半度，回行一舍，水下三刻與七分刻之四。大要曰：常以日之加於宿上也，人氣在太陽，是故日行一舍，人氣行三陽行與陰分，常如是無已，天與地同紀，紛紛紛紛，終而復始，一日一夜水下百刻而盡矣。
		When water flows for one ke, human qi is located in Taiyang; when water flows for two ke, human qi resides in Shaoyang; when water flows for three ke, humanqi resides in Yangming; when water flows for four ke, human qi enters the Yin aspect. When water flows for five ke, human qi is in Taiyang; when water flows for six ke, human qi resides inshaoyang; when water flows for seven ke, human qi isin Yangming; when water flows for eight ke, human qi enters theyin aspect. When water flows for nine ke, human qi is intaiyang; when water flows for ten ke, human qi residesin Shaoyang; when water flows for eleven ke, human qi is inyangming; when water flows for twelve ke, human qi entersthe Yin aspect. When water flows for thirteen ke, human qi is intaiyang; when water flows for fourteen ke, human qi residesin Shaoyang; when water flows for fifteen ke, human qi is inyangming; when water flows for sixteen ke, human qi enterstheyin aspect. When water flows for seventeen ke, human qi is int'aiyang; when water flows for eighteen ke, human qi residesinshaoyang; when water flows for nineteen ke, human qi is inyangming; when water flows for twenty ke, human qi enterstheyin aspect. When water flows for twenty-one ke, human qi is intaiyang; when water flows for twenty-two ke, human qi residesinshaoyang; when water flows for twenty-three ke, human qi isinyangming; when water flows for twenty-four ke, human qi entersthyin aspect. When water flows for twenty-five ke, human qi is instaiyang; this marks half a day's measure. From Fang to Bi, fourteen sheds correspond to fifty ke of water flow; the sun travels half a degree. When returning and traveling one shed, it corresponds to three ke plus four-sevenths of a ke. The essential principle says: "Usually, when the sun is added to a lodge in the heavens, human qi resides in Taiyang. Therefore, as the sun travels one shed, human qi moves through the three Yang meridians and Yin aspect; this continues without ceasing. Heaven and Earth share the same record—constantly recurring, endlessly repeating. In one day and night, the water flows for a hundred ke and completes its cycle."


77. 九宮八風 Nine Palaces and Eight Winds

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九宮八風:	
大1一常以冬至之日，居葉蟄之宮四十六日，明日居天留四十六日，明日居倉門四十六日，明日居陰洛四十五日，明日居天宮四十六日，明日居玄委四十六日，明日居倉果四十六日，明日居新洛四十五日，明日復居葉蟄之宮，曰冬至矣。
		The Great One usually, on the day of Dongzhi (Winter Solstice), resides in the Yezhe Palace for forty-six days. The next day, it resides in Tianliu for forty-six days; the following day, in Cangmen for forty-six days; then in Yinluo for forty-five days; followed by Tiangu for forty-six days; Xuanwei for forty-six days; Cangguo for forty-six days; Xinluo for forty-five days. The next day it returns to reside in the Yezhe Palace, and this is called Dongzhi (the Winter Solstice).

1. 大 : 或作「太」。《四庫全書》本作「太」。
2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九宮八風:	
太一日遊，以冬至之日，居葉蟄之宮，數所在日，從一處至九日，復返於一。常如是無已，終而復始。
		On the day of Dongzhi, Taiyi begins its daily journey and resides in the Yezhe Palace. Counting from one day to nine days at each location, it then returns to the first position. This process continues endlessly, cycling back and beginning anew.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九宮八風:	
太一移日，天必應之以風雨，以其日風雨則吉，歲美民安少病矣。先之則多雨，後之則多汗。太一在冬至之日有變，占在君；太一在春分之日有變，占在相；太一在中宮之日有變，占在吏；太一在秋分之日有變，占在將；太一在夏至之日有變，占在百姓。所謂有變者，太一居五宮之日，病風折樹木，揚沙石，各以其所主，占貴賤。因視風所從來而占之，風從其所居之鄉來為實風，主生，長養萬物；從其沖後來為虛風，傷人者也，主殺，主害者。謹候虛風而避之，故聖人日避虛邪之道，如避矢石然，邪弗能害，此之謂也。
		When Taiyi moves on a given day, the heavens must respond with wind and rain; if there is wind and rain on that day, it is auspicious. The year will then be prosperous, the people peaceful, and diseases rare. If this occurs before the appointed time, there will be excessive rain; if it occurs after, there will be much perspiration (or heat). When Taiyi undergoes a change on the day of Dongzhi, the omen relates to the ruler; When Taiyi undergoes a change the day of Chunjun (Spring Equinox), the omen concerns the prime minister; When Taiyi undergoes a change while in Zhonggong, the omen pertains to officials; When Taiyi undergoes a transformation on the day of Qiufen (Autumn Equinox), it is an omen concerning generals; When Taiyi undergoes change on the day of Xia Zhi, the omen concerns the common people. What is meant by "undergoes a change" refers to the days when Taiyi resides in one of the five palaces, and there are signs such as illness-inducing winds breaking trees, raising sand and stones. Each omen should be interpreted according to what it presides over, indicating fortune or misfortune for those concerned. One should observe the direction from which the wind comes and interpret accordingly. If the wind originates from the direction where Taiyi resides, it is a "shifeng" (true wind), indicating growth and nurturing of all things; If the wind comes from the opposite direction, it is a "xufeng" (false or empty wind), which harms people; it signifies destruction and misfortune. One must carefully observe the xufeng (false wind) and avoid it. Therefore, a sage daily avoids false evils as one would avoid arrows or stones; evil cannot then cause harm. This is what is meant by that statement.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九宮八風:	
是故太一入徙立於中宮，乃朝八風，以占吉凶也。風從南方來，名曰大弱風，其傷人也，內舍於心，外在於脈，氣主熱。風從西南方來，名曰謀風，其傷人也，內舍於脾，外在於肌，其氣主為弱。風從西方來，名曰剛風，其傷人也，內舍於肺，外在於皮膚，其氣主為燥。風從西北方來，名曰折風，其傷人也，內舍於小腸，外在於手太陽脈，脈絕則溢，脈閉則結不通，善暴死。風從北方來，名曰大剛風，其傷人也，內舍於腎，外在於骨與肩背之膂筋，其氣主為寒也。風從東北方來，名曰凶風，其傷人也，內舍於大腸，外在於兩脅腋骨下及肢節。風從東方來，名曰嬰兀風，其傷人也，內舍於肝，外在於筋紐，其氣主為身濕。風從東南方來，名曰弱風，其傷人也，內舍於胃，外在肌肉，其氣主體重。此八風皆從其虛之鄉來，乃能病人。三虛相搏，則為暴病卒死。兩實一虛，病則為淋露寒熱。犯其兩濕之地，則為痿。故聖人避風，如避矢石焉。其有三虛而偏中於邪風，則為仆偏枯矣。
		Therefore, when Taiyi enters and resides in Zhonggong (the central palace), it faces the Eight Winds to determine auspiciousness or misfortune. When wind comes from the south, it is called Dauruo Wind. When this wind harms people, internally it resides in the heart, externally affects the pulse, and its nature is heat. When wind comes from the southwest, it is called Moufeng (Planning Wind). When this wind harms people, it internally resides in the spleen and externally affects the muscles; its nature causes weakness. When wind comes from the west, it is called Gangfeng (Stiff Wind). When this wind harms a person, internally it resides in the lungs and externally affects the skin; its nature causes dryness. When wind comes from the northwest, it is called Zhefeng (Breaking Wind). When this wind harms humans, internally it resides in the small intestine and externally affects the Taiyang meridian of the hand. If the pulse is cut off, there will be overflow; if blocked, it becomes stagnant and unpassable, often leading to sudden death. When wind comes from the north, it is called Dagangfeng (Great Stiff Wind). When this wind injures people, internally it resides in the kidneys and externally affects the bones and the sinews of the shoulders and back; its nature brings cold. When wind comes from the northeast, it is called Xiongfeng (Fierce Wind). When this wind harms individuals, internally it resides in the large intestine and externally affects both sides of the armpits, beneath the ribs, as well as the limbs and joints. When wind comes from the east, it is called Yingwu Wind. When this wind injures a person, internally it resides in liver and externally affects tendons; its nature causes bodily dampness. When wind comes from the southeast, it is called Ruofeng (Weak Wind). When this wind harms human beings, internally it resides in stomach and externally affects muscles; its nature causes body heaviness. These Eight Winds can only harm people when they come from the direction of one's weakness. When three weaknesses combine, it results in sudden illness or unexpected death. With two strengths and one weakness, the resulting illness manifests as dripping discharge (leucorrhea), coldness, and fever. If it affects two damp areas, it results in atrophy or paralysis. Therefore, a sage avoids wind as one would avoid arrows and stones. If there are three weaknesses and the person is additionally struck by an evil wind, it will result in collapse or hemiplegia (one-sided paralysis). 


		78. 九鍼論 Discourse on the Nine Needles


1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
黃帝曰：余聞九鍼於夫子，眾多博大矣，余猶不能寤，敢問九鍼焉生，何因而有名？歧伯曰：九鍼者，天地之大數也，始於一而終於九。故曰：一以法天，二以法地，三以法人，四以法時，五以法音，六以法律，七以法星，八以法風，九以法野。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard from my teacher about the Nine Needles, which are numerous and extensive; yet I still cannot fully understand. I dare to ask, how did the Nine Needles originate? What is the basis for their names? Qibo said: The Nine Needles represent the great numbers of heaven and earth; they begin with one and end with nine. Therefore, it is said: one corresponds to heaven, two corresponds to earth, three corresponds to humanity, four corresponds to the seasons, five corresponds to sound, six corresponds to law, seven corresponds to stars, eight corresponds to wind, and nine corresponds to the wilderness.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
黃帝曰：以鍼應九之數，奈何？歧伯曰：夫聖人之起天地之數也，一而九之，故以立九野。九而九之，九九八十一，以起黃鍾數焉，以鍼應數也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: How do the needles correspond to these nine numbers? Qibo said: The sage, when establishing the numerical system of heaven and earth, begins with one and extends to nine; thus he establishes the Nine Wilds. Nine multiplied by nine is eighty-one, from which the number of Huangzhong originates; this is how the needles correspond to numbers.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
一者，天也。天者，陽也。五藏之應天者肺，肺者，五藏六府之蓋也，皮者，肺之合也，人之陽也。故為之治鍼，必以大其頭而銳其末，令無得深入而陽氣出。
		One represents heaven. Heaven represents yang. Among the five zang organs, the lung corresponds to heaven; the lung is the canopy of the five zang and six fu organs. The skin is associated with the lung and represents yang in a person. Therefore, when making needles for treatment, one must make the head large and the tip sharp, so that they cannot penetrate deeply and yang qi can be released.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
二者，地也。人之所以應土者，肉也。故為之治鍼，必筩其身而員其末，令無得傷肉分，傷則氣得竭。
		Two represents earth. What in a person corresponds to the earth is the flesh. Therefore, when making needles for treatment one must make their bodies hollow and round at the tip, so as not to injure the flesh. If injured, qi may be exhausted.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
三者，人也。人之所以成生者，血脈也。故為之治鍼，必大其身而員其末，令可以按脈物陷，以致其氣，令邪氣獨出。
		Three represents humanity. What enables a person to sustain life is the blood and vessels. Therefore, when making needles for treatment they must have a large body and rounded tip, so that one can press along the meridians to guide qi downward, allowing pathogenic influences to be expelled.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
四者，時也。時者，四時八風之客於經絡之中，為瘤病者也。故為之治鍼，必筩其身而鋒其末，令可以寫熱出血，而痼病竭。
		Four represents the seasons. The seasons refer to the four seasons and eight winds that invade the meridians, causing lumps and diseases. Therefore, when making needles for treatment the body must be hollow and the tip sharp, allowing heat to be purged through bloodletting, thus eliminating chronic diseases.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
五者，音也。音者，冬夏之分，分於子午，陰與陽別，寒與熱爭，兩氣相搏，合為癰膿者也。故為之治鍼，必令其末如劍鋒，可以取大膿。
		Five represents sound. Sound refers to the division between winter and summer, marked by the times of Zi and Wu. Yin and yang separate; cold and heat contend. When these two qi forces clash, they combine to form abscesses. Therefore, when making needles for treatment their tips must be shaped like a sword's edge, so they can remove large abscesses.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
六者，律也。律者，調陰陽四時而合十二經脈，虛邪客於經絡而為暴痺者也。故為之治鍼，必令尖如釐，且員其銳，中身微大，以取暴氣。
		Six represents law. Law refers to the regulation of yin and yang in accordance with the four seasons, harmonizing them with the twelve meridians. When pathogenic influences invade the meridians they cause sudden episodes of bi syndrome. Therefore, when making needles for treatment it is essential to make their tips as fine as a hair and round the sharp end. The middle of the needle should be slightly thick in order to remove sudden surges of pathogenic qi.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
七者，星也。星者，人之七竅，邪之所客於經，而為痛痺，合於經絡者也。故為之治鍼，令尖如蚊虻喙，靜以徐往，微以久留，正氣因之，真邪俱往，出鍼而養者也。
		Seven represents stars. Stars correspond to the seven orifices of a person; pathogenic influences that invade the meridians and cause pain and bi syndrome are associated with the meridians. Therefore, when making needles for this purpose, their tips should be as fine as the mouthparts of a mosquito or horsefly. Insert them gently and slowly, leave them in place slightly longer; thus, true qi follows naturally, pathogenic influences are expelled together, and after removing the needle, the body is nourished.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
八者，風也。風者，人之股肱八節也。八正之虛風，八風傷人，內舍於骨解腰脊節腠理之間為深痺也。故為之治鍼，必長其身，鋒其末，可以取深邪遠痺。
		Eight represents wind. Wind corresponds to the eight joints of a person's limbs. The external wind from the Eight Directions harms people, lodging internally between the joints of bones, the waist and spine, and in the interstices of muscles and skin, causing deep bi syndrome. Therefore, when making needles for such conditions, the body must be long and the tip sharp in order to remove deep-seated pathogenic influences and distant bi syndrome.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
九者，野也。野者，人之節解皮膚之間也。淫邪流溢於身，如風水之狀，而留不能過於機關大節者也。故為之治鍼，令尖如挺，其鋒微員，以取大氣之不能過於關節者也。
		Nine represents the wilderness. The wilderness corresponds to the spaces between a person's joints, muscles, and skin. Excessive pathogenic influences spread throughout the body like floods or water, lingering and unable to pass through major joints. Therefore, when making needles for treating such conditions, their tips must be as pointed as a rod and slightly rounded at the sharp end in order to remove stagnant qi that cannot pass through joints.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
黃帝曰：鍼之長短有數乎？歧伯曰：一曰鐵鍼者，取法於巾鍼，去末寸半，卒銳之，長一寸六分，主熱在頭身也。二曰員鍼，取法於絮鍼，筩其身而卵其鋒，長一寸六分，主治分間氣。三曰鍉鍼，取法於黍粟之銳，長三寸半，主按脈取氣，令邪出。四曰鋒鍼，取法於絮鍼，筩其身，鋒其末，長一寸六分，主癰熱出血。五曰鈹鍼，取法於劍鋒，廣二分半，長四寸，主大癰膿，兩熱爭者也。六曰員利鍼，取法於釐鍼，微大其末，反小其身，令可深內也，長一寸六分。主取癰痺者也。七曰毫鍼，取注于毫毛，長一寸六分，主寒熱痛痺在絡者也。八曰長鍼，取法於綦鍼，長七寸，主取深邪遠痺者也。九曰大鍼，取法於鋒鍼，其鋒微員，長四寸，主取大氣不出關節者也。鍼形畢矣，此九鍼大小長短法也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Do needles have specific lengths? Qibo said: The first is the tie zhen, modeled after the jin zhen. Remove half an inch from its tip and sharpen it suddenly; it measures one cun and six fen in length, used for treating heat located in the head or body. The second is the yuan zhen, modeled after xu zhen. Hollow its body and make its tip oval-shaped; it measures one cun six fen in length and is mainly used to treat qi stagnation between muscle layers. The third is the di zhen, modeled after a sharp grain of millet; it measures three cun and five fen in length. It is used to press along meridians to extract qi and expel pathogenic influences. The fourth is the feng zhen, modeled after xu zhin. Hollow its body and sharpen its tip; it measures one cun six phen in length and is used for abscesses with heat to promote bloodletting. The fifth is the bi zhen, modeled after a sword's tip. It has a width of two cun and five fen, and measures four cun in length; it is used for large abscesses where heat accumulates intensely. The sixth is the yuanli zhen, modeled after li zhen. Slightly enlarge its tip and reduce the size of its body to allow for deeper insertion; it measures one cun six phan in length. It is mainly used for treating abscesses and bi syndrome. The seventh is the hao zhen, modeled after fine body hair; it measures one cun six fu in length and is used to treat cold-heat pain and bi syndrome located in the collateral vessels. The eighth is the chang zhen, modeled after qi zhen; it measures seven cun in length and is used to remove deeply seated pathogenic influences or distant bi syndrome. The ninth is the da zhen, modeled after feng zhen. Its tip is slightly rounded; it measures four cun in length and is primarily used to treat stagnant qi that does not pass through joints. The forms of the needles are now complete; these represent the sizes, lengths, and principles of the Nine Needles.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
黃帝曰：願聞身形，應九野，奈何？歧伯曰：請言身形之應九野也，左足應立春，其日戊寅己丑。左脅應春分，其日乙卯。左手應立夏，其日戊辰己巳。膺喉首頭應夏至，其日丙午。右手應立秋，其中戊申己末。右脅應秋分，其日辛酉。右足應立冬，其日戊戌己亥。腰尻下竅應冬至，其日壬子。六府膈下三藏應中州，其大禁，大禁太一所在之日，及諸戊己。凡此九者，善候八正所在之處。所主左右上下身體有癰腫者，欲治之，無以其所直之日潰治之，是謂天忌日也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I would like to hear how the human body corresponds to the Nine Wilds; how is this? Qibo said: Please allow me to explain how the human body corresponds with the Nine Wilds. The left foot corresponds to Lichun (the Beginning of Spring), which occurs on days Wuyin and Jichou. The left hypochondrium corresponds to Chunjie (the Vernal Equinox), which occurs on day Yimao. The left hand corresponds to Lixia (the Beginning of Summer), which occurs on days Wuchen and Jisi. The chest, throat, head, and neck correspond to Xiazhi (the Summer Solstice), which occurs on day Bingwu. The right hand corresponds to Liqiu (the Beginning of Autumn), which occurs on days Wu Shen and Jiwei. The right hypochondrium corresponds to Qiufen (the Autumnal Equinox), which occurs on day Xinyou. The right foot corresponds to Lidong (the Beginning of Winter), which occurs on days Wuxu and Jihai. The waist, coccyx, and lower orifices correspond to Dongzhi (the Winter Solstice), which occurs on day Rensi. The six fu organs and the area below the diaphragm corresponding to the three zang organs belong to Zhongzhou (the Central Land). The most important restriction is on days when Taiyi resides there, as well as all days Wuxi. All these nine correspondences should be carefully observed in relation to the Eight Directions' positions. If there are abscesses or swellings in the left, right, upper, or lower parts of the body and one wishes to treat them, one should not drain or treat them on the days corresponding to those areas. These are known as heavenly taboos (tianji) days.

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
形東志苦，病生於脈，治之於灸刺。形苦志東，病生於筋，治之以熨引。形東志東，病生於肉，治之以鍼石。形苦志苦，病生於咽喝，治之以甘藥。形數惊恐，筋脈不通，病生於不仁，治之以按摩謬藥。是謂形。
		Those with a form oriented toward the east and a preference for bitterness tend to develop diseases in their vessels, which should be treated through moxibustion or acupuncture. Those with a form inclined toward hardship and an orientation toward the east tend to develop diseases in their sinews, which should be treated by fomentation or stretching. Those with a form and preference both oriented toward the east tend to develop disease in their flesh, which should be treated with acupuncture and moxibustion. Those with a form inclined towards hardship and a preference for bitterness are prone to diseases in the throat, which should be treated using sweet medicines. Those who frequently experience fear and alarm may suffer from obstruction of sinews and vessels, leading to numbness (bingshengyushiren). This should be treated with massage and medicinal therapy. This is called the relationship between form and disease.

15
打開字典
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
五藏氣，心主噫，肺主咳，肝主語，脾主吞，腎主欠。
		The five zang organs govern certain functions: the heart governs belching, the lungs govern coughing, the liver governs speech, the spleen governs swallowing, and the kidneys govern yawning.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
六府氣，膽為怒，胃為氣逆穢，大腸小腸為泄，膀胱不約為遺溺，下焦溢為水。
		The six fu organs also govern certain functions: the gallbladder is associated with anger, the stomach with upward rushing and vomiting, the large and small intestines with diarrhea, the bladder when not properly controlled leads to incontinence of urine, and excessive overflow from the lower jiao results in water retention.

17
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
五味：酸入肝，辛入肺，苦入心，甘入脾，鹹入腎，淡入胃，是謂五味。
		The five flavors: sour enters the liver, pungent enters the lungs, bitter enters the heart, sweet enters the spleen, salty enters the kidneys, and bland enters the stomach. This is called the Five Flavors.

18
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
五并：精氣并肝則憂，并心則喜，并肺則悲，并腎則恐，并脾則畏，是謂五精之氣，并於藏也。
		The five emotional responses: when vital qi combines with the liver, it results in sorrow; when combined with the heart, it causes joy; when combined with the lungs, it brings sadness; when combined with the kidneys, it produces fear; and when combined with the spleen, it leads to timidity. This is known as the Five Essential Qi responses, which combine with the zang organs.

19
打開字典
顯示相似段落

五惡：肝惡風，心惡熱，肺惡寒，腎惡燥，脾惡濕，此五藏氣所惡也。
		The five aversions: the liver dislikes wind, the heart dislikes heat, the lungs dislike cold, the kidneys dislike dryness, and the spleen dislikes dampness. These are the environmental conditions that each of the Five Zang organs finds harmful to their qi.

20

	九鍼論:	
五液：心主汗，肝主泣，肺主涕，腎主唾，脾主液，此五液所出也。
		The five bodily fluids: the heart governs perspiration, the liver governs tears, the lungs govern nasal mucus, the kidneys govern saliva, and the spleen governs body fluid. These are the sources of the Five Fluids.

五勞：久視傷血，久臥傷氣，久坐傷肉，久立傷骨，久行傷筋，此五久勞所病也。
		The five types of overexertion: prolonged looking injures blood, excessive lying down injures qi, too much sitting injures flesh, standing for long periods injures bones, and walking excessively injures sinews. These are the ailments caused by these Five Forms of Prolonged Overexertion.

五走：酸走筋，辛走氣，苦走血，鹹走骨，甘走肉，是謂五走也。
		The five movements: sour moves to the sinews, pungent moves to qi, bitter moves to blood, salty moves to bones, and sweet moves to flesh. This is called The Five Movements.

23
打開字典

	九鍼論:	
五裁：病在筋，無食酸；病在氣，無食辛；病在骨，無食鹹；病在血，無食苦；病在肉，無食甘。口嗜而欲食之，不可多也，必自裁也，命曰五裁。
		The five dietary restrictions: if the disease is in the sinews, avoid sour foods; if the disease is in qi, avoid pungent foods; if the disease is in bones, avoid salty foods; if the disease is in blood, avoid bitter foods; if the disease is in flesh, avoid sweet foods. Even if one craves and desires to eat these flavors, they must not be consumed excessively; one must exercise self-restraint. This is called the "Five Dietary Restrictions."

五發：陰病發於骨，陽病發於血，陰病發於肉，陽病發於冬，陰病發於夏。
		The five patterns of disease manifestation: yin diseases manifest in the bones, yang diseases manifest in blood; yin diseases also manifest in flesh, while yang diseases manifest during winter and yin diseases during summer.

	九鍼論:	
五邪：邪入于陽，則為狂；邪入于陰，則為血痺；邪入于陽，轉則為癲疾；邪入于陰，轉則為瘖；陽入之於陰，病靜；陰出之於陽，病喜怒。
		The five pathogenic influences: when pathogenic factors invade the yang aspect, it results in mania; when they invade the yin aspect, it causes blood bi syndrome; when pathogenic factors enter yang and transform, it becomes epilepsy; when they enter yin and transform, it results in muteness; when yang enters yin, the disease becomes quiescent; when yin emerges into yang, the illness is characterized by alternating episodes of anger and joy.

五藏：心藏神，肺藏魄，肝藏魂，脾藏意，腎藏精志也。
		The five zang: the heart stores shen (spirit), the lungs store po (corporeal soul), the liver stores hun (ethereal soul), the spleen stores yi (thoughts), and the kidneys store jing and zhi (essence and will).

打開字典

顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
陽明多血多氣，太陽多血少氣，少陽多氣少血，太陰多血少氣，厥陰多血少氣，少陰多氣少血。故曰刺陽明出血氣，刺太陽出血惡氣，刺少陽出氣惡血，刺太陰出血惡氣，刺厥陰出血惡氣，刺少陰出氣惡血也。
		Yangming has abundant blood and qi; Taiyang has abundant blood but less qi; Shaoyang has more qi but less blood. Taixin also has abundant blood with less qi, while Jueyin has abundant blood but little qi, and Shaoyin has more qi than blood. Therefore, it is said that needling Yangming releases both blood and qi; needling Taiyang releases blood but with difficulty in releasing qi; needling Shaoyang releases qi but with difficulty in releasing blood. Needling Taixin also releases blood with difficulty in releasing qi. Needling Jueyin releases blood with difficulty in qi, while needling Shaoyin releases qi with difficulty in blood.
