打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	九鍼論:	
足陽明太陰為裏表，少陽厥陰為表裏，太陽少陰為表裏，是謂足之陰陽也。手陽明太陰為表裏，少陽心主為表裏，太陽少陰為表裏，是謂手之陰陽也。
		The foot Yangming and Taixin meridians are interior-exterior pairs; the foot Shaoyang and Jueyin form another pair, as do the foot Taiyang and Shaoyin. This is referred to as the yin-yang relationships of the leg meridians. The hand Yangming and Taixin form an exterior-interior pair; the hand Shaoyang and Xin Zhu (Heart Protector) also form a pair, as do the hand Taiyang and Shaoyin meridians. This is referred to as yin-yang of the arm meridians. 


		79. 歲露論 Theory of Annual Exposure


1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：經言夏日傷暑，秋病瘧，瘧之發以時，其故何也？歧伯對曰：邪客於風府，病循膂而下，衛氣一日一夜，常大會於風府，其明日日下一節，故其日作晏，此其先客於脊背也。故每至於風府則腠理開，腠理開則邪氣入，邪氣入則病作，此所以日作尚晏也。衛氣之行風府，日下一節，二十一日下至尾底，二十二日入脊內，注于伏沖之脈，其行九日，出於缺盆之中，其氣上行，故其病稍益至。其內搏於五藏，橫連募原，其道遠，其氣深，其行遲，不能日作，故次日乃蓄積而作焉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qibo, "According to the classics, excessive heat in summer causes shu, and malaria occurs in autumn. Malaria attacks at certain times; what is the reason for this?" Qibo replied, "Pathogenic influences reside in Fengfu. The illness follows the back downward. Wei Qi completes a full cycle through the body once every day and night, regularly converging at Fengfu. Each subsequent day it moves down one segment, so its onset is delayed by one day each time. This occurs because the pathogen initially lodges in the spine and back." Therefore, whenever it reaches Fengfu, the pores open. When the pores open, pathogenic influences enter; when pathogenic influences enter, illness occurs. This is why the onset of symptoms continues to be delayed each day. Wei Qi's passage through Fengfu moves downward one segment each day. After twenty-one days, it reaches the tailbone; on the twenty-second day, it enters within the spine and flows into the Fuchong meridian. It travels for nine more days before emerging at the hollow of the clavicle (Quepang). As its Qi ascends, the illness gradually intensifies. Its internal influence reaches the five zang organs and laterally connects with the Mu Yuan. The path is long, the Qi is deep, and its movement is slow; therefore, it cannot cause symptoms every day. Hence, it accumulates until the next day before manifesting.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝曰：衛氣每至於風府，腠理乃發，發則邪入焉。其衛氣日下一節，則不當風府，奈何？歧伯曰：風府無常，衛氣之所應，必開其腠理，氣之所舍節，則其府也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Whenever Wei Qi reaches Fengfu, the pores become open; when they are open, pathogenic influences can enter." If Wei Qi moves downward one segment each day and thus does not reach Fengfu, how is this possible? Qibo said, "Fengfu has no fixed location. Wherever Wei Qi responds, the pores must open there; the segment where Qi resides becomes its 'fengfu' at that time."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝曰：善。夫風之與瘧也，相與同類，而風常在，而瘧特以時休，何也？歧伯曰：風氣留其處，瘧氣隨經絡，沉以內搏，故衛氣應，乃作也。帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Well said." Wind and malaria are similar in nature. Wind illness is constant, while malaria specifically recurs at certain times—why is this so? Qibo said, "Wind Qi lingers in its location, while malaria Qi follows the meridians and collaterals inwardly and deeply. Therefore, when Wei Qi responds to it, symptoms occur." The Emperor said, "Well said."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝問於少師曰：余聞四時八風之中人也，故有寒暑，寒則皮膚急而腠理閉；暑則皮膚緩而腠理開。賊風邪氣，因得以入乎？將必須八正虛邪，乃能傷人乎？少師答曰：不然。賊風邪氣之中人也，不得以時，然必因其開也，其入深，其內極病，其病人也，卒暴。因其閉也，其入淺以留，其病也，徐以遲。
		The Yellow Emperor asked the Shaoshi, "I have heard that when people are affected by the eight winds of the four seasons, there is cold and heat. Cold causes the skin to tighten and the pores to close; heat causes the skin to loosen and the pores to open. Therefore, can harmful winds and pathogenic influences take advantage of this opportunity to enter?" Must one necessarily be affected by the eight correct winds or deficient pathogenic influences in order to be harmed?" The Shaoshi replied, "Not necessarily." When harmful winds and pathogenic influences affect a person, they do not follow seasonal timing. However, they must take advantage of when the pores are open. These influences penetrate deeply into the body, causing severe internal illness that manifests suddenly and violently in the patient. When the pores are closed, these influences enter only superficially and remain there; the resulting illness develops slowly and progresses gradually.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝曰：有寒溫和適，腠理不開，然有卒病者，其故何也？少師答曰：帝弗知邪入乎。雖平居其腠理開閉緩急，其故常有時也。黃帝曰：可得聞乎？少師曰：人與天地相參也，與日月相應也。故月滿則海水西盛，人血氣積，肌肉充，皮膚致，毛髮堅，腠理郗，煙垢著，當是之時，雖遇賊風，其入淺不深。至其月郭空，則海水東盛，人氣血虛，其衛氣去，形獨居，肌肉減，皮膚縱，腠理開，毛髮殘，膠理薄，煙垢落，當是之時，遇賊風則其入深，其病人也，卒暴。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Sometimes a person is in an environment that is neither cold nor hot but rather mild and comfortable, the pores remain closed, yet sudden illness still occurs. What is the reason for this?" The Shaoshi replied, "Your Majesty does not know how pathogenic influences enter." Even in normal conditions, the opening and closing of pores varies with speed and tightness, which occurs at certain times. The Yellow Emperor said, "May I hear about it?" The Shaoshi said, "Human beings correspond with heaven and earth, and respond to the sun and moon." Therefore, when the moon is full, seawater swells toward the west; human blood and Qi accumulate, muscles become full, skin becomes dense, hair and body hairs grow strong, pores close tightly, and dirt adheres. At this time, even if one encounters harmful winds, they will only enter superficially, not deeply. When the moon wanes and becomes hollow, seawater swells toward the east; human Qi and blood become deficient, Wei Qi departs, the body remains alone without protection, muscles weaken, skin loosens, pores open, hair deteriorates, interstitial tissues thin, and dirt falls off. At this time, if one encounters harmful winds, these influences will penetrate deeply, causing sudden and violent illness in the person.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝曰：其有卒然暴死暴病者，何也？少師答曰：三虛者，其死暴疾也；得三實者邪不能傷人也。黃帝曰：願聞三虛。少師曰：乘年之衰，逢月之空，失時之和，因為賊風所傷，是謂三虛。故論不知三虛，工反為麤。帝曰：願聞三實。少師曰：逢年之盛，遇月之滿，得時之和，雖有賊風邪氣，不能危之也。黃帝曰：善乎哉論！明乎哉道！請藏之金匱，命曰三實。然，此一夫之論也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "What causes some people to suddenly die or fall seriously ill?" The Shaoshi replied, "When three deficiencies occur together, sudden and severe illness or death can result; when one possesses three strengths, pathogenic influences cannot harm a person." The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to hear about the three deficiencies." The Shaoshi said, "When one encounters the decline of a year, coincides with the waning of the moon, and loses harmony with seasonal timing, and is then harmed by harmful winds, this is called the three deficiencies." Therefore, if a discussion does not recognize the three deficiencies, the physician will be considered unskilled. The Emperor said, "I would like to hear about the Three Strengths." The Shaoshi said, "When one encounters a flourishing year, meets the full moon, and attains harmony with seasonal timing, even if harmful winds or pathogenic influences are present, they cannot endanger him." The Yellow Emperor said, "What a well-reasoned discussion!" How clear and profound is this principle! Please preserve it in a golden chest, and name it "The Three Strengths." Indeed, this is the view of one individual.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝曰：願聞歲之所以皆同病者，何因而然？少師曰：此八正之候也。黃帝曰：候之奈何？少師曰：候此者，常以冬至之日，太一立於葉蟄之宮，其至也，天必應之以風雨者矣。風雨從南方來者，為虛風，賊傷人者也。其以夜半至也，萬民皆臥而弗犯也，故其歲民少病。其以晝至者，萬民懈惰而皆中於虛風，故萬民多病。虛邪入客於骨而不發於外，至其立春，陽氣大發，腠理開，因立春之日，風從西方來，萬民又皆中於虛風，此兩邪相搏，經氣結代者矣。故諸逢其風而遇其雨者，命曰遇歲露焉，因歲之和，而少賊風者，民少病而少死。歲多賊風邪氣，寒溫不和，則民多病而死矣。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I would like to know why people in a given year often suffer from similar illnesses—what is the cause of this?" The Shaoshi said, "This is due to the influence of the Eight Correct Winds." The Yellow Emperor said, "How can one observe these influences?" The Shaoshi said, "To observe these influences, one should always begin on the day of Winter Solstice. On that day, Taiyi stands at Yezhi Palace; when it arrives, Heaven must respond with wind and rain." Winds and rains coming from the south are called Xufeng, harmful winds that injure people. If such winds arrive at midnight, all the people are asleep and not exposed to them; therefore, in that year, few illnesses occur among the people. If such winds arrive during the day, the people are relaxed and idle and thus all exposed to Xufeng; therefore, many illnesses occur among the people in that year. Deficient pathogenic influences enter and reside in the bones without manifesting externally. By the time Lichun (Beginning of Spring) arrives, Yang Qi greatly increases, pores open, and on the day of Lichun, winds come from the west; all people are again affected by Xufeng. When these two types of pathogenic influences combine, they cause stagnation and disruption in the meridian Qi. Therefore, those who encounter these winds and rains are said to have encountered the annual exposure. When a year is harmonious with nature and has few harmful winds, people suffer fewer illnesses and fewer deaths. If a year has many harmful winds and pathogenic influences, with imbalances in cold and warmth, then people will suffer more illnesses and deaths.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	歲露論:	
黃帝曰：虛邪之風，其所傷貴賤何如，候之奈何？少師答曰：正月朔日，太一居天留之宮，其日西北風，不雨，人多死矣。正月朔日，平旦北風，春，民多死。正月朔日，平旦北風行，民病多者，十有三也。正月朔日，日中北風，夏，民多死。正月朔日，夕時北風，秋，民多死。終日北風，大病死者十有六。正月朔日，風從南方來，命曰旱鄉；從西方來，命曰白骨，將國有殃，人多死亡。正月朔日，風從東方來，發屋，揚沙石，國有大災也。正月朔日，風從東南方行，春有死亡。正月朔
日1，天利溫不風糶賤，民不病；天寒而風，糶貴，民多病。此所謂候歲之風，殘傷人者也。二月丑不風，民多心腹病；三月戌不溫，民多寒熱；四月已不暑，民多癉病；十月申不寒，民多暴死。諸所謂風者，皆發屋，折樹木，揚沙石起毫毛，發腠理者也。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "What are the differences in how deficient pathogenic winds affect people of high or low status? How can one observe this?" The Shaoshi replied, "On the first day of the first lunar month, Taiyi resides in Tianliu Palace. If on that day there is a northwest wind and no rain, many people will die." On the first day of the first month, if at dawn there is a north wind in spring, many people will also die. On the first day of the first moon, if at dawn a north wind blows and continues to blow, thirteen out of ten people will fall ill. On the first day of the lunar month, if there is a north wind at noon and it continues into summer, many people will die. On the first day of the Lunar New Year, if there is a northerly wind in the evening and it continues into autumn, many people will die. If a north wind blows all day long, sixteen out of ten people will suffer serious illness or death. On the first day of the year, if winds come from the south, it is called "Hanxiang"; if they come from the west, it is called "Bangu," indicating that a disaster will befall the country and many people will die. On the first day of January, if winds come from the east, they tear off roofs and raise sand and stones; this indicates a great disaster for the country. On the first day of January (the first lunar month), if winds come from the southeast, there will be deaths in spring. On the first day of Lunar New Year, when Heaven is favorable with warm weather and no winds, grain prices are low and people do not fall ill; if it is cold and windy, grain becomes expensive and the people suffer many illnesses. This is what is meant by observing the annual winds that harm people. In the second month, if on the Chou day there are no winds, many people will suffer from heart and abdominal illnesses; in the third month, if it is not warm on the Xu day, many people will have chills and fever; in the fourth month, if it does not become hot on the Ji day, many people will suffer from skin diseases; and in the tenth month, if it is not cold on the Shen day, many people will experience sudden deaths. All so-called winds of this kind tear off roofs, break trees and plants, raise sand and stones, lift fine hairs, and open the pores.

1. 日 : 舊脫。 


80. 大惑論 Discourse on Profound Confusion

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝問於歧伯曰：余嘗上於清冷之台，中階而顧，匍匐而前，則惑。余私異之，竊內怪之，獨瞑獨視，安心定氣，久而不解。獨博獨眩，披髮長跪，俛而視之，後久之不已也。卒然自上，何氣使然？歧伯對曰：五藏六府之精氣，皆上注于目而為之精。精之窠為眼，骨之精為瞳子，筋之精為黑眼，血之精為絡其窠，氣之精為白眼，肌肉之精為約束，裹擷筋骨血氣之精，而與脈并為系。上屬於腦，後出於項中。故邪中於項，因逢其身之虛，其入深，則隨眼系以入于腦。入于腦則腦轉，腦轉則引目系急。目系急則目眩以轉矣。邪其精，其精所中不相比也，則精散。精散則視歧，視歧見兩物。目者，五藏六府之精也，營衛魂魄之所常營也，神氣之所生也。故神勞則魂魄散，志意亂。是故瞳子黑眼法於陰，白眼赤脈法於陽也。故陰陽合傳而精明也。目者，心使也。心者，神之舍也，故神精亂而不轉。卒然見非常處精神魂魄，散不相得，故曰惑也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked Qi Bo, "I once ascended the Qingleng Terrace. When I reached the middle of the steps and looked back, I had to crawl forward, which left me confused." I found this strange privately and secretly felt puzzled by it. I closed my eyes and looked intently on my own, calming my mind and stabilizing my breath, yet the confusion remained unresolved for a long time. I stood alone, dazed and disoriented, with my hair loose and kneeling upright for a long time, bowing my head to look at it, yet the condition did not subside even after a prolonged period. Suddenly coming from above, what kind of qi caused this? Qi Bo replied, "The vital energy (qi) of the five zang and six fu organs all ascend to nourish the eyes and form their clarity." The essence of the bones forms the pupil, the essence of tendons forms the black part of the eye, the essence of blood forms the network surrounding the eye socket, the essence of qi forms the white part of the eye, and the essence of muscles serves as the constraint that binds together the essences of sinews, bones, blood, and qi, connecting with the vessels to form a system. They ascend upward to connect with the brain and exit from behind the neck. Therefore, when pathogenic factors strike the back of the neck and encounter a deficiency in the body, they penetrate deeply and follow the eye system to enter the brain. When they enter the brain, the brain becomes affected; when the brain is affected, it pulls on the eye system tightly. When the eye system is taut, dizziness and spinning vision occur. If pathogenic factors attack the essence, and if the affected parts of the essence are not in harmony with each other, then the essence disperses. When the essence disperses, vision becomes distorted; when vision is distorted, one sees two objects where there should be only one. The eyes are the essence of the five zang and six fu viscera; they are where nutritive qi, defensive qi, soul (hun), and spirit (po) constantly circulate. They are also the source from which mental energy is generated. Therefore, when the mind is overexerted, the hun and po disperse, and one's will and thoughts become chaotic. Hence, the pupil and black part of the eye correspond to yin, while the white part and red vessels correspond to yang. Therefore, when yin and yang harmonize and transmit together, vision becomes clear and bright. The eyes are controlled by the heart. The heart is the dwelling place of the spirit; therefore, when the spirit and mental energy become disordered, vision fails to function properly. Suddenly encountering an unusual situation causes the spirit, qi, hun, and po to scatter and become disordered; this is why it is called confusion.

2
打開字典
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：余疑其然。余每之東苑，未曾不惑，去之則復，余唯獨為東苑勞神乎？何其異也？歧伯曰：不然也。心有所喜，神有所惡，卒然相惑，則精氣亂，視誤，故惑，神移乃復。是故間者為迷，甚者為惑。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "I have doubts about this." Whenever I go to the Dongyuan Garden, I am always confused. When I leave it, my confusion subsides. Could it be that I alone exert my mind excessively at the Dongyuan? How strange is this phenomenon? lington Qi Bo said, "Not quite." When the heart has a preference and the spirit has an aversion, sudden confusion arises, causing the vital energy to become disordered and vision to be mistaken; this is why one becomes confused. Only when the spirit stabilizes does clarity return. Therefore, mild cases are called confusion (mi), while severe ones are termed bewilderment (huo).

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：人之善忘者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：上氣不足，下氣有餘，腸胃實而心肺虛。虛則營衛留於下，久之不以時上，故善忘也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What kind of qi causes people to be prone to forgetfulness?" Qi Bo replied, "When the upper qi is insufficient and the lower qi is excessive, with the intestines and stomach being full while the heart and lungs are deficient." When there is deficiency, the nutritive and defensive qi remain in the lower part of the body; over time, they do not ascend at the proper time, hence forgetfulness occurs.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：人之善飢而不嗜食者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：精氣并於脾，熱氣留於胃，胃熱則消穀，穀消故善飢。胃氣逆上，則胃脘寒，故不嗜食也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What kind of qi causes a person to feel hungry often yet have no desire to eat?" Qi Bo replied, "When the vital essence and qi congregate in the spleen, and heat accumulates in the stomach, the heat in the stomach consumes food rapidly; as a result, one feels hungry frequently." When the stomach qi rebels upward, it causes coldness in the epigastric region, hence the lack of appetite.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：病而不得臥者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：衛氣不得入于陰，常留於陽。留於陽則陽氣滿，陽氣滿則陽蹻盛，不得入于陰則陰氣虛，故目不瞑矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What kind of qi causes illness that prevents a person from sleeping?" Qi Bo replied, "When the defensive qi cannot enter yin and remains constantly in yang." Remaining in yang causes the yang qi to become excessive; when yang qi is excessive, the yin jiao becomes overly active. When defensive qi cannot enter yin, yin qi becomes deficient, hence the eyes do not close and sleep does not occur.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：病目而不得視者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：衛氣留於陰，不得行於陽，留於陰則陰氣盛，陰氣盛則陰蹻滿，不得入于陽則陽氣虛，故目閉也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What kind of qi causes an illness in which one cannot see?" Qi Bo replied, "When the defensive qi remains in yin and cannot move into yang. Remaining in yin causes excessive yin qi; when yin qi is excessive, the yang jiao becomes full. When it cannot enter yang, yang qi becomes deficient, hence the eyes remain closed."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：人之多臥者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：此人腸胃大而皮膚濕，而分肉不解焉。腸胃大則衛氣留久；皮膚濕則分肉不解，其行遲。夫衛氣者，晝日常行於陽，夜行於陰，故陽氣盡則臥，陰氣盡則寤。故腸胃大，則衛氣行留久；皮膚濕，分肉不解，則行遲。留於陰也久，其氣不清，則欲瞑，故多臥矣。其腸胃小，皮膚滑以緩，分肉解利，衛氣之留於陽也久，故少瞑焉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "What kind of qi causes a person who sleeps excessively?" Qi Bo replied, "This person has large intestines and stomach, moist skin, and the intermuscular spaces are not well separated." Large intestines and stomach cause the defensive qi to remain for a prolonged time; moist skin causes the intermuscular spaces not to separate properly, thus slowing its movement. Defensive qi normally circulates in yang during the day and in yin at night. Therefore, when yang qi is exhausted, one sleeps; when yin qi is exhausted, one awakens. Therefore, if the intestines and stomach are large, defensive qi lingers for a long time; if the skin is moist and the intermuscular spaces do not separate properly, its movement becomes slow. When it lingers in yin for a long time and the qi does not become clear, one desires to close their eyes; thus, they sleep excessively. If the intestines and stomach are small, the skin is smooth and relaxed, and the intermuscular tissues are loose and well-separated, then defensive qi lingers in yang for a long time; therefore, one closes their eyes infrequently.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：其非常經也，卒然多臥者，何氣使然？歧伯曰：邪氣留於上焦，上焦閉而不通，已食若飲湯，衛氣留久於陰而不行，故卒然多臥焉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "In cases not following the usual pattern, what kind of qi causes someone to suddenly sleep excessively?" Qi Bo replied, "When pathogenic factors remain in the upper jiao, blocking it and preventing its function. After eating or drinking soup, defensive qi lingers too long in yin without moving; thus, one suddenly sleeps excessively."

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	大惑論:	
黃帝曰：善。治此諸邪，奈何？歧伯曰：先其藏府，誅其小過，後調其氣，盛者寫之，虛者補之，必先明知其形志之苦樂，定乃取之。
		The Yellow Emperor said, "Well said." How should one treat these various pathogenic influences? Qi Bo replied, "First examine the zang and fu organs, address minor imbalances, then regulate their qi. Drain what is excessive and tonify what is deficient. One must first clearly understand the patient's physical condition and emotional state—whether they suffer or are at ease—and only after this understanding should treatment be administered." 

81. 癰疽 Yongju

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
黃帝曰：余聞腸胃受穀，上焦出氣，以溫分肉，而養骨節，通腠理。中焦出氣如露，上注谿谷，而滲孫脈，津液和調，變化而赤為血。血和則孫脈先滿溢，乃注于絡脈，皆盈，乃注于經脈，陰陽已張，因息乃行。行有經紀，周有道理，與天合同，不得休止。切而調之，從虛去實，寫則不足，疾則氣減，留則先後。從實去虛，補則有餘，血氣已調，形氣乃持。余已知血氣之平與不平，未知癰疽之所從生，成敗之時，死生之期，有遠近，何以度之，可得聞乎？
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard that the intestines and stomach receive food, while the upper jiao emits qi to warm the flesh between muscles, nourish bones and joints, and promote the flow of interstitial spaces. The middle jiao emits qi like dew, which rises to the valleys and streams [of the body], permeates the collateral vessels, harmonizes bodily fluids, and transforms into red blood. When the blood is harmonized, the collateral vessels are first filled to overflow, then the qi and blood flow into the linking vessels. When these are full, they then flow into the main meridians. Once yin and yang have expanded, with each breath the blood circulates accordingly. The circulation follows a pattern and order; it completes its cycle in accordance with principles and harmonizes with heaven's rhythm, never ceasing or stopping. Carefully regulate it: remove excess from the deficient areas; if purging is excessive, deficiency results; if too rapid, qi diminishes; and if lingering too long, imbalance between before and after occurs. Remove deficiency from the excessive areas; when tonifying is excessive, excess results. Once blood and qi are properly regulated, form and vital energy will be maintained in harmony. I already understand the balanced and imbalanced states of blood and qi, but I do not yet know from where abscesses arise, when they succeed or fail, their timing for life and death, whether distant or near. How can one measure these? May I hear your explanation?

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
歧伯曰：經脈留行不止，與天同度，與地合紀。故天宿失度，日月薄蝕；地經失紀，水道流溢，草萓不成，五穀不殖；徑路不通，民不往來，巷聚邑居，則別離異處。血氣猶然，請言其故。夫血脈營衛，周流不休，上應星宿，下應經數。寒邪客於經絡之中，則血泣，血泣則不通，不通則衛氣歸之，不得復反，故癰腫。寒氣化為熱，熱勝則腐肉，肉腐則為膿。膿不寫則爛筋，筋爛則傷骨，骨傷則髓消，不當骨空，不得泄寫，血枯空虛，則筋骨肌肉不相榮，經脈敗漏，熏於五藏，藏傷故死矣。
		Qibo said: The meridians circulate ceaselessly, in harmony with the celestial order and aligned with terrestrial principles. Therefore, when heavenly constellations deviate from their proper positions, solar and lunar eclipses occur; when terrestrial principles are disrupted, waterways overflow, grasses do not grow properly, and the five grains fail to thrive; when pathways become blocked, people cannot travel freely; when they gather in alleys or settle in villages, they remain separated and isolated. Blood and qi are similarly affected—please explain the reasons. The blood vessels, nutritive and defensive qi circulate ceaselessly; they correspond above to the constellations and below to the meridian numbers. When cold pathogenic factors invade the meridians and collaterals, blood becomes stagnant; when blood stagnates, circulation is blocked. When circulation is blocked, defensive qi accumulates there and cannot return, thus causing abscesses and swellings. Cold transforms into heat; when heat prevails, it corrupts flesh, and corrupted flesh becomes pus. If pus is not drained, it will rot the tendons; when tendons rot, bones are injured. When bones are injured, marrow is consumed and cannot flow into bone cavities for elimination. Blood becomes dry and deficient, so that tendons, bones, and muscles no longer nourish each other. The meridians become damaged and leaky, spreading corruption to the five zang organs; when these organs are injured, death occurs.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
黃帝曰：願盡聞癰疽之形，與忌曰名。歧伯曰：癰發於嗌中，名曰猛疽。猛疽不治，化為膿，膿不寫，塞咽，半日死。其化為膿者，寫則合豕膏，冷食，三日而已。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I wish to hear in full about the forms of abscesses and carbuncles, as well as their names for days to be avoided. 邏 Qibo said: An abscess that arises in the throat is called Mengchu. If a Mengchu abscess is left untreated, it turns into pus; if the pus is not drained, the throat becomes blocked, and death occurs within half a day. When such an abscess turns into pus, if it is drained and treated with Shigao (a medicinal preparation), along with a cold diet, the condition will resolve within three days.

4
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於頸，名曰夭疽。其癰大以赤黑，不急治，則熱氣下入淵腋，前傷任脈，內熏肝肺。熏肝肺，十餘日而死矣。
		An abscess that arises on the neck is called Yaochu. This type of abscess appears large and dark red or black; if not treated urgently, the heat will descend into the armpit region, anteriorly injure the Ren meridian, and internally affect the liver and lungs. When the liver and lungs are affected by this corruption, death will occur within more than ten days.

5
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
陽留大發，消腦留項，名曰腦爍。其色不樂，項痛而如刺以鍼。煩心者，死不可治。
		A severe eruption of heat that consumes brain tissue and lingers at the back of the neck is called Naoshuo. Its appearance is unpleasant, with neck pain as sharp as a needle prick. If the patient experiences mental distress and agitation, it indicates an incurable condition leading to death.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於肩及臑，名曰疵癰。其狀赤黑，急治之，此令人汗出至足，不害五藏。癰發四五日，逞焫之。
		An abscess that arises on the shoulder or upper arm is called Ciyong. Its appearance is red and black; if treated urgently, this condition causes perspiration to reach the feet but does not harm the five zang organs. If an abscess develops after four or five days, it should be treated with cauterization.

7
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於腋下赤堅者，名曰米疽。治之以砭石，欲細而長，疏砭之，塗以豕膏，六日已，勿裹之。其癰堅而不潰者，為馬刀挾癭，急治之。
		An abscess that appears red and hard beneath the armpit is called Miju. It should be treated with a sharpened stone needle—fine and long in shape. Gently lance the abscess, apply Shigao (pig fat ointment), and it will resolve within six days; do not bandage or wrap it. If the abscess is hard and does not rupture, it becomes a Madao Jiaying (a type of goiter-like condition); treat it urgently.

8
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於胸，名曰井疽。其狀如大豆，三四日起，不早治，下入腹，不治，七日死矣。
		An abscess that arises on the chest is called Jingchu. It appears as large as a soybean and develops within three to four days; if not treated early, it descends into the abdomen. If left untreated, death occurs in seven days.

9
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於膺，名曰甘疽。色青，其狀如穀實𦸈𧁾，常苦寒熱，急治之，去其寒熱，十歲死，死後出膿。
		An abscess that arises on the upper chest is called Ganju. It appears bluish in color, with a shape resembling the seed of a plant. The patient often suffers from chills and fever; treat it urgently to remove these symptoms. If not treated properly, death will occur after ten days, followed by pus discharge post-mortem.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於脅，名曰敗疵。敗疵者，女子之病也，灸之，其病大癰膿，治之，其中乃有生肉，大如赤小豆，坐陵翹草根各一升，以水一斗六升煮之，竭為取三升，則強飲厚衣，坐於釜上，令汗出至足已。
		An abscess that arises on the flank is called Baici. Baici is a disease primarily affecting women. It should be treated with moxibustion; if the condition develops into a large abscess filled with pus, treatment will reveal living flesh inside as big as a red adzuki bean. Take one sheng each of the roots of Linger Qiao herb and other medicinal herbs, decoct them in sixteen sheng of water until reduced to three sheng. The patient should drink it strongly while wearing warm clothing, sit over a cauldron, and induce perspiration until it reaches the feet, thus resolving the condition.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於股脛，名曰股脛疽。其狀不甚變，而癰膿搏骨，不急治，三十日死矣。
		An abscess that arises on the thigh or lower leg is called Gutingchu. Its appearance does not change greatly, but the pus adheres to the bone; if not treated urgently, death will occur in thirty days.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於尻，名曰銳疽。其狀赤堅大，急治之，不治，三十日死矣。
		An abscess that arises at the buttocks is called Ruiju. It appears red, hard, and large; treat it urgently. If left untreated, death will occur in thirty days.

13
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於股陰，名曰赤施。不急治，六十日死。在兩股之內，不治，十日而當死。
		An abscess that arises between the thigh and genital area is called Chishi. If not treated urgently, death occurs within sixty days. An abscess located between the inner thighs, if left untreated, will result in death within ten days.

14
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於膝，名曰疵癰。其狀大，癰色不變，寒熱，如堅石，勿石，石之者死，須其柔，乃石之者，生。
		An abscess that arises on the knee is called Ciyong. Its appearance is large, and the color of the abscess does not change; the patient experiences chills and fever, with the mass as hard as a stone. Do not lance it immediately—those who do so will die. Wait until it becomes softer before lancing; only then can survival be achieved.

15
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
諸癰疽之發於節而相應者，不可治也。發於陽者，百日死；發於陰者，三十日死。
		All abscesses and carbuncles that arise at joints and correspond to each other are incurable. Those arising on the yang (exterior or upper) parts of the body will result in death within one hundred days; those arising on the yin (interior or lower) parts will lead to death within thirty days.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於脛，名曰兔嚙，其狀赤至骨，急治之，不治害人也。
		An abscess that arises on the leg is called Tune, and its appearance appears red to the bone. It must be treated urgently; if left untreated, it will harm the person.

17
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於內踝，名曰走緩。其狀癰也，色不變，數石其輸，而止其寒熱，不死。
		An abscess that arises on the inner ankle is called Zouhuan. Its appearance resembles an abscess, but its color does not change. Frequently lance the affected area and stop the chills and fever—this will prevent death.

18
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於足上下，名曰四淫。其狀大癰，急治之，百日死。
		An abscess that arises above or below the foot is called Siyin. Its appearance is a large abscess; if not treated urgently, it will result in death within one hundred days.

19
打開字典
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於足傍，名曰厲癰。其狀不大，初如小指，發，急治之，去其黑者；不消輒益，不治，百日死。
		An abscess that arises beside the foot is called Liyong. Its appearance is not large, initially resembling the size of a little finger; when it erupts, treat it urgently and remove the blackened part. If it does not subside but instead worsens, and if left untreated, death will occur within one hundred days.

20
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
發於足趾，名脫癰。其狀赤黑，死不治；不赤黑，不死。不衰，急斬之，不則死矣。
		An abscess that arises on a toe is called Tuoyong. Its appearance is red and black—this condition is fatal if left untreated; if it does not appear red and black, death will not occur. If the condition does not regress, treat it urgently by excising it; otherwise, death will result.

21
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
黃帝曰：夫子言癰疽，何以別之？歧伯曰：營衛稽留於經脈之中，則血泣而不行，不行則衛氣從之而不通，壅遏而不得行，故熱。大熱不止，熱勝，則肉腐，肉腐則為膿。然不能陷，骨髓不為燋枯，五藏不為傷，故命曰癰。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Sir, how can one distinguish between abscesses and carbuncles? Qibo said: When the nutritive and defensive qi stagnate within the meridians, blood becomes stagnant and does not flow. When blood does not flow, defensive qi follows it and also becomes obstructed; this obstruction prevents circulation, thus generating heat. If the intense heat persists without subsiding, when heat prevails over the body's defenses, flesh becomes corrupted; corrupted flesh then turns into pus. However, if the abscess does not penetrate deeply and bone marrow is not scorched or dried up, and the five zang organs are not injured, it is called a yong (abscess).

22
打開字典
顯示相似段落
顯示影印本
	癰疽:	
黃帝曰：何謂疽？歧伯曰：熱氣淳盛，下陷肌膚，筋髓枯，內連五藏，血氣竭，當其癰下，筋骨良肉皆無餘，故命曰疽。疽者，上之皮夭以堅，上如牛領之皮。癰者，其皮上薄以澤。此其候也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is meant by ju (carbuncle)? Qibo said: When intense heat is extremely severe, it penetrates deeply into the muscles and skin; tendons and marrow become dried up. Internally, it connects to the five zang organs, depleting blood and qi. In such cases, beneath the abscess, all healthy flesh of tendons and bones disappears without remainder—this condition is called ju (carbuncle). Ju presents with the skin on its upper part appearing shriveled and hard, resembling the thick hide of a bull's neck. Yong appears with thin and moist skin on its surface. These are the distinguishing signs.
